## VI A Bluff Friend / VI 直性子的朋友

===Original===
The almanac had announced spring; nature appeared quite unaware of the fact, but, so far as we were concerned, the almanac was right. Spring was the era of hope, of change, and hope was growing in our hearts like “Jack’s bean,” in spite of lowering wintry skies. We were as eager as robins, sojourning in the south, to take our flight northward.

My duties to my employers had ceased the 1st of March: I had secured tenants who would take possession of our rooms as soon as we should leave them; and now every spare moment was given to studying the problem of country living and to preparations for departure. I obtained illustrated catalogues from several dealers in seeds, and we pored over them every evening. At first they bewildered us with their long lists of varieties, while the glowing descriptions of new kinds of vegetables just being introduced awakened in us something of a gambling spirit.

“How fortunate it is,” exclaimed my wife, “that we are going to the country just as the vegetable marvels were discovered! Why, Robert, if half of what is said is true, we shall make our fortunes.”

===Chinese===
历书上早已宣告春天来临，大自然却仿佛浑然不觉；不过就我们而言，历书说得没错。春天是希望的时节、变化的时节，尽管冬日天空阴沉低垂，希望却像童话里“杰克的豆子”一样在我们心头生长。我们像客居南方的知更鸟一样，急切地盼着展翅北飞。

三月一日，我对雇主的职责便告终了；房客也已物色停当，我们一搬走，他们就搬进我们的屋子。如今，每一刻空闲都用来琢磨乡村生活这个课题，并做起行的准备。我从几家种子商那里弄来几种带插图的目录，每天晚上全家伏在灯下细细研读。起初，那长长的品种名单看得我们眼花缭乱，而那些新问世的蔬菜品种被描写得天花乱坠，在我们心里唤起了几分赌徒般的兴致。

“多巧啊，”妻子嚷道，“这些蔬菜奇品刚刚被发现，我们就要下乡了！嗳，罗伯特（Robert），只要传闻有一半是真的，我们就要发财了。”

===Original===
With us, hitherto, a beet had been a beet, and a cabbage a cabbage; but here were accounts of beets which, as Merton said, “beat all creation,” and pictures of prodigious cabbage heads which well-nigh turned our own. With a blending of hope and distrust I carried two of the catalogues to a shrewd old fellow in Washington Market. He was a dealer in country produce who had done business so long at the same stand that among his fellows he was looked upon as a kind of patriarch. During a former interview he had replied to my questions with a blunt honesty that had inspired confidence. The day was somewhat mild, and I found him in his shirtsleeves, smoking his pipe among his piled-up barrels, boxes, and crates, after his eleven o’clock dinner. His day’s work was practically over; and well it might be, for, like others of his calling, he had begun it long before dawn. Now his old felt hat was pushed well back on his bald head, and his red face, fringed with a grizzled beard, expressed a sort of heavy, placid content. His small gray eyes twinkled as shrewdly as ever. With his pipe he indicated a box on which I might sit while we talked.

“See here, Mr. Bogart,” I began, showing him the seed catalogues, “how is a man to choose wisely what vegetables he will raise from a list as long as your arm? Perhaps I shouldn’t take any of those old-fashioned kinds, but go into these wonderful novelties which promise a new era in horticulture.”

The old man gave a contemptuous grunt; then, removing his pipe, he blew out a cloud of smoke that half obscured us both as he remarked, gruffly, “ ‘A fool and his money are soon parted.’ ”

===Chinese===
在我们家里，从前的甜菜就只是甜菜，卷心菜就只是卷心菜；可如今目录里尽是些——照默顿（Merton）的话说——“盖过世上一切”的甜菜，还有那些硕大无朋的卷心菜图片，几乎把我们自己的脑袋也转晕了。我怀着希望与狐疑各半的心情，带上其中两本目录，去找华盛顿市场（Washington Market）里一个精明的老头儿。他是个经营农产品的商人，在同一个摊位上做了这么多年的买卖，在同行中间俨然被看成一位元老。先前一次见面时，他回答我的问题直来直去、毫不客套，那种坦率反倒叫人信得过。那天天气还算和暖，我到他那里，他刚吃过十一点钟的正餐，只穿着衬衫，坐在堆积的木桶、木箱和板条箱中间抽着烟斗。一天的工作实际上已经收尾；这也难怪，干他这一行的，天不亮老早就开工了。此刻他那顶旧毡帽推到了脑后，露出秃顶，红彤彤的脸膛四周围着一圈花白络腮胡，透出一种沉甸甸、安安稳稳的满足。一双灰色的小眼睛依旧闪烁着往日那股精明劲儿。他用烟斗指了指一只木箱，叫我坐下说话。

“博加特（Bogart）先生，你听我说，”我拿着种子目录开了口，“这单子长得赛过你的胳膊，叫人怎么挑得明白该种些什么菜？要依我说，那些老式品种兴许一样都不该要，干脆全种这些了不起的新品——它们可是预示着园艺新纪元的。”

老头儿轻蔑地哼了一声，接着把烟斗从嘴上拿开，喷出一团烟雾，把两个人都罩了个半透，瓮声瓮气地说：“傻瓜的钱，留不长。”

===Original===
This was about as rough as March weather; but I knew my man, and perhaps proved that I wasn’t a fool by not parting with him then and there.

“Come now, neighbor,” I said, brusquely, “I know some things that you don’t, and there are affairs in which I could prove you to be as green as I am in this matter. If you came to me I’d give you the best advice that I could, and be civil about it into the bargain. I’ve come to you because I believe you to be honest and to know what I don’t. When I tell you that I have a little family dependent on me, and that I mean if possible to get a living for them out of the soil, I believe you are man enough both to fall in with my plan and to show a little friendly interest. If you are not, I’ll go farther and fare better.”

As I fired this broadside he looked at me askance, with the pipe in the corner of his mouth, then reached out his great brown paw, and said—

“Shake.”

===Chinese===
这话来得跟三月的天气一样生硬；可我摸得清这个人的脾气，当时没有扭头就走——这一点，或许正好证明我不是傻瓜。

“得了，老街坊，”我也没好气地说，“有些事你不懂，我懂；换个别的事，我也能证明你跟我在这桩事上一样外行。你要是来找我，我会把我知道的最好主意尽力告诉你，说话还会客客气气。我来找你，是因为我相信你为人诚实，又懂我不懂的东西。我告诉你，我拖家带口，一家人都指靠着我过活，我要是想方设法从土里给一家人刨出个生计来——我相信你是个男子汉，既会成全我这份打算，也会拿出一点朋友式的热心。你要是不肯，我就另投门路，兴许还顺当些。”

我这排炮刚放完，他就斜眼瞅着我，烟斗叼在嘴角，随后伸出那只褐色的大爪子，说道——

“握个手。”

===Original===
I knew it was all right now—that the giving of his hand meant not only a treaty of peace but also a friendly alliance. The old fellow discoursed vegetable wisdom so steadily for half an hour that his pipe went out.

“You jest let that newfangled truck alone,” he said, “till you get more forehanded in cash and experience. Then you may learn how to make something out of them novelties, as they call ’em, if they are worth growing at all. Now and then a good penny is turned on a new fruit or vegetable; but how to do it will be one of the last tricks that you’ll learn in your new trade. Hand me one of them misleadin’ books, and I’ll mark a few solid kinds such as produce ninety-nine hundredths of all that’s used or sold. Then you go to What-you-call-’em’s store, and take a line from me, and you’ll git the genuine article at market gardeners’ prices.”

“Now, Mr. Bogart, you are treating me like a man and a brother.”

===Chinese===
我知道这下子全妥了——他伸手过来，不啻是缔结了和约，还结成了友好的同盟。这老头儿一板一眼地大讲种菜的学问，一口气讲了半个钟头，连烟斗都熄了。

“那些新花样，你先撇开别碰，”他说，“等你手头的钱和经验都更宽裕些再说。到那时候，要是那些——他们管叫——新玩意儿果真值得种，你再去学怎么从里头赚钱。隔三差五，是有人靠一种新果品、新菜蔬捞着好钱的；可这里头的门道，是你入新行当之后最晚才学得会的一招。把那种蒙人的书递一本给我，我给你圈出几个踏实品种——市面上用掉的、卖掉的，九成九出自这几样。然后你上那家叫什么来着的铺子，就说是我招呼的，保你能按菜农批发的价钱买到地道的正品。”

“瞧您，博加特先生，您这是拿我当人、当兄弟看待了。”

===Original===
“Oh thunder! I’m treating you like one who, p’raps, may deal with me. Do as you please about it, but if you want to take along a lot of my business cards and fasten ’em to anything you have to sell, I’ll give you all they bring, less my commission.”

“I’ve no doubt you will, and that’s more than I can believe of a good many in your line, if all’s true that I hear. You have thrown a broad streak of daylight into my future. So you see the fool didn’t part with his money, or with you either, until he got a good deal more than he expected.”

“Well, well, Mr. Durham, you’ll have to get used to my rough ways. When I’ve anything to say, I don’t beat about the bush. But you’ll always find my checks good for their face.”

===Chinese===
“嗨，得了吧！我是把你当个——没准儿——要跟我做买卖的人看待。怎么办随你的便；不过你要是愿意带上一把我的名片，凡是拿来卖的东西都给拴上一张，卖得的钱我全数给你，只扣我的佣金。”

“你这话我信。可要是把听来的传闻当真，你们这一行里的许多人，我可就不敢这么信了。你这番话，像往我的前程里投进了一大道天光。你瞧，那个傻瓜既没把钱白散掉，也没跟你散伙，直到捞到了比他指望的多得多才罢休。”

“好啦，好啦，达勒姆（Durham）先生，你得慢慢习惯我这粗人做派。有话我从不绕弯子。不过我的支票，你回回都能照票面如数兑现。”

===Original===
“Yes, and the face back of them is that of a friend to me now. We’ll shake again. Goodbye;” and I went home feeling as if I had solid ground under my feet. At supper I went over the whole scene, taking off the man in humorous pantomime, not ridicule, and even my wife grew hilarious over her disappointed hopes of the “newfangled truck.” I managed, however, that the children should not lose the lesson that a rough diamond is better than a smooth paste stone, and that people often do themselves an injury when they take offence too easily.

“I see it all, papa,” chuckled Merton; “if you had gone off mad when he the same as called you a fool, you would have lost all his good advice.”

“I should have lost much more than that, my boy, I should have lost the services of a good friend and an honest man to whom we can send for its full worth whatever we can’t sell to better advantage at home. But don’t mistake me, Merton, toadyism never pays, no matter what you may gain by it; for you give manhood for such gain, and that’s a kind of property that one can never part with and make a good bargain. You see the old man didn’t mean to be insolent. As he said, it was only his rough, blunt way of saying what was uppermost in his mind.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，如今这支票背后那张脸，在我眼里已经是一张朋友的脸了。咱们再握一回手。再见。”回到家，我只觉得脚下踏着实打实的地面。晚饭桌上，我把这场会面从头演了一遍，用滑稽的哑剧把那老头儿学得惟妙惟肖——是逗趣，不是奚落——连妻子也为她那落了空的“新花样”开怀大笑起来。不过我仍设法让孩子们牢牢记住这个道理：一颗粗粝的钻石，胜过一颗光滑的假宝石；人若动不动就动气见怪，往往是自己害了自己。

“我全明白了，爸爸，”默顿咯咯直笑，“他那话就差明说你是傻瓜了，你要是当场气冲冲地走掉，这些好主意可就全丢了。”

“孩子，丢掉的远不止这个。我会失去一位好朋友、一个诚实人的帮忙——往后家里凡是卖不出更好价钱的东西，都可以送去给他，他能照实足的价钱收下。可你别会错了我的意思，默顿，阿谀奉承从来不上算，不论能从中捞到什么；因为那样的进项，是拿人格换来的，而人格这份家当，一旦出让，就再也做不成一笔合算的买卖了。你看，老头儿并没存心无礼。他自己说了，那不过是他那粗直的性子——心里搁着什么，就直说什么罢了。”

## XXIII A Bonfire and a Feast / XXIII 篝火与盛宴

===Original===
It amused and interested me to see upon the children’s faces such an eager expectancy as they hurried through our midday meal. Nothing greater than a bonfire was in prospect, yet few costly pleasures could have afforded them such excitement. I found myself sharing in their anticipation to a degree that surprised me, and was led to ask myself why it is that outdoor pursuits often take so strong a hold upon the fancy. I recalled traits shown by one of my former employers. He was a gray-headed man, possessing great wealth and an elegant city home, while his mind was occupied by a vast and complicated business. When he learned that I was going to the country, he would often come to me, and, with kindling eyes and animated tones, talk of his chickens, cows, fruit trees and crops. He proved that the best product of his farm was the zest it brought him into his life—a zest that was failing in his other occupations and interests. What was true of him I knew to be equally so of many others to whom wealth brings no greater luxury than the ability to indulge in expensive farming. A lifetime in the city does not destroy the primal instinct which leads men to the soil nor does a handsome dividend from stocks give the unalloyed pleasure awakened by a basket of fresh eggs or fruit. This love of the earth is not earthiness, but has been the characteristic of the best and greatest minds. Washington would turn from the anxieties of a campaign and the burdens of state to read, with absorbing interest, the reports of the agent who managed his plantation, and to write out the minutest details for the overseer’s guidance.

In my limited way and sphere I was under the influence of the same impulses; and, as I looked around the table at those so dear to me, I felt that I had far more at stake. I had not come back to Nature merely to amuse myself or to gratify a taste, but to cowork with her in fulfilling the most sacred duties. With the crops of the coming years these children must be nourished and fitted for their part in life, and I felt that all my faculties must be employed to produce the best results from my open-air toil.

===Chinese===
孩子们匆匆扒完午饭，一张张小脸上满是热切的期待，那模样叫我觉得有趣，也动了心思。其实等着他们的不过是一场篝火，可再昂贵的玩乐，只怕也难得让他们这样兴奋。我自己竟也跟着盼了起来，盼得连自己都吃惊，不由得暗自思量：户外的事情，为什么常常能这样牢牢抓住人的心思？我想起从前一位雇主身上的情形。他是位头发花白的老先生，家财万贯，在城里住着雅致的宅子，头脑里装的又是一桩庞大复杂的生意。可他一听说我要下乡，就常常来找我，眼睛发亮、兴致勃勃地谈他的鸡、他的牛、他的果树和庄稼。他的例子证明：他的农场给他出的最好的一样东西，是它给他生活添进来的那股兴味——这股兴味，在他其他的营生和爱好里早已日渐枯竭。我知道，他的情形在许多旁人身上同样可见：对那些人来说，财富带来的头一号奢侈，也不过是能花钱摆弄一份阔气的农庄。在城里住上一辈子，也灭不掉人心里奔向泥土的原始本能；股票分来的红利再丰厚，也给不了一篮鲜蛋、一篮鲜果所唤起的那种纯粹的快乐。这种对土地的热爱并非贪恋尘俗，古往今来，多少最优秀、最伟大的心灵都带有这个特点。华盛顿也会撇开戎马倥偬的焦灼和治国的重担，津津有味地细读管事送来的庄园报告，一笔一笔写下细之又细的指示，好供监工遵照办理。

我虽眼界有限、天地狭小，受的却是同一股冲动的驱使；环顾饭桌旁这些我最亲爱的人，我更觉得自己身上系着远重得多的担子。我回到大自然身边，不单是为了消遣，也不单是为了满足一点雅兴，而是要同她一道劳作，去尽最神圣的职责。往后年月里的这些收成，要用来哺养这些孩子，把他们养壮实、教练好，好担起各自在人生里的角色；我只觉得，必须使出全副本领，让我露天的辛勤耕耘结出最好的果实。

===Original===
Therefore, why should not I also be interested in the prospective bonfire? It would transmute the unsightly rubbish of the place into fertilizing ashes, and clear the ground for the plow. The mellow soil would produce that which would give brain and muscle—life to those whose lives were dear.

He who spreads his table with food secured by his own hands direct from nature should feel a strong incentive to do his best. The coarse, unvaried diet, common to many farmers’ homes, is the result of stolid minds and plodding ways. A better manhood and womanhood will be developed when we act upon the truth that varied and healthful sustenance improves blood and brain, and therefore character.

I was growing abstracted, when my wife remarked, “Robert, will you deign to come back from a remote region of thought and take some rice pudding?”

===Chinese===
那么，眼前这场就要燃起的篝火，我为什么不该也惦记着它？它能把这片地方难看的垃圾化成肥田的灰烬，替犁头清出地面。松软肥沃的泥土会结出东西来，给我挚爱的亲人们供给头脑与筋骨的养分——供给生活。

凡靠自己的双手直接从大自然取得食物、摆上自家饭桌的人，都该有一股劲头，把一切做到最好。许多农家的饭食粗劣单调，那是脑筋迟钝、墨守成规的结果。只要我们照着这条真理行事——丰富而有益健康的饮食能养血健脑，进而塑造品格——就会有更硬朗的男子、更贤淑的女子成长起来。

我正想得出神，妻子开了口：“罗伯特（Robert），可否请你屈尊从遥远的思绪里回来，吃点米饭布丁？”

===Original===
“You may all fare the better for my thoughts,” I replied.

The children, however, were bolting their pudding at railroad speed, and I perceived that the time demanded action. Winnie and Bobsey wished me to light the fire at once, but I said: “No, not till mamma and Mousie are ready to come out. You must stay and help them clear away the things. When all is ready, you two shall start the blaze.”

Very soon we were all at the brush pile, which towered above our heads, and I said: “Merton, it will burn better if we climb over it and trample it down a little. It is too loose now. While we do this, Winnie and Bobsey can gather dry grass and weeds that will take fire quickly. Now which way is the wind?”

===Chinese===
“我的这些念头，说不定倒叫大家有口福呢，”我回答。

可孩子们早就在以火车飞驰的速度狼吞虎咽地对付布丁了，我看得出，光想不干的时刻已经过去，该动起来了。温妮（Winnie）和鲍勃西（Bobsey）巴不得我立刻点火，我却说：“不行，得等妈妈和小耗子（Mousie）准备好出来才行。你们俩留下，帮着把桌子收拾干净。等一切就绪，这把火就由你们俩来点。”

一转眼，我们全家都到了柴草堆前，它堆得比人还高。我说：“默顿（Merton），咱们爬上去把它踩瓷实一点，火烧起来才旺，现在这样太松了。我们踩的这工夫，温妮和鲍勃西去捡干草和枯草，那样的草见火就着。好了，现在风往哪边刮？”

===Original===
“There isn’t any wind, papa,” Merton replied.

“Let us see. Put your forefingers in your mouths, all of you, then hold them up and note which side feels the coolest.”

“This side!” cried one and another.

“Yes; and this side is toward the west; therefore, Winnie, put the dry grass here on the western side of the heap, and what air is stirring will carry the blaze through the pile.”

===Chinese===
“爸爸，没有风啊，”默顿答道。

“咱们试试看。你们都把食指伸进嘴里蘸一蘸，再竖起来，看哪一边觉得最凉。”

“这边！”这个喊一声，那个喊一声。

“对；这一边就是西边。那么，温妮，把干草放在这儿，柴堆的西面，只要有一丝风在动，火苗就能顺着烧穿整个柴堆。”

===Original===
Little hands that trembled with eagerness soon held lighted matches to the dry grass; there was a yellow flicker in the sunshine, then a blaze, a crackle, a devouring rush of flames that mounted higher and higher until, with the surrounding column of smoke, there was a conflagration which, at night, would have alarmed the countryside. The children at first gazed with awe upon the scenes as they backed farther away from the increasing heat. Our beacon fire drew Junior, who came bounding over the fences toward us; and soon he and Merton began to see how near they could dash in toward the blaze without being scorched. I soon stopped this.

“Show your courage, Merton, when there is need of it,” I said. “Rash venturing is not bravery, but foolishness, and often costs people dear.”

When the pile sank down into glowing embers, I turned to Bobsey, and added: “I have let you light a fire under my direction. Never think of doing anything of the kind without my permission, for if you do, you will certainly sit in a chair, facing the wall, all day long, with nothing to cheer you but bread and water and a sound whipping. There is one thing which you children must learn from the start, and that is, you can’t play with fire except under my eyes.”

===Chinese===
一双双小手兴奋得直发颤，很快就把点着的火柴凑向干草；阳光下闪起一朵黄色的火苗，接着腾起烈焰，响起噼啪的爆裂声，火舌贪婪地往上蹿，越蹿越高，连同四周盘旋的烟柱，汇成一场冲天大火——这要是在夜里，准会惊动四乡。孩子们起初又敬又畏地瞪眼看着，一边感到热浪渐盛，一步步往后退。我们的火堆像座烽火台，把小琼斯（Junior）也引来了，他翻过一道道篱笆，蹦蹦跳跳地朝我们奔来；不多会儿，他就和默顿较上了劲，看谁敢朝火冲得最近又不被燎着。我很快制止了这把戏。

“默顿，有需要的时候再拿出你的胆量来，”我说。“鲁莽逞能不是勇敢，是愚蠢，往往叫人付出高昂的代价。”

等柴堆塌陷下去，只剩满地通红的炭火，我转身对着鲍勃西补上一句：“我是让你在我的指点下点了一回火。往后没有我的许可，这种事你想都别想，不然的话，你就给我在椅子上坐一整天，脸朝着墙，陪你作伴的只有面包、清水和一顿结结实实的好打。你们这些孩子，有一样东西得从头学会：不经我看着，谁也不许玩火。”

===Original===
At this direful threat Bobsey looked as grave as his round little face permitted, and, with the memory of his peril in the creek fresh in mind, was ready enough with the most solemn promises. A circle of unburned brush was left around the embers. This I raked in on the hot coals, and soon all was consumed.

“Now I have a suggestion,” cried my wife. “We’ll have some roast potatoes, for here are lots of hot coals and ashes.” Away scampered Winnie to the cellar for the tubers. Our bonfire ended in a feast, and then the ashes were spread far and wide. When the exciting events were past, Winnie and Bobsey amused themselves in other ways, Mousie venturing to stay with them while the sun remained high. Merton and I meanwhile put the horse to the cart and covered all the ground, especially the upper and poorer portions, with a good dressing from the barnyard.

In the evening Junior gave Merton a good hint about angleworms. “Follow the plow,” he said, “and pick ’em up and put ’em in a tight box. Then sink the box in a damp place and nearly fill it with fine earth, and you always have bait ready when you want to go a-fishing. After a few more warm days the fish will begin to bite first-rate.”

===Chinese===
听了这句可怕的警告，鲍勃西那张圆圆的小脸摆出了所能有的最严肃的神情，加之在小溪里险遭不测的经历还记忆犹新，他把最郑重的保证满口应承下来。炭火四周还留着一圈没烧到的柴枝，我把它们统统耙到热炭上，不多时便烧了个干净。

“现在我来提个建议，”妻子嚷道，“咱们烤些土豆吃，这儿有的是热炭热灰。”温妮一溜烟跑向地窖取薯块去了。我们的篝火就这样以一场盛宴收场，随后把灰烬撒得又远又匀。热闹的节目过后，温妮和鲍勃西自去玩别的花样，只要太阳还高，小耗子也大着胆子留在那儿陪他们。这工夫，我和默顿套马驾车，把场院里的粪肥厚厚地铺满整片地——尤其是上头那几块贫瘠的。

傍晚，小琼斯给默顿出了一个捞蚯蚓的好主意。“跟着犁走，”他说，“见了就捡，捡了装进一只盖得严严实实的箱子。再把箱子埋进一块潮湿地里，里头差不多装满细土，往后你想去钓鱼，鱼饵随取随有。再暖和上几天，鱼咬钩可就要欢实起来了。”

===Original===
Early the next morning Mr. Jones was on hand with his stout team, and, going twice in every furrow, he sunk his plow to the beam. “When you loosen the soil deep in this style,” he said, “ye needn’t be afraid of dry weather unless it’s an amazin’ long spell. Why, bless you, Mr. Durham, there’s farmers around here who don’t scratch their ground much deeper than an old hen would, and they’re always groanin’ over droughts. If I can get my plow down eighteen inches, and then find time to stir the surface often in the growin’ season, I ain’t afraid of a month of dry weather.”

We followed Mr. Jones for a few turns around the garden, I inhaling the fresh wholesome odors of the soil with pleasure, and Merton and the two younger children picking up angleworms.

Our neighbor soon paused and resumed: “I guess I’ll give you a hint that’ll add bushels of pertaters to yer crop. After I’ve plowed the garden, I’ll furrow out deep a lot of rows, three feet apart. Let Merton take a hoe and scrape up the fine old manure in the barnyard. Don’t use any other kind. Then sprinkle it thickly in the furrows, and draw your hoe through ’em to mix the fertilizer well with the soil. Drop your seed then, eight inches apart in the row, and cover with four inches of dirt. One can’t do this very handy by the acre, but I’ve known such treatment to double the crop and size of the pertaters in a garden or small patch.”

===Chinese===
第二天一大早，琼斯（Jones）先生就赶着他那几匹壮实的牲口来了，每道犁沟来回犁上两遍，把犁头一直压到犁梁没进土里。“像这样把土翻得又深又松，”他说，“天旱就不必怕，除非旱得邪乎、没完没了。嘿，老天在上，达勒姆（Durham）先生，这一带有些庄稼人，刨地比老母鸡刨食深不了多少，却成天为旱灾唉声叹气。要是我能把犁压下去十八英寸，生长季节又有工夫勤翻动表土，就是旱上一个月我也不怕。”

我们跟着琼斯先生绕着园子转了几圈，我惬意地闻着泥土那清新宜人的气息，默顿和两个小的则一路捡蚯蚓。

不多会儿，我们的邻居停下来，接着说道：“我看我再给你支一招，能叫你家的土豆多收上好几蒲式耳。园子犁完之后，我再开出许多间距三英尺的深沟。叫默顿拿把锄头，把场院里细碎的陈粪搜集拢来，别的粪一概不用。然后把粪厚厚地撒进沟里，拿锄头顺着沟来回拉一遍，让肥和土和匀。接着下种，一行里每隔八英寸下一块，覆上四英寸厚的土。论亩这么干就不大顺手了，可在园子里、小片地上，我见过这么侍弄的，土豆的收成和个头都翻了一番。”

===Original===
I took the hint at once, and set Merton at work, saying that Winnie and Bobsey could gather all the worms he wanted. Then I went for a half bushel of early potatoes, and Mr. Jones showed me how to cut them so as to leave at least two good “eyes” to each piece. Half an hour later it occurred to me to see how Merton was getting on. I found him perspiring, and almost panting with fatigue, and my conscience smote me. “There, my boy,” I said, “this is too hard work for you. Come with me and I’ll show you how to cut the potatoes. But first go into the house, and cool off while you drink a glass of milk.”

“Well, papa,” he replied, gratefully, “I wouldn’t mind a change like that. I didn’t want you to think I was shirking, but, to tell the truth, I was getting played out.”

“Worked out, you mean. It’s not my wish that you should ever be either played or worked out, nor will you if you take play and work in the right degree. Remember,” I added, seriously, “that you are a growing boy, and it’s not my intention to put you at anything beyond your strength. If, in my inexperience, I do give you too hard work, tell me at once. There’s plenty to do that won’t overtax you.”

===Chinese===
我立刻采纳了这个主意，派默顿干了起来，说温妮和鲍勃西可以把他要的蚯蚓全数捡来。随后我去取了半蒲式耳早熟土豆，琼斯先生教我怎么切种薯，好让每块上至少留两个饱满的“芽眼”。半个钟头以后，我忽然想到该去看看默顿干得怎样了。只见他满头大汗，累得几乎气喘吁吁，我心里一阵过意不去。“好啦，我的孩子，”我说，“这活儿对你太重了。跟我来，我教你切土豆。不过先回屋里去，一边凉凉快快地歇着，一边喝杯牛奶。”

“好啊，爸爸，”他感激地答道，“这样的调换我求之不得。我只是不愿让您觉得我在偷懒；不瞒您说，我实在已经快‘玩儿完’了。”

“你是说快‘干儿完’了吧。玩儿完也罢，干儿完也罢，都不合我的心意；只要把玩和干调配得当，你就两样都垮不了。记住，”我郑重地补了一句，“你是个正在长身子的孩子，我决不打算派你干力所不及的事。要是我没经验，真给了你太重的活，马上告诉我。要干的活有的是，不至于压得你吃不消。”

===Original===
So we exchanged labors, and by the time the garden was plowed and the furrows were made I had scraped up enough fine material in the barnyard to give my tubers a great start. I varied my labor with lessons in plowing, for running in my head was an “old saw” to the effect that “he who would thrive must both hold the plow and drive.”

The fine weather lasted long enough for us to plant our early potatoes in the most approved fashion, and then came a series of cold, wet days and frosty nights. Mr. Jones assured us that the vegetable seeds already in the ground would receive no harm. At such times as were suitable for work we finished trimming and tying up the hardy raspberries, cleaning up the barnyard, and carting all the fertilizers we could find to the land that we meant to cultivate.

===Chinese===
于是我们交换了活计。等园子犁完、沟也开好，我在场院里已经搜集到足够的细粪肥，好叫我的种薯占个先手、开局领先。干活的当口，我还穿插着上了几课犁地，因为我脑子里正转着一句“老话”，大意是：“想发家的人，既要扶得了犁，又要赶得了车。”

这样的好天气又持续了一阵，足够我们按最合规矩的章法把早土豆种下地；随后便是一连串又冷又湿的日子，夜里还上了霜。琼斯先生叫我们放宽心，说已经入土的菜籽受不着害。趁着天气适宜做工的时候，我们给耐寒的树莓修完枝、绑扎停当，把场院收拾干净，又把能找到的肥料统统运到我们打算耕种的地里。

## V A Country Christmas in a City Flat / V 城市公寓里的乡村圣诞节

===Original===
On Christmas morning we all brought out our purchases and arranged them on a table. Merton was almost wild when he found a bright single-barreled gun with accoutrements standing in the corner. Even Mousie exclaimed with delight at the bright-colored papers of flower seeds on her plate. To Winnie were given half a dozen china eggs with which to lure the prospective biddies to lay in nests easily reached, and she tried to cackle over them in absurd imitation. Little Bobsey had to have some toys and candy, but they all presented to his eyes the natural inmates of the barnyard. In the number of domestic animals he swallowed that day he equalled the little boy in Hawthorne’s story of “The House of the Seven Gables,” who devoured a gingerbread caravan of camels and elephants purchased at Miss Hepzibah Pyncheon’s shop.

Our Christmas dinner consisted almost wholly of such vegetables as we proposed to raise in the coming summer. Never before were such connoisseurs of carrots, beets, onions, parsnips, and so on through almost the entire list of such winter stock as was to be obtained at our nearest greengrocery. We celebrated the day by nearly a dozen dishes which the children aided my wife in preparing. Then I had Merton figure the cost of each, and we were surprised at the cheapness of much of country fare, even when retailed in very small quantities.

===Chinese===
圣诞节早晨，我们把各自买的礼物统统拿了出来，在一张桌子上摆开。默顿（Merton）发现墙角立着一支锃亮的单管猎枪，连带全套枪具，欢喜得简直要发狂。连小耗子（Mousie）也对着盘子里那些色彩鲜艳的花卉种子纸包高兴得叫出声来。温妮（Winnie）得到的是六只瓷蛋，好用来引诱将来要进窝下蛋的小母鸡，让它们把蛋下在人容易够着的窝里；她还对着那几只瓷蛋煞有介事地学母鸡咯咯叫，模样滑稽极了。小鲍勃西（Bobsey）自然少不了玩具和糖果，可在他的眼里，这些东西统统成了谷仓场院里天然的家畜家禽。论这一天里吞下肚的家畜家禽数目，他足以媲美霍桑（Hawthorne）《七个尖角阁的房子》里的那个小男孩——那孩子在赫普兹芭·平琼（Hepzibah Pyncheon）小姐的铺子里买下一整队姜饼骆驼和大象，一股脑儿全吃进了肚子。

我们的圣诞晚餐，几乎全由我们打算在来年夏天亲手种出来的那类蔬菜组成。胡萝卜、甜菜、洋葱、欧洲防风——凡就近菜铺里能买到的冬季菜蔬，几乎整张单子上的每一样，他们都品评得头头是道；从前哪里见过这样的行家。为庆祝这个节日，我们张罗了差不多十二道菜，由孩子们帮着妻子一起下厨。随后我让默顿把每道菜的成本逐一算清，我们不禁惊讶：许多乡土食物竟如此便宜，哪怕按极小的分量零买，价钱也不高。

===Original===
This brought up another phase of the problem. In many respects I was like the children, having almost as much to learn as they—with the advantage, however, of being able to correct impressions by experience. In other words, I had more judgment; and while I should certainly make mistakes, not many of them would be absurd or often repeated. I was aware that most of the homely kitchen vegetables cost comparatively little, even though (having in our flat no good place for storage) we had found it better to buy what we needed from day to day. It was therefore certain that, at wholesale in the country, they would often be exceedingly cheap. This fact would work both ways: little money would purchase much food of certain kinds, and if we produced these articles of food they would bring us little money.

I will pass briefly over the period that elapsed before it was time for us to depart, assured that the little people who are following this simple history are as eager to get away from the dusty city flat to the sunlight, breezy fields, brooks, and woods as were the children in my story. It is enough to say that, during all my waking hours not devoted to business, I read, thought, and studied on the problem of supporting my family in the country. I haunted Washington Market in the gray dawn and learned from much inquiry what products found a ready and certain sale at some price, and what appeared to yield to the grower the best profits. There was much conflict of opinion, but I noted down and averaged the statements made to me. Many of the marketmen had hobbies, and told me how to make a fortune out of one or two articles; more gave careless, random, or ignorant answers; but here and there was a plain, honest, sensible fellow who showed me from his books what plain, honest, sensible producers in the country were doing. In a few weeks I dismissed finally the tendency to one blunder. A novice hears or reads of an acre of cabbages or strawberries producing so much. Then he figures, “if one acre yields so much, two acres will give twice as much,” and so on. The experience of others showed me the utter folly of all this; and I came to the conclusion that I could give my family shelter, plain food, pure air, wholesome work and play in plenty, and that not very soon could I provide much else with certainty. I tried to stick closely to common sense; and the humble circumstances of the vast majority living from the soil proved that there was in these pursuits no easy or speedy road to fortune. Therefore we must part reluctantly with every penny, and let a dollar go for only the essentials to the modest success now accepted as all we could naturally expect. We had explored the settled States, and even the Territories, in fancy; we had talked over nearly every industry from cotton and sugarcane planting to a sheep ranch. I encouraged all this, for it was so much education out of school hours; yet all, even Merton, eventually agreed with me that we had better not go far away, but seek a place near schools, markets, churches, and well inside of civilization.

===Chinese===
这就引出了问题的另一个层面。在许多方面，我同孩子们相差无几，要学的东西几乎跟他们一样多——不过我占一点便宜：能够凭经验去纠正印象。换句话说，我的判断力强些；固然免不了要出错，可荒唐的错、一错再错的情形不会多。我心里清楚，寻常的家常菜蔬价钱大多相对低廉；只是我们公寓里没有个妥当的储藏地方，所以发觉还是天天要多少买多少来得合算。因此可以断定，在乡下按批发价购进，这些东西往往便宜得出奇。这个事实有两面的作用：花一点点钱，就能买进某几类的大量食物；可我们若是自己种出这些吃食，卖上去也换不来几个钱。

动身之前的那段日子，我只略略带过，不细说了；我相信，追随这段平凡故事的小读者们，一定跟我故事里的孩子们一样，巴不得早些离开满是灰尘的城市公寓，奔向阳光普照、清风拂面的田野、溪流和树林。只须交代一句就够了：凡是醒着而不用照管生意的时辰，我都用来围绕“在乡下养活一家人”这个课题读书、思考、钻研。天色微明我就泡在华盛顿市场（Washington Market）里，多方打听，渐渐弄明白：哪些农产品总能以某种价钱迅速而稳妥地脱手，哪些看来能给种植者带来最好的收益。众说纷纭，莫衷一是，我便把听来的说法一一记下，折中取其平均。许多市场商人各有自家得意的门道，告诉你单靠一两样东西就能发家；更多的人答话敷衍了事、信口开河，或者干脆一窍不通；但这里那里总有一个朴实、诚实、明事理的家伙，肯翻开账本给我看：乡下那些同样朴实、诚实、明事理的生产者，究竟在做些什么。不出几个星期，我便最终改掉了一种易犯的糊涂毛病。新手总听人说起，或读到某处一英亩卷心菜或草莓出了多少产量，心里便盘算：“一英亩收成这么多，两英亩就能收双倍”，如此等等。别人的经验叫我看清了这种算法愚不可及；我由此得出结论：我能供给家人的，是栖身之所、粗茶淡饭、纯净的空气、充裕而有益身心的劳作和游玩；至于别样种种，短期内我未必有把握置办多少。我竭力紧扣常识不放；绝大多数靠土地过活的人处境寒微，这一事实证明，这些营生里没有一条通向财富的轻松捷径。因此，我们花掉每一个便士都得依依不舍，一块钱只肯用在那份小小成功必不可少的地方——而这份小小的成功，如今已被我们认作自然而然可以指望的全部。我们曾在幻想中游遍已建州的各个州份，甚至连准州也没有放过；从种植棉花、甘蔗到经营牧羊场，几乎各行各业都议论遍了。这一切我都加以鼓励，因为这是课外的教育；可是到头来，连默顿在内，大家终于都同意我的看法：我们最好不要走远，就在学校、市场、教堂近旁找一处地方，深深扎根于文明圈子之中。

===Original===
“See here, youngsters, you forget the most important crop of all that I must cultivate,” I said one evening.

“What is that?” they cried in chorus.

“A crop of boys and girls. You may think that my mind is chiefly on corn and potatoes. Not at all. It is chiefly on you; and for your sakes mamma and I decided to go to the country.”

===Chinese===
“你们听着，小家伙们，我必得培植的收成里头，顶要紧的一样，你们给忘啦，”一天晚上我说。

“是什么呀？”他们齐声喊道。

“一茬男孩女孩。你们也许以为我心里头装的主要是玉米和土豆。根本不是。我最惦记的是你们；正是为了你们，妈妈和我才决定搬到乡下去。”

===Original===
At last, in reply to my inquiries and my answers to advertisements, I received the following letter:—

I had been to see two or three places that had been “cracked up” so highly that my wife thought it better to close the bargain at once before someone else secured the prize—and I had come back disgusted in each instance.

“The soul of wit” was in John Jones’s letter. There was also a downright directness which hit the mark, and I wrote that I would go to Maizeville in the course of the following week.

===Chinese===
后来，回应我的四处探询和我对广告的应征信，我终于收到了下面这样一封信：——

在这之前，我去看过两三处地方，那些地方被吹捧得天花乱坠，连妻子都觉得，最好趁别人还没把这份宝贝弄到手，赶紧拍板成交——可每一回我都扫兴而归。

“简洁是智慧的灵魂”，约翰·琼斯（John Jones）的信里正有这份灵魂；字里行间还透着一股直截了当的劲儿，句句说到点子上。我便回信说，下星期之内就去梅兹维尔（Maizeville）。

## XXVI A Country Sunday / XXVI 乡间的礼拜天

===Original===
Hitherto the Sabbaths had been stormy and the roads bad, and we had given the days to rest and family sociability. But at last there came a mild, sunny morning, and we resolved to find a church home. I had heard that Dr. Lyman, who preached in the nearest village had the faculty of keeping young people awake. Therefore we harnessed the old bay horse to our market wagon, donned our "go-ter-meetin's," as Junior called his Sunday clothes, and started. Whatever might be the result of the sermon, the drive promised to do us good. The tender young grass by the roadside, and the swelling buds of trees, gave forth delicious odors; a spring haze softened the outline of the mountains, and made them almost as beautiful as if clothed with foliage; robins, song sparrows, and other birds were so tuneful that Mousie said she wished they might form the choir at the church. Indeed, the glad spirit of Spring was abroad, and it found its way into our hearts. We soon learned that it entered largely also into Dr. Lyman's sermon. We were not treated as strangers and intruders, but welcomed and shown to a pew in a way that made us feel at home. I discovered that I, too, should be kept awake and given much to think about. We remained until Sunday school, which followed the service, was over, and then went home, feeling that life both here and hereafter was something to be thankful for. After dinner, without even taking the precaution of locking the door, we all strolled down the lane and the steeply sloping meadow to our wood lot and the banks of the Moodna Creek. My wife had never seen this portion of our place before, and she was delighted with its wild beauty and seclusion. She shivered and turned a little pale, however, as she saw the stream, still high and swift, that had carried Bobsey away.

Junior joined us, and led the children to a sunny bank, from which soon came shouts of joy over the first wildflowers of the season. I placed my wife on a rock, and we sat quietly for a time, inhaling the fresh woody odors, and listening to the murmurs of the creek and the song of the birds. Then I asked: "Isn't this better than a city flat and a noisy street? Are not these birds pleasanter neighbors than the Daggetts and the Ricketts?"

Her glad smile was more eloquent than words could have been. Mousie came running to us, holding in her hand, which trembled from excitement, a little bunch of liverworts and anemones. Tears of happiness actually stood in her eyes, and she could only falter, "O mamma! just *look*!" and then she hastened away to gather more.

===Chinese===
此前那些礼拜天总是风雨不断、道路泥泞，我们便把日子用在休息与阖家团聚上。可这一回终于盼来一个和暖晴朗的早晨，我们决意去寻一处安身常往的教堂。我听说，在最近的那个村子里讲道的莱曼博士（Dr. Lyman）有一样本事，能让年轻人听道不打瞌睡。于是我们把那匹枣红老马套上赶集的马车，换上“上礼拜堂的行头”——小琼斯（Junior）就是这样称呼他礼拜天衣裳的——出发了。且不说那场布道效果如何，单是这趟驱车出游准保对我们有益。路边柔嫩的青草、枝头鼓胀的芽苞，吐出清甜的气息；一抹春霭柔化了群山的轮廓，使群山几乎美得如同披上了绿叶；知更鸟、歌雀和其他鸟儿唱得那样动听，小耗子（Mousie）说她真希望这些鸟儿能凑成教堂的唱诗班。的确，春天欢欣的灵气正弥漫四野，也悄悄潜入了我们的心田。我们很快便发现，它也大大地进了莱曼博士的讲章。人们并不把我们当作陌生人和不速之客，而是热诚相迎，引我们坐上教堂长椅，令我们有宾至如归之感。我发觉，连我自己也会听得毫无睡意，还有许多值得回味思索的东西。礼拜之后是主日学校（Sunday school），我们一直待到散课才回家，心里觉得，今生也罢，来世也罢，活着都是一件值得感谢的事。饭后，我们连锁门这样的小心防备都省了，一家人信步走下小路和陡斜的草坡，来到我们的林地和穆德纳溪（Moodna Creek）畔。这一片地方妻子从前没有见过，她满心欢喜于它的野趣与清幽。可是，当她望见那条溪水——依旧涨得又高又急、把鲍勃西（Bobsey）卷走的那条溪——时，她打了个寒噤，脸色微微发白了。

小琼斯也赶上来会合，把孩子们领向一处向阳的土坡；不多时，坡那边便传来阵阵欢呼——他们发现了今年头一茬野花。我把妻子安顿在一块岩石上，两人静静地坐了一会儿，呼吸着林间清新的气息，听溪水潺潺低语，听鸟儿声声鸣啭。这时我问道：“这总比城里的公寓和闹哄哄的街道强些吧？这些鸟儿做邻居，岂不比达格茨（Daggett）家和里克茨（Rickett）家叫人舒心？”

她那欢喜的笑容，比千言万语更有说服力。小耗子跑着来到我们跟前，手里捧着一小束獐耳细辛和银莲花，那只手兴奋得直颤。她眼里真的噙着幸福的泪花，只结结巴巴地挤出一句：“哦，妈妈！你快*看*呀！”话音未落，她又匆匆跑去采了更多的花。

===Original===
"That child belongs to nature," I said, "and would always be an exile in the city. How greatly she has improved in health already!"

The air grew damp and chill early, and we soon returned to the house. Monday was again fair, and found us absorbed in our busy life, each one having plenty to do. When it was safe to uncover the raspberries, Merton and I had not lost a moment in the task. At the time of which I write we put in stakes where they were missing, obtaining not a few of them from the wood lot. We also made our second planting of potatoes and other hardy vegetables in the garden. The plants in the kitchen window were thriving, and during mild, still days we carried them to a sheltered place without, that they might become inured to the open air.

Winnie already had three hens sitting on their nests full of eggs, and she was counting the days until the three weeks of incubation should expire, and the little chicks break their shells. One of the hens proved a fickle biddy, and left her nest, much to the child's anger and disgust. But the others were faithful, and one morning Winnie came bounding in, saying she had heard the first "peep." I told her to be patient and leave the brood until the following day, since I had read that the chicks were stronger for not being taken from the nest too soon. She had treated the mother hens so kindly that they were tame, and permitted her to throw out the empty shells, and exult over each newcomer into a brief existence.

===Chinese===
“这孩子本属于大自然，”我说，“搁在城里，她永远是个流放者。瞧瞧，她的身子骨已经好了一大截！”

空气早早地变得潮湿阴凉，我们不多时便回了家。星期一又是晴好天气，我们重新埋头于繁忙的生活，人人都有干不完的事。树莓地一等到可以揭开覆盖物的时候，我和默顿（Merton）便立刻动手，一刻也不曾耽搁。写到此处的那阵子，我们在缺桩的地方补立了支架，其中不少便是从林地里弄来的。我们还在园子里下了第二轮种，种了土豆和其他耐寒的菜蔬。厨房窗台上的秧苗长得正旺，遇上温和无风的日子，我们就把它们搬到屋外一处避风的地方，好叫它们渐渐受得住露天吹拂。

温妮（Winnie）已有三只母鸡伏在满窝的蛋上抱窝，她掰着指头数日子，巴望三个星期的孵抱期一满，小鸡雏便啄破蛋壳。谁知其中一只母鸡是个三心二意的家伙，竟弃了窝，气得这孩子又恼又嫌。好在另外两只忠心耿耿。一天早晨，温妮一路蹦跳着进屋来，说她听见了头一声“唧唧”。我嘱咐她耐心些，让这一窝再留到第二天，因为我读过，小鸡雏不过早挪出窝，会长得更壮实。她素来把鸡妈妈们待得极好，母鸡都让她喂驯了，任凭她拣出空蛋壳丢掉，对着每一个新降临世间、注定一世短促的小家伙欢天喜地。

===Original===
Our radishes had come up nicely; but no sooner had the first green leaves expanded than myriads of little flea-like beetles devoured them. A timely article in my horticultural paper explained that if little chickens were allowed to run in the garden they would soon destroy these and other insects. Therefore I improvised a coop by laying down a barrel near the radishes and driving stakes in front of it to confine the hen, which otherwise, with the best intentions, would have scratched up all my sprouting seeds. Hither we brought her the following day, with her downy brood of twelve, and they soon began to make themselves useful. Winnie fed them with Indian meal and mashed potatoes and watched over them with more than their mother's solicitude, while Merton renewed his vigilance against hawks and other enemies.

With this new attraction, and wildflowers in the woods, the tying up of raspberries became weary prose to Winnie and Bobsey; but I kept them at it during most of the forenoon of every pleasant day and if they performed their task carelessly, I made them do it over. I knew that the time was coming when many kinds of work would cease to be play to us all, and that we might as well face the fact first as last. After the morning duties were over, and the afternoon lessons learned, there was plenty of time for play, and the two little people enjoyed it all the more.

Merton, also, had two afternoons in the week and he and Junior began to bring home strings of sweet little sunfish and winfish. Boys often become disgusted with country life because it is made hard and monotonous for them.

===Chinese===
我们的小萝卜出苗出得很好；可头一批绿叶刚一舒展，无数跳蚤似的小甲虫便把它们啃了个精光。我那份园艺报上适时登出一篇文章，说只要放几只小鸡到园子里跑动，它们很快便能消灭这类小虫和其他害虫。于是我因陋就简搭了个鸡圈：在小萝卜地旁放倒一只木桶，又在桶前钉上几根木桩，把母鸡圈在里头——不然的话，这位满怀好心的鸡妈妈非把我刚出芽的种子全刨出来不可。第二天，我们把她连同她那一窝十二只毛茸茸的小鸡雏一起搬到那儿，它们很快便开始派上用场。温妮用玉米面和捣烂的土豆喂它们，照看它们的那份牵挂比鸡妈妈自己还要周到；默顿则重新警觉起来，防备老鹰和其他敌人。

有了这桩新鲜乐子，再加上林子里处处野花，给树莓绑枝的活计，在温妮和鲍勃西眼里就成了一篇冗长乏味的散文。可每逢晴朗的日子，我照旧让他们把上午的大部分时光耗在这上头；活儿要是干得马虎，我就叫他们返工重做。我知道，那样的时辰终会到来：许多种活计对我们全家都不再是游戏；这个事实，与其最后面对，不如现在就面对。上午的活儿料理完，下午的功课也温熟了，剩下的辰光尽够玩耍，两个小人儿反倒玩得更加尽兴。

默顿也一样，每星期有两个下午归他支配，他和小琼斯开始往家拎回一串串鲜美的小太阳鱼和文鱼（winfish）。男孩子们之所以常常对乡村生活心生厌倦，多半是因为大人把日子弄得又苦又单调。

## XII A March Evening in Eden / XII 伊甸园的三月之夜

===Original===
At length we were fast at the Maizeville Landing, although long after the usual hour of arrival. I was anxious indeed to learn whether John Jones would meet us, or whether, believing that we would not come in such a storm, and tired of waiting, he had gone home and left us to find such shelter as we could.

But there he was, looking in the light of the lanterns as grizzled as old Time himself, with his eyebrows and beard full of snowflakes. He and I hastily carried the three younger children ashore through the driving snow, and put them in a corner of the storehouse, while Merton followed with his mother.

“Mr. Jones,” I exclaimed, “you are a neighbor to be proud of already. Why didn’t you go home and leave us to our fate?”

===Chinese===
我们总算在梅兹维尔码头（Maizeville Landing）靠稳了岸，只是比平常到站的时辰晚了许久。我心里着实焦急，很想知道约翰·琼斯（John Jones）究竟来没来接我们；还是说他认定我们不会在这样的暴风雪里动身，等得没了耐心，早已回家去了，撂下我们自己去寻个力所能及的落脚处。

可他就在那儿——灯笼的光影里，他须眉上挂满雪花，灰白苍老，活像光阴老人本人。我和他冒着扑面的风雪，急急忙忙把三个小的孩子抱上岸，安置在货栈的一个角落里，默顿（Merton）跟着母亲随后也下了船。

“琼斯先生，”我叫道，“有这样一位邻居，已经值得骄傲了。您怎么不回家去，任凭我们听天由命呢？”

===Original===
“Well,” he replied, laughing, “ ’twouldn’t take you long to get snowed under tonight. No, no; when I catch fish I mean to land ’em. Didn’t know but what in such a buster of a storm you might be inclined to stay on the boat and go back to the city. Then where would my bargain be?”

“No fear of that. We’re in for it now—have enlisted for the war. What shall we do?”

“Well, I vow I hardly know. One thing first, anyhow—we must get Mrs. Durham and the kids into the warm waiting room, and then look after your traps.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，”他笑着回答，“今儿晚上，用不了多大工夫你们就得让雪给埋了。不行，不行；我下的钩钓着的鱼，就非得弄上岸来不可。我就怕你们碰上这么一场狂暴的风雪，兴许一动念，干脆待在船上回城里去了。那我这桩买卖上哪儿找去？”

“这倒不用担心。我们如今是义无反顾了——等于应征入伍，誓要打完这场仗。你看我们该怎么办？”

“这个，说实话我也说不好。不管怎样，先办一件事——得让达勒姆太太（Mrs. Durham）和孩子们进暖和的候车室待着，然后再照应你们的行李。”

===Original===
The room was already crowded, but we squeezed them in, white from scarcely more than a moment’s exposure to the storm. Then we took hold and gave the deckhands a lift with my baggage, Merton showing much manly spirit in his readiness to face the weather and the work. My effects were soon piled up by themselves, and then we held a council.

“Mrs. Durham’ll hardly want to face this storm with the children,” began Mr. Jones.

“Are you going home?” I asked.

===Chinese===
屋里本已挤得满满当当，可我们还是把他们塞了进去；一家人不过在风雪里站了一小会儿，脸就已经冻白了。接着我们搭把手，帮甲板工人搬我的行李；默顿毫不畏缩，迎着风雪抢活干，很显出几分男子汉的气概。我的家什很快单独堆作一堆，随后我们便商量开了。

“达勒姆太太多半不愿意带着孩子们闯这样一场风雪，”琼斯先生开了口。

“您这是要回家去吗？”我问。

===Original===
“Yes, sir. I’d rather travel all night for the sake of being home in the morning.”

“To tell the truth I feel the same way,” I continued, “but reason must hold the reins. Do you think you could protect Mrs. Durham and the children from the storm?”

“Yes, I think we could tuck ’em in so they’d scarcely know it was snowin’, and then we could sled your things up in the mornin’. ’Commodations on the landin’ tonight will be pretty crowded.”

“We’ll let her decide, then.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，先生。为着一早到家，我宁可摸黑走上一整夜。”

“不瞒您说，我也是这个心思，”我接着说，“可理智必须握住缰绳。您看，您能把达勒姆太太和孩子们护得严严实实、不遭这场风雪的罪吗？”

“能。我想咱们能把他们裹掖得妥妥帖帖，叫他们几乎觉不出天上下雪；明儿一早，再把你们的东西用雪橇拉上去。码头上今晚的住处可挤得够呛。”

“那就让她自己拿主意。”

===Original===
When I explained how things were and what Mr. Jones had said, she exclaimed, “Oh, let us go home.”

How my heart jumped at her use of the word “home” in regard to a place that she had never seen. “But, Winifred,” I urged, “do you realize how bad a night it is? Do you think it would be safe for Mousie?”

“It isn’t so very cold if one is not exposed to the wind and snow,” she replied, “and Mr. Jones says we needn’t be exposed. I don’t believe we’d run as much risk as in going to a little hotel, the best rooms of which are already taken. Since we can do it, it will be so much nicer to go to a place that we feel is our own!”

===Chinese===
我把眼下的情形和琼斯先生的话一说，她就嚷了起来：“哦，咱们回家吧。”

听她把那个她从未见过的地方叫作“家”，我的心猛地一跳。“可是，温妮弗蕾德（Winifred），”我提醒她，“你可想清楚了，今夜的天有多坏？让小耗子（Mousie）冒这个险，妥当吗？”

“只要不迎着风雪，天其实不算太冷，”她答道，“琼斯先生也说了，咱们用不着挨冻。我看好歹比去那家小旅馆冒的险小，那儿的上房早叫人占下了。既然能做到，去一个咱们觉得是自个儿家的地方，可就好得多了！”

===Original===
“I must say that your wishes accord with mine.”

“Oh, I knew that,” she replied, laughing. “Mr. Jones,” she added, sociably, “this man has a way of telling you what he wishes by his looks before asking your opinion.”

“I found that out the day he came up to see the place,” chuckled my neighbor, “and I was half a mind to stick him for another hundred for being so honest. He don’t know how to make a bargain any more than one of the children there. Well, I’ll go to the shed and get the hosses, and we’ll make a pull for home. I don’t believe you’ll be sorry when you get there.”

===Chinese===
“我不能不说，你的愿望正合我的心意。”

“哦，这我知道，”她笑着回答。又亲亲热热地添了一句：“琼斯先生，这个人有个本事，还没开口问你，眼睛里先把他的心思亮给你看了。”

“他头一回上来瞧这个地方那天，我就看出来了，”我的邻居抿嘴直乐，“就冲他这份老实，我当时都起过意，多宰他一百块钱。论做买卖讲价钱，他还不抵那边随便哪个孩子在行。得，我这就去棚里牵马套车，咱们铆足了劲往家赶。我不信你们到了那儿会后悔。”

===Original===
Mr. Jones came around to the very door with the rockaway, and we tucked my wife and children under the buffalo robes and blankets till they could hardly breathe. Then we started out into the white, spectral world, for the wind had coated everything with the soft, wet snow. On we went at a slow walk, for the snow and mud were both deep, and the wheeling was very heavy. Even John Jones’s loquacity was checked, for every time he opened his mouth the wind half filled it with snow. Someone ahead of us, with a lantern, guided our course for a mile or so through the dense obscurity, and then he turned off on another road. At first I hailed one and another in the black cavern of the rockaway behind me, and their muffled voices would answer, “All right.” But one after another they ceased to answer me until all were fast asleep except my wife. She insisted that she was only very drowsy, but I knew that she was also very, very tired. Indeed, I felt myself, in a way that frightened me, the strange desire to sleep that overcomes those long exposed to cold and wind.

I must have been nodding and swaying around rather loosely, when I felt myself going heels over head into the snow. As I picked myself up I heard my wife and children screaming, and John Jones shouting to his horses, “Git up,” while at the same time he lashed them with his whip. My face was so plastered with snow that I could see only a dark object which was evidently being dragged violently out of a ditch, for when the level road was reached, Mr. Jones shouted, “Whoa!”

“Robert, are you hurt?” cried my wife.

===Chinese===
琼斯先生把四轮轻便马车一直赶到门口，我们把我妻子和孩子们用野牛皮袍子和毯子裹了个严实，裹得他们几乎透不过气来。随后我们便驶进那个白茫茫、影绰绰的世界，风已经给万物都罩上了一层又软又湿的雪。我们一路缓缓地走，雪深泥也深，车轮转得十分沉重。就连约翰·琼斯那张闲不住的嘴也闭上了——他每回一张口，风就往他嘴里灌半口雪。前头有个提灯笼的人，在浓得化不开的黑暗里为我们引了约莫一英里的路，随后便拐上另一条道去了。起初，我不住声地朝身后那节黑洞洞的车厢里一个个招呼，闷声闷气的应答便会传来：“没事儿。”可是他们一个接一个地不再应声，到后来除了我妻子，全都睡熟了。她一口咬定自己只是有点儿犯困，可我知道，她是实在累极了。说实话，连我自己也感觉到一股奇怪的睡意渐渐压来——那正是久冒风寒之人摆脱不掉的困意——这感觉吓得我心里发毛。

我多半是迷迷糊糊打着盹儿，身子松松垮垮地东倒西歪，忽觉一个倒栽葱栽进了雪里。我爬起身来，只听妻子和孩子们连声惊叫，约翰·琼斯正朝他的马吆喝：“驾！”一面挥鞭猛抽。我的脸上糊满了雪，什么也看不清，只望见一团黑影，分明正被猛力地从沟里往外拖；等车上了平路，琼斯先生才吆喝一声：“吁——！”

“罗伯特（Robert），你伤着没有？”妻子喊道。

===Original===
“No, are you?”

“Not a bit, but I’m frightened to death.”

Then John Jones gave a hearty guffaw and said:

“I bet you our old shanghai rooster that you don’t die.”

“Take you up,” answered my wife, half laughing and half crying.

===Chinese===
“没有。你呢？”

“一点儿没伤着，可我吓了个半死。”

这时约翰·琼斯放声大笑，说道：

“我拿咱们那只上海种的老公鸡跟你打赌，赌你死不了。”

“这个赌我接了，”妻子答道，一半是笑，一半是哭。

===Original===
“Where are we?” I asked.

“I’m here. Haven’t the remotest idea where you be,” replied Mr. Jones.

“You are a philosopher,” I said, groping my way through the storm toward his voice.

“I believe I was a big fool for tryin’ to get home such a night as this; but now that we’ve set about it, we’d better get there. That’s right. Scramble in and take the reins. Here’s my mittens.”

===Chinese===
“咱们这是在哪儿？”我问。

“我在这儿。你们在哪儿，我可一点儿谱儿也没有，”琼斯先生答道。

“你真是位哲学家，”我说着，在风雪里摸索着朝他声音的方向挪过去。

“我琢磨着，挑这么个夜晚赶路回家，我准是个头号的大傻瓜；可既然已经动身在半道上了，那就索性赶到家。对，就这么着。爬上来，把缰绳接过去。手套给你。”

===Original===
“What are you going to do?”

“I’m going to ’light and smell out the road. This is equal to any blizzard I’ve read of out West.”

“How far have we to go now?”

“Half a mile, as nigh as I can make out;” and we jogged on again.

===Chinese===
“那你干什么去？”

“我下去探路，拿鼻子把道儿闻出来。这场风雪，比我听说过的西部任何一场暴风雪都不差。”

“咱们还得走多远？”

“照我估摸，还有半英里；”我们又颠颠儿地朝前挪。

===Original===
“Are you sure you are not hurt?” Mousie asked me.

“Sure; it was like tumbling into a feather bed.”

“Stop a bit,” cried Mr. Jones. “There’s a turn in the road here. Let me go on a little and lay out your course.”

===Chinese===
“你当真没伤着吗？”小耗子问我。

“当真；就像一头栽进了鸭绒褥子里。”

“等一等，”琼斯先生喊道。“这儿道上有个拐弯。让我先往前走一段，给你们探好该走的道。”

===Original===
“Oh, I wish we had stayed anywhere under shelter,” said my wife.

“Courage,” I cried. “When we get home, we’ll laugh over this.”

“Now,” shouted Mr. Jones, “veer gradually off to the left toward my voice—all right;” and we jogged on again, stopping from time to time to let our invisible guide explore the road.

Once more he cried, “Stop a minute.”

===Chinese===
“唉，但凡找个有遮掩的地方待着不动，也比这强，”妻子说。

“鼓起劲来，”我喊道。“等到了家，这桩险事就成了咱们的笑谈了。”

“好了，”琼斯先生高声喊道，“朝左一点一点偏过去，朝我声音这边来——行了；”我们又颠颠儿地走起来，时不时停一停，好让我们这位看不见的向导去探路。

他又喊了一声：“停一停。”

===Original===
The wind roared and shrieked around us, and it was growing colder. With a chill of fear I thought, “Could John Jones have mistaken the road?” and I remembered how four people and a pair of horses had been frozen within a few yards of a house in a Western snowstorm.

“Are you cold, children?” I asked.

“Yes, I’m freezing,” sobbed Winnie. “I don’t like the country one bit.”

“This *is* different from the Eden of which we have been dreaming,” I thought grimly. Then I shouted, “How much farther, Mr. Jones?”

===Chinese===
风在我们四周呼啸怒号，天也越来越冷。一股寒彻骨髓的恐惧攫住了我：约翰·琼斯会不会认错了道？我想起在西部的一场暴风雪里，曾有四个人和两匹马冻死在离一幢房子不过几步远的地方。

“孩子们，冷不冷？”我问。

“冷，我都冻僵了，”温妮（Winnie）抽抽搭搭地说。“乡下我一点儿也不喜欢。”

“这可真不是我们朝思暮想的那个伊甸园（Eden），”我不无苦涩地想。随即我高声喊：“还有多远，琼斯先生？”

===Original===
The howling of the wind was my only answer. I shouted again. The increasing violence of the tempest was the only response.

“Robert,” cried my wife, “I don’t hear Mr. Jones’s voice.”

“He has only gone on a little to explore,” I replied, although my teeth chattered with cold and fear.

“Halloo—oo!” I shouted. The answering shriek of the wind in the trees overhead chilled my very heart.

===Chinese===
回答我的只有风的呼啸。我又喊了一遍。回应我的，是越发狂暴的风雪。

“罗伯特，”妻子喊道，“我听不见琼斯先生的声音了。”

“他不过是往前走了几步去探路，”我回答说，虽然我的牙齿冻得和吓得咯咯直打颤。

“喂——哦！”我放声大喊。头顶树梢间风声的尖啸算是给我的回音，直凉到我的心底。

===Original===
“What *has* become of Mr. Jones?” asked my wife, and there was almost anguish in her tone, while Winnie and Bobsey were actually crying aloud.

“Well, my dear,” I tried to say, reassuringly, “even if he were very near to us we could neither see nor hear him.”

Moments passed which seemed like ages, and I scarcely knew what to do. The absence of all signs of Mr. Jones filled me with a nameless and unspeakable dread. Could anything have happened to him? Could he have lost his way and fallen into some hole or over some steep bank? If I drove on, we might tumble after him and perish, maimed and frozen, in the wreck of the wagon. One imagines all sorts of horrible things when alone and helpless at night.

===Chinese===
“琼斯先生到底出什么事了？”妻子问，话音里几乎透着悲声；温妮和鲍勃西（Bobsey）更是放声大哭起来。

“哎，亲爱的，”我竭力把话说得宽心些，“就算他离咱们很近，咱们也一样看不见他、听不见他。”

一刻又一刻过去，恍如隔了经年，我几乎不知道怎么办才好。琼斯先生音信全无，我心里涌起一种无名而难以言表的恐惧。他会不会出了事？会不会迷了路，掉进了什么窟窿，或是跌下了哪处陡坡？我若赶车往前闯，兴许会跟着他一同翻下去，落得车毁人伤，冻毙在雪窝里。人在黑夜里孤立无援的时候，什么骇人的情形都会想得出来。

===Original===
“Papa,” cried Merton, “I’ll get out and look for Mr. Jones.”

“You are a good, brave boy,” I replied. “No; you hold the reins, and I’ll look for him and see what is just before us.”

At that moment there was a glimmer of light off to the left of us.

===Chinese===
“爸爸，”默顿喊道，“我下去找琼斯先生。”

“真是个好孩子，有胆量，”我答道。“不行；缰绳你来握着，我去找他，顺便看看咱们眼前到底是什么。”

就在这时，左前方闪现出一点微光。

## IV A Momentous Expedition / IV 一次重大的远征

===Original===
By the time Christmas week began we all had agreed to do without candy, toys, and knickknacks, and to buy books that would tell us how to live in the country. One happy evening we had an early supper and all went to a well-known agricultural store and publishing house on Broadway, each child almost awed by the fact that I had fifteen dollars in my pocket which should be spent that very night in the purchase of books and papers. To the children the shop seemed like a place where tickets direct to Eden were obtained, while the colored pictures of fruits and vegetables could portray the products of Eden only, so different were they in size and beauty from the specimens appearing in our market stalls. Stuffed birds and animals were also on the shelves, and no epicure ever enjoyed the gamy flavor as we did. But when we came to examine the books, their plates exhibiting almost every phase of country work and production, we felt like a long vista leading toward our unknown home was opening before us, illumined by alluring pictures. To Winnie was given a book on poultry, and the cuts representing the various birds were even more to her taste than cuts from the fowls themselves at a Christmas dinner. The Nimrod instincts of the race were awakened in Merton, and I soon found that he had set his heart on a book that gave an account of game, fish, birds, and mammals. It was a natural and wholesome longing. I myself had felt it keenly when a boy. Such country sport would bring sturdiness to his limbs and the right kind of color into his face.

“All right, Merton,” I said: “you shall have the book and a breech-loading shotgun also. As for fishing tackle, you can get along with a pole cut from the woods until you have earned money enough yourself to buy what you need.”

===Chinese===
圣诞周开始的时候，我们全家已经商量妥当：糖果、玩具、小摆设一概不要，改买那些能教我们怎样在乡下过日子的书。一个快活的晚上，我们早早吃过晚饭，一家人前往百老汇（Broadway）上一家大名鼎鼎的农业书店兼出版社；我口袋里装着十五块钱，当晚就要统统花在书刊上，这桩大事让每个孩子都几乎生出几分敬畏。在孩子们眼里，这家铺子简直像个发售伊甸园（Eden）直达车票的地方；那些印得色彩鲜艳的果蔬图样，画的无非是伊甸园的出产——同我们市场摊子上见到的样品相比，它们的大小和美色真有天壤之别。架子上还摆着鸟类和兽类的标本；要说品咂那股野味儿，再讲究的老饕也不如我们津津有味。可等到翻开书细看，书中插图几乎把乡间劳作与生产的方方面面一一展示出来，我们便觉得，一条长长的通景正在眼前展开，笔直通向我们那尚不知坐落何处的新家，一路都被动人的图画照得亮堂堂。温妮（Winnie）分到一本讲家禽的书；书中各色禽鸟的版画，比圣诞晚餐上从禽鸟身上切下来的肉还合她的心意。默顿（Merton）身上，那股祖传的宁录（Nimrod，圣经中的著名猎手）式本能被唤醒了；我很快发觉，他一心惦记上了一本书，里头讲的是猎物、鱼、飞禽和走兽。这种渴望出于天性，也有益身心。我自己做男孩的时候，就曾感同身受。这样的乡间游猎，会让他的四肢更结实，脸上添上那种恰到好处的血色。

“行，默顿，”我说，“这本书归你，再添一支后膛装填的猎枪。至于渔具嘛，你先用林子里砍来的竿子凑合，等自己挣够了钱，再买你需要的。”

===Original===
The boy was almost overwhelmed. He came to me, and took my hand in both his own.

“O papa,” he faltered, and his eyes were moist, “did you say a gun?”

“Yes, a breech-loading shotgun on one condition—that you’ll not smoke till after you are twenty-one. A growing boy can’t smoke in safety.”

He gave my hand a quick, strong pressure, and was immediately at the farther end of the store, blowing his nose suspiciously. I chuckled to myself: “I want no better promise. A gun will cure him of cigarettes better than a tract would.”

===Chinese===
这孩子简直承受不住了。他走到我跟前，用双手握住我的一只手。

“哦，爸爸，”他声音发颤，眼里噙着泪，“您真的说，要给我一支枪？”

“是的，一支后膛装填的猎枪，但有一个条件——二十一岁之前不许抽烟。正在长身体的孩子抽烟，没有不出岔子的。”

他把我的手飞快地用力一捏，随即一溜烟到了店铺另一头，背对着人直擤鼻子，瞧那样子分明另有一番缘故。我心里暗暗好笑：“再好的保证我也不稀罕了。治他抽香烟的毛病，一支枪比一册劝善小书管用得多。”

===Original===
Mousie was quiet, as usual; but there was again a faint color in her cheeks, a soft lustre in her eyes. I kept near my invalid child most of the time, for fear that she would go beyond her strength. I made her sit by a table, and brought the books that would interest her most. Her sweet, thin face was a study, and I felt that she was already enjoying the healing caresses of Mother Nature. When we started homeward she carried a book about flowers next to her heart.

Bobsey taxed his mother’s patience and agility, for he seemed all over the store at the same moment, and wanted everything in it, being sure that fifteen dollars would buy all and leave a handsome margin; but at last he was content with a book illustrated from beginning to end with pigs.

What pleased me most was to see how my wife enjoyed our little outing. Wrapped up in the children, she reflected their joy in her face, and looked almost girlish in her happiness. I whispered in her ear, “Your present shall be the home itself, for I shall have the deed made out in your name, and then you can turn me out of doors as often as you please.”

===Chinese===
小耗子（Mousie）照例安安静静；只是两颊又泛起淡淡的红晕，眼睛里有了柔和的光。我多半时候都守着我这个多病的孩子，生怕她兴致太高，撑坏了身子。我让她在桌边坐下，把最能引起她兴趣的书一本本取来。她那甜美瘦削的小脸实在耐看，我感到，大自然母亲那抚慰人心的爱抚，她已经在领受了。动身回家时，她把一本讲花草的书紧紧搂在心口。

鲍勃西（Bobsey）可把妈妈的耐心和腿脚都考较够了：他仿佛同一时刻遍布店里每个角落，店里件件东西都想要，还满有把握，认为十五块钱买遍全店之后还能富富裕裕剩下一大截；到头来，一本书从头到尾画满了猪，他就心满意足了。

最让我高兴的，是看妻子如何享受我们这趟小小的出游。她一心扑在孩子身上，脸上映着他们的欢乐，幸福得几乎有了少女的模样。我贴着她耳朵悄声说：“你的礼物就是那个家本身——我会把地契写在你的名下，往后你高兴，随时可以把我赶到门外去。”

===Original===
“Which will be every pleasant day after breakfast,” she said, laughing. “You know you are very safe in giving things to me.”

“Yes, Winifred,” I replied, pressing her hand on the sly; “I have been finding that out ever since I gave myself to you.”

===Chinese===
“那自然是天天吃过早饭之后喽，”她笑着说，“你知道的，东西交到我手里，万无一失。”

“是啊，温妮弗蕾德（Winifred），”我偷偷握了握她的手，答道，“自从我把自己交给你那天起，我就一直在领教这一点了。”

===Original===
I bought Henderson’s *Gardening for Profit* and some other practical books. I also subscribed for a journal devoted to rural interests and giving simple directions for the work of each month. At last we returned. Never did a jollier little procession march up Broadway. People were going to the opera and evening companies, and carriages rolled by, filled with elegantly dressed ladies and gentlemen; but my wife remarked, “None of those people are so happy as we are, trudging in this roundabout way to our country home.”

Her words suggested our course of action during the months which must intervene before it would be safe or wise for us to leave the city. Our thoughts, words, and actions were all a roundabout means to our cherished end, and yet the most direct way that we could take under the circumstances. Field and garden were covered with snow, the ground was granitelike from frost, and winter’s cold breath chilled our impatience to be gone; but so far as possible we lived in a country atmosphere, and amused ourselves by trying to conform to country ways in a city flat. Even Winnie declared she heard the cocks crowing at dawn, while Bobsey had a different kind of grunt or squeal for every pig in his book.

===Chinese===
我买了亨德森（Henderson）的《园艺生利》（Gardening for Profit）和另外几本实用的书，还订了一份专门关注乡间事务、按月简单指点农事的刊物。最后我们动身回家。百老汇上还从没走过一支更快活的小小队伍。人们正赶去歌剧院和晚间聚会，一辆辆马车辘辘驶过，载满衣着华贵的男男女女；我妻子却说：“那些人没有一个比得上我们快活——我们正绕着弯儿、一步一步走向我们的乡间新家呢。”

她这句话，恰好点明了此后几个月我们行事的方针——离开城之前必得捱过的那段时日里，唯有等到稳妥明智之时才动得身。我们的所思、所言、所行，都是通向那个心爱目标的一条迂回之路，却也是眼下情形所能采取的最直的路。田野和园圃盖着积雪，地面冻得花岗岩一般硬，冬天的寒气直往我们那股巴不得立刻启程的急切劲儿上浇；但我们尽可能生活在乡土的气息里，在城里的公寓中学着按乡下人的规矩过日子，自得其乐。连温妮都宣称，她听见黎明的鸡叫了；鲍勃西呢，他书里的每一头猪，他都能学出一种不同的哼哼唧唧。

## I A Problem / I 一个难题

===Original===
“Where are the children?”

“They can’t be far away,” replied my wife, looking up from her preparations for supper. “Bobsey was here a moment ago. As soon as my back’s turned he’s out and away. I haven’t seen Merton since he brought his books from school, and I suppose Winnie is upstairs with the Daggetts.”

“I wish, my dear, you could keep the children at home more,” I said, a little petulantly.

“I wish you would go and find them for me now, and tomorrow take my place—for just one day.”

“Well, well,” I said, with a laugh that had no mirth in it; “only one of your wishes stands much chance of being carried out. I’ll find the children now if I can without the aid of the police. Mousie, do you feel stronger tonight?”

These words were spoken to a pale girl of fourteen, who appeared to be scarcely more than twelve, so diminutive was her frame.

===Chinese===
“孩子们都上哪儿去了？”

“跑不远的，”妻子应声道，一面抬头张望，手里的晚饭还没张罗完。“鲍勃西（Bobsey）刚才还在这儿呢。我的背刚一转过去，他就溜出门跑没影了。默顿（Merton）打从学校把书本拿回来之后，我就没再见着他；温妮（Winnie）嘛，我猜是在楼上达格茨（Daggett）家里。”

“亲爱的，我真希望你能叫孩子们多待在家里，”我说，语气有点不耐烦。

“我倒希望你这会儿就去替我把他们找回来，明天再替我当家——就一天。”

“好啦，好啦，”我说，笑里却没有半点笑意；“你许的两个愿，多半只有一个还有几分指望兑现。我现在就去把孩子们找回来，但愿不必惊动警察。小耗子（Mousie），你今晚觉得好些了吗？”

这话是对一个面色苍白的十四岁女孩说的；她身量那么瘦小，看上去简直还不到十二岁。

===Original===
“Yes, papa,” she replied, a faint smile flitting like a ray of light across her features. She always said she was better, but never got well. Her quiet ways and tones had led to the household name of “Mousie.”

As I was descending the narrow stairway I was almost overthrown by a torrent of children pouring down from the flats above. In the dim light of a gas burner I saw that Bobsey was one of the reckless atoms. He had not heard my voice in the uproar, and before I could reach him, he with the others had burst out at the street door and gone tearing toward the nearest corner. It seemed that he had slipped away in order to take part in a race, and I found him “squaring off” at a bigger boy who had tripped him up. Without a word I carried him home, followed by the jeers and laughter of the racers, the girls making their presence known in the early December twilight by the shrillness of their voices and by manners no gentler than those of the boys.

I put down the child—he was only seven years of age—in the middle of our general living room, and looked at him. His little coat was split out in the back; one of his stockings, already well-darned at the knees, was past remedy; his hands were black, and one was bleeding; his whole little body was throbbing with excitement, anger, and violent exercise. As I looked at him quietly the defiant expression in his eyes began to give place to tears.

“There is no use in punishing him now,” said my wife. “Please leave him to me and find the others.”

“I wasn’t going to punish him,” I said.

“What are you going to do? What makes you look at him so?”

===Chinese===
“嗯，爸爸，”她答道，一抹淡淡的微笑掠过脸庞，像一缕微光。她总说自己好些了，却从来没有好起来过。她那文静的举止、轻柔的语声，替她赢来了全家叫开的诨名——“小耗子”。

我正顺着狭窄的楼梯往下走，一股从楼上各户公寓倾泻而下的孩子洪流，险些把我冲了个趔趄。在煤气灯昏黄的光影里，我认出鲍勃西正是那伙天不怕地不怕的小原子中的一个。喧闹声中他没听见我的喊声，还没等我够着他，他就同别人一道冲出临街大门，朝着最近的街角飞奔而去。看来他是偷跑出去参加赛跑的；我找到他时，他正冲着一个绊倒了他的大孩子“摆开拳脚”。我一言不发，抱起他往家走，身后跟着赛跑孩子们的一片嘲弄哄笑；十二月初的薄暮里，女孩子们用尖利的嗓门、并不比男孩斯文多少的做派，表明她们也混在其中。

我把这孩子放下——他才七岁——放在全家共用的起居室当中，端详着他。他的小外套后背绽了口子；一只长袜膝盖处本已补了又补，如今是彻底没救了；两只小手乌黑，其中一只还在淌血；整个小身躯因兴奋、愤怒和剧烈奔跑而突突直颤。我静静地望着他，他眼里那股犟劲，便渐渐让位给了眼泪。

“这会儿罚他也没用，”妻子说，“把他交给我吧，你去找其余几个。”

“我本来就没打算罚他，”我说。

“那你打算怎么办？你干吗这样盯着他看？”

===Original===
“He’s a problem I can’t solve—with the given conditions.”

“O Robert, you drive me half wild. If the house was on fire you’d stop to follow out some train of thought about it all. I’m tired to death. Do bring the children home. When we’ve put them to bed you can figure on your problem, and I can sit down.”

As I went up to the Daggetts’ flat I was dimly conscious of another problem. My wife was growing fretful and nervous. Our rooms would not have satisfied a Dutch housewife, but if “order is heaven’s first law” a little of Paradise was in them as compared to the Daggetts’ apartments. “Yes,” I was told, in response to my inquiries; “Winnie is in the bedroom with Melissy.”

The door was locked, and after some hesitation the girls opened it. As we were going downstairs I caught a glimpse of a newspaper in my girl’s pocket. She gave it to me reluctantly, and said “Melissy” had lent it to her. I told her to help her mother prepare supper while I went to find Merton. Opening the paper under a street lamp, I found it to be a cheap, vile journal, full of flashy pictures that so often offend the eye on newsstands. With a chill of fear I thought, “Another problem.” The Daggett children had had the scarlet fever a few months before. “But here’s a worse infection,” I reflected. “Thank heaven, Winnie is only a child, and can’t understand these pictures;” and I tore the paper up and thrust it into its proper place, the gutter.

“Now,” I muttered, “I’ve only to find Merton in mischief to make the evening’s experience complete.”

In mischief I did find him—a very harmful kind of mischief, it appeared to me. Merton was little over fifteen, and he and two or three other lads were smoking cigarettes which, to judge by their odor, must certainly have been made from the sweepings of the manufacturer’s floor.

===Chinese===
“他是一道我解不开的难题——按眼下给定的这些条件。”

“哦，罗伯特（Robert），你简直要把我逼疯了。哪怕房子着了火，你也会停下脚步，把整件事从头到尾想个通透。我都累死了。快把孩子们找回来吧。等安顿他们上了床，你尽管去演算你的难题，我也能坐下来歇口气了。”

我朝楼上达格茨家的公寓走去时，隐隐约约又意识到了另一道难题。我的妻子正变得越来越烦躁易怒。我们那几间屋子，在讲究的荷兰主妇眼里也未必过得关，可倘若“秩序乃上苍的第一法则”，那么同达格茨家的房间一比，我们这儿就算得上一方小小乐园了。我上前问了一声，得到的回答是：“不错，温妮正同梅莉茜（Melissy）在卧室里呢。”

门上了锁，两个女孩迟疑了一下才把门打开。下楼时，我瞥见我女儿的口袋里露出一角报纸。她不情愿地把它交给我，说是梅莉茜借给她的。我叫她去帮妈妈张罗晚饭，自己去找默顿。在路灯下展开那张报纸，我发现这是一份便宜下流的小报，满纸都是报摊上时常刺人眼目的那种花哨图片。我心里掠过一阵寒意：“又是一道难题。”达格茨家的孩子们几个月前出过猩红热。“可这里有更毒的传染，”我思忖着，“谢天谢地，温妮还是个孩子，看不懂这些图画；”于是我把报纸撕得粉碎，扔进它本该去的地方——阴沟。

“这下，”我喃喃道，“只差再撞见默顿干坏事，今晚这一出就齐全了。”

坏事我果然撞见了——在我看来，还是一种害处极大的坏事。默顿刚过十五岁，他和另外两三个小子正抽着香烟；单凭那股子气味判断，那些烟准是拿卷烟厂地板上扫拢来的碎屑做的。

===Original===
“Can’t you find anything better than that to do after school?” I asked, severely.

“Well, sir,” was the sullen reply, “I’d like to know what there is for a boy to do in this street.”

During the walk home I tried to think of an answer to his implied question. What would I do if I were in Merton’s place? I confess that I was puzzled. After sitting in school all day he must do something that the police would permit. There certainly seemed very little range of action for a growing boy. Should I take him out of school and put him into a shop or an office? If I did this his education would be sadly limited. Moreover he was tall and slender for his age, and upon his face there was a pallor which I dislike to see in a boy. Long hours of business would be very hard upon him, even if he could endure the strain at all. The problem which had been pressing on me for months—almost years—grew urgent.

With clouded brows we sat down to our modest little supper. Winifred, my wife, was hot and flushed from too near acquaintance with the stove, and wearied by a long day of toil in a room that would be the better for a gale of wind. Bobsey, as we called my little namesake, was absorbed—now that he was relieved from the fear of punishment—by the wish to “punch” the boy who had tripped him up. Winnie was watching me furtively, and wondering what had become of the paper, and what I thought of it. Merton was somewhat sullen, and a little ashamed of himself. I felt that my problem was to give these children something to do that would not harm them, for do *something* they certainly would. They were rapidly attaining that age when the shelter of a narrow city flat would not answer, when the influence of a crowded house and of the street might be greater than any we could bring to bear upon them.

I looked around upon the little group for whom I was responsible. My will was still law to them. While my little wife had positive ways of her own, she would agree to any decided course that I resolved upon. The children were yet under entire control, so that I sat at the head of the table, commander in chief of the little band. We called the narrow flat we lived in “home.” The idea! with the Daggetts above and the Ricketts on the floor beneath. It was not a home, and was scarcely a fit camping ground for such a family squad as ours. Yet we had stayed on for years in this long, narrow line of rooms, reaching from a crowded street to a little backyard full of noisy children by day, and noisier cats by night. I had often thought of moving, but had failed to find a better shelter that was within my very limited means. The neighborhood was respectable, so far as a densely populated region can be. It was not very distant from my place of business, and my work often kept me so late at the office that we could not live in the suburb. The rent was moderate for New York, and left me some money, after food and clothing were provided, for occasional little outings and pleasures, which I believe to be needed by both body and mind. While the children were little—so long as they would “stay put” in the cradle or on the floor—we did not have much trouble. Fortunately I had good health, and, as my wife said, was “handy with children.” Therefore I could help her in the care of them at night, and she had kept much of her youthful bloom. Heaven had blessed us. We had met with no serious misfortunes, nor had any of our number been often prostrated by prolonged and dangerous illness. But during the last year my wife had been growing thin, and occasionally her voice had a sharpness which was new. Every month Bobsey became more hard to manage. Our living room was to him like a cage to a wild bird, and slip away he would, to his mother’s alarm; for he was almost certain to get into mischief or trouble. The effort to perform her household tasks and watch over him was more wearing than it had been to rock him through long hours at night when he was a teething baby. These details seem very homely no doubt, yet such as these largely make up our lives. Comfort or discomfort, happiness or unhappiness, springs from them. There is no crop in the country so important as that of boys and girls. How could I manage my little home garden in a flat?

I looked thoughtfully from one to another, as with children’s appetites they became absorbed in one of the chief events of the day.

===Chinese===
“放学以后，你就找不出比这像样点的事做吗？”我厉声问道。

“这个嘛，先生，”他闷声闷气地顶了一句，“我倒想请教，在这条街上，一个男孩子还能有什么事可做。”

回家的一路上，我竭力想为他那句弦外之音找一个答案。倘若我处在默顿的位置，我会怎么做？我得承认，我也给难住了。在学校里坐了一整天之后，他总得做点警察容许的事。对一个正在发育的男孩来说，可供施展的天地确实少得可怜。我是不是该让他退学，送他进一家铺子或写字间？可这样一来，他的教育就要大打折扣。况且论年纪，他算是长得又高又瘦的，脸上带着一种苍白之色，是我在男孩子身上最不愿见到的。长时间的营生劳碌对他会非常吃力，就算他熬得住那份紧绷。那道压在我心头数月——几乎数年——的难题，此刻变得迫在眉睫了。

我们一家人愁眉不展地坐下来，吃那顿简朴的小晚饭。温妮弗蕾德（Winifred）——我的妻子——离炉火太近，烤得满脸通红；她在那间哪怕灌进一阵狂风也会爽利些的屋子里劳碌了一整天，早已筋疲力尽。鲍勃西——我们这样称呼跟我同名的小儿子——如今既解除了受罚的忧惧，便一门心思只想去“揍”那个绊倒他的男孩。温妮偷偷拿眼瞟我，猜着那张报纸的下落，猜着我对它作何感想。默顿有几分怏怏不乐，也有点自惭。我明白，我的难题就是要给这些孩子找些不伤身败性的事情做——因为他们横竖总得*做点什么*。他们很快就要长到那个年纪：狭窄的城市公寓再也庇护不住，拥挤的房屋和街头的熏染，其影响也许会大过我们做父母的千般着力。

我环顾着这一小群人，他们的养育之责全在我肩上。我的意志对他们来说仍是法律。我的小妻子虽自有主见，但只要我拿定了明确的主意，她都会依从。孩子们尚未脱出全盘的管束，因此我坐在餐桌的首位，是这支小小队伍的统帅。我们管自己住的这套狭窄公寓叫“家”。亏想得出！楼上住着达格茨家，楼下住着里克茨（Rickett）家。这算什么家，简直连我们这样一大家人马安营扎寨都不配。可我们就在这长长一溜窄屋里一住多年——它一头通向拥挤的街道，一头连着小小的后院，白天满院是闹嚷嚷的孩子，夜里闹嚷嚷的猫更多。我常常想搬家，却始终找不到一处我那点微薄财力负担得起的更好的栖身之所。这一带的街坊还算体面——就人口稠密之地而论。这里离我的事务所不算远，而我的差事常常把我拖到深夜，我们因而住不进郊区。就纽约的行情而论，房租还算适中；扣掉衣食开销，还能剩下一点钱，供我们偶尔小小的出游和消遣——我相信这于身心都是必需的。孩子们还小的那些年——只要他们肯在摇篮里、地板上“待着不动”——我们倒没多少麻烦。所幸我身体康健，而且照我妻子的话说，带孩子“有一手”。所以我夜里能帮她照看孩子，她也就保住了大半青春的容色。上天眷顾我们。我们没有遭过什么大灾大难，家里也没有谁屡屡被缠绵凶险的病痛击倒。可是最近这一年，妻子渐渐瘦了下去，说话偶尔也带出一种前所未有的尖刻。鲍勃西一个月比一个月难管。我们的起居室于他，就像笼子于一只野鸟，他总要溜出去，直惹得他母亲提心吊胆；因为他一出去，十有八九要闯祸惹麻烦。她要一边操持家务一边看住他，这份煎熬，比当年他出乳牙时整夜整夜摇着他入睡还要磨人。这些细故听来想必十分家常，然而我们的生活，多半正是由这些细故织成的。舒适与否、幸福与否，皆由此而生。举国上下，没有哪一茬收成比得上男孩女孩这一茬。可我这小小的家园圃地，在一套公寓里叫我如何耕种？

我若有所思地一个个望过去；他们正凭着一副孩子的胃口，埋头扑在这件一天里数得着的大事上。

===Original===
“Well,” said my wife, querulously, “how are you getting on with your problem?”

“Take this extra bit of steak and I’ll tell you after the children are asleep,” I said.

“I can’t eat another mouthful,” she exclaimed, pushing back her almost untasted supper. “Broiling the steak was enough for me.”

“You are quite tired out, dear,” I said, very gently.

Her face softened immediately at my tone and tears came into her eyes.

“I don’t know what is the matter with me,” she faltered. “I am so nervous some days that I feel as if I should fly to pieces. I do try to be patient, but I know I’m growing cross!”

===Chinese===
“哎，”妻子带着几分怨气问道，“你的难题解得怎么样了？”

“把你这份多出来的牛排吃了吧，等孩子们睡下我就讲给你听，”我说。

“我一口也吃不下了，”她嚷道，把几乎没动过的晚饭推开。“光烤这块牛排，就够我受的了。”

“你是真的累坏了，亲爱的，”我说，声音放得十分轻柔。

听我这语气，她的脸色立时柔和下来，眼里涌上了泪水。

“我也不知自己是怎么了，”她声音发颤。“有些日子我神经紧张得只觉得自己就要四分五裂。我不是不肯耐着性子，可我知道，我越来越爱发脾气了！”

===Original===
“Oh now, mamma,” spoke up warmhearted Merton; “the idea of your being cross.”

“She is cross,” Bobsey cried; “she boxed my ears this very day.”

“And you deserved it,” was Merton’s retort. “It’s a pity they are not boxed oftener.”

“Yes, Robert, I did,” continued my wife, sorrowfully. “Bobsey ran away four times, and vexed me beyond endurance, that is, such endurance as I have left, which doesn’t seem to be very much.”

“I understand, dear,” I said. “You are a part of my problem, and you must help me solve it.” Then I changed the subject decidedly, and soon brought sunshine to our clouded household. Children’s minds are easily diverted; and my wife, whom a few sharp words would have greatly irritated, was soothed, and her curiosity awakened as to the subject of my thoughts.

===Chinese===
“哎呀，妈妈，”心肠火热的默顿开腔了，“您说什么脾气坏，哪有的事。”

“她就是脾气坏，”鲍勃西嚷道，“就在今天，她还打了我耳刮子呢。”

“那是你活该，”默顿回敬道，“可惜没多打你几回。”

“是啊，罗伯特，我打了，”妻子接着说，语气忧伤。“鲍勃西跑了四趟，把我气得忍无可忍——我是说，就凭我如今剩下来的这点耐性，看样子已所剩无几了。”

“我明白，亲爱的，”我说，“你也是我难题的一部分，你得帮我一道来解它。”说着我便斩钉截铁地岔开话头，不多时，我们这个愁云笼罩的家便透进了阳光。孩子的心思最容易岔开；而我那妻子——往常几句尖刻话就能把她大大惹恼的——这时也心平气和了，好奇心反倒被勾了起来，很想知道我心里盘算的是什么。

## XXXVI A Thunderbolt / XXXVI 晴天霹雳

===Original===
From the experiences just related, it can be seen how largely the stress and strain of the year centred in the month of July. Nearly all our garden crops needed attention; the grass of the meadow had to be cured into hay, the currants and cherries to be picked, and fall crops, like winter cabbages, turnips, and celery, to be put in the ground. Of the latter vegetable, I set out only a few short rows, regarding it as a delicious luxury to which not very much time could be given.

Mr. Jones and Junior, indeed all our neighbors, were working early and late, like ourselves. Barns were being filled, conical haystacks were rising in distant meadows, and everyone was busy in gathering nature’s bounty.

We were not able to make much of the Fourth of July. Bobsey and Winnie had some firecrackers, and, in the evening, Merton and Junior set off a few rockets, and we all said, “Ah!” appreciatively, as they sped their brief fiery course; but the greater part of the day had to be spent in gathering the ripening blackcaps and raspberries. By some management, however, I arranged that Merton and Junior should have a fine swim in the creek, by Brittle Rock, while Mousie, Winnie, and Bobsey waded in sandy shallows, further down the stream. They all were promised holidays after the fruit season was over, and they submitted to the necessity of almost constant work with fairly good grace.

===Chinese===
从上文讲述的种种经历可以看出，这一年间的紧张操劳，大半都汇聚在七月里。园子里的作物几乎样样都等着照料；草场上的青草得翻晒成干草，醋栗和樱桃得摘下来，还有冬卷心菜、芜菁、芹菜这类秋季作物得下地栽种。最后说的这种蔬菜，我只栽了短短的几行，把它当成一种难得的美味，舍不得多破费工夫。

琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）和小琼斯（Junior），说实在的，还有我们所有的邻居，都同我们一样起早贪黑地忙着。各家的谷仓渐渐装满，远处草场上竖起一座座圆锥形的干草垛，人人都在忙着收获大自然的丰饶馈赠。

七月四日独立日，我们也没能好好庆祝一番。鲍勃西（Bobsey）和温妮（Winnie）得了些鞭炮；到了晚上，默顿（Merton）和小琼斯放了几支烟花，看着它们拖着短短的火尾窜上天去，我们都赞叹地“啊”出了声；可这一天的大半光阴，还是不得不花在采摘渐渐熟透的黑树莓和红树莓上。不过，我好歹设法安排了一下，让默顿和小琼斯到溪边脆岩（Brittle Rock）旁畅畅快快地游了一回泳，小耗子（Mousie）、温妮和鲍勃西则在溪水下游的浅水沙滩上蹚水玩耍。果子季节一过，就许他们全体放假，因此他们颇为识大体，顺顺当当地接受了这几乎不间断劳作的境况。

===Original===
The results of our labor were cheering. Our table was supplied with delicious vegetables, which, in the main, it was Mousie’s task to gather and prepare. The children were as brown as little Indians, and we daily thanked God for health. Checks from Mr. Bogart came regularly, the fruit bringing a fair price under his good management. The outlook for the future grew brighter with the beginning of each week; for on Monday he made his returns and sent me the proceeds of the fruit shipped previously. I was able to pay all outstanding accounts for what had been bought to stock the place, and I also induced Mr. Jones to receive the interest in advance on the mortgage he held. Then we began to hoard for winter.

The Bagleys did as well as we could expect, I suppose. The children did need the “gad” occasionally and the father indulged in a few idle, surly, drinking days; but, convinced that the man was honestly trying, I found that a little tact and kindness always brought him around to renewed endeavor. To expect immediate reform and unvaried well-doing would be asking too much of such human nature as theirs.

As July drew to a close, my wife and I felt that we were succeeding better than we had had reason to expect. In the height of the season we had to employ more children in gathering the raspberries, and I saw that I could increase the yield in coming years, as I learned the secrets of cultivation. I also decided to increase the area of this fruit by a fall planting of some varieties that ripened earlier and later, thus extending the season and giving me a chance to ship to market for weeks instead of days. My strawberry plants were sending out a fine lot of new runners, and our hopes for the future were turning largely toward the cultivation of this delicious fruit.

===Chinese===
我们辛劳的成果着实令人振奋。饭桌上不断有鲜美的蔬菜，而采回来、烹制出来，多半是小耗子的差事。孩子们晒得黑黝黝的，活像一群小印第安人，我们天天感谢上帝赐下健康。博加特先生（Mr. Bogart）的支票按时寄到，果子经他一手妥善操办，卖出了公道的价钱。前途的光景一星期比一星期亮堂；因为每逢星期一，他便结一回账，把先前发运果子的货款寄给我。置办庄园家什所欠下的账目，我已能一一付清；我还劝得琼斯先生把他手上那笔抵押借款的利息提前收下。这之后，我们便开始为过冬囤聚了。

巴格利（Bagley）一家干得同我们所能指望的一样好，我想可以这样评说。孩子们偶尔也确实需要“鞭策”一下，当父亲的也有过几天懒散乖戾、借酒消磨的日子；不过，我认定这个人是真心实意想学好，便发现只要稍稍施点婉转、加点和气，总能让他回心转意，重新卖力气。指望他们那样的人性立刻改过自新、一贯向善，未免要求太高了。

七月将尽，我和妻子都觉得，我们的光景比当初有理由指望的还要好。收获最盛的时节，我们不得不多雇些孩子来摘树莓；我看出，随着我渐渐摸到栽培的诀窍，往后几年的产量还能提高。我还拿定主意，秋季再栽种几个成熟期或早或晚的品种，扩大这种果子的种植面积，这样便拉长了上市的季节，让我向市场发货的机会从几天变成几个星期。我们的草莓秧正抽出一大批茁壮的新匍匐茎，我们对前途的指望，也就大多转到了这种可口果子的栽培上。

===Original===
Old Jacox had plodded faithfully over the meadow with his scythe, and the barn was now so well filled that I felt our bay horse and brindle cow were provided for during the months when fields are bare or snowy.

Late one afternoon, he was helping me gather up almost the last load down by the creek, when the heavy roll of thunder warned us to hasten. As we came up to the high ground near the house, we were both impressed by the ominous blackness of a cloud rising in the west. I felt that the only thing to do was to act like the captain of a vessel before a storm, and make everything “snug and tight.” The load of hay was run in upon the barn floor, and the old horse led with the harness on him to the stall below. Bagley and the children, with old Jacox, were started off so as to be at home before the shower, doors and windows were fastened, and all was made as secure as possible.

Then we gathered in our sitting room, where Mousie and my wife had prepared supper; but we all were too oppressed with awe of the coming tempest to sit down quietly, as usual. There was a deathlike stillness in the sultry air, broken only at intervals by the heavy rumble of thunder. The strange, dim twilight soon passed into the murkiest gloom, and we had to light the lamp far earlier than our usual hour. I had never seen the children so affected before. Winnie and Bobsey even began to cry with fear, while Mousie was pale and trembling. Of course, we laughed at them and tried to cheer them; but even my wife was nervously apprehensive, and I admit that I felt a disquietude hard to combat.

===Chinese===
老杰科克斯（Jacox）扛着长柄大镰刀，一步一个脚印、忠忠实实地把草场割完了；如今谷仓已经装得满满当当，我想，即便到了田野光秃或大雪盖地的月份，我们那匹枣红马和那头花斑牛的草料也有了下落。

一天傍晚，天色已晚，他正帮我在溪边收拾差不多最后一车干草，忽然雷声沉沉，滚滚而来，催我们赶快。等我们上了房舍近旁的高地，两人都被西边升起的一团乌云震住了——那黑色透着不祥。我心想，眼下唯一该做的，就是学那风暴当前的海船船长，把样样东西都弄得“严严实实、稳稳当当”。那车干草径直赶进谷仓，卸在仓底；老马连挽具一起牵到下面的厩里。巴格利带着孩子们，还有老杰科克斯，都被打发即刻动身，好赶在骤雨之前到家；门窗一一闩牢，一切都布置得尽量稳妥。

随后我们都聚进起居室，小耗子和妻子已把晚饭备好；可是那场将至的暴风雨慑住了我们每个人的心神，谁也不能像平常那样安安稳稳地入座。闷热的空气里是一片死一般的沉寂，只偶尔被隆隆的低沉雷声打破。异样昏沉的天色很快转入最浓的晦暗，我们只得比平常早得多就点起灯来。我从来没见过孩子们这般受惊。温妮和鲍勃西甚至吓得哭出了声，小耗子则脸色苍白，浑身发抖。我们自然笑着宽慰他们，给他们壮胆；可是连我妻子也神情紧张、暗自惊惶，我承认我自己也感到一种难以克制的忧惧。

===Original===
Slowly and remorselessly the cloud approached, until it began to pass over us. The thunder and lightning were simply terrific. Supper remained untasted on the table, and I said: “Patience and courage! A few moments more and the worst will be over!”

But my words were scarcely heard, so violent was the gust that burst upon us. For a few moments it seemed as if everything would go down before it, but the old house only shook and rocked a little.

“Hurrah!” I cried. “The bulk of the gust has gone by, and now we are all right!”

===Chinese===
乌云缓慢而不容抗拒地逼近，终于开始从我们头顶上压过去。雷电之烈，简直骇人。晚饭摆在桌上，无人动一箸，我说道：“沉住气，拿出勇气来！再过片刻，最凶的关头就熬过去了！”

可我的话几乎听不见，因为扑到我们身上的那阵狂风实在太猛。有那么一会儿，仿佛天地间的一切都要在它面前倒伏；然而这幢老房子不过是摇了摇、晃了晃。

“好哇！”我喊道。“狂风的主力已经过去，这下咱们平安无事了！”

===Original===
At that instant a blinding gleam and an instantaneous crash left us stunned and bewildered. But as I recovered my senses, I saw flames bursting from the roof of our barn.

===Chinese===
就在那一刹那，一道炫目的电光、一声猝然的霹雳，震得我们神魂恍惚、懵然不知所措。待我神志稍定，只见我们谷仓的屋顶上，火苗已经蹿起。

## XXXIX A Trip to the Seashore / XXXIX 海滨一日游

===Original===
In the comparative leisure which the children enjoyed during August, they felt amply repaid for the toil of the previous months. We also managed to secure two great gala days. The first was spent in a trip to the seashore; and this was a momentous event, marred by only one slight drawback. The *Mary Powell*, a swift steamer, touched every morning at the Maizeville Landing. I learned that, from its wharf, in New York, another steamer started for Coney Island, and came back in time for us to return on the *Powell* at 3:30 p.m. Thus we could secure a delightful sail down the river and bay, and also have several hours on the beach. My wife and I talked over this little outing, and found that if we took our lunch with us, it would be inexpensive. I saw Mr. Jones, and induced him and his wife, with Junior, to join us. Then the children were told of our plan, and their hurrahs made the old house ring. Now that we were in for it, we proposed no halfway measures. Four plump spring chickens were killed and roasted, and to these were added so many ham sandwiches and hard-boiled eggs, that I declared that we were provisioned for a week. My wife nodded at Bobsey, and said, “Wait and see!”

Whom do you think we employed to mount guard during our absence? No other than Bagley. Mr. Jones said that it was like asking a wolf to guard the flock, for his prejudices yielded slowly; but I felt sure that this proof of trust would do the man more good than a dozen sermons.

===Chinese===
八月里，孩子们得享一段比较清闲的日子，觉得先前几个月的辛劳得到了丰厚的报偿。我们也设法安排出了两个盛大的欢庆日子。头一个便消磨在海滨之游上；这是一桩了不得的大事，只有一点小小的缺憾略损其美。行驶飞快的轮船「玛丽·鲍威尔」号（Mary Powell）每天早晨都停靠梅兹维尔码头（Maizeville Landing）。我打听到，在纽约，从「鲍威尔」号停靠的那座码头，另有一班轮船开往康尼岛（Coney Island），返航的时刻也正赶得上，好让我们搭下午三点半的「鲍威尔」号回家。这样，我们既能沿河顺湾享一程畅快的航行，又能在海滩上盘桓几个钟头。我和妻子把这桩小小的出游合计了一番，发现只要自带午餐，花费并不大。我去见琼斯先生（Mr. Jones），说动了他和他的太太，带着小琼斯（Junior），同我们一道去。随后把计划告诉了孩子们，一片欢呼声震得老屋嗡嗡作响。既然横竖是去定了，我们备办起来就绝不含糊。四只肥嫩的童子鸡宰了烤上，又添上许许多多的火腿三明治和煮鸡蛋，多得我直说，这份储备够我们吃上一个礼拜。妻子朝鲍勃西（Bobsey）点了点头，说：“等着瞧吧！”

你们猜，我们出门期间雇了谁来看家守卫？不是别人，正是巴格利（Bagley）。琼斯先生说，这无异于请一头狼来看守羊群，因为此人的成见消解得很慢；可我确信，这份信任的明证，比十几场布道对这个人更有益处。
