===Original===
And so passed the last day in our city rooms. Except as wife and children were there, they had never appeared very homelike to me, and now they looked bare and comfortless indeed. The children gloated over their appearance, for it meant novelty to them. “The old camp is about broken up,” Merton remarked, with the air of a veteran. But my wife sighed more than once.

“What troubles you, Winifred?”

“Robert, the children were born here, and here I’ve watched over them in sickness and health so many days and nights.”

“Well, my dear, the prospects are that in our new home you will not have to watch over them in sickness very much. Better still, you will not have to be so constantly on your guard against contagions that harm the soul as well as the body. I was told that there are rattlesnakes on Schunemunk, but greater dangers for Winnie and Merton lurk in this street—yes, in this very house;” and I exulted over the thought that we were about to bid Melissa Daggett a final goodbye.

===Chinese===
我们在城里寓所的最后一天，就这样过去了。这些屋子，若不是有妻子儿女住着，在我眼里从来就不怎么像个家；如今看上去，更是四壁空空、索然无着。孩子们对着这副光景反倒乐不可支，因为这在他们意味着新鲜。“老营盘眼看就要拔完啦，”默顿（Merton）一副老兵的派头说道。可我妻子不止叹了一回气。

“温妮弗蕾德（Winifred），什么事叫你心烦？”

“罗伯特（Robert），孩子们是在这儿出生的，无论病中还是平日，我在这儿守着他们，过了多少个日日夜夜啊。”

“好了，亲爱的，照眼下的情形看，到了新家，你就不必这样时常守着他们生病了。更好的是，你也不必再时时刻刻提防那些既害身体、又蚀灵魂的污浊侵染。人家告诉我，舒内芒克山（Schunemunk）上有响尾蛇；可对温妮和默顿来说，更凶险的祸患就潜伏在这条街上——对，就在这座房子里。”想到马上就要同梅莉茜·达格茨（Melissa Daggett）永远道别，我心里真有说不出的畅快。

===Original===
“Oh, I know. I’m glad; but then—”

“But then a woman’s heart takes root in any place where she has loved and suffered. That tendency makes it all the more certain that you’ll love your new home.”

“Yes; we may as well face the truth, Robert. We shall suffer in the new home as surely as in the old. There may be stronger sunshine, but that means deeper shadow.”

===Chinese===
“哦，我知道。我也高兴；只不过——”

“可话又说回来，女人的心，在哪儿爱过、受过苦，就在哪儿扎了根。正是这份心性，越发叫人笃定：你准会爱上咱们的新家。”

“是啊，我们还是面对实话吧，罗伯特。在新家我们照样要吃苦，同在老家一样躲不掉。那里的阳光或许更烈，可烈日底下，影子也就更深。”

## XVIII Butternuts and Bobsey's Peril / XVIII 灰胡桃与鲍勃西的险情

===Original===
I restrained the children until after dinner, which my wife hastened. By that time Junior was on hand with a small wagonload of pails and pans.

“Oh, dear, I wanted you to help me this afternoon,” my wife had said, but, seeing the dismayed look on the children’s faces, had added, “Well, there’s no hurry, I suppose. We are comfortable, and we shall have stormy days when you can’t be out.”

I told her that she was wiser than the queen of Sheba and did not need to go to Solomon.

===Chinese===
我按捺住孩子们，一直等到午饭过后——这顿午饭是妻子赶着张罗出来的。到那时，小琼斯（Junior）已经带着满满一小车的桶盆罐锅到了。

“哎呀，我还指望你们下午帮我干活呢，”妻子方才这样说过；可一看见孩子们满脸的沮丧，她又添了一句：“嗯，我想这事也不急。咱们家里过得挺舒坦，往后总会有刮风下雨的日子，那时你们想出门也出不成。”

我告诉她，她比示巴女王还要有智慧，用不着去请教所罗门。

===Original===
The horse was put in the barn, for he would have mired in the long spongy lane and the meadow which we must cross. So we decided to run the light wagon down by hand.

Junior had the auger with which to bore holes in the trees. “I tapped ’em last year, as old Mr. Jamison didn’t care about doin’ it,” said the boy, “an’ I b’iled the pot of sap down in the grove; but that was slow, cold work. I saved the little wooden troughs I used last year, and they are in one of the pails. I brought over a big kittle, too, which mother let me have, and if we can keep this and yours a-goin’, we’ll soon have some sugar.”

Away we went, down the lane, Junior and Merton in the shafts, playing horses. I pushed in some places, and held back in others, while Winnie and Bobsey picked their way between puddles and quagmires. The snow was so nearly gone that it lay only on the northern slopes. We had heard the deep roar of the Moodna Creek all the morning, and had meant to go and see it right after breakfast; but providing a chickenhome had proved a greater attraction to the children, and a better investment of time for me. Now from the top of the last hillside we saw a great flood rushing by with a hoarse, surging noise.

===Chinese===
马被牵进了谷仓——那又长又松软塌陷的车道，再加上我们必得穿过的草甸，非把它陷进泥里不可。于是大家决定，把轻便马车用手推下去。

小琼斯带着一把钻子，好往树上钻孔。“去年那些树就是我钻的眼儿，老贾米森先生（Mr. Jamison）懒得费那个事，”这孩子说，“后来我又把一锅树汁搁在林子里熬；不过那活儿又慢又冻人。去年用的小木槽我都留着呢，就搁在其中一个桶里。我还捎来一口大铁锅，是我妈准我拿的。只要咱们把我这口和你们那口一齐烧上，很快就能熬出糖来。”

我们动身了，顺着车道往下走，小琼斯和默顿（Merton）套在车辕里扮马；有的地方我上前帮着推，有的地方我又拽住车往后带，温妮（Winnie）和鲍勃西（Bobsey）则在泥塘水洼之间挑着道儿走。积雪已经化得差不多了，只有北坡上还剩着一点。整整一上午，我们都听见穆德纳溪（Moodna Creek）深沉的轰鸣，本打算一吃过早饭就去看它；可给鸡张罗个窝这件事，对孩子们吸引力更大，对我也是更划算的时光投资。这会儿，从最后一道山坡顶上，我们望见一股大水奔腾而过，发出嘶哑汹涌的喧声。

===Original===
“Winnie, Bobsey, if you go near the water without me you march straight home,” I cried.

They promised never to go, but I thought Bobsey protested a little too much. Away we went down the hill, skirting what was now a good-sized brook. I knew the trees, from a previous visit; and the maple, when once known, can be picked out anywhere, so genial, mellow, and generous an aspect has it, even when leafless.

The roar of the creek and the gurgle of the brook made genuine March music, and the children looked and acted as if there were nothing left to be desired. When Junior showed them a tree that appeared to be growing directly out of a flat rock, they expressed a wonder which no museum could have excited.

===Chinese===
“温妮、鲍勃西，要是没有我跟着，你们敢往水边去，就给我一直走回家去！”我大声喝道。

他们保证绝不乱跑，可我觉得鲍勃西赌咒发誓得未免过头了些。我们下了山坡，沿着一条如今已颇有规模的小溪绕行。凭着先前那一次来访，我认得那些树；而枫树嘛，只要认过一回，到哪里都认得出——它那份神情是这样的和蔼、圆熟、慷慨，哪怕落尽了叶子也不改。

大溪的轰响，小溪的潺潺，合成了一支地道的三月乐曲；孩子们的神情举止，仿佛人世间再无所求。小琼斯指给他们看一棵像是从一块平整的岩石里直接长出来的树时，他们流露出的那份惊叹，是任何博物馆都引不出来的。

===Original===
But scenery, and even rural marvels, could not keep their attention long. All were intent on sap and sugar, and Junior was speedily at work. The moment he broke the brittle, juicy bark, the tree’s lifeblood began to flow.

“See,” he cried, “they are like cows wanting to be milked.”

As fast as he inserted his little wooden troughs into the trees, we placed pails and pans under them, and began harvesting the first crop from our farm.

===Chinese===
然而风景，甚至乡野的奇观，都拴不住他们的心思多久。一个个全惦记着树汁和糖，小琼斯立刻动起手来。他一钻透那又脆又多汁的树皮，树木的命脉便流淌出来。

“瞧，”他喊道，“这些树就跟等着挤奶的奶牛一个样。”

他往树上插小木槽插得多快，我们就把桶盆摆到槽下接得多快——就这样，我们开始收割自家农场的头一茬收成。

===Original===
This was rather slow work, and to keep Winnie and Bobsey busy I told them they could gather sticks and leaves, pile them up at the foot of a rock on a dry hillside, and we would have a fire. I meanwhile picked up the dead branches that strewed the ground, and with my axe trimmed them for use in summer, when only a quick blaze would be needed to boil the supper kettle. To city-bred eyes wood seemed a rare luxury, and although there was enough lying about to supply us for a year, I could not get over the feeling that it must all be cared for.

To children there are few greater delights than that of building a fire in the woods, and on that cloudy, chilly day our blaze against the rock brought solid comfort to us all, even though the smoke did get into our eyes. Winnie and Bobsey, little bundles of energy that they were, seemed unwearied in feeding the flames, while Merton sought to hide his excitement by imitating Junior’s stolid, businesslike ways.

Finding him alone once, I said: “Merton, don’t you remember saying to me once, ‘I’d like to know what there is for a boy to do in this street’? Don’t you think there’s something for a boy to do on this farm?”

===Chinese===
这活儿实在缓慢，为了让温妮和鲍勃西有事可干，我告诉他们，可以去捡些枯枝落叶，堆在干燥山坡上一块岩石的脚下，我们好生一堆火。与此同时，我拾起满地散落的枯枝，用斧子把它们修整出来，留待夏天使用——那时只需一把旺火，就够把晚饭的锅子烧开。在城里长大的眼睛看来，木柴简直是稀罕的奢侈品；尽管四下里散落的柴火够我们烧上一年，我却总也摆脱不掉一个念头：这些柴非得好好收拾归置不可。

对孩子们来说，很少有比在树林里生火更大的乐事了。在那个阴冷的日子，我们贴着岩石生起的火堆，给了每个人实实在在的暖意，尽管烟确实熏了我们的眼睛。温妮和鲍勃西，这两个浑身是劲的小家伙，喂起火来仿佛永远不知疲倦；默顿呢，则有意学小琼斯那副不动声色、公事公办的做派，好遮掩自己的兴奋。

有一回我瞅见他单独一人，便说：“默顿，你还记得吗，你从前对我说过：‘我倒想知道，一个男孩待在这条街上能有什么可干的’？如今你不觉得，在这座农场上，男孩有的是可干的吗？”

===Original===
“O papa!” he cried, “I’m just trying to hold in. So much has happened, and I’ve had such a good time, that it seems as if I had been here a month; then again the hours pass like minutes. See, the sun is low already.”

“It’s all new and exciting now, Merton, but there will be long hours—yes, days and weeks—when you’ll have to act like a man, and to do work because it ought to be done and must be done.”

“The same would be true if we stayed in town,” he said.

===Chinese===
“哦，爸爸！”他叫道，“我这是在拼命忍着呢。发生了这么多事，我又玩得这么痛快，简直像已经在这儿待了一个月；可一转眼，几个钟头又像几分钟似的飞过去了。您瞧，太阳已经这么低了。”

“如今样样都新鲜，样样都叫人兴奋，默顿；可往后会有长长的时辰——不错，长长的日子，长长的几个星期——那时你就得拿出男子汉的样子来，去干活，不为了别的，只因那活儿应当干、也非干不可。”

“就是留在城里，也免不了这样。”他说。

===Original===
Soon I decided that it was time for the younger children to return, for I meant to give my wife all the help I could before bedtime. We first hauled the wagon back, and then Merton said he would bring what sap had been caught. Junior had to go home for a time to do his evening “chores,” but he promised to return before dark to help carry in the sap.

“There’ll be frost tonight, and we’ll get the biggest run in the morning,” was his encouraging remark, as he made ready to depart.

Mrs. Jones had been over to see my wife, and they promised to become good friends. I set to work putting things in better shape, and bringing in a good pile of wood. Merton soon appeared with a brimming pail. A kettle was hung on the crane, but before the sap was placed over the fire all must taste it, just as it had been distilled by nature. And all were quickly satisfied. Even Mousie said it was “too watery,” and Winnie made a face as she exclaimed, “I declare, Merton, I believe you filled the pails from the brook!”

===Chinese===
不久我便断定，年幼的孩子们该回去了，因为我打算在睡觉前把能帮的忙都帮给妻子。我们先动手把车拖回家，随后默顿说，他去把接住的树汁运回来。小琼斯不得不先回家一趟，去做他晚间的那些“零活儿”，不过他答应天黑前赶回来，帮着把树汁搬进屋。

“今儿夜里要结霜，明儿一早树汁准流得顶欢。”这是他临动身前撂下的鼓劲话。

琼斯太太（Mrs. Jones）过来串过门，见过我妻子，两人许诺要做一对好朋友。我着手把屋里的东西拾掇得更像样，又搬进好大一堆柴火。不多时，默顿提着满满一桶出现了。一口锅挂上了炉边的吊架，可树汁还没架上火，人人就都得先尝一口——尝一尝大自然亲手蒸馏出来的原汁。大家很快都尝过了。连小耗子（Mousie）都说它“太水了”，温妮更是做了个鬼脸，嚷道：“我说默顿，我敢说你这些桶是从小溪里灌来的！”

===Original===
“Patience, youngsters; sap, as well as some other things, is better for boiling down.”

“Oh what a remarkable truth!” said my wife, who never lost a chance to give me a little dig.

I laughed, and then stood still in the middle of the floor, lost in thought.

===Chinese===
“耐心点，小家伙们；树汁这东西，跟旁的有些东西一样，熬一熬才更够味。”

“哦，多么了不起的真理！”妻子说。她从来不放过任何轻轻扎我一针的机会。

我哈哈大笑，随后又站在地板中央一动不动，陷入了沉思。

===Original===
“A brown study! What theory have you struck now, Robert?”

“I was thinking how some women kept their husbands in love with them by being saucy. It’s an odd way, and yet it seems effective.”

“It depends upon the kind of sauce, Robert,” she said with a knowing glance and a nod.

By the time it was dark, we had both the kettles boiling and bubbling over the fire, and fine music they made. With Junior for guest, we enjoyed our supper, which consisted principally of baked apples and milk.

===Chinese===
“又发愣啦！你这回又琢磨出什么理论来了，罗伯特（Robert）？”

“我在琢磨，有些女人怎么凭着嘴尖舌辣，就把自己丈夫的心拴得牢牢的。这法子古怪，可看来挺管用。”

“那得看是哪一种‘辣’了，罗伯特，”她说着递来一个会意的眼色，还点了点头。

天黑下来的时候，两口锅都已在火上熬得咕嘟作响，那声音着实悦耳动听。有小琼斯作客，我们高高兴兴地吃了晚饭——晚饭主要是烤苹果和牛奶。

===Original===
> “ ‘Bubble, bubble,’ ‘Toil’ and no ‘trouble’—”

“Yet, worth speaking of,” said my wife; “but it must come, I suppose.”

“We won’t go halfway to meet it, Winifred.”

===Chinese===
> “‘咕嘟咕嘟’，有‘辛劳’而没有‘祸事’——”

“可‘祸事’还是值得一说的，”妻子说，“不过我想，它迟早要来。”

“我们决不会半路迎上去接它，温妮弗蕾德（Winifred）。”

===Original===
When the meal was over, Junior went out on the porch and returned with a mysterious sack.

“Butternuts!” he ejaculated.

Junior was winning his way truly, and in the children’s eyes was already a good genius, as his father was in mine.

“O papa!” was the general cry, “can’t we crack them on the hearth?”

===Chinese===
饭吃完了，小琼斯走到廊子上，转回来时拎着一只神神秘秘的口袋。

“灰胡桃（butternut）！”他脱口喊道。

小琼斯真是越来越得人心了；在孩子们眼里，他已经是个带来好运的福星，就像他父亲在我眼里一样。

“哦，爸爸！”大家一齐嚷起来，“我们能不能就在炉膛边上砸着吃？”

===Original===
“But you’ll singe your very eyebrows off,” I said.

“Mine’s so white ’twouldn’t matter,” said Junior; “nobody’d miss ’em. Give me a hammer, and I’ll keep you goin’.”

And he did, on one of the stones of the hearth, with such a lively *rat-tat-snap*! that it seemed a regular rhythm.

“Cracked in my life well-nigh on to fifty bushel, I guess,” he explained, in answer to our wonder at his skill.

===Chinese===
“可你们会把眉毛都燎光的。”我说。

“我这眉毛都白透了，燎了也不碍事，”小琼斯说，“反正没人会惦记它们。给我一把锤子，管保让你们砸个不停。”

他果然说到做到，就在炉膛边的一块石头上砸起来，那一阵欢快的*嗒嗒——咔嚓*！竟像一支规规整整的鼓点。

“我这辈子砸过的胡桃，怕是快满五十蒲式耳了，”他解释道，算是回答我们对他的手艺的惊叹。

===Original===
And so the evening passed, around the genial old fireplace; and before the children retired they smacked their lips over syrup sweet enough to satisfy them.

The following morning—Saturday—I vibrated between the sugar camp and the barn and other outbuildings, giving, however, most of the time to the help of my wife in getting the house more to her mind, and in planning some work that would require a brief visit from a carpenter; for I felt that I must soon bestow nearly all my attention on the outdoor work. I managed to keep Bobsey under my eye for the most part, and in the afternoon I left him for only a few moments at the sugar bush while I carried up some sap. A man called to see me on business, and I was detained. Knowing the little fellow’s proneness to mischief, and forgetfulness of all commands, I at last hastened back with a half guilty and worried feeling.

I reached the brow of the hill just in time to see him throw a stick into the creek, lose his balance, and fall in.

With an exclamation of terror, his own cry forming a faint echo, I sprang forward frantically, but the swift current caught and bore him away.

===Chinese===
就这样，一家人围着那和蔼可亲的老壁炉，把一个晚上消磨过去；孩子们上楼睡觉之前，还咂着嘴唇尝了糖浆——那份甜头足够让他们心满意足。

第二天——星期六——我在熬糖营地和谷仓及其他外屋之间来回奔走；不过大部分时间，还是用来帮妻子把屋子收拾得更合她的心意，并筹划一些需要木匠来短短跑一趟的活计，因为我感到，自己很快就得把几乎全部心思放到户外的活儿上去了。鲍勃西多半被我盯在眼皮底下；到了下午，我上山去提树汁，只把他在枫糖林里单独留了那么一小会儿。偏偏有个人为公事登门找我，我便被耽搁住了。一想到这个小家伙历来爱闯祸、把我们叮嘱的话全忘到九霄云外，我最终怀着又内疚又担忧的心情匆匆赶了回去。

我奔到坡顶，恰好看见他朝溪水里扔一根棍子，身子一歪，失足跌了进去。

我惊骇地叫出了声，他那声呼喊仿佛是我叫声的隐隐回音；我发疯似的纵身扑上前去，可是湍急的水流已经卷住他，把他冲走了。

## XXXII Cherries, Berries, and Berry-Thieves / XXXII 樱桃、莓果与偷莓贼

===Original===
An evening or two after this we were taught that not even in our retired nook had we escaped the dangers of city life. Winnie and Bobsey, in their rambles after strawberries, had met two other children, and, early in the acquaintance, fortunately brought them to the house. The moment I saw the strange girl, I recognized a rural type of Melissa Daggett, while the urchin of Bobsey’s age did not scruple to use vile language in my hearing. I doubt whether the poor little savage had any better vernacular. I told them kindly but firmly that they must not come on the place again without my permission.

After supper I went over and asked Mr. Jones about these children, and he replied, significantly, looking around first to make sure that no one heard him:

“Mr. Durham, steer clear of those people. You know there are certain varmints on a farm to which we give a wide berth and kill ’em when we can. Of course we can’t kill off this family, although a good contribution could be taken up any day to move ’em a hundred miles away. Still about everybody gives ’em a wide berth, and is civil to their faces. They’ll rob you more or less, and you might as well make up your mind to it, and let ’em alone.”

===Chinese===
这一两晚之后，我们便领教了：即便躲在我们这处僻静的角落，也未能逃开城市生活的种种危险。温妮（Winnie）和鲍勃西（Bobsey）出去寻草莓游逛的时候，遇见了另外两个孩子，而且刚一结识，就幸运地把他们带到了家里。我一见那陌生姑娘，便认出她是梅莉茜·达格茨（Melissa Daggett）一类的乡下翻版；而那个同鲍勃西年纪相仿的小顽童，竟当着我的面毫无顾忌地满口脏话。我疑心这可怜的小野人嘴里也吐不出什么更好的语言。我和气然而坚决地告诉他们，不经我的许可，不许再踏进我们这个地方。

晚饭后我过去问琼斯先生（Mr. Jones），这几个孩子是什么来路。他先朝四下里张望，确信没有旁人听见，才意味深长地答道：

“达勒姆先生（Mr. Durham），那路人你可得避而远之。你知道，农场上总有那么几种祸害，我们向来绕着走，能除掉的时候就除掉。这一家子我们当然没法除掉，虽说随便哪一天都募得起一笔可观的捐款，把他们送到一百英里以外去。话虽如此，差不多人人见了他们都绕道走，当面倒还客客气气。他们多少要偷你一点，你索性认了这件事，别去招惹他们。”

===Original===
“Suppose I don’t let them alone?”

“Well—remember, now, this is wholly between ourselves—there’s been barns burned around here. Everybody’s satisfied who sot ’em afire, but nothin’ can be proved. Your cow or horse, too, might suddenly die. There’s no tellin’ what accidents would happen if you got their ill will.”

“I can’t take the course you suggest toward this family,” I said, after a little thought. “It seems to me wrong on both sides. On one hand, they are treated as outlaws, and that would go far to make them such; on the other, they are permitted to levy a sort of blackmail and commit crime with impunity. Of course I must keep my children away from them; but, if the chance offers, I shall show the family kindness, and if they molest me I shall try to give them the law to the utmost.”

“Well,” concluded Mr. Jones, with a shrug, “I’ve warned you, if they git down on yer, yer’ll find ’em snakes in the grass.”

===Chinese===
“倘若我不随他们去呢？”

“嗯——记着，这话只在你我之间说——这一带烧过谷仓。放火的人个个心安理得，可什么证据也抓不着。你的牛啊马啊，也说不定哪天突然就死了。你要是招了他们的恨，会出什么样的‘意外’，谁也说不准。”

“对这一家人，我可不能照你说的法子办，”我略想了想，说道，“依我看，这事两头都不对。一头，人们把他们当法外之徒看待，这本身就会把他们真逼成法外之徒；另一头，又听任他们勒索一种变相的买路钱，犯了事也逍遥法外。当然，我一定要让孩子离他们远远的；可只要有机会，我会对这一家表示善意；他们若来侵害我，我就尽力依法究办，一追到底。”

“好吧，”琼斯先生耸耸肩，收住了话头，“反正我提醒过你了：他们要是恨上你，你就会发现，他们是草里藏着的蛇。”

===Original===
Returning home, I said nothing to Winnie and Bobsey against their recent companions, but told them that if they went with them again, or made the acquaintance of other strangers without permission, they would be put on bread and water for an entire day—that all such action was positively forbidden.

It was evident, however, that the Melissa Daggett element was present in the country, and in an aggravated form. That it was not next door, or, rather, in the next room, was the redeeming feature. Residents in the country are usually separated by wide spaces from evil association.

It must not be thought that my wife and children had no society except that afforded by Mr. Jones’s family. They were gradually making pleasant and useful acquaintances, especially among those whom we met at church; but as these people have no material part in this simple history, they are not mentioned.

===Chinese===
回到家，我没有在温妮和鲍勃西面前数落他们的新伙伴，只告诉他们：要是再同那两个孩子一起玩，或者不经许可同别的陌生人结识，就要罚他们一整天只许吃面包、喝白水——这一类举动是断然禁止的。

不过显而易见，梅莉茜·达格茨那路成分，乡下也有，而且有过之而无不及。所幸它不在我们隔壁，——更确切地说，不在隔壁的房间里。这算是一桩可取之处。乡下人家彼此相隔开阔，通常离恶习的熏染远远的。

但切不可以为，我的妻子和孩子除了琼斯先生一家，便没有别的社交。他们正渐渐结交一些可亲而有益的相识，尤其在教堂里遇见的那些人当中；只是这些人在这部简朴的历史里算不上要紧角色，所以不曾提到。

===Original===
The most important activities of the season were now drawing very near. The cherries were swelling fast; the currants were growing red, and were already pronounced “nice for pies;” and one morning Merton came rushing in with a red raspberry from the Highland Hardy variety. I was glad the time was at hand when I should begin to receive something besides advice from Mr. Bogart; for, careful as we had been, the drain on my capital had been long and steady, and were eager for the turn of the tide.

I had bought a number of old Mr. Jamison’s crates, had painted out his name and replaced it with mine. I now wrote to Mr. Bogart for packages best adapted to the shipping of cherries, currants, and raspberries. For the first he sent me baskets that held about a peck. These baskets were so cheap that they could be sold with the fruit. For currants, crates containing twenty-four quart baskets were forwarded. These, he wrote, would also do for blackcaps this season, and for strawberries next year. For the red raspberries he sent me quite different crates, filled with little baskets holding only half a pint of fruit. Limited supplies of these packages were sent, for he said that a telegram would bring more the same day.

The corn and potatoes were becoming weedy again. This time I made use of a light plow, Merton leading old Bay as at first. Then, with our hoes, we gave the rows a final dressing out. By the time we had finished, some of our grass was fit to cut, the raspberries needed a careful picking over, and the cherries on one tree were ready for market. The children and robins had already feasted, but I was hungry for a check from New York.

===Chinese===
这个季节顶要紧的农事，眼看就要到了。樱桃鼓胀得飞快；红醋栗渐渐泛红，已经被评定为“做馅饼很好了”；一天早晨，默顿（Merton）捧着一颗“高地耐寒”（Highland Hardy）品种的红树莓，冲进屋里来。我暗自欢喜：不久之后，博加特先生（Mr. Bogart）给我的就不光是指导，还会有别的进项了；因为尽管我们处处节俭，我的本钱却长年不断地往外流，我们一心巴望着时来运转。

我买下了老贾姆森先生（Mr. Jamison）的几只板条箱，用油漆涂掉箱上他的名字，换上了我的。这时我写信给博加特先生，要一批最适合装运樱桃、红醋栗和树莓的包装。装樱桃用的，他寄来约莫一配克一只的篮子。这种篮子便宜得很，可以连篮带果一并出卖。装红醋栗的，寄来的是装有二十四只夸脱篮的板条箱。他信上说，这种箱子这一季还能装黑帽树莓（blackcaps），明年还能装草莓。红树莓用的则是全然不同的板条箱，里面盛着只装半品脱果子的小篮。这些包装寄来的数量有限，因为他说，只要拍个电报，当天就能再运一批来。

玉米和马铃薯地里又长起草来。这一回我用的是轻犁，仍像当初那样，由默顿牵着老枣红马（old Bay）在前头走。随后我们又用锄头把垄间最后清理了一遍。活刚干完，我们的草场已有一部分可以开割，树莓需要细心拣摘一遍，有一棵树上的樱桃也可以应市了。孩子们和知更鸟早已大饱口福，我馋的却是一张从纽约寄来的支票。

===Original===
I had long since decided not to attempt to carry on haying alone at this critical season, but had hired a man, too aged to hold his own among the harvesters on the neighboring farms. Mr. Jones had said of him: “He’s a careful, trusty old fellow, who can do a good day’s work yet if you don’t hurry him. Most of your grass is in the meadow, some parts fit to cut before the others. Let the old man begin and mow what he can, every day. Then you won’t have to cure and get in a great lot of hay all at once, and perhaps, too, when your raspberries most need pickin’.”

So, during the last days of June, old Mr. Jacox, who came at moderate wages, put in his scythe on the uplands. I spread the grass and raked it up when dry, and, with the aid of Merton and a rude, extemporized rack on the market wagon, got the hay gradually into the barn. This labor took only part of the day; the rest of the time was employed in the garden and in picking fruit.

On the last day of June we gathered a crate of early raspberries and eight baskets of cherries. In the cool of the afternoon, these were placed in the wagon, and with my wife and the three younger children, I drove to the Maizeville Landing with our first shipment to Mr. Bogart.

===Chinese===
在这个紧要关头，我早已打定主意，不独自张罗割草晒草的事，而是雇了一个人——他年纪太老，在邻近农场的割草人当中已挣不来饭碗。琼斯先生谈起他时说过：“这老头儿仔细、可靠，只要你别催他，一天的整活他还是做得动的。你的草多半在草场上，有的地方先熟，有的地方后熟。就让老头儿每天开镰，能割多少割多少。这样你就不必把一大批干草一下子晒好、收进；再说，赶上你树莓顶需要采摘的时候，人手说不定正好腾得开。”

于是，在六月末的几天里，老杰科克斯先生（Mr. Jacox）以适中的工钱应雇而来，到高处的旱地上挥镰开割。草由我摊开翻晒，干了便耙拢起来；凭着默顿搭手，加上运货马车上现凑的一个粗陋草架，干草渐渐运进了谷仓。这桩活只占去一天里的一部分；其余的时间，便用来料理园子和采摘果子。

六月的最后一天，我们收了一箱早树莓、八篮樱桃。下午凉快下来的时候，这些果子装上了马车；我带着妻子和三个小的，赶到梅兹维尔码头（Maizeville Landing），把头一批货发给博加特先生。

===Original===
“We are ‘p’oducers,’ at last, as Bobsey said,” I cried, joyously. “And I trust that this small beginning will end in such big loads as will leave us no room for wife and children, but will eventually give them a carriage to ride in.”

Merton remained on guard to watch our precious ripening fruit.

After our departure he began a vigilant patrol of the place, feeling much like a sentinel left on guard. About sundown, he told me, as he was passing through the raspberry field, he thought he caught a glimpse of an old straw hat dodging down behind the bushes. He bounded toward the spot, a moment later confronting three children with tin pails. The two younger proved to be Winnie’s objectionable acquaintances that I had told to keep off the place. The eldest was a boy, not far from Merton’s age, and had justly won the name of being the worst boy in the region. All were the children of the dangerous neighbor against whom Mr. Jones had warned me.

===Chinese===
“咱们终于是‘生厂者’啦——这是鲍勃西的说法，”我喜滋滋地大声说。“我指望这个小小的开头，日后会变成满满当当的大车装载，挤得车上再腾不出妻子孩子的座位，到头来，却好给他们换一辆出行的马车来坐。”

默顿留下来守卫，看护我们那些宝贝似的、正在成熟的果子。

我们走后，他便开始小心翼翼地在庄上巡行，自觉像个留守的哨兵。他后来告诉我，太阳快落山时，他正穿过树莓地，仿佛瞥见一顶旧草帽往树丛后面一缩。他一个箭步朝那地方蹿过去，转眼便同三个提着白铁桶的孩子迎面撞上。两个小的，正是温妮那两个叫人头疼的相识——我早吩咐过他们，不许再踏上这块地方；顶大的一个是男孩，年纪同默顿相差无几，在这一带早已名不虚传地博得了“顶坏的孩子”的名声。三个孩子，全是琼斯先生提醒我提防的那个危险人家的儿女。

===Original===
The boy at first regarded Merton with a sullen, defiant look, while his brother and sister coolly continued to steal the fruit.

“Clear out,” cried Merton. “We’ll have you put in jail if you come here again.”

“You shut up and clear out yerself,” said the boy, threateningly, “or I’ll break yer head. Yer pap’s away, and we ain’t afraid of you. What’s more, we’re goin’ ter have some cherries before—”

Now Merton had a quick temper, and at this moment sprang at the fellow who was adding insult to injury, so quickly that he got in a blow that blackened one of the thief’s eyes.

===Chinese===
那男孩起先阴沉着脸，挑衅地瞪着默顿，他的弟弟妹妹却若无其事，照旧偷他们的果子。

“滚出去！”默顿喝道。“再敢上这儿来，就把你们送进牢里去。”

“你给我闭上嘴，自己滚吧，”那男孩气势汹汹地说，“不然我就砸烂你的脑袋。你爸不在家，我们才不怕你呢。还有，这樱桃我们说什么也要弄几个再——”

默顿本是个急性子，此刻见这家伙偷了东西还要逞凶骂人，登时扑了上去，出手快极，一拳打得那贼乌了一只眼。

===Original===
Then they clinched, and, although his antagonist was the heavier, Merton thinks he could have whipped him had not the two younger marauders attacked him, tooth and nail, like cats. Finding himself getting the worst of it, he instinctively sent out a cry for his stanch friend Junior.

Fortunately, this ally was coming along the road toward our house, and he gave an answering halloo.

The vagrants, apparently, had a wholesome fear of John Jones, junior, for, on hearing his voice, they beat a hurried retreat; but knowing that no one was at the house, and in the spirit of revengeful mischief, they took their flight in that direction. Seeing Mousie’s flowerbed, they ran and jumped upon that, breaking down half the plants, then dashed off through the coops, releasing the hens, and scattering the broods of chickens. Merton and Junior, who for a few moments had lost sight of the invaders in the thick raspberry bushes, were now in hot pursuit, and would have caught them again, had they not seen a man coming up the lane, accompanied by a big dog. Junior laid a hand on headlong Merton, whose blood was now at boiling heat, and said, “Stop.”

===Chinese===
接着两人扭作一团。他的对手虽然更沉些，默顿自忖本来打得赢他，怎奈那两个小强盗像猫一样连抓带咬，一齐扑到他身上。眼见自己渐渐吃了亏，他出于本能，高声呼唤他那位忠实的朋友小琼斯（Junior）。

幸好这位援军正沿着大路朝我们家走来，闻声高高地应了一声。

显然，这伙流浪儿对约翰·琼斯家的小琼斯着实有些忌惮，一听见他的喊声，便仓皇后撤；可他们知道家里没有人，又存着报复捣乱的心，便朝那个方向逃去。一看见小耗子（Mousie）的花圃，他们就跑过去从上面一路践踏，踏坏了半畦花草；跟着又冲过鸡笼，放走了母鸡，惊得一群群小鸡四散奔逃。默顿和小琼斯方才在茂密的树莓丛里一时望不见这些入侵者的踪影，此刻已紧紧追了上来，眼看又要抓住他们，却见巷口走来一个男人，还带着一条大狗。小琼斯一把按住直往前冲的默顿——他的血此刻正烧得滚沸——说道：“站住。”

## XXVIII Corn, Color, and Music / XXVIII 玉米、色彩与音乐

===Original===
In early May the grass was growing lush and strong, and Brindle was driven down the lane to the meadow, full of thickets, which bordered on the creek. Here she could supply herself with food and water until the late autumn.

With the first days of the month we planted, on a part of the garden slope, where the soil was dry and warm, very early, dwarf sweet corn, a second early variety, Burr's Mammoth, and Stowell's Evergreen.

“These several kinds,” I said, “will give us a succession of boiling ears for weeks together. When this planting is up a few inches high, we will make another, for, by so doing, my garden book says we may have this delicious vegetable till frost comes.”

===Chinese===
五月初，青草正长得丰茂茁壮，花斑牛（Brindle）被人赶着，沿小道下到溪边那片灌木丛生的草场。在那儿，它自能觅草饮水，一直待到深秋。

进月初头几天，我们就在园子坡地上挑了块土性干爽暖和的地方，种下极早熟的矮生甜玉米，又种了次早熟的「伯尔巨人」（Burr's Mammoth）和「斯托威尔常青」（Stowell's Evergreen）。

“这几个品种，”我说，“能叫咱们一连几个星期都有嫩玉米穗接连下锅。等这一茬蹿出几英寸高，我们就再种一茬；我那本园艺书上讲，这样办，这美味的吃食就能一直吃到下霜。”

===Original===
After reading and some inquiry during the winter I had decided to buy only McLean's Gem peas for seed. This low-growing kind required no brush and, therefore, far less labor. By putting in a row every ten days till the last of June, we should enjoy green peas of the sweet, wrinkled sort till tired, if that were possible. We also planted early dwarf wax beans, covering the seed, as directed, only two inches deep. It was my ambition to raise a large crop of lima beans, having read that few vegetables yield more food to a small area than they. So, armed with an axe and a hatchet, Merton and I went into some young growth on the edge of our wood lot and cut thirty poles, lopping off the branches so as to leave little crotches on which the vines could rest for support. Having sharpened these poles we set them firmly in the garden, four feet apart each way, then dug in some very fine and decayed manure around each pole, and left the soil for a day or two to grow warm and light. My book said that, if the earth was cold, wet, or heavy the beans would decay instead of coming up. The 10th of the month being fine and promising, I pressed the eye or germ side of the beans into the soil and covered them only one inch deep. In the evening we set out our cabbage and cauliflower plants where they should be allowed to mature. The tomato plants, being more tender than their companions started in the kitchen window, were set about four inches apart in a sheltered place. We could thus cover them at night and protect them a little from the midday sun for a week or two longer.

Nor were Mousie's flowering plants forgotten. She had watched over them from the seed with tireless care, and now we made a bed and helped the happy child to put her little nurslings in the open ground where they were to bloom. The apple trees made the air fragrant, and some of the delicate pink of their blossoms was in Mousie's cheeks.

“Truly,” I thought, as I looked into her sparkling eyes, “if we can but barely live in the country, I am glad we came.”

===Chinese===
一冬天的阅读和打听下来，我拿定主意，豌豆种只买「麦克莱恩宝石」（McLean's Gem）一种。这矮生品种无须搭枝支架，因此省力得多。只要隔十天下一行，一直种到六月底，我们就能吃上甜皱皮种的青豌豆，吃到发腻为止——倘若真有腻的一天。我们还种了早熟矮生黄荚菜豆（wax beans），照书上的指点，覆土只有两英寸深。我另有一桩心愿，想收一大茬利马豆（lima beans），因为书上说，论小块地方的出产，没有几种蔬菜胜得过它。于是默顿（Merton）和我带上斧子和手斧，到林子边一片幼树丛里砍下三十根杆子，把枝条削尽，只留些小杈，好让豆藤攀着借力。杆子削尖之后，牢牢插进园里，纵横相距各四英尺，再绕着每根杆子锄进些细碎腐熟的粪肥，把土搁上一两天，让它变得温暖松软。书上说得明白：土若又冷又湿又板结，豆种便会烂掉，出不得苗。这个月十号天气晴好，大有指望，我便把豆粒长着豆眼——也就是胚——的那一面按进土里，覆土仅一英寸深。傍晚，我们把卷心菜和花椰菜的秧苗移栽到让它们长大成熟的地里。番茄秧比一同在厨房窗台上育出来的伙伴更娇嫩，就移到一处避风的地方，株距四英寸上下；这样往后一两个星期里，夜里可以给它们遮盖，正午的烈日也能稍加遮挡。

小耗子（Mousie）的花草也没给忘掉。打从下种起，她就不知疲倦地悉心照看；这会儿我们做好一个花畦，帮这个欢天喜地的孩子把她那些小小的苗苗栽进露天地里，让它们日后在那儿开花。苹果花把空气熏得芬芳；那花朵娇嫩的粉红，也有几分染上了小耗子的脸颊。

望着她那双晶亮的眼睛，我暗自想道：“说真的，哪怕在乡下只能勉强糊口，我也庆幸我们来了。”

===Original===
The next morning Merton and I began our great undertaking—the planting of the other acre of ground, next to the potatoes, with field corn. Mr. Jones had harrowed it comparatively smooth, I had a light plow with which to mark out the furrows four feet apart each way. At the intersection of these furrows the seed was to be dropped. I found I could not drive our old bay straight across the field to save my life, and neighbor Jones laughed till his sides ached at the curves and crooks I first left behind me.

“Here, Merton,” I cried, nothing daunted, “we must work together again. Get a pole and stand it on the farther side of the plot four feet in from the edge of the sod. That's right. Now come here; take old Bay by the head, and, with your eyes fixed on the pole, lead him steadily toward it.”

A furrow was now made of which Mr. Jones himself need not have been ashamed; and he laughed as he said, at parting “You'll do. I see you've got enough Yankee in you to try more ways than one.”

We kept at work manfully, although the day was warm, and by noon the plot was furrowed one way. After dinner we took an hour's partial rest in shelling our corn and then resumed our work, and in the same manner began furrowing at right angles with the first rows. The hills were thus about four feet apart each way. Merton dropped the corn after we had run half a dozen furrows.

===Chinese===
第二天一早，默顿和我开始了一桩大工程——把挨着马铃薯地的另一英亩地种上大田玉米。琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）已经把它耙得相当平整；我有一架轻便犁，用来划出纵横各隔四英尺的犁沟，沟沟相交之处，便是下种的窝。我发觉，任凭怎么赶，咱那匹老枣红马也休想走得笔直；我头几趟犁出的弯弯曲曲，惹得邻居琼斯笑得直不起腰。

“喂，默顿，”我毫不气馁，大声说，“咱们又得搭手干了。去拿根杆子，立在这块地那一头，从草皮边往里量四英尺。对了。这会儿过来，牵住老枣红的笼头，眼睛盯牢那根杆子，牵着它稳稳当当地朝那边走。”

这一回犁出的沟，连琼斯先生本人也没得挑了。临别时他大笑着撂下一句：“你行的。我看你骨子里那股扬基机灵劲儿够足，晓得一个法子不成，就再试一个。”

那天虽然暖和，我们却干得虎虎有生气，到晌午，整块地已顺着一个方向划满了沟。饭后我们剥玉米种，权当歇了一个钟头的晌，随后接着上工，照原先的法子，与头几行成直角再开沟。这样，每个玉米窝纵横相距都在四英尺上下。开出五六道沟以后，默顿就跟在后头点种。

===Original===
“Drop five kernels,” I said; for Mr. Jones had told us that four stalks were enough and that three would do, but had added: “I plant five kernels, for some don't come up, and the crows and other vermints take others. If all of 'em grow, it's easier to pull up one stalk at the first hoeing than to plant over again.”

We found that putting in the corn was a lighter task than planting the potatoes even though we did our own furrowing; and by the middle of May we were complacent over the fact that we had succeeded with our general spring work far better than we had hoped, remembering that we were novices who had to take so much counsel from books and from our kind, practical neighbor.

The foliage of the trees was now out in all its delicately shaded greenery, and midday often gave us a foretaste of summer heat. The slight blaze kindled in the old fireplace, after supper, was more for the sake of good cheer than for needed warmth, and at last it was dispensed with. Thrushes and other birds of richer and fuller song had come, and morning and evening we left the door open that we might enjoy the varied melody.

===Chinese===
“一窝下五粒，”我嘱咐道；因为琼斯先生先前告诉我们，四棵苗尽够，三棵也成，可他又添了一句：“我下五粒，是总有些出不来苗，乌鸦和别的祸害还要叼走一些。就算全出齐了，头遍锄地时多拔一棵就是，总比回头补种省事。”

我们发现，纵然开沟全得自己动手，种玉米到底比种马铃薯轻省；到了五月中旬，我们已颇有几分得意：想想我们不过是班生手，事事要向书本讨教，向那位厚道务实的邻居求计，今春的活计竟干得比指望的好出许多。

树木这时已披上深深浅浅的嫩绿，满目青翠；晌午常常透出几分暑意，让我们先尝了尝夏天的味道。晚饭后在老壁炉里点起一小撮火，与其说为了御寒，不如说图个暖意融融的兴致，到后来索性连这也免了。画眉和别的歌喉愈发丰美嘹亮的鸟儿都来了，早晚我们敞着屋门，好尽情享受那一片抑扬多变的乐音。

===Original===
Our first plantings of potatoes and early vegetables were now up and looked promising. So a new phase of labor—that of cultivation—began. New broods of chickens were coming off, and Winnie had many families to look after. Nevertheless, although there was much to attend to, the season was bringing a short breathing spell, and I resolved to take advantage of it. So I said one Friday evening: “If tomorrow is fair, we'll take a vacation. What do you say to a day's fishing and sailing on the river?”

A jubilant shout greeted this proposal, and when it had subsided, Mousie asked, “Can't Junior go with us?”

“Certainly,” I replied; “I'll go over right after supper, and make sure that his father consents.”

Mr. Jones said, “Yes,” and Merton and Junior were soon busy with their preparations, which were continued until the long twilight deepened into dusk.

===Chinese===
我们头几茬种下的马铃薯和早熟菜蔬都已出苗，看着很有指望。于是劳动进入了一个新阶段——中耕培育。新出的一窝窝小鸡接连不断，温妮（Winnie）手底下要照看的小家庭多着呢。尽管事情不少，这个时节却送来一小段喘息的空当，我决意趁一趁。于是一个星期五的晚上，我说：“明天要是天晴，咱们放一天假。到河上钓一天鱼、扬一天帆，你们说怎么样？”

这提议引来一片欢腾的呐喊；欢声落定，小耗子问道：“可不可以带小琼斯（Junior）一块儿去？”

“当然可以，”我答道，“晚饭后我就过去，跟他父亲问个准话，看他肯不肯。”

琼斯先生答应了声“行”。默顿和小琼斯随即忙着张罗起来，一直忙到漫长的暮光渐渐沉入昏暗。

===Original===
**This volume**

 is lovingly dedicated to

 **“Johnnie.”**

===Chinese===
**本书**

满怀深情地献给

**“约翰尼（Johnnie）。”**

## XXII Early April Gardening / XXII 四月初的园艺活

===Original===
Spring was coming on apace, and we all made the most of every pleasant hour. The second day after the auction proved a fine one; and leaving Winnie and Merton in charge of the house, I took my wife, with Bobsey and Mousie, who was well bundled up, to see the scientific grape grower, and to do some shopping. At the same time we assured ourselves that we were having a pleasure drive; and it did me good to see how the mother and daughter, who had been kept indoors so long, enjoyed themselves. Mr. Jones was right. I received better and clearer ideas of vine pruning in half an hour from studying work that had been properly done, and by asking questions of a practical man, than I could ever have obtained by reading. We found that the old bay horse jogged along, at as good a gait as we could expect, over the muddy road, and I was satisfied that he was quiet enough for my wife to drive him after she had learned how, and gained a little confidence. She held the reins as we drove home, and, in our own yard, I gave her some lessons in turning around, backing, etc.

“Some day,” I said, “you shall have a carriage and a gay young horse.” When we sat down to supper, I was glad to see that a little color was dawning in Mousie’s face.

===Chinese===
春光一日紧似一日，我们都恨不得把每一个晴好的钟点都用在实处。拍卖后的第二天果然是个好天；我把家里交给温妮（Winnie）和默顿（Merton）照看，自己带着妻子，还有鲍勃西（Bobsey）和裹得严严实实的小耗子（Mousie），去拜访那位深谙科学的葡萄种植行家，顺道办了点采买。同时我们只当这是一趟兜风散心的快游；看到久困室内的母女俩玩得那样畅快，我心里也着实受用。琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）说得不错。看看人家干得地道的活计，再向一位实干家问上几句，半个钟头里，我对葡萄修剪所得的明白透彻的心得，比从书本上所能得来的不知强多少。我们发现，老枣红马在泥泞路上不紧不慢地颠着小跑，步子已是所能指望的最好的了；我因此断定，这马性子足够安稳，等妻子学会了赶车、添了几分胆量，尽可以放心交给她来赶。回家路上缰绳便由她握着；进了自家院子，我还教了她几手转弯、倒车之类的功夫。

“总有一天，”我说，“你要有一辆自己的马车，一匹欢蹦乱跳的年轻马。”坐下来吃晚饭时，我高兴地看到，小耗子的脸上已经隐隐透出些许血色。

===Original===
The bundles we brought home supplemented our stores of needful articles, and our life began to take on a regular routine. The carpenter came and put up the shelves, and made such changes as my wife desired; then he aided me in repairing the outbuildings. I finished pruning the trees, while Merton worked manfully at the raspberries, for we saw that this was a far more pressing task than gathering wood, which could be done to better advantage in the late autumn. Every morning Winnie and Bobsey were kept steadily busy in carrying our trimmings to the brush heap, which now began to assume vast proportions, especially as the refuse from the grapevine and raspberry bushes was added to it. As the ground became settled after the frost was out, I began to set the stakes by the side of such raspberry canes as needed tying up; and here was a new light task for the two younger children. Bobsey’s little arms could go around the canes and hold them close to the stake, while Winnie, a sturdy child, quickly tied them with a coarse, cheap string that I had bought for the purpose. Even my wife came out occasionally and helped us at this work. By the end of the last week in March I had all the fruit trees fairly pruned and the grapevines trimmed and tied up, and had given Merton much help among the raspberries. In shallow boxes of earth on the kitchen table, cabbage, lettuce, and tomato seeds were sprouting beside Mousie’s plants. The little girl hailed with delight every yellowish green germ that appeared above the soil.

The hens had spent their first few days in inspecting their quarters and becoming familiar with them; but one morning there was a noisy cackle, and Winnie soon came rushing in with three fresh-laid eggs. A week later we had all we could use, and my wife began to put some by for the first brooding biddies to sit upon.

===Chinese===
带回家的几包东西添补了各色必需的用度，我们的日子开始有了固定的章法。木匠来了，安好搁板，又照妻子的心意改动了些地方；随后又帮我修缮院外的棚屋。我剪完了果树；默顿则在树莓地里卖力苦干，因为我们看得分明，这桩活计比拾柴要紧得多——拾柴尽可以留到晚秋，那时办起来更为合算。每天早晨，温妮和鲍勃西都稳稳当当地忙着一件事：把剪下的枝条运到柴枝堆上去。那柴枝堆渐渐堆得颇具规模了，尤其再加上葡萄藤和树莓枝的废料。地里的霜一化尽、泥土沉实之后，我便开始给需要绑缚的树莓枝在旁边打桩；这又给两个小的添了一桩轻省的新差事。鲍勃西两条小胳膊正好搂得住枝条，把它紧紧贴在桩上；温妮是个结实的孩子，手也麻利，用我特意为此买的粗贱麻绳三两下便把枝条绑好。连妻子也时不时出来，帮我们搭把手。到三月最后一个礼拜的末尾，果树都已修得像模像样，葡萄藤也修剪停当、绑扎完毕，我还帮着默顿在树莓丛里干了好多活。厨房桌上摆着几只盛土的浅箱，卷心菜、莴苣和番茄的种子正在箱里发芽，就挨着小耗子那些花草。土面上每冒出一点黄绿色的幼芽，小姑娘都要欢天喜地地招呼一声。

母鸡们头几天光顾着查看自己的新居，慢慢混了个脸熟；可一天早晨，窝里响起一片聒噪的咯咯声，温妮随即捧着三个刚下的鲜蛋飞奔进屋。一个星期过后，鸡蛋已经多到吃不完，妻子开始攒下一些，留给头一批抱窝的老母鸡去孵。

===Original===
The first day of April promised to be unusually dry and warm, and I said at the breakfast table: “This is to be a great day. We’ll prove that we are not April fools by beginning our garden. I was satisfied yesterday that a certain warm slope was dry enough to dig and plant with hardy vegetables, and I’ve read and studied over and over again which to plant first, and how to plant them. I suppose I shall make mistakes, but I wish you all to see how I do it, and then by next spring we shall have learned from experience how to do better. No doubt, some things might have been planted before, but we’ve all been too busy. Now, Merton, you go and harness old Bay to the cart I bought with the place, and I’ll get out my treasure of seeds. Mousie, by ten o’clock, if the sun keeps out of the clouds, you can put on your rubbers and join us.”

Soon all was bustle and excitement. Among my seeds were two quarts of red and two of white onion sets, or little bits of onions, which I had kept in a cool place, so that they should not sprout before their time. These I took out first. Then with Merton I went to the barnyard and loaded up the cart with the finest and most decayed manure we could find, and this was dumped on the highest part of the slope that I meant to plant.

===Chinese===
四月头一天，看样子会出奇地干爽暖和。我在早饭桌上说：“今天要办成一桩大事。咱们这就动手开园子，好证明自己不是愚人节里遭人耍弄的傻瓜。昨天我已经看准了，有一面向阳的坡地干得能下锹，可以种耐寒的菜蔬；先种哪些、怎么个种法，我又读又琢磨，反复了不知多少遍。我想我难免出错，可我要你们大家都看看我是怎么干的，这样到明年开春，咱们就能凭经验知道怎么干得更好。不用说，有些东西本该早些下种，只是咱们一直太忙。这样吧，默顿，你去把老枣红套上那辆随这地方一并买来的车；我去把我那批宝贝种子搬出来。小耗子，要是太阳一直不被云彩遮住，到十点钟，你就穿上胶套鞋，来跟我们一起干。”

转眼间里里外外一片忙碌欢腾。我的种子当中，有两夸脱红皮、两夸脱白皮的洋葱种球——也就是一个个小洋葱头——我一直存放在阴凉地方，免得它们没到时候就发了芽。头一样取出来的便是它们。随后我同默顿到谷仓场院，把能寻到的最细最腐熟的粪肥装上大车，卸在我打算下种的那面坡的最高处。

===Original===
“Now, Merton, I guess you can get another load, while I spread this heap and begin to dig;” and he went off with the horse and cart, having an increased idea of his importance. I marked a long strip of the sunny slope, fifteen feet wide, and spread the manure evenly and thickly, for I had read, and my own sense confirmed the view, that a little ground well enriched would yield more than a good deal of poor land. I then dug till my back ached; and I found that it began to ache pretty soon, for I was not accustomed to such toil.

“After the first seeds are in,” I muttered, “I’ll have the rest of the garden plowed.”

When I had dug down about four feet of the strip, I concluded to rest myself by a change of labor; so I took the rake and smoothed off the ground, stretched a garden line across it, and, with a sharp-pointed hoe, made a shallow trench, or drill.

===Chinese===
“喂，默顿，我想你可以再去拉一车，我这便把这堆粪撒开，动手挖地；”他赶着马车上路了，自觉越发是个顶事的大人物。我在向阳坡上划出一条长条地，宽十五英尺，把粪肥铺撒得又匀又厚；因为书上读到过，自己的见识也印证了这个道理：一小块肥沃的地，出产胜过一大片贫瘠的地。接着我便一锹一锹挖起来，直挖得腰酸背痛；而且发觉这酸痛来得着实快，因为我素来不惯于这般劳苦。

“等头一批种子下了地，”我咕哝道，“园里剩下的地我就请人来犁。”

那条长地朝里挖进去约莫四英尺，我便决定换样活计歇一歇腰；于是拿起耙子把地耧平，横着拉上一根园艺准绳，再用尖头锄开出一道浅沟，也就是播种沟。

===Original===
“Now, Winnie and Bobsey,” I said, “it is time for you to do your part. Just stick these little onions in the trench about four inches apart;” and I gave each of them a little stick of the right length to measure the distance; for they had vague ideas of four inches. “Be sure,” I continued, “that you get the bottom of the onion down. This is the top, and this is the bottom. Press the onion in the soil just enough to make it stand firm, so. That’s right. Oh, you’re learning fast. Now I can rest, you see, while you do the planting.”

In a few moments they had stuck the fifteen feet of shallow trench, or drill, full of onions, which I covered with earth, packing it lightly with my hoe. I then moved the line fourteen inches further down and made another shallow drill. In this way we soon had all the onion sets in the ground. Merton came back with his load in time to see how it was done, and nodded his head approvingly. I now felt rested enough to dig awhile, and Merton started off to the barnyard again. We next sowed, in even shallower drills, the little onion seed that looked like gunpowder, for my garden book said that the earlier this was planted the better. We had completed only a few rows when Mr. Jones appeared, and said: “Plantin’ onions here? Why, neighbor, this ground is too dry and light for onions.”

“Is it? Well, I knew I’d make mistakes. I haven’t used near all my onion seed yet, however.”

===Chinese===
“好啦，温妮、鲍勃西，”我说，“该你们出力了。把这些小洋葱头插进沟里，大约隔四英寸一个；”我给了他们一人一根长短正合适的小棍，好拿来量距离，因为他们对四英寸毫无概念。“记住，”我接着说，“葱头底下要朝下。这是上头，这是底下。把葱头按进土里，按到立得稳当就成——喏，这样。对了。嗬，学得真快。你们瞧，这下我可以歇着，看你们种了。”

不消片刻，那条十五英尺长的浅沟便插满了葱头；我培上土，用锄头轻轻拍实。随后我把准绳往下挪了十四英寸，又开出另一道浅沟。这样干下去，不多时，所有的洋葱种球便都下了地。默顿拉着一车粪回来，恰好赶上瞧见这活是怎么干的，赞许地点了点头。我觉得歇得也差不多了，又可以挖上一阵，默顿便又动身往谷仓场院去了。接着，我们在更浅一些的沟里撒下那种看着像火药似的小小洋葱籽，因为我那本园艺书上讲，这籽下得越早越好。才种完几行，琼斯先生便露面了，说道：“在这儿种洋葱哪？嗐，邻居，这地太干又太松，种洋葱可不合适。”

“是吗？咳，我就知道自己免不了要出错。好在洋葱籽还远没用完呢。”

===Original===
“Oh, well, no great harm’s done. You’ve made the ground rich, and, if we have a moist season, like enough they’ll do well. P’raps it’s the best thing, after all, ’specially if you’ve put in the seed thick, as most people do. Let ’em all grow, and you’ll have a lot of little onions, or sets, of your own raisin’ to plant early next spring. Save the rest of your seed until you have some rich, strong, deep soil ready. I came over to say that if this weather holds a day or two longer I’ll plow the garden; and I thought I’d tell you, so that you might get ready for me. The sooner you get your early pertaters in the better.”

“Your words almost take the ache out of my back,” I said. “I fear we shouldn’t have much of a garden if I had to dig it all, but I thought I’d make a beginning with a few early vegetables.”

“That’s well enough, but a plow beats a fork all hollow. You’ll know what I mean when you see my plow going down to the beam and loosenin’ the ground from fifteen to twenty inches. So burn your big brush pile, and get out what manure you’re goin’ to put in the garden, and I’ll be ready when you are.”

===Chinese===
“嗐，没什么大碍。地倒是让你给肥了，赶上雨水勤的年成，多半长得还不赖。说不定这反倒是最稳妥的路子，尤其你要是籽下得密——大多数人都是这么下的——就索性让它们全长着，到明年初春，你便有自己培育的一大堆小洋葱，也就是种球，可以早早下地了。剩下的籽先收着，等备好一块肥沃、有劲、深厚的地再说。我过来是要说一声，这天气再撑上一两天，我就来给你犁园子；想着先跟你打个招呼，好让你趁早拾掇出来。早熟马铃薯越早下地越好。”

“您这几句话，几乎把我腰上的酸痛都说跑了，”我说。“这园子若全凭我一锹一锹挖出来，只怕成不了什么气候；不过我想先种几样早熟菜蔬，起个头。”

“起个头自然好，可论翻地，犁比叉强出十万八千里。等瞧见我的犁一直吃到犁杠，把地疏松到十五到二十英寸深，你就明白我的意思了。所以，把你那大柴枝堆烧了，把要进园的粪肥都搬出来备着，你几时备好，我几时便到。”

===Original===
“All right. Thank you. I’ll just plant some radishes, peas, and beans.”

“Not beans yet, Mr. Durham. Don’t put those in till the last of the month, and plant them very shallow when you do.”

“How one forgets when there’s not much experience to fall back upon! I now remember that my book said that beans, in this latitude, should not be planted until about the 1st of May.”

“And lima beans not till the 10th of May,” added Mr. Jones. “You might put in a few early beets here, although the ground is rather light for ’em. You could put your main crop somewhere else. Well, let me know when you’re ready. Junior and me are drivin’ things, too, this mornin’;” and he stalked away, whistling a hymn tune in rather lively time.

===Chinese===
“好，多谢。那我就先种点小萝卜、豌豆和菜豆。”

“菜豆还不到时候，达勒姆先生（Mr. Durham）。月底再下种，到那时也务必要种得极浅。”

“没多少经验可依傍，人就是这样丢三落四！这下我想起来了，书上说过，在这个纬度，菜豆要到五月一日前后方能下种。”

“利马豆更得挨到五月十号，”琼斯先生补了一句。“这儿倒可以点上几行早熟甜菜，虽说这地对它们来说嫌松了些。你那正经的大田一茬，尽可以另找地方。好了，几时备好，知会我一声。今儿早上，我跟小琼斯（Junior）也都在赶着活计往前奔呢；”说罢他迈开大步走了，嘴里吹着一支赞美诗的调子，节拍还相当轻快。

===Original===
I said: “Youngsters, I think I’ll get my garden book and be sure I’m right about sowing the radish and beet seed and the peas. Mr. Jones has rather shaken my confidence.”

When Merton came with the next load I told him that he could put the horse in the stable and help us. As a result, we soon had several rows of radishes and beets sown, fourteen inches apart. We planted the seed only an inch deep, and packed the ground lightly over it. Mousie, to her great delight, was allowed to drop a few of the seeds. Merton was ambitious to take the fork, but I soon stopped him, and said: “Digging is too heavy work for you, my boy. There is enough that you can do without overtaxing yourself. We must all act like good soldiers. The campaign of work is just opening, and it would be very foolish for any of us to disable ourselves at the start. We’ll plant only half a dozen rows of these dwarf peas this morning, and then this afternoon we’ll have the bonfire and get ready for Mr. Jones’s plow.”

At the prospect of the bonfire the younger children set up shouts of exultation, which cheered me on as I turned over the soil with the fork, although often stopping to rest. My back ached, but my heart was light. In my daily work now I had all my children about me, and their smaller hands were helping in the most practical way. Their voices were as joyous as the notes of the robins, song sparrows, and bluebirds that were singing all about us. A soft haze half obscured the mountains, and mellowed the sunshine. From the springing grass and fresh-turned soil came odors sweet as those which made Eden fragrant after “a mist went up from the earth and watered the whole face of the ground.”

===Chinese===
我说：“孩子们，我看我还是把园艺书取来，核一核小萝卜籽、甜菜籽和豌豆的下种之法，把心里没底的地方弄确实。琼斯先生这番话，可把我那点自信动摇得够呛。”

默顿拉着下一车粪回来，我叫他把马牵进马棚，也来搭把手。这样，不大工夫，我们便种下了好几行小萝卜和甜菜，行距十四英寸。籽只埋一英寸深，上头把土轻轻压实。小耗子得蒙恩准，亲手点了几粒籽，真是乐坏了。默顿一心想抢那把粪叉，我随即拦住了他：“挖掘这活对你太重，我的孩子。不至于劳累过度的活计，够你干的呢。咱们人人都得学做好兵。这场劳动的战役才刚刚拉开帷幕，谁要是一开头就把自个儿累垮，那可太不明智了。今儿上午，这些矮生豌豆咱们只种六行；下午点起那堆大火，好迎琼斯先生的犁。”

一听说有篝火可瞧，小的几个顿时欢呼雀跃；我抡着叉子翻土，虽不时停下歇息，心头却被这欢呼烘得暖洋洋的。腰是酸的，心里却是轻快的。如今我天天劳作，孩子们全在身边，一双双小手都在最切实的地方帮衬着。他们的欢声笑语，恰似四周鸣唱的知更鸟、歌雀和蓝鸲的啭鸣。一层柔柔的薄霭半掩着远山，把日光浸得温润。新生的青草和新翻的泥土里升起阵阵甜香，恰似当年“有雾气从地上腾，滋润遍地”之后，使伊甸园芬芳馥郁的那股气息。

===Original===
All the children helped to plant the peas, which we placed carefully and evenly, an inch apart, in the row, and covered with two inches of soil, the rows being two feet distant one from another. I had decided to plant chiefly McLean’s Little Gem, because they needed no stakes or brush for support. We were almost through our task when, happening to look toward the house, I saw my wife standing in the doorway, a framed picture.

“Dinner,” she called, in a voice as sweet to me as that of the robin singing in the cherry tree over her head.

The children stampeded for the house, Winnie crying: “Hurry up, mamma, for right after dinner papa will set the great brush pile on fire, and we’re going to dance round it like Indians. You must come out, too.”

===Chinese===
孩子们全都动手帮着种豌豆。我们把豆粒仔仔细细、匀匀整整地放进沟里，一粒挨一粒，相距一英寸，覆上两英寸厚的土，行与行相隔两英尺。我早已打定主意，以「麦克莱恩小宝石」（McLean’s Little Gem）为主，因为这品种无须木桩或枝条撑扶。活计眼看要收尾，我偶然朝屋子那边一望，只见妻子立在门口，活脱脱一幅镶了框的画。

“吃饭啦，”她唤道。那声音在我听来，同在她头顶樱桃树上啼唱的知更鸟一般甜美。

孩子们一窝蜂朝屋里奔去，温妮一路嚷着：“快点呀，妈妈，一吃完饭，爸爸就要点着那座大柴枝堆啦，我们要学印第安人围着火堆跳舞。你也一定得出来。”

## XXXIV Given a Chance / XXXIV 得到一个机会

===Original===
"Come in, Mr. Bagley," I said, "and bring the children. I want to talk with you all. Merton, you go home with Junior."

"But, papa—" he objected.

"Do as I bid you," I said, firmly, and I entered the squalid abode.

The man and the children followed me wonderingly. I sat down and looked the man steadily in the eye for a moment.

===Chinese===
“进来吧，巴格利先生（Mr. Bagley），”我说，“把孩子们都带上。我要跟你们大家谈一谈。默顿（Merton），你跟小琼斯（Junior）回家去。”

“可是，爸爸——”他提出异议。

“照我吩咐的做。”我口气坚决地说，随即走进了那所又脏又乱的屋子。

那男人和孩子们满心纳闷地跟了进来。我坐下，目不转睛地直望着他的眼睛，盯了片刻。

===Original===
"Let us settle one thing first," I began. "Do you think I am afraid of you?"

"S'pose not, with sich backin' as yer got," was the somewhat nervous reply.

"I told Mr. Jones after I came home last night that I should fight this thing alone if no one stood by me. But you see that your neighbors have reached the limit of forbearance. Now, Mr. Bagley, I didn't remain to threaten you. There has been enough of that, and from very resolute, angry men, too. I wish to give you and yours a chance. You've come to a place where two roads branch; you must take one or the other. You can't help yourself. You and your children won't be allowed to steal or prowl about any more. That's settled. If you go away and begin the same wretched life elsewhere, you'll soon reach the same result; you and your son will be lodged in jail and put at hard labor. Would you not better make up your mind to work for yourself and family, like an honest man? Look at these children. How are you bringing them up?—Take the road to the right. Do your level best, and I'll help you. I'll let bygones be bygones, and aid you in becoming a respectable citizen."

===Chinese===
“咱们先把一件事定下来，”我开口道，“你是不是以为我怕你？”

“您有那么硬的后援撑着，想来不会怕。”他回答，语气有些发虚。

“昨儿晚上到家以后，我就对琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）说过，要是没人肯站在我一边，我就一个人把这件事斗到底。可你也看见了，街坊们已经忍到了头。巴格利先生，我留下来不是为了吓唬你。那一套已经够多了，放话的还是些顶坚决、顶动怒的人。我想给你和你一家一个机会。你如今走到了岔路口，两条路，你必须拣一条走。这由不得你。你和你的孩子们，再不许偷摸，再不许四处游荡。这是定了的。你要是搬到别处，重开这号可怜巴巴的日子，很快还会落到同一个下场：你和你儿子会被关进监狱，押去服苦役。你何不打定主意，像个体面人那样，靠自己的力气养活自己和家人？你瞧瞧这几个孩子。你把他们往什么道上带呢？——往右边这条道走。拿出全部力气来，我会帮你。过去的账我一概不算，还要帮你变成一个受人敬重的公民。”

===Original===
"Oh, Hank, do be a man, now that Mr. Durham gives you a chance," sobbed his wife; "you know we've been living badly."

"That's it, Bagley. These are the questions you must decide. If you'll try to be a man, I'll give you my hand to stand by you. My religion, such as it is, requires that I shall not let a man go wrong if I can help it. If you'll take the road to the right and do your level best, there's my hand."

The man showed his emotion by a slight tremor only, and after a moment's thoughtful hesitation he took my hand and said, in a hoarse, choking voice: "You've got a claim on me now which all the rest couldn't git, even if they put a rope around my neck. I s'pose I have lived like a brute, but I've been treated like one, too."

===Chinese===
“哦，汉克（Hank），如今达勒姆先生（Mr. Durham）给了你一个机会，你可千万要做个男子汉啊，”他的妻子呜咽道，“你也知道，咱们这日子过得不像话。”

“正是这话，巴格利。这些问题必须由你自己拿主意。只要你肯试着做一个男子汉，我就把手伸给你，扶你一把。我那点信仰虽说浅薄，却要求我：只要力所能及，就不能眼看着一个人往邪路上滑。你若肯拣右边这条路，拿出全部力气来——我的手就在这儿。”

那男人只在微微一颤之间流露了心头的激动；他沉思踌躇了片刻，便握住我的手，用嘶哑哽咽的嗓音说：“从今往后，你对我算是有一份恩义了，这份恩义，其余那些人就是拿绳子套上我的脖子也休想讨得去。我这辈子活得像头畜生，可人家也一直拿我当畜生待。”

===Original===
"If you'll do as I say, I'll guarantee that within six months you'll be receiving all the kindness that a self-respecting man wants," I answered.

Then, turning to his wife, I asked, "What have you in the house to eat?"

"Next to nothin'," she said, drying her eyes with her apron, and then throwing open their bare cupboard.

"Put on your coat, Bagley, and come with me," I said.

He and his wife began to be profuse with thanks.

===Chinese===
“你只要照我说的做，我担保不出六个月，你就能得到一个自重的人所需要的一切善待。”我回答。

随后我转向他的妻子，问道：“家里还有什么吃的？”

“差不多什么都没有。”她说着，用围裙擦了擦眼睛，然后把那只空空如也的碗柜敞开来。

“穿上外套，巴格利，跟我来。”我说。

他和他妻子连声道起谢来，没完没了。

===Original===
"No, no!" I said, firmly. "I'm not going to give you a penny's worth of anything while you are able to earn a living. You shall have food at once; but I shall expect you to pay for it in work. I am going to treat you like a man and a woman, and not like beggars."

A few minutes later, some of the neighbors were much surprised to see Bagley and myself going up the road together.

My wife, Merton, and tenderhearted Mousie were at the head of the lane watching for me. Reassured, as we approached, they returned wonderingly to the house, and met us at the door.

"This is Mrs. Durham," I said. "My dear, please give Mr. Bagley ten pounds of flour and a piece of pork. After you're had your dinner, Mr. Bagley, I shall expect you, as we've agreed. And if you'll chain up that dog of yours, or, better still, knock it on the head with an axe, Mrs. Durham will go down and see your wife about fixing up your children."

===Chinese===
“不，不！”我斩钉截铁地说，“只要你们还挣得动饭吃，我就一个铜板的施舍也不给。吃的马上就有，可我要你们拿活儿来抵偿。我要把你们当男子汉、当女人那样看待，而不是当叫花子。”

几分钟以后，好些街坊大为惊讶，看见巴格利和我并肩沿大路走去。

我妻子、默顿，还有心肠软的小耗子（Mousie），正在车道口上守望着我。见我们走近，他们放了心，又满腹狐疑地回到屋里，在门口迎住我们。

“这位是达勒姆太太（Mrs. Durham），”我说，“亲爱的，请给巴格利先生十磅面粉，再给一块腌猪肉。巴格利先生，等你吃过午饭，我就等着你过来，咱们已经讲定了。还有，你要是把那条狗拴起来，或者，更好一点，一斧子结果了它，达勒姆太太就肯下去一趟，跟你太太商量怎么把你那些孩子拾掇利落。”

===Original===
Winifred gave me a pleased, intelligent look, and said, "Come in, Mr. Bagley;" while Merton and I hastened away to catch up with neglected work.

"Your husband's been good to me," said the man, abruptly.

"That's because he believes you are going to be good to yourself and your family," was her smiling reply.

"Will you come and see my wife?" he asked.

===Chinese===
温妮弗蕾德（Winifred）给了我一个欣喜而又心领神会的眼色，说了声“巴格利先生，请进”；默顿和我便赶紧走开，去补做耽误下的活计。

“你男人待我真好。”那男人没头没脑地说。

“那是因为他相信，你会好好待你自己、好好待你一家人。”她笑吟吟地回答。

“您肯去看望我太太吗？”他问。

===Original===
"Certainly, if I don't have to face your dog," replied Winifred.

"I'll kill the critter soon's I go home," muttered Bagley.

"It hardly pays to keep a big, useless dog," was my wife's practical comment.

In going to the cellar for the meat, she left him alone for a moment or two with Mousie; and he, under his new impulses, said: "Little gal, ef my children hurt your flowers agin, let me know, and I'll thrash 'em!"

===Chinese===
“当然肯，只要不用跟你的狗照面。”温妮弗蕾德回答。

“我一到家就宰了那畜生。”巴格利咕哝道。

“养一条又大又没用的狗，本来就划不来。”我妻子很实在地评了一句。

她下地窖去取肉，把他和小耗子单独留了一两分钟；他在新起的心思驱使下说道：“小姑娘，要是我的孩子再糟蹋你的花，你只管告诉我，看我不好好捶他们！”

===Original===
The child stole to his side and gave him her hand, as she replied, "Try being kind to them."

Bagley went home with some new ideas under his tattered old hat. At half past twelve he was on hand, ready for work.

"That dog that tried to bite ye is dead and buried," he said, "and I hope I buried some of my dog natur' with 'im."

"You've shown your good sense. But I haven't time to talk now. The old man has mown a good deal of grass. I want you to shake it out, and, as soon as he says it's dry enough, to rake it up. Toward night I'll be out with the wagon, and we'll stow all that's fit into the barn. Tomorrow I want your two eldest children to come and pick berries."

===Chinese===
小姑娘悄悄挪到他身边，把手伸给他，答道：“您试试对他们和气一点吧。”

巴格利顶着那顶破旧的老帽子回家了，帽檐底下装着几个新鲜的念头。十二点半，他已经到场，做好了干活的准备。

“那条想咬您的狗，已经死了，也埋了。”他说，“但愿我连自己那点狗性子，也一块儿给埋进土里了。”

“你这事办得明白。可我这会儿没工夫多讲。老头儿已经割了好大一片草。我要你把草抖开摊匀，等他说干得够了，就搂成堆。傍晚我赶车出来，咱们把干得合宜的都收进谷仓。明天，我要你最大的两个孩子来摘浆果。”

===Original===
"I'm in fer it, Mr. Durham. You've given me your hand, and I'll show yer how that goes furder with me than all the blood and thunder talk in Maizeville," said Bagley, with some feeling.

"Then you'll show that you can be a man like the rest of us," I said, as I hastened to our early dinner.

My wife beamed and nodded at me. "I'm not going to say anything to set you up too much," she said. "You are great on problems, and you are solving one even better than I hoped."

"It isn't solved yet," I replied. "We have only started Bagley and his people on the right road. It will require much patience and good management to keep them there. I rather think you'll have the hardest part of the problem yet on your hands. I have little time for problems now, however, except that of making the most of this season of rapid growth and harvest. I declare I'm almost bewildered when I see how much there is to be done on every side. Children, we must all act like soldiers in the middle of a fight. Every stroke must tell. Now, we'll hold a council of war, so as to make the most of the afternoon's work. Merton, how are the raspberries?"

===Chinese===
“这活我包下了，达勒姆先生。您把手伸给了我，我要让您瞧瞧，这个比整个梅兹维尔（Maizeville）连吓带吼的狠话都顶用。”巴格利有些动情地说。

“那你就是让大家看看，你也能像我们大家一样做个男子汉。”我说着，赶回去吃那顿开得早的午饭。

妻子笑盈盈地朝我直点头。“我可不想说什么好话把你捧得忘乎所以，”她说，“你一贯爱琢磨问题，眼下这一个，你解得比我指望的还漂亮。”

“问题还没解决呢，”我回答，“我们只是把巴格利和他一家领上了正路。要让他们在正路上站住，还得有极大的耐心和很高明的调理。我倒觉得，这难题最棘手的部分，往后要落在你的手上。不过如今我没什么工夫琢磨问题了，只除了一桩——把这个疯长和收获的季节用到十足。说真的，眼见四面八方都有这么多活等着干，我简直有点晕头转向。孩子们，咱们人人都得像厮杀正酣的士兵那样行事，一举一动都要见到成效。现在，咱们开个军事会议，好把下午的活儿安排到最好。默顿，树莓怎么样了？”

===Original===
"There are more ripe, papa, than I thought there would be."

"Then, Winnie, you and Bobsey must leave the weeding in the garden and help Merton pick berries this afternoon."

"As soon as it gets cooler," said my wife, "Mousie and I are going to pick, also."

"Very well," I agreed. "You can give us raspberries and milk tonight, and so you will be getting supper at the same time. Until the hay is ready to come in, I shall keep on hoeing in the garden, the weeds grow so rapidly. Tomorrow will be a regular fruit day all around, for there are two more cherry trees that need picking."

===Chinese===
“熟了的比我预想的多，爸爸。”

“那么，温妮（Winnie），今天下午你跟鲍勃西（Bobsey）放下菜园里锄草的活儿，去帮默顿摘浆果。”

“等天再凉快些，”妻子说，“我和小耗子也去摘。”

“很好，”我同意道，“晚上你就给我们上树莓加牛奶，晚饭也就顺带做了。在干草能进仓之前，我在菜园里接着锄草，杂草长得实在太快。明天干脆就是一个四面八方的果子日，因为还有两棵樱桃树等着摘呢。”

===Original===
Our short nooning over, we all went to our several tasks. The children were made to feel that now was the chance to win our bread for months to come, and that there must be no shirking. Mousie promised to clear away the things while my wife, protected by a large sunshade, walked slowly down to the Bagley cottage. Having seen that Merton and his little squad were filling the baskets with raspberries properly, I went to the garden and slaughtered the weeds where they threatened to do the most harm.

At last I became so hot and wearied that I thought I would visit a distant part of the upland meadow, and see how Bagley was progressing. He was raking manfully, and had accomplished a fair amount of work, but it was evident that he was almost exhausted. He was not accustomed to hard work, and had rendered himself still more unfit for it by dissipation.

"See here, Bagley," I said, "you are doing well, but you will have to break yourself into harness gradually. I don't wish to be hard upon you. Lie down under this tree for half an hour, and by that time I shall be out with the wagon."

"Mr. Durham, you have the feelin's of a man for a feller," said Bagley, gratefully. "I'll make up the time arter it gets cooler."

===Chinese===
短短的午歇一过，我们就各奔各的活计。孩子们被叮嘱得明明白白：眼下正是为往后几个月挣口粮的时候，一丝一毫也偷懒不得。小耗子答应收拾杯盘，我妻子则撑着一把大阳伞，慢慢地往巴格利家的小屋去了。我见默顿和他那一小队人马正像模像样地往篮子里装树莓，便进了菜园，朝杂草为害最凶的地方大肆剿杀过去。

到后来我又热又乏，便想到高地草甸的远处走一趟，看看巴格利干得如何。他在起劲地搂草，干的活也着实不少，可明摆着，他几乎已经筋疲力尽了。重活他本就不惯，加上平日放荡，身子更不堪劳作了。

“听着，巴格利，”我说，“你干得不坏，可你也得像新上套的马，一步一步慢慢来。我不想对你苛刻。到这棵树底下躺半个钟头，到那时我也就赶车出来了。”

“达勒姆先生，您待我这个苦命人，可有副人的心肠。”巴格利感激地说，“等凉快下来，我把这工夫补上。”

===Original===
Returning to the raspberry patch, I found Bobsey almost asleep, the berries often falling from his nerveless hands. Merton, meanwhile, with something of the spirit of a martinet, was spurring him to his task. I remembered that the little fellow had been busy since breakfast, and decided that he also, of my forces, should have a rest. He started up when he saw me coming through the bushes, and tried to pick with vigor again. As I took him up in my arms, he began, apprehensively, "Papa, I will pick faster, but I'm so tired!"

I reassured him with a kiss which left a decided raspberry flavor on my lips, carried him into the barn, and, tossing him on a heap of hay, said, "Sleep there, my little man, till you are rested."

He was soon snoring blissfully, and when I reached the meadow with the wagon, Bagley was ready to help with the loading.

"Well, well!" he exclaimed, "a little breathin'-spell does do a feller good on a hot day."

===Chinese===
回到树莓地里，只见鲍勃西几乎睡着了，树莓不断从他没了力气的小手里掉落。默顿呢，俨然带着几分小教官的架势，还在催他赶活。我想起这小家伙从早饭起就忙个不停，便决定：在我这支兵员里，他也该有一员的歇息。他见我从灌木丛间走来，一骨碌站起身，又打起精神去摘。我把他抱进怀里，他惴惴不安地开口了：“爸爸，我会摘得快些的，可我实在太累啦！”

我吻了吻他，叫他安心，这一吻在我唇上留下了一股地道的树莓味；随后把他抱进谷仓，往干草堆上一放，说：“睡吧，我的小人儿，睡到缓过乏来为止。”

不多时，他已经美滋滋地打起了呼噜。等我赶车到了草甸，巴格利已候在一旁，准备帮着装车。

“哎，哎！”他感叹道，“大热天里，稍稍缓口气，对人是真管用。”

===Original===
"No doubt about it," I said. "So long as you are on the right road, it does no harm to sit down a bit, because when you start again it's in the right direction."

After we had piled on as much of a load as the rude, extemporized rack on my market wagon could hold, I added, "You needn't go to the barn with me, for I can pitch the hay into the mow. Rake up another load, if you feel able."

"Oh, I'm all right now," he protested.

By the time I had unloaded the hay, I found that my wife and Mousie were among the raspberries, and that the number of full, fragrant little baskets was increasing rapidly.

===Chinese===
“这还用说，”我说，“只要你走在正路上，坐下来歇一歇没有坏处，因为再动身时，方向还是对的。”

等我们把那辆赶集马车上那副粗陋的临时草架装到再也搁不下时，我又添了一句：“你不必跟我去谷仓，草我能自己叉进草棚。你若还撑得住，就再搂一车。”

“嗐，我这会儿全缓过来了。”他忙不迭地说。

等我卸完草回来，只见妻子和小耗子已经进了树莓地，那些装得满满的、透着清香的小篮子正眼见着多起来。

===Original===
"Winifred, isn't this work, with your walk to the Bagley cottage, too much for you?"

"Oh, no," she replied, lightly. "An afternoon in idleness in a stifling city flat would have been more exhausting. It's growing cool now. What wretched, shiftless people those Bagleys are! But I have hopes of them. I'm glad Bobsey's having a nap."

"You shall tell me about your visit tonight. We are making good progress. Bagley is doing his best. Winnie," I called, "come here."

She brought her basket, nearly filled, and I saw that her eyes were heavy with weariness also.

===Chinese===
“温妮弗蕾德，又是干活，又走了趟巴格利家的小屋，你不会太吃不消吗？”

“哦，不会，”她轻松地回答，“要在城里那套憋闷的公寓里闲坐一个下午，才更乏人呢。这会儿已经凉快起来了。巴格利那一家人，过得多么潦倒、多么没章法啊！不过我对他们抱着指望。鲍勃西在打盹，我很高兴。”

“你那趟探望的事，晚上讲给我听。眼下进展顺利，巴格利在尽力干。温妮，”我喊道，“过来。”

她提着一只快装满的篮子走过来，我看得出，她的眼睛也已困倦得沉甸甸的了。

===Original===
"You've done well today, my child. Now go and look after your chickens, big and little. Then your day's work is done, and you can do what you please;" and I started for the meadow again.

By six o'clock, we had in the barn three loads of hay, and Merton had packed four crates of berries ready for market. Bobsey was now running about, as lively as a cricket, and Winnie, with a child's elasticity, was nearly as sportive. Bagley, after making up his half hour, came up the lane with a rake, instead of his ugly dog as on the evening before. A few moments later, he helped me lift the crates into the market wagon; and then, after a little awkward hesitation, began:

"I say, Mr. Durham, can't ye give a feller a job yerself? I declar' to you, I want to brace up; but I know how it'll be down at Rollins's. He'll be savage as a meat axe to me, and his men will be a-gibin'. Give me a job yerself, and I'll save enough out o' my wages to pay for his chickens, or you kin keep 'nuff back to pay for 'em."

I thought a moment, and then said, promptly: "I'll agree to this if Rollins will. I'll see him tonight."

===Chinese===
“你今天干得很好，我的孩子。现在去看看你的鸡吧，大的小的都照看一遍。这样你一天的活儿就完了，随你想玩什么都行。”说罢，我又动身往草甸去了。

到六点钟，谷仓里已经收进三车干草，默顿也装好了四箱浆果，只等上市。这时的鲍勃西又满地乱跑了，欢蹦得像只蟋蟀；温妮带着孩子的那股弹跳劲儿，也差不离地撒起欢来。巴格利把那半个钟头的工夫补足以后，扛着搂草耙顺着车道走上来了，跟前一个傍晚不一样，没有跟着他那条丑狗。过了一会儿，他帮我把箱子抬上赶集马车；然后，有些笨拙地踌躇了一阵，开了口：

“我说，达勒姆先生，您就不能自个儿给老弟派个活儿？我跟您交个实底，我是真心想撑起来的；可我知道，去了罗林斯（Rollins）那儿会是什么光景。他会凶得跟剁肉的斧子似的，他的伙计们还要百般奚落我。您自个儿雇我吧，我从工钱里省出钱来赔他的鸡，要么您就先扣下够数的一截，替我赔上。”

我思量了片刻，随即爽快地说：“只要罗林斯肯答应，我就照这个办。今晚我去见他。”

===Original===
"Did yer wife go to see my wife?"

"Yes, and she says she has hopes of you all. You've earned your bread today as honestly as I have, and you've more than paid for what my wife gave you this morning. Here's a quarter to make the day square, and here's a couple of baskets of raspberries left over. Take them to the children." "Well, yer bring me right to the mark," he said, emphasizing his words with a slap on his thigh. "I've got an uphill row to hoe, and it's good ter have some human critters around that'll help a feller a bit."

I laughed as I clapped him on the shoulder, and said: "You're going to win the fight, Bagley. I'll see Rollins at once, for I find I shall need another man awhile."

"Give me the job then," he said, eagerly, "and give me what you think I'm wuth;" and he jogged off home with that leaven of all good in his heart—the hope of better things.

===Chinese===
“您太太去看过我太太了吗？”

“去了，她还说，她对你们一家都抱着指望。今天你挣面包，跟我挣得一样正正当当，我太太早上给你的那些东西，你也早已赔过了。这里有两角五分钱，好把今天的账扯平；还有多出来的两篮树莓，拿去给孩子们吧。”“好哇，您这一下真把我带到点儿上了，”他说着，在大腿上猛拍一记来加重话头，“我这垄地是得往坡上犁的，身边能有几个拿人当人的肯帮老弟一把，真是难得。”

我笑着在他肩上拍了一记，说：“这一仗你准能打赢，巴格利。我这就去找罗林斯，因为我盘算下来，手头还需再添一个人手，用上一阵子。”

“那这活儿就给我吧，”他急切地说，“您觉得我值多少就给多少。”说罢，他便颠颠地往家去了，心里揣着那份使一切变好的酵头：指望往后的日子。

## XXXIII Given His Choice / XXXIII 得到一个选择

===Original===
Junior had good reason for bringing Merton to a sudden halt in his impetuous and hostile advance. The man coming up the lane, with a savage dog, was the father of the ill-nurtured children. He had felt a little uneasy as to the results of their raid upon our fruit, and had walked across the fields to give them the encouragement of his presence, or to cover their retreat, which he now did effectually.

It took Junior but a moment to explain to my boy that they were no match "for the two brutes," as he expressed himself, adding, "The man is worse than the dog."

Merton, however, was almost reckless from anger and a sense of unprovoked wrong, and he darted into the house for his gun.

"See here, Merton," said Junior, firmly, "shoot the dog if they set him on us, but never fire at a human being. You'd better give me the gun; I am cooler than you are."

===Chinese===
小琼斯（Junior）把默顿（Merton）那股冒冒失失、来意汹汹的冲劲猛地拦住，自有他充分的理由。顺着小路走来的那个男人带着一条凶恶的狗，正是那一群缺乏管教的孩子的父亲。他对自己那伙孩子洗劫我们的果子会落个什么下场，心里也有些不踏实，便穿过田地步行过来，好亲自到场给他们壮胆，或是替他们撤退打掩护——而这一回，这掩护他打得着实彻底。

小琼斯没费多少口舌，就让我的儿子明白了：照他自己的说法，他们两个不是“那两个畜生”的对手；他还添了一句：“那人比狗还坏。”

然而默顿满腔怒火，又觉得平白受了欺侮，几乎什么都不顾了，一转身就蹿进屋里去拿枪。

“听着，默顿，”小琼斯斩钉截铁地说，“他们要是放狗咬咱们，你就打狗；可千万不能朝人开枪。枪还是交给我吧，我比你冷静。”

===Original===
They had no occasion to use the weapon, however. The man shook his fist at them, while his children indulged in taunts and coarse derision. The dog, sharing their spirit and not their discretion, started for the boys, but was recalled, and our undesirable neighbors departed leisurely.

All this was related to me after nightfall, when I returned with my wife and younger children from the Maizeville Landing. I confess that I fully shared Merton's anger, although I listened quietly.

"You grow white, Robert," said my wife. "I suppose that's the most dangerous kind of heat—white-heat. Don't take the matter so to heart. We can't risk getting the ill will of these ugly people. You know what Mr. Jones said about them."

"This question shall be settled in twenty-four hours!" I replied. "That man and his family are the pest of the neighborhood, and everyone lives in a sort of abject dread of them. Now, the neighbors must say 'yes' or 'no' to the question whether we shall have decency, law, and order, or not. Merton, unharness the horse. Junior, come with me; I'm going to see your father."

===Chinese===
不过，那件家伙到底没有用上。那男人朝他们挥舞拳头，他的孩子们则肆意辱骂，满口粗话地奚落不休。那条狗倒是承袭了主人的凶劲，没承袭主人的算计，径直朝两个孩子扑去，却被唤了回去。我们这些不受欢迎的邻居，就这样大摇大摆、慢悠悠地走掉了。

这一切是入夜以后才讲给我听的，那时我正带着妻子和年幼的孩子们从梅兹维尔码头（Maizeville Landing）回来。我承认，默顿的怒气我感同身受，虽然我听的时候一声不响。

“罗伯特（Robert），你一生气，脸色就发白，”妻子说，“我看那才是最危险的一种火——白热的火。这件事别太往心里去。咱们可冒不起招惹这伙可憎的人的险。琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）是怎么议论他们的，你知道。”

“这个问题，二十四小时之内必须了断！”我答道，“那家伙和他一家是四邻八舍的祸害，人人都在一种低声下气的恐惧里过日子。如今，这地方究竟还要不要体面、要不要法律和秩序，街坊们必须对这个问题说一声‘要’或者‘不要’。默顿，去把马卸下来。小琼斯，跟我来；我这就去见你父亲。”

===Original===
I found Mr. Jones sleepy and about to retire, but his blue eyes were soon wide open, with an angry fire in them.

"You take the matter very quietly, Mr. Durham;" he said; "more quietly than I could."

"I shall not fume about the affair a moment. I prefer to act. The only question for you and the other neighbors to decide is, Will you act with me? I am going to this man Bagley's house tomorrow, to give him his choice. It's either decency and law-abiding on his part, now, or prosecution before the law on mine. You say that you are sure that he has burned barns, and made himself generally the terror of the region. Now, I won't live in a neighborhood infested by people little better than wild Indians. My feelings as a man will not permit me to submit to insult and injury. What's more, it's time the people about here abated this nuisance."

"You are right, Robert Durham!" said Mr. Jones, springing up and giving me his hand. "I've felt mean, and so have others, that we've allowed ourselves to be run over by this rapscallion. If you go tomorrow, I'll go with you, and so will Rollins. His hen roost was robbed t'other night, and he tracked the thieves straight toward Bagley's house. He says his patience has given out. It only needs a leader to rouse the neighborhood, but it ain't very creditable to us that we let a newcomer like you face the thing first."

===Chinese===
琼斯先生这时睡意正浓，正要歇息，可他那双蓝眼睛很快便睁大了，眼里燃起一簇怒火。

“达勒姆先生（Mr. Durham），您这事对待得可真平静，”他说，“换了我，可平静不到这个份上。”

“这件事我一分钟也不打算干生气，我要动手。你和别的街坊要决定的只有一件事：肯不肯跟我一道动手？明天我就到这个巴格利（Bagley）的家里去，给他自己选择：要么，打现在起做个守规矩、遵法律的人；要么，就由我出面送他见官法办。你说过，你肯定他烧过谷仓，早成了这一带人人谈之色变的祸星。听着，我可不乐意住在一片让一群比野蛮印第安人好不了多少的家伙搅得乌烟瘴气的地方。身为男子汉，这般受辱受害，我的血性咽不下去。再说，这一带的乡亲也早该除一除这个祸害了。”

“你说得对，罗伯特·达勒姆！”琼斯先生霍地站起身，向我伸出手来。“由着这个泼皮作践，我心里早就憋屈，旁人也都一样。你明天去，我一定奉陪，罗林斯（Rollins）也会去。前儿夜里他的鸡窝遭了偷，他顺着脚印一直追到巴格利家跟前。他说他的耐性已经使到头了。这地方只缺一个领头的把四邻唤起来；可让初来乍到的你打头阵，我们脸上实在不光彩。”

===Original===
"Very well," I said, "it's for you and your neighbors to show now how much grit and manhood you have. I shall start for Bagley's house at nine tomorrow. Of course I shall be glad to have company, and if he sees that the people will not stand any more of his rascality, he'll be more apt to behave himself or else clear out."

"He'll have to do one or the other," said Mr. Jones, grimly. "I'll go right down to Rolling's. Come, Junior, we may want you."

At eight o'clock the next morning, a dozen men, including the constable, were in our yard. My wife whispered, "Do be prudent, Robert." She was much reassured, however, by the largeness of our force.

===Chinese===
“很好，”我说，“那就看你们和你们的街坊如今还拿得出多少胆气和丈夫气概。明天九点，我动身去巴格利家。自然，有人同行我求之不得；他若看清乡亲们再不容他撒泼使坏，多半就会规规矩矩，要不就趁早滚蛋。”

“这两条路他总得挑一条，”琼斯先生冷冷地说。“我这就下罗林斯家去。走，小琼斯，说不定用得着你。”

第二天早上八点，十来个人已经聚在我们院子里，治安员也在内。妻子悄声叮嘱：“罗伯特，千万谨慎。”不过，眼见我们人多势众，她也就宽心了许多。

===Original===
We soon reached the dilapidated hovel, and were so fortunate as to find Bagley and all his family at home. Although it was the busiest season, he was idle. As I led my forces straight toward the door, it was evident that he was surprised and disconcerted, in spite of his attempt to maintain a sullen and defiant aspect. I saw his evil eye resting on one and another of our group, as if he was storing up grudges to be well paid on future dark nights. His eldest son stood with the dog at the corner of the house, and as I approached, the cur, set on by the boy, came toward me with a stealthy step. I carried a heavy cane, and just as the brute was about to take me by the leg, I struck him a blow on the head that sent him howling away.

The man for a moment acted almost as if he had been struck himself. His bloated visage became inflamed, and he sprang toward me.

"Stop!" I thundered. My neighbors closed around me, and he instinctively drew back.

"Bagley," I cried, "look me in the eye." And he fixed upon me a gaze full of impotent anger. "Now," I resumed, "I wish you and your family to understand that you've come to the end of your rope. You must become decent, law-abiding people, like the rest of us, or we shall put you where you can't harm us. I, for one, am going to give you a last chance. Your children were stealing my fruit last night, and acting shamefully afterward. You also trespassed, and you threatened these two boys; you are idle in the busiest time, and think you can live by plunder. Now, you and yours must turn the sharpest corner you ever saw. Your two eldest children can come and pick berries for me at the usual wages, if they obey my orders and behave themselves. One of the neighbors here says he'll give you work, if you try to do it well. If you accept these terms, I'll let the past go. If you don't, I'll have the constable arrest your boy at once, and I'll see that he gets the heaviest sentence the law allows, while if you or your children make any further trouble, I'll meet you promptly in every way the law permits. But, little as you deserve it, I am going to give you and your family one chance to reform, before proceeding against you. Only understand one thing, I am not afraid of you. I've had my say."

===Chinese===
不多时我们便到了那所破烂的窝棚，运气不坏，巴格利和他全家都在。虽是农忙时节，他却闲散着。当我带着人马径直朝门口走去时，分明看得出他又惊又慌，尽管他竭力装出一副阴沉乖戾、不肯服软的样子。我看见他那双恶毒的眼睛在我们一行人身上挨个儿溜来溜去，仿佛在把一笔笔仇恨记下账来，好趁日后哪个漆黑的夜晚加倍偿还。他的长子牵着狗站在屋角，我走近时，那条癞皮狗受了小主人的唆使，蹑手蹑脚地朝我凑过来。我手里提着一根沉甸甸的手杖，就在那畜生张口要咬我腿的一霎，照它头上狠狠一击，打得它嗥叫着逃开了。

一时间，这男人的举止几乎像挨了打的是他自己。他那浮肿的面孔涨得通红，朝我猛扑过来。

“站住！”我雷霆也似的喝道。街坊们立刻围拢到我身旁，他本能地缩了回去。

“巴格利，”我喝道，“看着我的眼睛。”他直勾勾地盯住我，满眼是无可发泄的怒火。“现在，”我接着说，“我要你和你全家都明白：你们的好日子已经到头了。你们必须像我们大伙儿一样，做规矩体面、遵纪守法的人，不然，我们就把你们弄到一个害不着我们的地方去。就拿我来说，我要给你最后一次机会。昨儿夜里，你的孩子们偷我的果子，事后还撒泼胡闹；你本人也擅闯他人的地界，还恐吓了这两个孩子；农忙时节你游手好闲，满以为可以靠抢掠过活。如今，你和你这一家人，必须来一个你们有生以来最急的急转弯。你两个大孩子可以来替我摘浆果，工钱照旧，只要他们听我的吩咐、守本分。这里的街坊就有一位说了，只要你肯卖力气干好，他也给你活儿做。这些条件你若应下，往事我一概不究；你若不依，我立刻叫治安员把你儿子抓起来，还要让他领受法律容许的最重刑罚；往后你或是你的孩子再敢生事，凡法律许可的手段，我都会当场面面俱到地回敬你。不过，尽管你实在不配，我还是打算在对你采取行动之前，给你和你全家一次改过自新的机会。只有一点你要弄清楚：我不怕你。我的话完了。”

===Original===
"I haven't had mine," said Rollins, stepping forward excitedly. "You, or your scapegrace boy there, robbed my hen roost the other night, and you've robbed it before. There isn't a man in this region but believes that it was you who burned the barns and haystacks. We won't stand this nonsense another hour. You've got to come to my hayfields and work out the price of those chickens, and after that I'll give you fair wages. But if there's any more trouble, we'll clean you out as we would a family of weasels."

"Yes, neighbor Bagley," added Mr. Jones, in his dry, caustic way, "think soberly. I hope you are sober. I'm not one of the threatening barkin' sort, but I've reached the p'int where I'll bite. The law will protect us, an' the hull neighborhood has resolved, with Mr. Durham here, that you and your children shall make no more trouble than he and his children. See?"

"Look-a-here," began the man, blusteringly, "you needn't come threatenin' in this blood and thunder style. The law'll protect me as well as—"

===Chinese===
“我的话还没说呢，”罗林斯激动地抢上前来，“你，要不就是你那个不成器的小子，前儿夜里掏了我的鸡窝，从前也没少偷。这一带没有一个男人不相信：谷仓和草垛就是你们烧的。这种混账勾当，我们再忍一个钟头也不能够。你必须上我的草场干活，把那几只鸡的价钱挣出来赔上，往后我再给你公道的工钱。可要是再生事端，我们就把你一家清除出去，跟剿一窝黄鼠狼一样。”

“是啊，巴格利街坊，”琼斯先生用他那种干巴巴、带刺儿的腔调补上一句，“清醒着点儿想想。但愿你这会儿就没醉。我可不是光会汪汪叫着吓唬人的那一号，可我已经到了张口就咬的地步。法律会保护我们，街坊四邻连同眼前这位达勒姆先生已经拿定了主意：你和你那些孩子，休想比人家达勒姆和他的孩子多闹一点乱子。听明白了没？”

“喂，你们听听，”那人虚张声势地开了腔，“犯不着摆出这么惊天动地、刀光血影的架势来吓唬我。法律照样也保护我，就跟——”

===Original===
Ominous murmurs were arising from all my neighbors, and Mr. Jones now came out strong.

"Neighbors," he said, "keep cool. The time to act hasn't come yet. See here, Bagley, it's hayin' and harvest. Our time's vallyble, whether yours is or not. You kin have just three minutes to decide whether you'll take your oath to stop your maraudin' and that of your children;" and he pulled out his watch.

"Let me add my word," said a little man, stepping forward. "I own this house, and the rent is long overdue. Follow neighbor Jones's advice or we'll see that the sheriff puts your traps out in the middle of the road."

"Oh, of course," began Bagley. "What kin one feller do against a crowd?"

===Chinese===
四下里的街坊响起一片不祥的低声怒吼，琼斯先生这会儿更是寸步不让、声色俱厉。

“街坊们，”他说，“都冷静点，动手的时候还没到。你听好，巴格利，眼下正是割草收庄稼的当口，我们的工夫金贵，你的金不金贵随你的便。只给你三分钟拿主意：到底赌不赌咒，从此住手，不再打劫，也管住你的孩子不再打劫。”说着，他掏出了怀表。

“我也添一句话，”一个矮小的汉子跨前一步，“这房子是我的产业，房租早已拖欠多时。你听琼斯街坊的劝告便罢；若不听，我们就让县治安官把你的破烂家什撂到大路正当中去。”

“哦，那自然，”巴格利开了腔，“一个人，还能拗得过一大帮子人么？”

===Original===
"Sw'ar, as I told you," said Mr. Jones, sharply and emphatically. "What do you mean by hangin' fire so? Do you s'pose this is child's play and make-believe? Don't ye know that when quiet, peaceable neighbors git riled up to our pitch, they mean what they say? Sw'ar, as I said, and be mighty sudden about it."

"Don't be a fool," added his wife, who stood trembling behind him. "Can't you see?"

"Very well, I sw'ar it," said the man, in some trepidation.

"Now, Bagley," said Mr. Jones, putting back his watch, "we want to convert you thoroughly this mornin'. The first bit of mischief that takes place in this borough will bring the weight of the law on you;" and, wheeling on his heel, he left the yard, followed by the others.

===Chinese===
“起誓，照我说的起誓，”琼斯先生厉声说道，一字一顿。“你这么拖泥带水，到底什么意思？你当这是小孩子过家家、装假玩儿么？你难道不知道，安分守己的街坊一旦叫人撩拨到我们这个地步，说出的话就是钉是钉、铆是铆？起誓，照我说的办，麻利点儿。”

“别犯傻了，”他的妻子插了一句，她一直站在他身后瑟瑟发抖。“你还看不明白么？”

“好吧，我起誓，”那人说道，语气里有几分战战兢兢。

“好了，巴格利，”琼斯先生把怀表揣了回去，“今天早上，我们要把你彻底改造过来。这地方上往后但凡再出一起祸事，法律的千钧重担就会落到你的头上。”说罢，他一个急转身走出院子，其余的人鱼贯跟上。

## XVII Good Bargains in Maple Sugar / XVII 枫糖里的好买卖

===Original===
By eleven o’clock we had all the basement cleaned except the one cow stall that was filled to the ceiling with litter; and Winnie had washed the windows. Then John Jones’s lank figure darkened the doorway, and he cried, “Hello, neighbor, what ye drivin’ at?”

“Look around and see, and then tell us where to get a lot of chickens.”

“Well, I declare! How you’ve slicked things up! You’re not goin’ to scrub the dirt floor, are you? Well, well, this looks like business—just the place for chickens. Wonder old man Jamison didn’t keep ’em here; but he didn’t care for fowls. Now I think of it, there’s to be a vandoo the first of the week, and there was a lot o’ chickens printed on the poster.”

I smiled.

===Chinese===
到十一点钟，整个地下室都已收拾干净，只剩那一格牛栏还没弄——栏里的褥草一直堆到天花板；温妮（Winnie）把窗户也都擦洗干净了。这时，约翰·琼斯（John Jones）那瘦长的身影堵住了门口，他大声嚷道：“喂，邻居，你们这是要干啥呀？”

“你四下里瞧瞧，瞧明白了，再告诉我们上哪儿能弄到一大群鸡。”

“嗐，真是的！你们把这儿拾掇得这样利索！不是连这泥地也要刷洗吧？嗯，嗯，这架势看着是正经要办起来了——养鸡再合适不过。怪的是贾米森老头（old man Jamison）先前怎么没在这儿养；原来他不喜欢家禽。对了，我想起来了，下礼拜初头有一场拍卖会（vandoo），告示上还印着有一批鸡要出手呢。”

我微微一笑。

===Original===
“Oh, I don’t mean that the chickens themselves was on the poster, but a statement that a lot would be sold at auction. I’ll bid ’em in for you if they’re a good lot. If you, a city chap, was to bid, some straw bidder would raise ’em agin you. I know what they’re wuth, and everybody there’ll know I do, and they’ll try no sharp games with me.”

“That will suit me exactly, Mr. Jones. I don’t want any game fowls of that kind.”

“Ha, ha! I see the p’int. Have you looked into the root cellar?”

“Yes; we opened the door and looked, but it was dark as a pocket.”

===Chinese===
“噢，我不是说告示上印着鸡本身，是说上面登明有一批鸡要拍卖。只要是一批好鸡，我就替你把它们拍进手里。你一个城里人去出价，随便哪个托价的家伙都会跟你抬上去。它们值几个钱，我心里有数，到场的人也都知道我有数，谁也别想跟我耍把戏。”

“那再合我的意不过了，琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）。‘把戏’鸡我可不想要。”

“哈哈！我听出你这话里的俏皮劲儿了。菜窖（root cellar）你进去看过没有？”

“看过；我们把门开了，朝里望了望，只是黑得像口袋底，什么也瞧不见。”

===Original===
