“Well, I don’t b’lieve in matches around a barn, but I’ll show you something;” and he opened the door, struck a match, and, holding it aloft, revealed a heap of turnips, another of carrots, five barrels of potatoes, and three of apples. The children pounced upon the last with appetites sharpened by their morning’s work.

“You see,” resumed Mr. Jones, “these were here when old man Jamison died. If I hadn’t sold the place I should have taken them out before long, and got rid of what I didn’t want. Now you can have the lot at a low figure,” which he named.

“I’ll take them,” I said, promptly.

“The carrots make it look like a goldmine,” cried Merton.

===Chinese===
“这么说吧，在牲口棚跟前我向来不兴划火柴，不过这会儿我让你们开开眼；”说着他推开窖门，划着一根火柴，高高举起，照见一堆芜菁、一堆胡萝卜、五桶马铃薯，还有三桶苹果。孩子们干了一上午的活，胃口给磨得尖尖的，立时便朝最后那几桶扑了过去。

“你瞧，”琼斯先生接着说，“贾米森老头咽气的时候，这些东西就搁在这儿。这地方要不是我把它卖了，过不了多久我也会把它们搬出来，把不想要的处理掉。如今这一整堆，你出个低价就能全数拿下，”说着他报了个数目。

“我要了，”我一口应承。

“这一照，胡萝卜简直让这儿像座金矿了，”默顿（Merton）嚷道。

===Original===
“Well, you’re wise,” resumed Mr. Jones. “You’ll have to get a cow and a horse, and here’s fodder for ’em handy. Perhaps I can pick ’em out for you, too, at the vandoo. You can go along, and if anything strikes your fancy I’ll bid on it.”

“O papa,” cried the children, in chorus, “can we go with you to the vandoo?”

“Yes, I think so. When does the sale take place?”

“Next Tuesday. That’s a good breed of potatoes. Jamison allus had the best of everything. They’ll furnish you with seed, and supply your table till new ones come. I guess you could sell a barrel or so of apples at a rise.”

===Chinese===
“嗯，你办得明白，”琼斯先生接着说，“你反正要添一头奶牛、一匹马，喂它们的草料这儿现成就有。到了拍卖会上，说不定我还能替你相看着挑。你只管同去，瞧见中意的东西，我来替你出价。”

“哦，爸爸，”孩子们齐声嚷道，“我们能跟你一起去拍卖会吗？”

“行，我想可以。拍卖定在哪一天？”

“下礼拜二。那马铃薯的种性不赖，贾米森老头样样都置办顶好的。这些既可留种，又能供你们的餐桌一直吃到新土豆下来。赶上行市看涨，你兴许还能卖出一两桶苹果。”

===Original===
“I’ve found a market for them already. Look at these children; and I’m good for half a barrel myself if they don’t decay too soon. Where could we find better or cheaper food? All the books say that apples are fattening.”

“That’s true of man and beast, if the books do say it. They’ll keep in this cool, dark cellar longer than you’d think—longer than you’ll let ’em, from the way they’re disappearin’. I guess *I’ll* try one.”

“Certainly, a dozen, just as if they were still yours.”

“They wasn’t mine—they belonged to the Jamison estate. I’ll help myself now quicker’n I would before. I might come it over a live man, you know, but not a dead one.”

===Chinese===
“这市场我已经找到了。瞧瞧这些孩子；再说，只要它们不烂得太快，我自己也包下半桶。上哪儿还能找得到比这更好又更便宜的吃食？书上一致说，苹果吃了养人长膘。”

“书上说得不差，人不拘、牲口不拘，都是这个理。在这又凉快又黑暗的地窖里，苹果存放的时日比你想的要长——只是照眼下这股消失的势头，怕是等不到那么久，就让你们给吃光了。依我说，*我*也来尝一个。”

“当然啦，拿吧，拿上一打，就当它们还是你自家的。”

“它们先前可不是我的——是贾米森家遗产上的东西。这么一说，我这会儿下手，倒比刚才痛快了。你是知道的，活人我兴许还能糊弄一把，死人跟前的便宜我可占不得。”

===Original===
“I’d trust you with either.”

While I was laughing at this phase of honesty, he resumed: “This is the kind of place to keep apples—cool, dry, dark, even temperature. Why, they’re as crisp and juicy as if just off the trees. I came over to make a suggestion. There’s a lot of sugar maple trees on your place, down by the brook. Why not tap ’em, and set a couple of pots b’ilin’ over your open fire? You’d kill two birds with one stone; the fire’d keep you warm, and make a lot of sugar in the bargain. I opinion, too, the children would like the fun.”

They were already shouting over the idea, but I said dubiously, “How about the pails to catch the sap?”

“Well,” said Mr. Jones, “I’ve thought of that. We’ve a lot of spare milk pails and pans, that we’re not usin’. Junior understands the business; and, as we’re not very busy, he can help you and take his pay in sugar.”

===Chinese===
“不管是活人还是死人，交到你手里，我都放心。”

我正笑他这副诚实的模样，他又开了口：“存苹果就得数这种地方——又凉、又干、又暗，温度还匀净。嗬，这些苹果又脆又水灵，跟才从树上摘下来似的。我过来是想出个主意。你那块地界上，沿着溪水往下，长着一大片糖枫。何不在树上钻眼取汁，就着敞火架起两口锅来熬呢？这叫一举两得：火给你们取了暖，外带还能熬出好些糖。依我看，孩子们也准喜欢这份乐子。”

听了这个主意，孩子们已经欢呼成一片；我却有些迟疑，问道：“接树液的桶上哪儿找呢？”

“这个嘛，”琼斯先生说，“我也早想过了。我们有一批闲着不用的挤奶桶和盆子。小琼斯（Junior）懂这一行；眼下我们又不大忙，他可以来帮你，工钱就用糖抵。”

===Original===
The subject of poultry was forgotten; and the children scampered off to the house to tell of this new project.

Before Mr. Jones and I left the basement, he said: “You don’t want any partition here at present, only a few perches for the fowls. There’s a fairish shed, you remember, in the upper barnyard, and when ’tain’t very cold or stormy the cow will do well enough there from this out. The weather’ll be growin’ milder ’most every day, and in rough spells you can put her in here. Chickens won’t do her any harm. Law sakes! when the main conditions is right, what’s the use of havin’ everything jes’ so? It’s more important to save your time and strength and money. You’ll find enough to do without one stroke that ain’t needful.” Thus John Jones fulfilled his office of mentor.

===Chinese===
家禽的事就此被抛在了脑后；孩子们一溜烟跑回屋里，把这桩新打算报与大伙去了。

琼斯先生和我离开地下室之前，他又嘱咐道：“眼下这儿不必打隔断，给鸡搭几根栖架就够了。上首谷仓场院里不是有个还算像样的棚子么，你记得的；打今儿往后，只要天不很冷、没有风雪，奶牛待在那儿就尽够了。天气差不多要一天暖似一天了，赶上天气恶劣的当口，你把她牵到这儿来就是。鸡碍不着她什么。老天爷！大条件都对头了，何苦样样都非得摆弄得一丝不差？省下时间、力气和钱，那才更要紧。一桩不必要的活计都不添，你要干的活就已够你忙的了。”约翰·琼斯就这样尽了他这位良师的职分。

## XLI Hoarding for Winter / XLI 囤粮过冬

===Original===
As was stated early in this simple history the original barn was built on a hillside, the rear facing the southeast; and since the foundations were still in a fair condition, and the site was convenient, I determined to build on the same spot, somewhat modifying the old plan. I had read of the importance of keeping manure under cover, and now arranged that by a trap door the cleanings of the horse and cow stable should be thrown into the basement, which, by a solid brick partition, should be so divided as to leave ample room for a dark cellar in which to store roots and apples. Through this trap door in the stable rich earth and muck from the banks of the creek could be thrown down also, covering the manure, and all could be worked over and mixed on rainy days. By this method I could make the most of my fertilizers, which may be regarded as the driving wheel of the farm.

I had decided that the poultry house and pigsty should form an extension to the barn, and that both should be built in the side of the bank also. They would thus have an exposure to the south, and at the same time, being formed in part by an excavation, would be cool in summer. The floor of the sty should have a slight downward slope, and be cemented. Therefore it could be kept perfectly clean. This residence of Bobsey’s future pets should be at the extreme end of the extension, and above it should be a room in which I could store picked-up apples, corn, and other food adapted to their needs, also a conduit by which swill could be poured into the trough below without the necessity of entering the pen. I proposed to keep only two or three pigs at a time, buying them when young from neighboring farmers, and fattening them for our own use according to my own ideas.

The poultry house, between the barn and sty, was to be built so that its side, facing the south, should be chiefly of glass. It was so constructed as to secure the greatest amount of light and warmth. Eggs in winter form the most profitable item in poultry keeping, and these depend on warmth, food, shelter, and cleanliness, with the essential condition that the hens are young. All the pullets of Winnie’s early broods therefore had been kept, and only the young cockerels eaten or sold. We had the prospect of wintering about fifty laying hens; and the small potatoes we had saved would form a large portion of their food. Indeed, for some weeks back, such small tubers, boiled and mashed with meal, had formed the main feed of our growing chickens.

===Chinese===
在这部朴素的记述开篇不久便已交代过，原先的谷仓是沿着一面山坡盖的，仓背朝向东南；既然旧地基还很完好，地势又便利，我便决意在原址重建，把旧图样稍加改动。我读到过粪肥加盖贮存的重要性，眼下便作了这样一番安排：马棚和牛栏里清扫出的粪草，从一扇活动地板门倒进地下室；地下室用一道坚实的砖砌隔墙隔开，留出宽绰的地方做一间黑暗的菜窖，好存放根菜和苹果。厩棚里这扇活板门，也用来把肥土和穆德纳溪（Moodna Creek）岸边挖来的河泥倒下去，覆盖在粪肥上头；逢着雨天，便把这些东西统统翻捣掺和。这样，我的肥料就能物尽其用，而肥料这种东西，尽可以看作农场的主动轮。

我已定下主意，让鸡舍和猪圈接在谷仓后头，构成一排附属建筑，两处也同样掘进土坡里去盖。这样它们朝南采光，同时又因为有一部分是向地下开挖而成，夏天还能保持阴凉。猪圈的地坪要带一点向下的坡度，并抹上水泥，如此便能保洁得干干净净。这处鲍勃西（Bobsey）未来的宠物们的居所，安排在这排附属建筑的最尽头；它上首再隔出一间屋子，好存放我拾来的落果、玉米，以及别的合乎猪子需要的饲料；还要设一条溜槽，泔水从这里倒下去，直落底下的食槽，用不着人走进猪栏。我打算一回只养两三口猪，向邻近农户买猪崽，照我自己的心思把它们喂肥，供自家食用。

鸡舍夹在谷仓与猪圈之间，盖法是朝南的一面主要镶玻璃，构造上务求采光和暖意都收得最足。冬天的鸡蛋，是养鸡收益最厚的一项，而蛋靠的是温暖、饲料、棚舍和清洁，外有一条顶要紧的条件：母鸡要年轻。因此，温妮（Winnie）早先几窝孵出的小母鸡全数留下，只把小公鸡宰了吃或是卖掉。我们有望囤下五十只上下下蛋的母鸡过冬；存下的那些小土豆，将充作它们食料的大头。其实往前几个星期，这种小薯煮烂了掺上谷粉捣碎，一直就是我们那些正在长个的鸡子的主食。

===Original===
I learned that Bagley was out of work, and employed him to excavate the bank for these new buildings. We saved the surface earth carefully for compost purposes, and then struck some clean, nice gravel, which was carted away to a convenient place for our roads and walks. On a hillside near the creek were large stones and rocks in great quantity, and some of these were broken up for the foundations. Along the edge of the creek we also found some excellent sand, and therefore were saved not a little expense in starting our improvements.

It did not take the masons long to point up and strengthen the old foundations, and early in September everything was under full headway, the sound of hammer, saw, and plane resounding all day long. It was Winnie’s and Bobsey’s task to gather up the shavings and refuse bits of lumber, and carry them to the woodhouse.

“The ease and quickness with which we can build fires next winter,” I said, “is a pleasant thing to think of.”

===Chinese===
我打听到巴格利（Bagley）失了业，便雇他开挖土坡，给这几处新建筑腾出地方。表土我们仔仔细细存起来备作堆肥；往下便挖到一层干净漂亮的砾石，一车车运到合宜的地方，留着铺我们的车道和走道。穆德纳溪近旁的山坡上，大石和岩石多得是，敲碎了一些用来砌地基。沿溪岸边又寻着上好的沙子，这一步兴修的工料钱，倒省下了不少。

瓦工们没费多久就把旧地基勾缝加固了；到了九月上旬，各项工程已全面铺开，锤子、锯子、刨子的声响整天价回荡不歇。温妮和鲍勃西的任务，是把刨花和零碎木料拾掇起来，送到柴房去。

“想到明年冬天生起火来会多么省事、多么快当，”我说，“真叫人心里痛快。”

===Original===
Meanwhile the garden was not neglected. The early flight of summer-boarders had greatly reduced the demand for vegetables, and now we began to hoard them for our own use. The lima beans were allowed to dry on the vines; the matured pods of the bush beans were spread in the attic; thither also the ripened onions were brought and placed in shallow boxes. As far as possible we had saved our own seed, and I had had a box made and covered with tin, so as to be mouseproof, and in this we placed the different varieties, carefully labelled. Although it was not “apple year,” a number of our trees were in bearing. The best of the windfalls were picked up, and, with the tomatoes and such other vegetables as were in demand, sent to the village twice a week. As fast as crops matured, the ground was cleared, and the refuse, such as contained no injurious seeds, was saved as a winter covering for the strawberry plants.

Our main labor, however, after digging the rest of the potatoes, was the setting of the remaining half-acre in the later varieties of the strawberry. Although the early part of September was very dry and warm, we managed to set out, in the manner I have described, two or three rows nearly every afternoon. The nights had now grown so long and cool that one thorough watering seemed to establish the plants. This was due chiefly to the fact that nearly every plant had a ball of earth attached to the roots, and had never been allowed to wilt at all in the transition. About the middle of the month there came a fine rain, and we filled the remainder of the ground in one day, all the children aiding me in the task. The plants first set out were now strong and flourishing. Each had a bunch of foliage six inches in diameter.

Thus, with helping on the new barn and other work, September saw a renewal of our early-summer activity.

===Chinese===
这一程子，菜园也没有荒疏。消夏的房客早早就走了，蔬菜的需求大减，如今我们开始把菜囤起来自家受用。利马豆就留在藤上晾干；矮生豆结熟了的豆荚摊在顶楼上；熟透的洋葱也送到那里，搁进浅浅的箱子里。菜种我们尽量留的自家所出，我还叫人特制了一只木箱，外面包上白铁皮，防得住老鼠，各个品种分别放进去，贴上签条，标得清清楚楚。虽说今年算不得“苹果年”，我们仍有好几棵树挂了果。风吹落的苹果，拣那最好的拾起来，连同西红柿和别的有销路的菜蔬，一星期两趟送进村里。庄稼一熟，就把地腾空；凡不含害草籽的残秸败叶，都收存起来，留作草莓秧越冬的覆盖。

可是，把余下的土豆起完之后，我们的主活，是把剩下的半英亩地栽满晚熟的草莓品种。九月上旬虽说又干又热，我们还是照我先前说过的法子，差不多每天下午总能栽上两三行。如今夜里时长天凉，只消浇一遍透水，秧苗仿佛就扎下了根。这主要因为几乎每株苗根上都带着一团土球，移栽的全过程中从没让它们打过一点儿蔫。月中前后下了一场好雨，剩下的地我们一天就栽满了，孩子们全都上手帮着我。头一批栽下的秧苗，这时已经壮实兴旺，每株都有一丛直径六英寸的叶子。

就这样，一边帮着盖新谷仓，一边忙别的活计，九月里，我们初夏时节的那股劲头又回来了。

===Original===
“The winds in the trees are whispering of winter,” I said to the children, “and all thrifty creatures—ants, bees, and squirrels—are laying up their stores. So must we.”

I had watched our maturing corn with great satisfaction. For a long time Merton had been able to walk through it without his straw hat being seen above the nodding tassels. One day, about the 20th of the month, Mr. Jones came over with some bundles of long rye straw in his wagon, and said, “Yer can’t guess what these are fer.”

“Some useful purpose, or you wouldn’t have brought them,” I replied.

“We’ll see. Come with me to the corn patch.”

===Chinese===
“树梢间的风，在悄悄念叨着冬天了，”我对孩子们说，“凡是有攒积心思的活物——蚂蚁、蜜蜂、松鼠——都在囤积各自的存粮。我们也该这样。”

我们那片日渐成熟的玉米，我看在眼里，说不出的称心。好些日子了，默顿（Merton）在玉米棵子里穿来穿去，他那顶草帽绝不会被瞧见露在摇曳的缨子梢上。约莫二十号那天，琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）赶着车过来了，车上放着几捆长长的黑麦秸，开口便说：“你猜不着这些是干啥用的。”

“横竖是个有用的用场，不然你也不会拉了来，”我回他一句。

“到时就知道。跟我上玉米地去。”

===Original===
As we started he took a bundle under his arm, and I saw that he had in his hand a tool called a corn knife. Going through the rows he occasionally stripped down the husks from an ear.

Finally he said: “Yes, it’s ready. Don’t yer see that the kernels are plump and glazed? Junior and I are going to tackle our corn ter-morrow, and says I to myself, ‘If ourn is ready to cut, so is neighbor Durham’s.’ The sooner it’s cut after it’s ready, the better. The stalks are worth more for fodder, and you run no risk from an early frost, which would spile it all. You and Merton pitch in as yer allers do, and this is the way ter do it.”

With his left hand gathering the stalks of a hill together above the ears, he cut them all off with one blow of the corn knife within six inches of the ground, and then leaned them against the stalks of an uncut hill. This he continued to do until he had made what he called a “stout,” or a bunch of stalks as large as he could conveniently reach around, the uncut hill of stalks forming a support in the centre. Then he took a wisp of the rye-straw, divided it evenly, and putting the ends together, twisted it speedily into a sort of rope. With this he bound the stout tightly above the ears by a simple method which one showing made plain to me.

===Chinese===
动身时他腋下夹了一捆麦秸，我瞧见他手里拿着一件家伙，名叫割玉米刀。顺着行垄走过去，他不时从一个棒子上剥开苞叶瞧瞧。

末了他开口道：“对喽，熟透了。你瞧这籽粒，又饱满又发亮，还会有错？明儿个我跟小琼斯（Junior）就动手割自家的玉米，我心里直念叨：‘我们家的熟到能割了，邻居达勒姆家的也一准儿熟了。’玉米熟了就割，越早越好。秸秆趁这时割，拿去作饲料更值钱，也不用担心早霜——早霜一来可就全毁了。你跟默顿还是老样子一齐下手，割法就是这么个割法。”

只见他左手把一穴玉米的秸秆在棒子上头拢作一把，割玉米刀离地六英寸之内手起一刀，齐齐割断，随即靠在旁边一穴未割的秸秆上。他就这样一穴接一穴地干下去，直割到扎成了他口中的“一抱”（stout）——也就是两臂正好合抱得拢的一大捆，正中间那穴未割的秸秆便是支撑。随后他抽出一绺黑麦秸，理得匀匀的，梢对梢并作一处，手上一捻，转眼便捻成一根草绳模样。凭着这个，他用一个再简单不过的法子，把绳圈在棒子上头把整捆勒得紧紧的——经他这么一演示，我就全看明白了。

===Original===
“Well, you are a good neighbor!” I exclaimed.

“Pshaw! What does this amount to? If a man can’t do a good turn when it costs as little as this, he’s a mighty mean feller. You forget that I’ve sold you a lot of rye-straw, and so have the best of yer after all.”

“I don’t forget anything, Mr. Jones. As you say, I believe we shall ‘make a go’ of it here, but we always remember how much we owe to you and Junior. You’ve taken my money in a way that saved my self-respect, and made me feel that I could go to you as often as I wished; but you have never taken advantage of me, and you have kept smart people from doing it. Do you know, Mr. Jones, that in every country village there are keen, weasel-like people who encourage newcomers by bleeding their pocketbooks at every chance? In securing you as a neighbor our battle was half won, for no one needs a good practical friend more than a city man beginning life in the country.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，你可真是位好邻居！”我赞叹道。

“嗐！这算得了什么？费这么点儿力气都不肯帮人一把的人，才是顶小气的东西。你倒忘了，这许多黑麦秸还是卖给你的呢，说到底，占便宜的到底是我。”

“我什么也没忘，琼斯先生。正如你说的，我相信我们在这地方能‘干出个名堂’来；可我们一直记着，我们欠你和小琼斯的情有多重。你收我的钱，收得保全了我的自尊，让我觉得随时可以来找你，愿意来多少回就来多少回；可你从来没占过我半点便宜，还拦着那些精明人，不许他们占。琼斯先生，你可知道，乡下村子里处处都有那么一帮精得像黄鼠狼似的人物，专会‘照应’新来的户——逮着机会就放人家钱包的血？有你这样的邻居，我们这一仗就已赢了一半；一个城里人到乡下来立业谋生，再没有谁比他更需要一个务实的好朋友了。”

===Original===
“Jerusalem! how you talk! I’m goin’ right home and tell my wife to call me Saint Jones. Then I’ll get a tin halo and wear it, for my straw hat is about played out;” and away he went, chuckling over his odd conceits, but pleased, as all men are, when their goodwill is appreciated. If there is one kind of meanness that disgusts average human nature more than another it is a selfish, unthankful reception of kindness, a swinish return for pearls.

After an early supper I drove to the village with what I had to sell, and returned with two corn hooks. At dusk of the following day, Bagley and I had the corn cut and tied up, my helper remarking more than once, “Tell you what it is, Mr. Durham, there hain’t a better eared-out patch o’ corn in Maizeville.”

On the following day I helped Bagley sharpen one of the hooks, and we began to cut the fodder corn which now stood, green and succulent, averaging two feet in height throughout the field.

===Chinese===
“老天爷！瞧你这一套一套的！我这就回家去，叫我老婆子往后管我叫圣琼斯。再置办个锡皮的光环戴戴上——反正我那顶草帽也快戴到头了。”说罢他就走了，为自己这些古怪念头暗自好笑；可是好意得了人家的领情，他心里究竟欢喜——世上男人个个如此。要说有哪一种卑鄙劲儿，比别的更叫寻常人的天性厌恶，那就是受了人家的恩惠，却自私而不识好歹，人家抛来珍珠，他报之以猪狗的嘴脸。

早早吃了晚饭，我赶车进村，把要卖的东西捎去，回来时带回两把割玉米钩刀。第二天黄昏时分，巴格利和我已把玉米割倒捆好，帮工的不止一回地念叨：“我跟你说吧，达勒姆先生，梅兹维尔（Maizeville）全镇，再找不出第二块结棒结得这么好的玉米地了。”

第二天，我帮巴格利磨快了一把钩刀，我们动手去割那片青饲料玉米——它这时还满田立着，青翠多汁，高矮全田平均约莫两英尺。

## II I State the Case / II 我来说明缘由

===Original===
I pondered deeply while my wife and Winnie cleared away the dishes and put Bobsey into his little crib. I felt that the time for a decided change had come, and that it should be made before the evils of our lot brought sharp and real trouble.

How should I care for my household? If I had been living on a far frontier among hostile Indians I should have known better how to protect them. I could build a house of heavy logs and keep my wife and children always near me while at work. But it seemed to me that Melissa Daggett and her kin with their flashy papers, and the influence of the street for Merton and Bobsey, involved more danger to my little band than all the scalping Modocs that ever whooped. The children could not step outside the door without danger of meeting someone who would do them harm. It is the curse of crowded city life that there is so little of a natural and attractive sort for a child to do, and so much of evil close at hand.

My wife asked me humorously for the news. She saw that I was not reading my paper, and my frowning brow and firm lips proved my problem was not of a trifling nature. She suspected nothing more, however, than that I was thinking of taking rooms in some better locality, and she was wondering how I could do it, for she knew that my income now left but a small surplus above expenses.

At last Winnie too was ready to go to bed, and I said to her, gravely: “Here is money to pay Melissa for that paper. It was only fit for the gutter, and into the gutter I put it. I wish you to promise me never to look at such pictures again, or you can never hope to grow up to be a lady like mamma.”

===Chinese===
妻子和温妮（Winnie）收拾碗碟、把鲍勃西（Bobsey）放进他的小床时，我在深深地思虑。我感到，痛下决心加以改变的时机已经到了，而且必须赶在我们境遇中的种种祸害酿成尖锐而真实的灾祸之前，就把这改变实行起来。

我该怎样照管我这一家子？倘若我住在遥远的边疆，与敌对的印第安人为邻，我倒更知道该怎样保护他们。我可以盖一座粗原木的屋子，干活时也让妻子儿女始终不离我左右。可在我眼里，梅莉茜·达格茨（Melissa Daggett）和她那帮亲戚，连同他们那些花里胡哨的报纸，再加上街头对默顿（Merton）和鲍勃西的熏染，给我这支小小队伍带来的危险，比自有史以来所有嗷嗷呐喊、专事剥头皮的莫多克人（Modoc）加在一起还要大。孩子们一迈出房门，就有撞见歹人受害的危险。拥挤的城市生活，其可诅咒之处正在于此：对孩子来说，自然又有趣的事情少得可怜，而近在咫尺的祸害却比比皆是。

妻子打趣地向我讨报上的新闻看。她看出我并没有在读报；我紧锁的眉头和抿紧的嘴唇表明，我这道难题绝非无足轻重的小事。不过她猜到的，也不过是我在盘算到好些的地段另赁住处，正暗自纳闷我如何办得到，因为她知道，我这点进项，在开销之外已所剩无几。

末了，温妮也准备上床去了。我神情郑重地对她说：“这是付给梅莉茜那份报纸的钱。那报纸只配进阴沟，我也已经把它扔进了阴沟。我要你答应我，从此再不看那样的图画，不然，你就休想指望长成妈妈那样的淑女。”

===Original===
The child flushed deeply, and went tearful and penitent to bed. Mousie also retired with a wistful look upon her face, for she saw that something of grave importance occupied my mind.

No matter how tired my wife might be, she was never satisfied to sit down until the room had been put in order, a green cloth spread upon the supper table and the student lamp placed in its centre.

Merton brought his schoolbooks, and my wife took up her mending, and we three sat down within the circle of light.

“Don’t do any more work tonight,” I said, looking into my wife’s face, and noting for a few moments that it was losing its rounded lines.

===Chinese===
小姑娘的脸霎时涨得通红，含着眼泪、满心悔悟地上床去了。小耗子（Mousie）也随后安歇，脸上带着一副怅然的神情，因为她看出，有一桩极为要紧的事正盘踞着我的心。

无论多么劳累，妻子不把屋子收拾停当，总不肯坐下：晚饭桌上铺好一方绿布，再把那盏读书灯摆到桌子正中。

默顿把课本书籍拿了来，妻子拿起针线活计，我们三个便在那一圈灯光里坐了下来。

“今晚别再做事了，”我说，一面端详妻子的脸；端详了片刻，我便发觉，那张脸上的丰润线条正在日渐消褪。

===Original===
Her hands dropped wearily into her lap, and she began gratefully: “I’m glad you speak so kindly tonight, Robert, for I am so nervous and out of sorts that I couldn’t have stood one bit of faultfinding—I should have said things, and then have been sorry all day tomorrow. Dear knows, each day brings enough without carrying anything over. Come, read the paper to me, or tell me what you have been thinking about so deeply, if you don’t mind Merton’s hearing you. I wish to forget myself, and work, and everything that worries me, for a little while.”

“I’ll read the paper first, and then, after Merton has learned his lessons, I will tell you my thoughts—my purpose, I may almost say. Merton shall know about it soon, for he is becoming old enough to understand the ‘why’ of things. I hope, my boy, that your teacher lays a good deal of stress on the **why** in all your studies.”

“Oh, yes, after a fashion.”

“Well, so far as I am your teacher, Merton, I wish you always to think why you should do a thing or why you shouldn’t, and to try not to be satisfied with any reason but a good one.”

===Chinese===
她疲惫地把双手搁在膝上，不无感激地开了口：“罗伯特（Robert），你今晚说话这么和气，我真高兴。我这会儿神经脆弱、浑身不对劲，半句指责也受不住——我准会顶撞几句，然后明天懊悔一整天。老天知道，每天的日子已经够受的了，可别再把烦恼从今天拖到明天。来，念报纸给我听，或者讲讲你究竟在深思什么，只要你不介意默顿听见。我想有那么一小会儿，忘掉我自己，忘掉活计，忘掉一切烦心的事。”

“我先把报纸念完，等默顿做完了功课，就把我的想法讲给你听——差不多可以说是我的打算。这件事默顿很快也会知道，他已经渐渐到了懂得事情‘为什么’的年纪。孩子，我希望你们的老师在一切功课上，都十分注重这个**为什么**。”

“哦，也还讲究吧，不过是马马虎虎罢了。”

“那好，默顿，凡在我说得上是你老师的地方，我都愿你无论做什么事，总想一想为什么该做、为什么不该做，并且力求做到：除了正当充分的理由，什么也不轻易满足。”

===Original===
Then I gleaned from the paper such items as I thought would interest my wife. At last we were alone, with no sound in the room but the low roar of the city, a roar so deep as to make one think that the tides of life were breaking waves.

I was doing some figuring in a notebook when my wife asked: “Robert, what is your problem tonight? And what part have I in it?”

“So important a part that I couldn’t solve it without you,” I replied, smiling at her.

“Oh, come now,” she said, laughing slightly for the first time in the evening; “you always begin to flatter a little when you want to carry a point.”

===Chinese===
随后，我从报上拣出几条想必妻子会感兴趣的消息，念给她听。终于屋里只剩我们两个人，四下里静悄悄的，唯有城市低沉的轰鸣；那轰鸣深沉浩大，叫人觉得仿佛是生命的潮水正拍岸化作浪涛。

我正在一个小本子上写着算着，妻子问道：“罗伯特，你今晚的难题是什么？我又在里头占什么份儿？”

“占着这样要紧的一份，没有你，我就解不开它，”我含笑答道。

“嗬，又来了，”她说，今晚头一回露出浅浅的笑容；“你想要人家依你的时候，总要先奉承上几句。”

===Original===
“Well, then, you are on your guard against my wiles. But believe me, Winifred, the problem on my mind is not like one of my ordinary brown studies; in those I often try to get back to the wherefore of things which people usually accept and don’t bother about. The question I am considering comes right home to us, and we must meet it. I have felt for some time that we could not put off action much longer, and tonight I am convinced of it.”

Then I told her how I had found three of the children engaged that evening, concluding: “The circumstances of their lot are more to blame than they themselves. And why should I find fault with you because you are nervous? You could no more help being nervous and a little impatient than you could prevent the heat of the lamp from burning you, should you place your finger over it. I know the cause of it all. As for Mousie, she is growing paler and thinner every day. You know what my income is; we could not change things much for the better by taking other rooms and moving to another part of the city, and we might find that we had changed for the worse. I propose that we go to the country and get our living out of the soil.”

“Why, Robert! what do you know about farming or gardening?”

“Not very much, but I am not yet too old to learn; and there would be something for the children to do at once, pure air for them to breathe, and space for them to grow healthfully in body, mind, and soul. You know I have but little money laid by, and am not one of those smart men who can push their way. I don’t know much besides bookkeeping, and my employers think I am not remarkably quick at that. I can’t seem to acquire the lightning speed with which things are done nowadays; and while I try to make up by long hours and honesty, I don’t believe I could ever earn much more than I am getting now, and you know it doesn’t leave much of a margin for sickness or misfortune of any kind. After all, what does my salary give us but food and clothing and shelter, such as it is, with a little to spare in some years? It sends a cold chill to my heart to think what should become of you and the children if I should be sick or anything should happen to me. Still, it is the present welfare of the children that weighs most on my mind, Winifred. They are no longer little things that you can keep in these rooms and watch over; there is danger for them just outside that door. It wouldn’t be so if beyond the door lay a garden and fields and woods. You, my overtaxed wife, wouldn’t worry about them the moment they were out of sight, and my work, instead of being away from them all day, could be with them. And all could do something, even down to pale Mousie and little Bobsey. Outdoor life and pure air, instead of that breathed over and over, would bring quiet to your nerves and the roses back to your cheeks. The children would grow sturdy and strong; much of their work would be like play to them; they wouldn’t be always in contact with other children that we know nothing about. I am aware that the country isn’t Eden, as we have imagined it—for I lived there as a boy—but it seems like Eden compared to this place and its surroundings; and I feel as if I were being driven back to it by circumstances I can’t control.”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你已提防着我的花言巧语了。不过相信我，温妮弗蕾德（Winifred），我心里这道难题，可不像我平日里那些出神的玄想。平日里，我常常爱去追问那些人们习以为常、懒得多想的事情的所以然；而眼下我考虑的这个问题，却是直逼我们自家门庭的，我们非面对不可。有些日子了，我一直觉得这样的事不能再拖延；到今晚，我更是认定了这一点。”

接着，我把今晚撞见三个孩子各自在忙些什么，原原本本讲给她听，末了说道：“错处更多在他们所处的境遇，而不在他们本人。再说，你神经紧张，我又为什么要怪你呢？你没法不紧张、没法不稍稍急躁，正如你把手指头伸到灯罩上头，就拦不住灯的热气烫着你一样。这一切的缘由，我心里明白。至于小耗子，她一天比一天苍白、瘦弱。我的进项如何，你是知道的；另赁几间屋子、搬到城里另一处去，境况也好不了多少，说不定反倒会发现更糟了。我的提议是：我们到乡下去，靠土地讨生活。”

“哎哟，罗伯特！种田种菜的事，你都懂得些什么呀？”

“懂得不多，可我学起来还不算太老；而且孩子们立刻就会有事可做，有纯净的空气可呼吸，有广阔的天地让他们的身体、心智和心灵健康地生长。你知道，我积蓄下来的钱少得很，也不是那种能硬闯出一条路子的精明人。除了簿记，我懂得不多；而在东家们眼里，我连簿记也算不上怎么麻利。如今办事讲究闪电般的速度，我似乎怎么也学不来这一手；我虽说想靠多熬钟点、靠诚实来补这份不足，可我不相信自己将来挣的钱能比眼下多出多少；你也知道，这点钱一旦遇上疾病或什么灾祸，几乎不留半点余地。说到底，我这份薪水供给我们的，除了衣食和住处——就眼下这个水平——再添上有些年头的些许节余，还有什么呢？一想到我若病倒，或是遭逢什么不测，你和孩子们将何以自处，我的心就凉了半截。可是，温妮弗蕾德，最沉地压在我心头的，还是孩子们眼下的福祉。他们已经不是那种你关在这几间屋里就能看护周全的小不点儿了；就在那扇门外，危险正候着他们。倘若门外是一片园圃、田野和树林，情形就不会是这样。你，我这条劳碌过甚的妻子，也不至于他们一出了视线就忧心忡忡；而我的活计，也无须整天撇下他们，尽可以就在他们身边做。人人都能做点事，连苍白的小耗子和年幼的鲍勃西也不例外。户外的生活、纯净的空气——不再是那翻来覆去呼吸的浊气——会安抚你的神经，让红晕重新回到你的脸颊。孩子们会长得结实强壮；他们的许多活计，做起来就像游戏；他们也不会总是跟我们一无所知的那些孩子厮混在一处。我知道，乡下并非我们想象中的那座伊甸园——我小时候在那样的地方住过——可比起这地方和它的四周环境，乡下就像伊甸园了；而且我觉得，自己正被一种我无力左右的境遇，一路驱赶着回到那里去。”

## XIX John Jones, Jun. / XIX 小约翰·琼斯

===Original===
My agonized shout as I saw Bobsey swept away by the swollen current of the Moodna Creek was no more prompt than his own shrill scream. It so happened, or else a kind Providence so ordered it, that Junior was further down the stream, tapping a maple that had been overlooked the previous day. He sprang to his feet, whirled around in the direction of the little boy’s cry, with the quickness of thought rushed to the bank and plunged in with a headlong leap like a Newfoundland dog. I paused, spellbound, to watch him, knowing that I was much too far away to be of aid, and that all now depended on the hardy country lad. He disappeared for a second beneath the tide, and then his swift strokes proved that he was a good swimmer. In a moment or two he caught up with Bobsey, for the current was too swift to permit the child to sink. Then, with a wisdom resulting from experience, he let the torrent carry him in a long slant toward the shore, for it would have been hopeless to try to stem the tide. Running as I never ran before, I followed, reached the bank where there was an eddy in the stream, sprang in up to my waist, seized them both as they approached and dragged them to solid ground. Merton and Winnie meanwhile stood near with white, scared faces.

Bobsey was conscious, although he had swallowed some water, and I was soon able to restore him, so that he could stand on his feet and cry: “I—I—I w-won’t d-do so any—any more.”

Instead of punishing him, which he evidently expected, I clasped him to my heart with a nervous force that almost made him cry out with pain.

===Chinese===
眼看鲍勃西（Bobsey）被穆德纳溪（Moodna Creek）暴涨的水流卷走，我失声惨叫；这一声惨叫，并不比他自己那声尖利的呼救来得更快。也真是凑巧——要不然就是仁慈的上天有意如此安排——小琼斯（Junior）正在下游不远处，给前一天漏掉的一棵枫树钻眼取汁。他一跃而起，朝着小男孩喊声的方向霍地转过身来，快得如同思想一闪，便冲到了岸边，像一条纽芬兰犬（Newfoundland dog）似的纵身一个猛子扎进水里。我像被定住了似的立在原地望着他，心里明白自己相距太远，帮不上手，此刻一切都系在这个健壮的乡下少年身上了。他有一秒钟工夫没入水下，随即那双迅疾划动的手臂证明他水性极好。不过一两分钟，他便追上了鲍勃西——水流太急，孩子想沉也沉不下去。接着，凭着经验教给他的道理，他索性听凭激流带着自己，斜斜地朝岸边冲出长长的一道——要同水流硬顶，那是全然无望的。我一路飞奔，平生从没跑得那样快，赶到溪水现出回旋的岸边，一脚踏进水里，深及腰部，趁两人漂近时一把抓住，把他们拖上了坚实的地面。这工夫，默顿（Merton）和温妮（Winnie）就站在近旁，一个个脸色煞白，吓得不轻。

鲍勃西神志还清醒，虽呛了几口水；我很快便让他缓了过来，他能自己站住脚了，哭道：“我——我——我以——以后再——再也不敢了。”

他分明以为免不了要挨一顿罚，我却没有责罚他，只是把他紧紧搂进怀里，搂得那样用力，险些叫他疼得喊出声来。

===Original===
Junior, meanwhile, had coolly seated himself on a rock, emptied the water out of his shoes, and was tying them on again, at the same time striving with all his might to maintain a stolid composure under Winnie’s grateful embraces and Merton’s interrupting handshakings. But when, having become assured of Bobsey’s safety, I rushed forward and embraced Junior in a transport of gratitude, his lip began to quiver and two great tears mingled with the water that was dripping from his hair. Suddenly he broke away, took to his heels, and ran toward his home, as if he had been caught in some mischief and the constable were after him. I believe that he would rather have had at once all the strappings his father had ever given him than to have cried in our presence.

I carried Bobsey home, and his mother, with many questionings and exclamations of thanksgiving, undressed the little fellow, wrapped him in flannel, and put him to bed, where he was soon sleeping as quietly as if nothing had happened.

Mrs. Jones came over, and we made her rubicund face beam and grow more round, if possible, as we all praised her boy. I returned with her, for I felt that I wished to thank Junior again and again. But he saw me coming, and slipped out at the back door. Indeed, the brave, bashful boy was shy of us for several days. When at last my wife got hold of him, and spoke to him in a manner natural to mothers, he pooh-poohed the whole affair.

===Chinese===
这工夫，小琼斯已经不声不响地在一块石头上坐了下来，倒掉鞋里的水，正重新把鞋系上；一面还竭力维持着一副木然的镇定，任凭温妮抱着他千恩万谢，默顿又拉住他的手一个劲儿地摇，搅得他不得安生。可是，当我确知鲍勃西已平安无事，抢步上前，怀着满腔感激一把搂住小琼斯时，他的嘴唇颤抖起来，两大颗眼泪，同从头发上滴下的水珠混在了一起。他猛地挣脱开，拔腿就朝自己家里跑，倒像是闯了祸、警察在后面追他似的。我相信，他宁可当场把父亲平生给过他的抽打统统受一遍，也不愿当着我们的面哭出声来。

我抱着鲍勃西回家；他母亲问长问短，连声感恩称谢，给小家伙脱掉湿衣，用绒布裹起来，放上床去，不多时他便安安稳稳地睡熟了，仿佛什么都不曾发生过。

琼斯太太（Mrs. Jones）也过来了；我们大家夸她的儿子，夸得她那张红扑扑的脸乐开了花，那脸盘看去仿佛也更圆了几分。我随她一道回去，因为我觉得自己要把小琼斯谢了又谢才好。可他一见我来，便从后门溜走了。真的，这个又勇敢又怕臊的孩子，一连好几天见了我们都躲躲闪闪。末了我妻子总算逮住了他，用做母亲的人惯有的那种口吻同他谈，他却把整件事满不在乎地轻轻撇开了。

===Original===
“I’ve swum in that crick so often that it was nothin’ to me. I only had to keep cool, and that was easy enough in snow water, and the swift current would keep us both up. I wish you wouldn’t say anything more about it. It kinder makes me feel—I don’t know how—all over, you know.”

But Junior soon learned that we had adopted him into our inmost hearts, although he compelled us to show our goodwill after his own offhand fashion.

Sunday was ushered in with another storm, and we spent a long, quiet, restful day, our hearts full of thankfulness that the great sorrow, which might have darkened the beginning of our country life, had been so happily averted.

===Chinese===
“那条溪我游过不知多少回啦，这点子事对我来说算不得什么。我只要沉住气就成，雪水虽然冰凉，沉住气还不容易？急流又会把我俩都托住。求你们别再提这件事了。一提，我心里就——我也说不清是个什么滋味——横竖浑身不得劲儿，你们晓得。”

不过小琼斯不久便明白，我们已经把他收进了自家心里最深处；尽管他逼得我们只能照他那种大大咧咧的做派，来向他表示好意。

礼拜天又是在一场风雨里来临的；我们安安静静、舒舒坦坦地歇了一整天，心里满怀着感激——那桩本可能给我们的乡下生活开头就蒙上阴影的大不幸，如今竟能这样有幸避开了。

===Original===
On Sunday night the wind veered around to the north, and on Monday morning the sky had a clear metallic hue and the ground was frozen hard. Bobsey had not taken cold, and was his former self, except that he was somewhat chastened in spirit and his bump of caution was larger. I was resolved that the day should witness a good beginning of our spring work, and told Winnie and Bobsey that they could help me. Junior, although he yet avoided the house, was ready enough to help Merton with the sap. Therefore soon after breakfast we all were busy.

Around old country places, especially where there has been some degree of neglect, much litter gathers. This was true of our new home and its surroundings. All through the garden were dry, unsightly weeds, about the house was shrubbery that had become tangled masses of unpruned growth, in the orchard the ground was strewn with fallen branches, and I could see dead limbs on many of the trees.

Therefore I said to my two little helpers: “Here in this open space in the garden we will begin our brush pile, and we will bring to it all the refuse that we wish to burn. You see that we can make an immense heap, for the place is so far away from any buildings that, when the wind goes down, we can set the pile on fire in safety, and the ashes will do the garden good.”

===Chinese===
礼拜天夜里，风转成了北风；礼拜一早晨，天空现出清亮的金属般的光泽，地面冻得硬邦邦的。鲍勃西没有着凉，又和从前一个样了，只是心性收敛了些，颅骨上管“谨慎”的那块凸包，想必也大了一圈。我打定主意，要让这一天为我们的春工开出个好头来，便嘱咐温妮和鲍勃西帮着我做。小琼斯虽然还是不进我们家的门，帮默顿收树液倒是满乐意的。于是早饭刚过，人人都忙起来了。

老式乡间宅子的四围，尤其是多少有些疏于打理的地方，总要积下许多废物杂物。我们的新居和它的四周也是如此。园子里到处是干枯难看的杂草；屋子周围的灌木丛久不修剪，纠缠成一蓬蓬乱枝；果园里满地散落着断折的枝条，许多树上还看得见枯死的大枝。

于是我对两个小帮手说：“就在园子里这块空地上，我们来动手堆枯枝堆，凡是想烧掉的废物都搬到这儿来。你们瞧，我们能堆出好大的一堆；这地方离任何房屋都远，等风小下去，把这一堆点着了也万无一失，烧出来的灰还能肥园子。”

===Original===
During the whole forenoon I pruned the shrubbery, and raked up the rubbish which the children carried by armfuls to our prospective bonfire. They soon wished to see the blaze, but I told them that the wind was too high, and that I did not propose to apply the match until we had a heap half as big as the house; that it might be several days before we should be ready, for I intended to have a tremendous fire.

Thus with the lesson of restraint was given the hope of something wonderful. For a long time they were pleased with the novelty of the work, and then they wanted to do something else, but I said: “No, no; you are gardeners now, and I’m head gardener. You must both help me till dinnertime. After that you can do something else, or play if you choose; but each day, even Bobsey must do some steady work to earn his dinner. We didn’t come to the country on a picnic, I can tell you. All must do their best to help make a living;” and so without scruple I kept my little squad busy, for the work was light, although it had become monotonous.

Mousie sometimes aided her mother, and again watched us from the window with great interest. I rigged upon the barrow a rack, in which I wheeled the rubbish gathered at a distance; and by the time my wife’s mellow voice called, “Come to dinner”—how sweet her voice and summons were after long hours in the keen March wind!—we had a pile much higher than my head, and the place began to wear a tidy aspect.

===Chinese===
一整个上午我都在修剪灌木，把垃圾耙拢起来，孩子们便一抱一抱地搬到那座将来的篝火堆上去。不多时他们就想看火苗了，我对他们说，风还太大，我不打算在堆到半座房子那么大以前就动手划火柴；照这样，也许还得准备上好几天，因为我要的就是一场了不得的大火。

就这样，在教给他们克制这一课的同时，也给了他们一份对奇景的盼望。开头好一阵子，他们还觉得这活儿新鲜有趣，过后就想去做点别的了。我说：“不行，不行；你们如今是园丁了，我是总园丁。你们俩都得帮我干到开饭。那以后你们可以做别的，愿意玩也随你们；可是每天，连鲍勃西在内，都得干点踏实的活儿，来挣他那份饭。我告诉你们，我们到乡下来可不是来野餐的。人人都得尽力，帮着把这个家维持下去；”于是我便心安理得地让我这支小小的队伍一直忙个不停——活儿原是轻省的，只是渐渐变得单调了。

小耗子（Mousie）有时帮母亲做事，有时又凭着窗户，兴致十足地望我们干活。我在独轮车上架了个架子，远处捡拢的垃圾就用它推运；等到妻子圆润的嗓音喊出“来吃饭吧”——在三月的寒风里劳作了大半天之后，这嗓音、这呼唤是多么甜美！——我们那堆枯枝已经比我的头顶还高出一大截，那地方也开始显出整洁的模样了。

===Original===
Such appetites, such red cheeks and rosy noses as the outdoor workers brought to that plain meal! Mousie was much pleased with the promise that the bonfire should not be lighted until some still, mild day when she could go out and stand with me beside it.

Merton admitted that gathering the sap did not keep him busy more than half the time; so after dinner I gave him a hatchet, and told him to go on with the trimming out of the fallen branches in our wood lot—a task that I had begun—and to carry all wood heavy enough for our fireplace to a spot where it could be put into a wagon.

“Your next work, Merton, will be to collect all your refuse trimmings, and the brush lying about, into a few great heaps; and by and by we’ll burn these, too, and gather up the ashes carefully, for I’ve read and heard all my life that there is nothing better for fruit then wood ashes. Some day, I hope, we can begin to put money in the bank; for I intend to give all a chance to earn money for themselves, after they have done their share toward our general effort to live and thrive. The next best thing to putting money in the bank is the gathering and saving of everything that will make the ground richer. In fact, all the papers and books that I’ve read this winter agree that as the farmer’s land grows rich he grows rich.”

===Chinese===
这些户外干活的人，给那顿便饭带来了多好的胃口、多红的面颊和多红的鼻子！小耗子听见我许下的话，心里美滋滋的：篝火要留到哪个无风温软的日子才点，好让她能出来站在我旁边一起看。

默顿自己也承认，收树液的活计至多占他一半的工夫；于是饭后我给了他一柄短斧，叫他接着去干我在林子里开了头的那件活——把断落的枝子修剪出来——再把凡是够分量、进得了壁炉的木柴搬到一处，好装上大车。

“你的下一件活，默顿，是把你修剪下来的废枝和四下散落的枯枝拢成几个大堆；过些日子，我们连这些也一并烧掉，再把灰仔仔细细地收起来，因为我这辈子读书、听人讲，论肥果树，没有一样比得上草木灰。我盼望有朝一日，我们也能开始往银行里存钱；我的打算是：各人先对全家谋生兴家这桩共同的事尽了自己的一份力，然后就让人人都有机会为自己挣钱。除了往银行存钱，其次顶要紧的，就是把一切能让地土更肥的东西都收集起来、储存起来。事实上，今年冬天我读过的报刊书籍，众口一词：农民的地越肥，他这个人就越富。”

## List of Illustrations / 插图目录

===Original===
1. A handwritten letter.

===Chinese===
1. 一封手写的信。

## XVI Making a Place for Chickens / XVI 给鸡安个家

===Original===
Before the meal was over, I said, seriously, “Now, boys, there must be no more hunting until I find out about the game laws. They should be obeyed, especially by sportsmen. I don’t think that we are forbidden to kill rabbits on our own place, particularly when they threaten to be troublesome; and the hunt this morning was so unexpected that I did not think of the law, which might be used to make us trouble. You killed the other rabbits on this place, Junior?”

“Yes, sir, both of ’em.”

“Well, hereafter you must look after hawks, and other enemies of poultry. Especially do I hope you will never fire at our useful songbirds. If boys throughout the country would band together to protect game when out of season, they would soon have fine sport in the autumn.”

===Chinese===
饭还没吃完，我就郑重地说：“好了，孩子们，在我弄清狩猎法规之前，不许再去打猎了。法规理应遵守，打猎的人尤其该遵守。我想，在我们自己的地界上打兔子并不犯禁，特别是当它们眼看要成祸害的时候；今天早晨这场狩猎来得太突然，我没有想到法规的事，可这类法规是可能被人拿来给我们找麻烦的。其余那几只兔子，也是在这块地上打死的吧，小琼斯（Junior）？”

“是的，先生，就那两只，都是。”

“好。往后你要留神老鹰和家禽的其他天敌。我尤其希望你永远不要朝我们有用的鸣禽开枪。要是全国的孩子们都能联合起来，在禁猎期保护猎物，那么到了秋天，他们很快就会有痛痛快快的狩猎了。”

===Original===
In the afternoon we let Winnie and Bobsey expend their energy in making paths and lanes in every direction through the snow, which was melting rapidly in the south wind. By three o’clock the rain began to fall, and when darkness set in there was a gurgling sound of water on every side. Our crackling fire made the warmth and comfort within seem tenfold more cheery.

A hearty supper, prepared in our own kitchen, made us feel that our home machinery had fairly started, and we knew that it would run more and more smoothly. March was keeping up its bad name for storm and change. The wind was again roaring, but laden now with rain, and in gusty sheets the heavy drops dashed against the windows. But our old house kept us dry and safe, although it rocked a little in the blasts. They soon proved a lullaby for our second night at home.

After breakfast the following morning, with Merton, Winnie, and Bobsey, I started out to see if any damage had been done. The sky was still clouded, but the rain had ceased. Our rubber boots served us well, for the earth was like an overfull sponge, while down every little incline and hollow a stream was murmuring.

===Chinese===
下午，我们让温妮（Winnie）和鲍勃西（Bobsey）到雪地里去施展精力，四下八方地踩出一条条小径和巷道；南风正吹，雪化得很快。三点钟光景，雨下起来了；到天色黑透时，四面八方都是汩汩的水声。我们那炉噼啪作响的火，把屋里的温暖舒适映衬得十倍的怡人。

一顿在自己厨房里备下的丰盛晚饭，让我们觉得家里这套机器总算正经开动起来了，而且我们知道，它往后会越转越顺当。三月的风暴无常，果真名不虚传。风又呼啸起来，这回却满载着雨水，沉重的雨点一阵阵扑打着窗子。好在我们这座老屋子虽在狂风里微微摇晃，却保我们干燥平安。不多时，那风雨声竟成了催眠曲，伴我们度过了在家的第二夜。

第二天早饭后，我带着默顿（Merton）、温妮和鲍勃西出门，去看有没有什么地方受了损坏。天上依旧阴云未散，好在雨已经停了。我们的胶靴帮了大忙，泥土活像一块吸饱了水的海绵，每一条小坡、每一处洼地，都有细流在低声潺湲。

===Original===
The old barn showed the need of a good many nails to be driven here and there, and a deal of mending. Then it would answer for cornstalks and other coarse fodder. The new barn had been fairly built, and the interior was dry. It still contained as much hay as would be needed for the keeping of a horse and cow until the new crop should be harvested.

“Papa,” cried Winnie, “where is the chicken place?”

“That is one of the questions we must settle at once,” I replied. “As we were coming out I saw an old coop in the orchard. We’ll go and look at it.”

It was indeed old and leaky, and had poultry been there the previous night they would have been half drowned on their perches. “This might do for a summer cottage for your chickens, Winnie,” I continued, “but never for a winter house. Let us go back to the barn, for I think I remember a place that will just suit, with some changes.”

===Chinese===
老谷仓显得到处都得再敲进许多钉子，要修缮的地方着实不少。修好之后，可以存放玉米秆和其他粗饲料。新谷仓盖得还算规整，里面是干的。仓里的干草还够一匹马一头牛吃到新草收割的时候。

“爸爸，”温妮喊道，“养鸡的地方在哪儿呀？”

“这正是我们得马上解决的一个问题，”我答道。“方才出来的时候，我瞧见果园里有一只旧鸡笼。咱们去瞧瞧去。”

那鸡笼果然又旧又漏，前夜要是有鸡栖在上头，准得在栖木上淹个半死。“温妮，这地方兴许能当你那些鸡的消夏小屋，”我接着说，“可绝不能当过冬的住房。咱们回谷仓去吧，我记得有个地方，稍加改动，保管正合适。”

===Original===
Now the new barn had been built on a hillside, and had an ample basement, from which a room extending well into the bank had been partitioned, thus promising all one could desire as a cellar for apples and roots. The entrance to this basement faced the east, and on each side of it was a window. To the right of the entrance were two cow stalls, and to the left was an open space half full of mouldy cornstalks and other rubbish.

“See here, Winnie and Merton,” I said, after a little examination, “I think we could clear out this space on the left, partition it off, make a door, and keep the chickens here. After that window is washed, a good deal of sunlight can come in. I’ve read that in cold weather poultry need warmth and light, and must be kept dry. Here we can secure all these conditions. Having a home for ourselves, suppose we set to work to make a home for the chickens.”

This idea delighted Winnie, and pleased Merton almost as much as hunting rabbits. “Now,” I resumed, “we will go to the house and get what we need for the work.”

===Chinese===
原来新谷仓是傍着山坡盖的，有一间宽敞的地下层，里面隔出了一间深深嵌进土坡的屋子，做储存苹果和块根的菜窖，正是求之不得。这地下层的入口朝东，入口两边各有一扇窗。入口右边是两个牛栏位，左边是一片空场，半场堆着发霉的玉米秆和别的破烂。

“温妮、默顿，你们瞧，”我略一察看便说，“我看咱们可以把左边这片空场清理出来，隔断开，安上一扇门，把鸡就养在这儿。那扇窗子一擦洗，就能进不少阳光。我读过，天冷的时候，家禽需要温暖和光亮，还必须保持干燥。在这儿，这些条件咱们都能办到。我们自己既然有了家，何不就来给鸡也安一个家呢。”

温妮听了这主意欢喜得很，默顿的高兴劲儿也几乎不亚于打兔子。“来，”我接着说，“咱们回屋去，把干活要用的东西取来。”

===Original===
“Winifred,” I said to my wife, “can you let Winnie have a small pail of hot water and some old rags?”

“What are you up to now?”

“You know all about cleaning house; we are going to clean barn, and make a place for Winnie’s chickens. There is a window in their future bedroom—roost-room I suppose I should call it—that looks as if it had never been washed, and to get off the dust of years will be Winnie’s task, while Merton, Bobsey, and I create an interior that should satisfy a knowing hen. We’ll make nests, too, children, that will suggest to the biddies that they should proceed at once to business.”

“But where are the chickens to come from?” my wife asked, as she gave the pan to Merton to carry for his sister.

“Oh, John Jones will put me in the way of getting them soon;” and we started out to our morning’s work. Mousie looked after us wistfully, but her mother soon found light tasks for her, and she too felt that she was helping. “Remember, Mousie,” I said, in parting, “that I have three helpers, and surely mamma needs one;” and she was content.

===Chinese===
“温妮弗蕾德（Winifred），”我对妻子说，“能给温妮一小桶热水和一些旧布片吗？”

“你们这又要搞什么名堂？”

“大扫除的门道你样样在行；我们呢，要去打扫谷仓，给温妮的鸡安一个家。它们将来的卧室——想必我该叫它栖屋——有一扇窗，瞧那样子像是从来没擦洗过；擦掉经年的尘垢，这活儿归温妮，我和默顿、鲍勃西呢，要布置出一套连顶内行的母鸡也会满意的内部装置。孩子们，我们还要做几个窝，让那些小母鸡一看就明白，该立刻开张办正事了。”

“可是鸡从哪儿来呢？”妻子问着，把盆递给默顿，让他替妹妹拿着。

“哦，约翰·琼斯（John Jones）很快就会指点我上哪儿弄去；”说完，我们便出门去做上午的活计。小耗子（Mousie）眼巴巴地望着我们走去，好在母亲很快给她找了些轻省活儿，她也觉得自己在帮着做事。“小耗子，记住，”我临走时说，“我有三个帮手，妈妈那儿准也缺一个；”她便心满意足了。

===Original===
Merton at first was for pitching all the old cornstalks out into the yard, but I said: “That won’t do. We shall need a cow as well as chickens, and these stalks must be kept dry for her bedding. We’ll pile them up in the inner empty stall. You can help at that, Bobsey;” and we set to work.

Under Winnie’s quick hands more and more light came through the window. With a fork I lifted and shook up the stalks, and the boys carried them to the empty stall. At last we came to rubbish that was so damp and decayed that it would be of no service indoors, so we placed it on a barrow and I wheeled it out to one corner of the yard. At last we came down to a hard earth floor, and with a hoe this was cleared and made smooth.

“Merton,” I said, “I saw an old broom upstairs. Run and get it, and we’ll brush down the cobwebs and sweep out, and then we shall be ready to see about the partition.”
===Chinese===
默顿起初主张把这些旧玉米秆统统抛到院子里去，我却说：“那可使不得。咱们不光要养鸡，还得添一头奶牛，这些玉米秆得干燥着存放，将来给她垫窝。咱们把它们堆进里面那个空着的栏位。这个你可以搭把手，鲍勃西；”说着我们就干了起来。

温妮那双利落的手越擦，窗子透进来的光就越多。我用叉子把玉米秆挑起来抖松，两个男孩把它们搬到空栏里去。末了清到底下的破烂，已经潮湿朽坏，屋里派不上用场了，我们就把它们装上手推车，我推到院子的一角倒掉。最后露出一层坚实的泥土地面，我们又用锄头把它清干净、整平滑。

“默顿，”我说，“我在楼上看见一把旧扫帚。跑去取来，咱们好掸掉蛛网、里外扫净，然后就张罗着打隔断的事。”

## VII Mr. Jones Shows Me the Place / VII 琼斯先生带我去看那地方

===Original===
The next day, according to appointment, I went to Maizeville. John Jones met me at the station, and drove me in his box sleigh to see the farm he had written of in his laconic note. I looked at him curiously as we jogged along over the melting snow. The day was unclouded for a wonder, and the sun proved its increasing power by turning the sleigh tracks in the road into gleaming rills. The visage of my new acquaintance formed a decided contrast to the rubicund face of the beef-eating marketman. He was sandy even to his eyebrows and complexion. His scraggy beard suggested poverty of soil on his lantern jaws. His frame was as gaunt as that of a scarecrow, and his hands and feet were enormous. He had one redeeming feature, however—a pair of blue eyes that looked straight at you and made you feel that there was no "crookedness" behind them. His brief letter had led me to expect a man of few words, but I soon found that John Jones was a talker and a good-natured gossip. He knew everyone we met, and was usually greeted with a rising inflection, like this, "How are you, John?"

We drove inland for two or three miles.

"No, I didn't crack up the place, and I ain't a-goin' to," said my real estate agent. "As I wrote you, you can see for yourself when we get there, and I'll answer all questions square. I've got the sellin' of the property, and I mean it shall be a good bargain, good for me and good for him who buys. I don't intend havin' any neighbors around blamin' me for a fraud;" and that is all he would say about it.

===Chinese===
第二天，我如约前往梅兹维尔（Maizeville）。约翰·琼斯（John Jones）到车站来接我，驾着他那辆箱式雪橇，载我去看他信上简短提到的那座农场。雪橇载着我们在渐消的残雪上一路颠颠地慢行，我便好奇地打量起他来。难得这天万里无云，太阳渐增的威力，把路上的雪橇辙印都照成了一道道闪亮的细流。我这位新相识的面相，同那位吃牛肉的市场贩子红润润的脸膛，恰成鲜明的对照：他连眉毛带肤色都是沙黄色；灯笼似的瘦长脸颊上，那把稀稀拉拉的胡子，叫人想起长不出好庄稼的瘠土；身架干瘦得如同稻草人，手脚却又大得出奇。不过他有一处长处聊为补救——一双蓝眼睛端端地直望着你，叫你觉得那背后没有半点“歪门歪道”。他那封短信原使我料想此人寡言少语，可不久我就发现，约翰·琼斯不仅健谈，还是个和和气气的闲话篓子。我们一路遇见的人他无有不识，人家招呼他，调子总是往上一挑的，就像这样：“约翰，你好哇？”

我们又朝内陆赶了两三英里。

“没有，我没夸过那地方，往后也不打算夸，”我的地产经纪人说。“信上我写过，到了那儿你自己一看便知，问什么我都老老实实照直回答。这产业的卖权在我手里，我一心要把它办成一桩公道的好买卖，对我有好处，对买主也有好处。我可不想叫左邻右舍日后戳着脊梁骨，骂我使诈；”关于这地方，他肯说的话就这么多。

===Original===
On we went, over hills and down dales, surrounded by scenery that seemed to me beautiful beyond all words, even in its wintry aspect.

"What mountain is that standing off by itself?" I asked.

"Schunemunk," he said. "Your place—well, I guess it will be yours before plantin' time comes—faces that mountain and looks up the valley between it and the main highlands on the left. Yonder's the house, on the slope of this big round hill, that'll shelter you from the north winds."

===Chinese===
我们继续赶路，翻过一坡又一坡，下了一谷又一谷；四下的景致，纵然是冬日模样，在我眼里也美得难以言表。

“那座孤零零立在一旁的是什么山？”我问。

“舒内芒克山（Schunemunk），”他说。“你那地方——唔，依我看，赶不到栽种时节，它就是你的了——正对着那座山，抬眼顺着山谷望进去，左边就是那道主山梁。喏，那头就是房子，坐落在这座又圆又大的山包的坡上，北风叫它一挡，你就不用怕了。”

===Original===
I shall not describe the place very fully now, preferring that it should be seen through the eyes of my wife and children, as well as my own.

"The dwelling appears old," I said.

"Yes; part of it's a good deal more'n a hundred years old. It's been added to at both ends. But there's timbers in it that will stand another hundred years. I had a fire made in the livin'-room this mornin', to take off the chill, and we'll go in and sit down after we've looked the place over. Then you must come and take potluck with us."

===Chinese===
这地方的景致，这会儿我不打算细讲，宁可留到日后，让它借我妻子和孩子的眼睛、也借我自己的眼睛来看。

“这房子看着旧了。”我说。

“是旧；有一截子远不止一百年了，两头都接建出去过。不过屋里的木料，再撑一百年也不碍事。今儿一早我叫人在起居室里生了火，好去去寒气；等咱们把这地方看遍了，就进去坐下歇脚。回头你务必留下来，跟我们吃顿便饭——有什么吃什么。”

===Original===
At first I was not at all enthusiastic, but the more I examined the place, and thought it over, the more it grew on my fancy. When I entered the main room of the cottage, and saw the wide, old-fashioned fireplace, with its crackling blaze, I thawed so rapidly that John Jones chuckled. "You're amazin' refreshin' for a city chap. I guess I'll crack on another hundred to the price."

"I thought you were not going to crack up the place at all."

"Neither be I. Take that old armchair, and I'll tell you all about it. The place looks rather run down, as you have seen. Old Mr. and Mrs. Jamison lived here till lately. Last January the old man died, and a good old man he was. His wife has gone to live with a daughter. By the will I was app'inted executor and trustee. I've fixed on a fair price for the property, and I'm goin' to hold on till I get it. There's twenty acres of plowable land and orchard, and a five-acre woodlot, as I told you. The best part of the property is this. Mr. Jamison was a natural fruit grower. He had a heap of good fruit here and wouldn't grow nothin' but the best. He was always a-speerin' round, and when he come across something extra he'd get a graft, or a root or two. So he gradually came to have the best there was a-goin' in these parts. Now I tell you what it is, Mr. Durham, you can buy plenty of new, bare places, but your hair would be gray before you'd have the fruit that old man Jamison planted and tended into bearing condition; and you can buy places with fine shade trees and all that, and a good show of a garden and orchard, but Jamison used to say that an apple or cherry was a pretty enough shade tree for him, and he used to say too that a tree that bore the biggest and best apples didn't take any more room than one that yielded what was fit only for the cider press. Now the p'int's just here. You don't come to the country to amuse yourself by developin' a property, like most city chaps do, but to make a livin'. Well, don't you see? This farm is like a mill. When the sun's another month higher it will start all the machinery in the apple, cherry, and pear trees and the small fruits, and it will turn out a crop the first year you're here that will put money in your pocket."

===Chinese===
起初我毫无热忱，可这地方越看、越琢磨，越叫我动心。等我走进农舍的正屋，望见那座老式宽大的壁炉里火苗噼啪作响，我这一颗心化得飞快，化得那样快，逗得约翰·琼斯呵呵直乐。“你这个城里人，倒真叫人痛快。我看哪，我还得给价钱再吹上一百块。”

“我还道你压根儿不打算吹嘘那地方的呢。”

“我本来也没吹。你坐那把旧扶手椅，我原原本本讲给你听。这地方瞧着相当破败，方才你也看见了。老贾米森（Jamison）先生和太太一向住到这里，直到不久前。今年一月老头儿过世了——他可是位顶好的老人。老伴儿如今跟一个闺女过活去了。遗嘱上委派我做了遗嘱执行人和受托人。这产业的价钱我已经定了公道的一口数，不到这个数我不撒手。能耕的地带果园一共二十英亩，另有五英亩的林子，先前我同你讲过。这产业顶要紧的好处还在这里：贾米森先生天生是块种果树的料。他这里好果子攒了一大堆，除了顶好的，他啥也不种。他老在四下里踅摸，一碰上出挑的品种，就讨一枝接穗，或是弄它一两株根苗。这么着，这一带顶好的果木，便渐渐都归了他。达勒姆（Durham）先生，如今我把话给你挑明：新的、光秃秃的地方，你尽可以买上一大把，可要把老贾米森栽下、侍弄到挂果的那些果树等出来，只怕你的头发都等白了；带着漂亮遮荫树啦、花园果园都像模像样的地方，你也买得着，可贾米森老爱说，苹果树、樱桃树给他当遮荫树就够体面的了；他还常说，结的苹果又大又好的树，占的地方并不比那等只配送进榨汁机的树多。如今，话就说到这个点子上。你到乡下来，不是像多数城里人那样，拿置产业、办产业当消遣，你是来谋生计的。喏，你可明白？这座农场就是一盘磨。等太阳再爬高一个月，苹果树、樱桃树、梨树和小果树上那一整套机器就要开动起来；你来的头一年，它就能转出一茬收成，把钱装进你的腰包。”

===Original===
Then he named the price, half down and the rest on mortgage, if I so preferred. It was within the limit that my means permitted.

I got up and went all over the house, which was still plainly furnished in part. A large woodhouse near the back door had been well filled by the provident old man. There was ample cellar room, which was also a safeguard against dampness. Then I went out and walked around the house. It was all so quaint and homely as to make me feel that it would soon become homelike to us. There was nothing smart to be seen, nothing new except a barn that had recently been built near one of the oldest and grayest structures of the kind I had ever seen. The snow-clad mountains lifted themselves about me in a way that promised a glimpse of beauty every time I should raise my eyes from work. Yet after all my gaze lingered longest on the orchard and fruit trees that surrounded the dwelling.

"That's sensible," remarked Mr. Jones, who followed me with no trace of anxiety or impatience. "Paint, putty, and pine will make a house in a few weeks, but it takes a good slice out of a century to build up an orchard like that."

===Chinese===
接着他说出了价钱：先付一半，余下的如我愿意可作抵押。这数目在我财力许可的范围之内。

我起身把房子里里外外走了一遍；屋里还有几分是原先那副家常简朴的陈设。后门近旁有间宽大的柴房，亏得那位老人过日子有算计，里面填得满满当当。地窖也很宽敞，还能防潮气。随后我出门，绕着屋子走了一圈。一切都那么古拙而家常，叫我觉得，用不了多久，它在我们眼里便是一个家了。四下见不到一点时髦气，也没有什么新东西，只有一座新近盖起的谷仓，紧挨着我平生所见最古老、最灰白的一座同类屋舍。披雪的群山在我四围耸立，仿佛许下诺言：往后我每回从活计上抬眼，都能望见一角美景。可绕到最后，我凝望得最久的，还是宅子四围那片果园和那些果树。

“这话在理，”琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）说。他一路跟着我，不见半点焦躁或不耐烦。“油漆、油灰，再添上松木，几个星期就能变出一幢房子；可要养成这么一片果园，得从一个世纪的年头里实实在在割下一大块来。”

===Original===
"That was just what I was thinking, Mr. Jones."

"Oh, I knowed that. Well, I've got just two more things to say, then I'm done and you can take it or leave it. Don't you see? The house is on a slope facing the southeast. You get the morning sun and the southern breeze. Some people don't know what they're worth, but I, who've lived here all my life, know they're worth payin' for. Again, you see the ground slopes off to the crick yonder. That means good drainage. We don't have any malary here, and that fact is worth as much as the farm, for I wouldn't take a section of the garden of Eden if there was malary around."

"On your honor now, Mr. Jones, how far is the corner around which they have the malaria?"

===Chinese===
“我心里想的正是这个，琼斯先生。”

“嗐，我知道你想着什么。好，我还有两句话，说完就完，买不买随你。你瞧见没有？房子坐落在一面朝东南的坡上，早晨的太阳、南来的风，你都占着。有人不晓得这两样值几个钱，可我在这里住了一辈子，知道它们值得花钱买。还有，你看见地势朝着那边小溪往下溜。这就是说，泄水痛快。我们这儿从来没有疟疾，单凭这一条就顶得上整座农场；周围要是有疟疾，就是把伊甸园（Eden）割一块给我，我也不要。”

“琼斯先生，如今你拿人格担保告诉我，他们得疟疾的那个拐角，离这儿有多远？”

===Original===
"Mr. Durham, it ain't a mile away."

I laughed as I said, "I shall have one neighbor, it seems, to whom I can lend an umbrella."

"Then you'll take the place?"

===Chinese===
“达勒姆先生，不到一英里。”

我笑着说道：“这么看来，我往后要有一位能借他雨伞的邻居了。”

“这么说，这地方你就要下了？”

===Original===
"Yes, if my wife is as well satisfied as I am. I want you to give me the refusal of it for one week at the price you named."

"Agreed, and I'll put it in black and white."

"Now, Mr. Jones," I began with an apologetic little laugh, "you grow one thing up here in all seasons, I fancy—an appetite. As I feel now, your potluck means good luck, no matter what is in it."

"Now you talk sense. I was a-hankerin' myself. I take stock right off in a man or a critter with an appetite. They're always improvin'. Yes, sir; Maizeville is the place to grow an appetite, and what's more we can grow plenty to satisfy it."

Mrs. Jones made a striking contrast to her husband, for she first impressed me as being short, red, and round; but her friendly, bustling ways and hearty welcome soon added other and very pleasant impressions; and when she placed a great dish of fricasseed chicken on the table she won a goodwill which her neighborly kindness has steadily increased.

===Chinese===
“要，只要我妻子也像我一样中意。请你给我一个星期的优先权，价钱照你开定的算。”

“成了，这事我要白纸黑字写下来。”

“嗳，琼斯先生，”我带着点儿告罪的笑开口说，“依我看，你们这地方一年四季只长一样东西——胃口。照我这会儿的感觉，你那顿便饭就意味着好运，锅里炖的是什么全不相干。”

“这才叫说话。我自己这会儿也正馋着呢。人也好，牲口也好，只要有胃口，我立马就信得过——这样的人见天儿都有长进。可不是嘛，梅兹维尔是顶会长胃口的地方；不光如此，填这胃口的东西，我们也种得出足足的。”

琼斯太太同她丈夫恰成鲜明的对照：头一眼的印象，她又矮、又红润、又圆墩墩的；可她那热络忙活的做派和真挚热忱的待客，很快又添上别的一些、而且是非常可喜的印象；待她把满满一大盘烩鸡端上桌来，她便赢得了我的好感——而她那待邻里的厚道情谊，又使这份好感有增无已。

## XXXI Nature Smiles and Helps / XXXI 大自然含笑相助

===Original===
The Saturday evening which brought us almost to the middle of June was welcomed indeed. The days preceding had been filled with hard, yet successful labor, and the weeds had been slaughtered by the million. The greater part of our crops had come up well and were growing nicely. In hoeing the corn, we had planted over the few missing hills, and now, like soldiers who had won the first great success of the campaign, we were in a mood to enjoy a rest to the utmost.

This rest seemed all the more delightful when we awoke on the following morning, to the soft patter of rain. The preceding days had been unusually dry and warm, so that the grass and tender vegetables were beginning to suffer. I was worrying about the raspberries also, which were passing out of blossom. The cultivator had been through them, and Merton and I, only the evening before, had finished hoeing out the sprouting weeds and surplus suckers. I had observed, with dread, that just as the fruit was forming, the earth, especially around the hills, was getting dry.

Now, looking out, I saw that the needful watering was not coming from a passing shower. The clouds were leaden from horizon to horizon; the rain fell with a gentle steadiness of a quiet summer storm, and had evidently been falling some hours already. The air was so fragrant that I threw wide open the door and windows. It was a true June incense, such as no art could distil, and when, at last, we all sat down to breakfast, of which crisp radishes taken a few moments before from our own garden formed a part, we felt that nature was carrying on our work of the past week in a way that filled our hearts with gratitude. The air was so warm that we did not fear the dampness. The door and windows were left open that we might enjoy the delicious odors and listen to the musical patter of the rain, which fell so softly that the birds were quite as tuneful as on other days.

===Chinese===
那个把我们带到六月中旬跟前的星期六夜晚，真是备受欢迎。此前连日的劳作辛苦，却也卓有成效，杂草已被成百万地剿杀。我们的庄稼大半出苗良好，长得喜人。锄玉米的时候，少数缺苗的穴都补种过了；此刻，恰似打赢了战役头一场大胜仗的士兵，我们正满心情愿要痛享一番歇息。

第二天清晨，我们在细雨轻柔的滴答声中醒来，只觉得这场歇息愈发的可喜。前些日子异常干燥炎热，青草和娇嫩的菜蔬都开始受熬煎了。我心里还惦着树莓，它们正打花谢走向坐果。中耕机已经从树莓地里过了一遍；就在头天傍晚，默顿（Merton）和我刚锄净了才冒头的杂草和多余的根出条。我忧心忡忡地留意到，恰恰在果子坐胎的当口，泥土——尤其是株丛四周——已经渐渐干了起来。

此刻向窗外一望，只见这场急需的浇灌并非过路阵雨所能顶替。铅灰色的浓云从天这边一直铺到天那边；雨下得温柔而绵密，是一场安安静静的夏雨，而且显然已经落了好几个钟头。空气馥郁芬芳，我把门窗全数敞开。这是真正的六月香氛，任何人工都蒸馏不出来；末了，大家坐下来吃早饭——桌上就有几分钟前刚从自家菜园里拔来的脆生生的小萝卜——我们只觉得，大自然正接着我们过去一星期的活计干下去，那副光景叫人满心感激。天气温暖，我们并不怕潮气。门窗就这样敞着，好领略那醉人的气息，听雨声叮咚如乐；雨落得那样轻柔，鸟儿照旧唱得同平日一样欢。

===Original===
The children joined me in the porch, and my wife came out laughing, and put her hand on my shoulder as she said, "You are not through with July and August yet."

Mousie held her hands out in the warm rain, saying: "I feel as if it would make me grow, too. Look at the green cherries up there, bobbing as the drops hit them."

"Rain isn't good for chickens," Winnie remarked, doubtfully.

"It won't hurt them," I replied, "for I have fed them so well that they needn't go out in the wet for food."

The clouds gave us a more and more copious downfall as the day advanced, and I sat on the porch, resting and observing with conscious gratitude how beautifully nature was furthering all our labor, and fulfilling our hopes. This rain would greatly increase the hay crops for the old horse and the cow; it would carry my vegetables rapidly toward maturity; and, best of all, would soak the raspberry ground so thoroughly that the fruit would be almost safe. What was true of our little plot was equally so of neighbor Jones's farm, and thousands of others. My wife sat with me much of the day, and I truly think that our thoughts were acceptable worship. By four in the afternoon the western horizon lightened, the clouds soon broke away, and the sun shone out briefly in undiminished splendor, turning the countless raindrops on foliage and grass into gems, literally, of the purest water. The birdsongs seemed almost ecstatic, and the voices of the children, permitted at last to go out of doors, vied with them in gladness.

===Chinese===
孩子们到门廊里来陪我；妻子笑着走出来，一只手搭上我的肩头说：“可你还没熬过七月和八月呢。”

小耗子（Mousie）把两只手伸在暖融融的雨里，说道：“我觉得这雨好像也要叫我长个儿似的。瞧上面那些青樱桃，雨点一打上去，它们就一颠一颠地点头。”

“下雨天对鸡可不好，”温妮（Winnie）将信将疑地说。

“淋不坏它们，”我回答，“我把它们喂得足足的，它们犯不着冒雨出去觅食。”

天色越晚，云头给我们的雨越发丰沛。我坐在门廊里歇息，怀着自觉的感激，看大自然何等美妙地推进我们全部的劳作，成全我们的盼愿。这场雨会大大增添老枣红马和母牛的干草收成；会催着我的菜蔬迅速走向成熟；顶要好的一项，是把树莓地浸得透透彻彻，果子便几乎万无一失了。我们这小块地上如此，邻居琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）的农场上如此，千万别处也都如此。这一天多半时光，妻子都陪我坐着；我确确实实觉得，我们心中的感念，便是一种蒙悦纳的敬拜。下午四点光景，西边天际放亮，乌云不久便告消散，太阳短暂露了一下面，光辉丝毫不减，把枝叶草尖上无数雨珠化作颗颗宝石——实实在在是至纯之水凝成的宝石。鸟啭几近如醉如狂；终于获准出门的孩子们，欢声与鸟鸣争起高下来。

===Original===
"Let July and August—yes, and bleak January—bring what they may," I said to my wife, "nevertheless, this is Eden."

In spite of the muddy walks, we picked our way around the garden, exclaiming in pleased wonder at the growth made by our vegetable nurslings in a few brief hours, while, across the field, the corn and potato rows showed green, strong outlines.

I found that Brindle in the pasture hadn't minded the rain, but only appeared the sleeker for it. When at last I came in to supper, I gave my wife a handful of berries, at which she and the children exclaimed. I had permitted a dozen plants of each variety of my garden strawberries to bear, that I might get some idea of the fruit. The blossoms on the other plants had been picked off as soon as they appeared, so that all the strength might go toward forming new plants. I found that a few of the berries of the two early kinds were ripe, also that the robins had been sampling them. In size, at least, they seemed wonderful compared with the wild fruit from the field, and I said:

"There will be lively times for us when we must get a dozen bushels a day, like these, off to Mr. Bogart."

===Chinese===
“随它七月八月——是啊，哪怕还有寒气砭骨的一月——爱带来什么都带来吧，”我对妻子说，“不管怎样，这里是伊甸园（Eden）。”

尽管小径泥泞，我们还是拣着脚下的路，绕园子走了一遍，眼见菜秧子们在短短几个钟头里蹿起的长势，欢喜得连声惊叹；田野那一头，一行行玉米和土豆现出青翠而刚劲的轮廓。

我发现牧场上的花斑牛（Brindle）压根没把雨放在心上，反倒显得毛色愈加油亮。末了我进屋吃晚饭，递给妻子一把浆果，她和孩子们见了都惊叹出声。园中草莓的各个品种，我都留了十二株让它们结果，好对果子的情形有个数；其余植株的花，一露头便摘掉，好叫养分丝毫不动地全拿去养新苗。两个早熟品种已有几颗果子熟了，还发现知更鸟已经先来尝过鲜。至少论个头，它们同田里的野果一比，实在惊人；我便说道：

“等哪天我们非得一天装上十二蒲式耳这样的草莓，送博加特先生（Mr. Bogart）那儿去，可有我们热闹的了。”

===Original===
The children, then, thought it would be the greatest fun in the world. By the time supper was over, Mr. Jones and Junior appeared, and my neighbor said in hearty goodwill:

"You got your cultivatin' done in the nick of time, Mr. Durham. This rain is a good hundred dollars in your pocket and mine, too."

I soon perceived that our enemies, the weeds, had millions in reserve, and on Monday—the day after the rain—with all the children helping, even Mousie part of the time, we went at the garden again. To Mousie, scarcely an invalid any longer, was given the pleasure of picking the first green peas and shelling them for dinner. We had long been enjoying the succulent lettuce and the radishes, and now I said to Winnie: "Tomorrow you can begin thinning out the beets, leaving the plants three inches apart. What you pull up can be cooked as spinach, or 'greens,' as country people say. Our garden will soon enable us to live like princes."

As the ground dried after the rain, a light crust formed on the surface, and in the wetter portions it was even inclined to bake or crack. I was surprised at the almost magical effect of breaking up the crust and making the soil loose and mellow by cultivation. The letting in of air and light caused the plants to grow with wonderful vigor.

===Chinese===
孩子们一听，都觉着这准是天底下顶有趣的事。晚饭刚罢，琼斯先生和小琼斯（Junior）就露面了，我的邻居一片热诚地说：

“达勒姆先生，你的中耕赶得正是节骨眼。这场雨就是实打实的一百块钱，进了你的腰包，也进了我的。”

我很快便发觉，我们的敌人杂草还有数以百万计的后备军；星期一——雨停后的头一天——孩子们一齐上阵，连小耗子也帮了半阵子忙，我们重又杀向园子。小耗子如今差不多已不算病号了，摘头一茬青豌豆、剥好了供午饭用的美差，便派给了她。多汁的莴苣和小萝卜，我们早已享用多时；这会儿我对温妮说：“明儿你可以开始给甜菜间苗了，株距留三英寸。拔下来的可以当菠菜煮了吃，就是乡下人说的‘青菜’。咱们这园子很快就能叫我们过上王爷一般的日子了。”

雨后地面渐干，表层结起一层薄壳，湿一些的地段甚至开始板结龟裂。把这层壳破开，借中耕让土壤疏松肥熟，其效力近乎魔法，令我惊讶。空气和阳光一经透入，作物便以惊人的劲头生长起来。

===Original===
On Wednesday morning Merton came running in, exclaiming, "O papa! there's a green worm eating all the leaves off the currant and gooseberry bushes."

I followed him hastily, and found that considerable mischief had already been done, and I went to one of my fruit books in a hurry to find out how to cope with this new enemy.

As a result, I said: "Merton, mamma wishes to go to the village. You drive her and Mousie down, and at the drugstore get two pounds of white hellebore, also a pound of Paris green, for I find that the potato bugs are getting too thick to be managed by hand. Remember that these are poisons, the Paris green a deadly one. Have them carefully wrapped up, and keep them from everything else. When you return I'll take charge of them. Also, get a new large watering can."

That afternoon I mixed a heaping tablespoonful of the hellebore through the contents of the watering can, on which I had painted the word "Poison." With this infusion I sprinkled thoroughly every bush on which I could find a worm, and the next morning we had the pleasure of finding most of these enemies dead. But some escaped or new ones were hatched, and we found that we could save our currants only by constant vigilance. Every evening, until the fruit was nearly ripe, we went over the bushes, and gave the vile little pests a dose wherever we found them. Our other can I also labelled "Poison," with dashes under it to show that it was to be used for Paris green alone. A teaspoonful of this deadly agent was enough, according to my book, for the amount of water held by the ordinary wooden pail. I kept this poison out of Bobsey's reach, and, indeed, where no one but myself could get at it, and, by its aid, destroyed the potato beetles and their larvae also. Whatever may be true in other parts of the world, in our region, certainly, success can be secured only by prompt, intelligent effort.

===Chinese===
星期三清早，默顿跑进屋来，嚷道：“哎呀，爸爸！有条青虫在把红醋栗和鹅莓丛的叶子全啃掉呢。”

我急忙跟他出去，一看祸害已经闹得不小，便赶忙去翻我那几部果树书里的一部，要找出对付这个新敌手的法子。

照书上的指点，我吩咐道：“默顿，妈妈想进村一趟。你赶车送她和小耗子下去，到药店里买两磅白藜芦，再买一磅巴黎绿——我发现土豆甲虫已经密得用手捉不过来了。记住，这两样都是毒药，巴黎绿更是烈性的。叫人仔细包好，别让它们挨着别的东西。你回来就交给我保管。另外，再买一把新的大洒水壶。”

那天下午，我把冒尖的一汤匙藜芦掺进洒水壶的水里——壶身上我已漆了“毒药”两个字。拿这药水，凡是找见虫子的灌木丛，我都喷了个透；第二天早晨，我们发现这些敌人多半已经毙命，好不痛快。可是仍有漏网的，又有新孵出来的；我们看出，要保住红醋栗，唯有时时刻刻警戒。每晚我们都把灌木丛巡查一遍，直到果子将近成熟；凡见着那些可恶的小害虫，就地赏它一剂。另一把壶我也漆上“毒药”，底下另添几道短杠，表明它只许用来配巴黎绿。照我那本书所说，一小茶匙这烈性药剂，足够兑一只普通木桶盛的清水。这种毒药我收在鲍勃西（Bobsey）够不着的地方——说实在的，除了我自己，谁也别想拿到；靠着它，土豆甲虫连同幼虫也一并剿灭了。世上别处情形如何我不敢说，可在我们这一带，千真万确：唯有敏捷而明智的努力，才能确保成功。

## III New Prospects / III 新的前景

===Original===
There is no need of dwelling further on the reasons for or against the step we proposed. We thought a great deal and talked it over several times. Finally my wife agreed that the change would be wise and best for all. Then the children were taken into our confidence, and they became more delighted every day as the prospect grew clearer to them.

“We’ll all be good soon, won’t we?” said my youngest, who had a rather vivid sense of his own shortcomings, and kept them in the minds of others as well.

“Why so, Bobsey?”

“ ’Cause mamma says that God put the first people in a garden and they was very good, better’n any folks afterwards. God oughter know the best place for people.”

===Chinese===
我们打算迈出的这一步，赞成也好、反对也罢，个中缘由不必再多费笔墨。我们反复思量，又商量了好几回。末了，妻子同意：这一变动是明智的，对大家都最好。随后，我们把孩子们也引为心腹，前景在他们眼里一天比一天清晰，他们的欢喜也一天比一天增多。

“我们很快就会个个都变好，是不是？”我家最小的这个说道。他对自己身上的毛病有一种格外鲜明的自觉，而且还总能让旁人也把这些毛病牢记在心。

“怎么这么说呢，鲍勃西（Bobsey）？”

“因为妈妈说，上帝把最早的人安置在一座园子里，他们可乖啦，比后来哪一辈的人都强。上帝最该知道人住在什么地方最好。”

===Original===
Thus Bobsey gave a kind of divine sanction to our project. Of course we had not taken so important a step without asking the Great Father of all to guide us; for we felt that in the mystery of life we too were but little children who knew not what should be on the morrow, or how best to provide for it with any certainty. To our sanguine minds there was in Bobsey’s words a hint of something more than permission to go up out of Egypt.

So it was settled that we should leave our narrow suite of rooms, the Daggetts and the Ricketts, and go to the country. To me naturally fell the task of finding the land flowing with milk and honey to which we should journey in the spring. Meantime we were already emigrants at heart, full of the bustle and excitement of mental preparation.

I prided myself somewhat on my knowledge of human nature, which, in regard to children, conformed to comparatively simple laws. I knew that the change would involve plenty of hard work, self-denial and careful managing, which nothing could redeem from prose; but I aimed to add to our exodus, so far as possible, the elements of adventure and mystery so dear to the hearts of children. The question where we should go was the cause of much discussion, the studying of maps, and the learning of not a little geography.

Merton’s counsel was that we should seek a region abounding in Indians, bears, and “such big game.” His advice made clear the nature of some of his recent reading. He proved, however, that he was not wanting in sense by his readiness to give up these attractive features in the choice of locality.

===Chinese===
鲍勃西这番话，等于给我们的计划盖上了一道神意的印可。当然，如此重大的一步，我们原就不曾不求万有的天父引领便贸然迈出；因为我们感到，在人生的奥秘面前，我们自己也不过是一群小孩子，不知道明天会有什么，也不知道怎样才能十拿九稳地为明天作好打算。而在我们这些满怀希望的人心里，鲍勃西的话隐隐透出的意味，可不止是恩准我们出埃及这一层。

于是事情就这么定了：我们要离开那几间狭窄的屋子，离开达格茨（Daggett）家和里克茨（Rickett）家，到乡下去。为我们开春后要迁往的那块流奶与蜜之地寻一个去处，这担子自然落在我的肩上。与此同时，我们在心里早已是移民了，满怀着精神筹备带来的忙碌和兴奋。

我对自己洞悉人性的本事颇有些自负，而就孩子而论，人性遵循的规律还算比较简单。我知道，这一变动意味着大量的苦活、克己和精打细算，任凭怎么点染也改不了它平淡的本色；但我打算给我们这场“出埃及”式的迁徙，尽可能添上孩子们最心爱的冒险与神秘的成分。我们该往哪儿去，这个问题引出了许许多多的商议、一遍遍的看地图，还顺带学到了不少地理知识。

默顿（Merton）出的主意是，我们该去找一处印第安人、熊和“这类大猎物”成群出没的地方。他这番高见，让人明白了他近来读的都是些什么书。不过，在挑选安家地点时，他痛痛快快地放弃了这些诱人的条件，倒也证明他并不缺少头脑。

===Original===
Mousie’s soft black eyes always lighted up at the prospect of a flower garden that should be as big as our sitting room. Even in our city apartments, poisoned by gas and devoid of sunlight, she usually managed to keep a little houseplant in bloom, and the thought of placing seeds in the open ground, where, as she said, “the roots could go down to China if they wanted to,” brought the first color I had seen in her face for many a day.

Winnie was our strongest child, and also the one who gave me the most anxiety. Impulsive, warmhearted, restless, she always made me think of an overfull fountain. Her alert black eyes were as eager to see as was her inquisitive mind to pry into everything. She was sturdily built for a girl, and one of the severest punishments we could inflict was to place her in a chair and tell her not to move for an hour. We were beginning to learn that we could no more keep her in our sitting room than we could restrain a mountain brook that foams into a rocky basin only to foam out again. Melissa Daggett was of a very different type—I could never see her without the word “sly” coming into my mind—and her small mysteries awakened Winnie’s curiosity. Now that the latter was promised chickens, and rambles in the woods, Melissa and her secrets became insignificant, and the ready promise to keep aloof from her was given.

As for Bobsey, he should have a pig which he could name and call his own, and for which he might pull weeds and pick up apples. We soon found that he was communing with that phantom pig in his dreams.

===Chinese===
一想到将有一座跟我们家客厅一般大的花园，小耗子（Mousie）那双温柔的黑眼睛总会倏地亮起来。哪怕在被煤气熏害、终年不见阳光的城市公寓里，她通常也能设法让一盆小小的花草开着花；而一想到要把种子撒进露天的泥土——照她说，“根要是愿意，尽可以一直扎到中国去”——她的脸上便泛起了血色，那是我多日以来头一回见到的颜色。

温妮（Winnie）是我们孩子里最结实的一个，也是最叫我操心费神的一个。她性子急、心肠热、一刻也闲不住，总让我想起一泓满得直往外溢的泉水。她那双机灵的黑眼睛什么都急于看个真切，那颗好探究的心也什么都想探个究竟。一个女孩家，她的身子骨算生得结实了，我们能加诸她的最重的惩罚之一，就是让她坐在一把椅子上，告诉她一个钟头之内不许动。我们渐渐明白，想把她圈在我们家客厅里，就跟拦住一道山涧一样办不到——那涧水白沫翻腾地涌进石潭，转眼又翻腾着漫了出去。梅莉茜·达格茨（Melissa Daggett）却是截然不同的另一路孩子——我一见到她，脑子里准会蹦出“狡黠”两个字——她那些小小的神秘勾起了温妮的好奇心。如今温妮既已得到鸡和林间漫游的许诺，梅莉茜和她的秘密顿时变得无足轻重，温妮便痛痛快快地答应不再同她来往。

至于鲍勃西嘛，他该有一头小猪，可以给它起名字、管它叫自己的，还能为它拔草、捡苹果。没过多久我们就发现，他连做梦都在跟那头幻影小猪拉家常。

## XXIV “No Blind Drifting” / XXIV 决不盲目漂流

===Original===
One long, stormy day I prepared an account book. On its left-hand pages I entered the cost of the place and all expenses thus far incurred. The right-hand pages were for records of income, as yet small indeed. They consisted only of the proceeds from the sale of the calf, the eggs that Winnie gathered, and the milk measured each day, all valued at the market price. I was resolved that there should be no blind drifting toward the breakers of failure—that at the end of the year we should know whether we had made progress, stood still, or gone backward. My system of keeping the accounts was so simple that I easily explained it to my wife, Merton, and Mousie, for I believed that, if they followed the effort at country living understandingly, they would be more willing to practice the self-denial necessary for success. Indeed, I had Merton write out most of the items, even though the record, as a result, was not very neat. I stopped his worrying over blots and errors, by saying, “You are of more account than the account book, and will learn by practice to be as accurate as anyone.”

My wife and Mousie also started another book of household expenses, that we might always know just where we stood and what our prospects were.

Weeks would elapse before our place would be food-producing to any great extent. In the meantime we must draw chiefly on our capital in order to live. Winifred and I resolved to meet this necessity in no careless way, feeling that not a penny should be spent which might be saved. The fact that I had only my family to support was greatly in our favor. There was no kitchen cabinet, that ate much and wasted more, to satisfy. Therefore, our revenue of eggs and milk went a long way toward meeting the problem. We made out a list of cheap, yet wholesome, articles of food, and found that we could buy oatmeal at four cents per pound, Indian meal at two and a half cents, rice at eight cents, samp at four, mackerel at nine, pork at twelve, and ham at fifteen cents. The last two articles were used sparingly, and more as relishes and for flavoring than as food. Flour happened to be cheap at the time, the best costing but seven dollars a barrel; of vegetables, we had secured abundance at slight cost; and the apples still added the wholesome element of fruit. A butcher drove his wagon to our door three times a week and, for cash, would give us, at very reasonable rates, certain cuts of beef and mutton. These my wife conjured into appetizing dishes and delicious soups.

===Chinese===
一个风雨连天的漫长日子，我备好了一本账簿。左边的页码上，我记下置办这处产业的花费以及迄今支出的一切费用；右边的页码则留着记载进项——眼下确实还少得可怜。进项只有卖牛犊所得的款项、温妮（Winnie）捡集的鸡蛋，以及每天称量的牛奶，一律按市价折算。我下定决心，决不朝着失败这堆暗礁盲目漂流——到年底，我们该知道自己究竟是前进了，原地踏步，还是倒退了。我记账的法子十分简单，轻易便向妻子、默顿（Merton）和小耗子（Mousie）解释清楚了，因为我相信，倘若他们对这番乡下生活的尝试心中有数，就会更乐意实行成功所必需的克己功夫。说实在的，大部分账目我都是让默顿抄写的，账本因此算不上整洁。见他对着墨渍和笔误发愁，我便一句宽慰止住了他：“你这个人可比账簿要紧，多练练手，你准能记得跟谁一样精确。”

妻子和小耗子也另立了一本家务开销簿，好让我们随时弄清自己的处境究竟怎样，前景又如何。

还得过上好几个星期，我们这地方才能在相当程度上出产食物。在此之前，要维持生活，主要就得动用本钱。温妮弗蕾德（Winifred）和我决意郑重其事地应付这份必需，一分可以省下的钱也绝不乱花。我只需养活一家人，这个事实对我们大为有利。这里没有什么“厨房内阁”等着供养——那班人吃得多，糟蹋得更多。这样一来，鸡蛋和牛奶的进项在应付难题上就派了大用场。我们开出一张价廉而滋养的食物单子，发现燕麦片每磅四美分就能买到，印第安玉米粉两美分半，大米八美分，玉米糁四美分，鲭鱼九美分，猪肉十二美分，火腿十五美分。后两样用得很省，与其说是当饭吃，不如说用来提味佐餐。面粉碰巧正贱，上好的每桶也只要七美元；蔬菜我们早已花小钱囤得足足的；苹果依旧给餐桌添上水果这份滋养。有个肉贩每星期三次把车赶到我们门口，只要付现钱，他便以十分公道的价钱卖给我们几样牛肉和羊肉的部位。经妻子像变戏法似的一番调制，这些都成了开胃的菜肴和鲜美的浓汤。

===Original===
Thus it can be seen that we had a varied diet at a surprisingly small outlay. Such details may appear to some very homely, yet our health and success depended largely upon thoughtful attention to just such prosaic matters. The children were growing plump and ruddy at an expense less than would be incurred by one or two visits from a fashionable physician in the city.

In the matter of food, I also gave more thought to my wife’s time and strength than to the little people’s wishes. While we had variety and abundance, we did not have many dishes at any one meal.

“We shall not permit mamma to be over the hot range any more than is necessary,” I said. “She and Mousie must give us, from day to day, what costs little in time as well as money.”

===Chinese===
由此可见，我们的伙食花样不少，花销却小得惊人。这类细节在有些人看来也许十分家常琐屑，可我们的健康与成败，多半正系于对这般平淡事务的用心留意。孩子们长得丰满红润，其花费还抵不上城里一位时髦医生上门一两次的诊金。

在吃的方面，我考虑妻子的时间和精力，也多过考虑小家伙们的口腹之愿。我们固然花样多、吃食足，可每一餐的菜色并不繁多。

“除非万不得已，我们决不许妈妈守在热灶台跟前，”我说，“她和默顿每天给我们做的饭菜，必须是既省时间又省钱的。”

===Original===
Fortunately, plain, wholesome food does not require much time in preparation. There would be better health in many homes if there was more economy in labor. For instance, the children at first clamored for griddlecakes, but I said, “Isn’t it nicer to have mamma sit down quietly with us at breakfast than to see her running back and forth from the hot stove?” and even Bobsey, though rather ruefully, voted against cakes, except on rare occasions.

The washtub I forbade utterly, and the services of a stout Irishwoman were secured for one day in the week. Thus, by a little management, my wife was not overtaxed. Indeed, she had so much leisure that she and Mousie began giving Winnie and Bobsey daily lessons, for we had decided that the children should not go to school until the coming autumn. Early in April, therefore, our country life was passing into a quiet routine, not burdensome, at least within doors; and I justly felt that if all were well in the citadel of home, the chances of the outdoor campaign were greatly improved.

===Chinese===
所幸家常而滋养的饭菜，烹制起来本不需太多工夫。许多人家若在劳作上更节省些，健康情况本来会更好。比方说，孩子们起先吵着要吃烙饼，我却说：“让妈妈安安静静坐下来同我们一起吃早饭，是不是比瞧着她在热炉子边跑来跑去强得多？”于是连鲍勃西（Bobsey）也投了烙饼的反对票——尽管满心不舍——只在难得的场合才吃上一回。

洗衣盆我可断然禁绝了，另雇了一位健壮的爱尔兰妇人，每星期来帮工一天。这样，稍加调度，妻子便不至于劳累过度。她闲余得很，竟同默顿一起给温妮和鲍勃西天天上起课来，因为我们已打定主意，孩子们要到今年秋天才送进学校。于是，四月初，我们的乡下生活渐渐归入一种安静的常规，至少在家里，这常规并不沉重；我也满有理由觉得，只要家庭这座堡垒之中一切安好，户外这场战役的胜算便会大大增加。

## XV Our Sunny Kitchen / XV 我们阳光明媚的厨房

===Original===
Before we reached the landing I had invested a goodly sum in four pairs of rubber boots, for I knew how hopeless it would be to try to keep Winnie and Bobsey indoors. As for Mousie, she would have to be prudent until the ground should become dry and warm.

There is no need of dwelling long on the bringing home of our effects and the getting to rights. We were back soon after ten, and found that Winnie and Bobsey, having exhausted the resources of the house, had been permitted to start at the front door, and, with an old fire shovel and a piece of board, had well-nigh completed a path to the well, piling up the snow as they advanced, so that their overshoes were a sufficient protection.

After we had carried in the things I interceded with Mr. Jones and then told the boys that they could take their guns and be absent two or three hours if they would promise to help faithfully the rest of the day.

I had bought at Maizeville Landing such provisions, tools, etc., as I should need immediately. Therefore I did not worry because the fickle March sky was clouding up again with the promise of rain. A heavy downpour now with snow upon the ground would cause almost a flood, but I felt that we could shut the door and find the old house a very comfortable ark.

===Chinese===
还没到码头，我就在四双胶靴上破费了一笔可观的数目，因为我知道，想把温妮（Winnie）和鲍勃西（Bobsey）关在屋里，纯属枉然。至于小耗子（Mousie），她就得安分些，等地面干爽暖和起来再说。
