家什运回家、归置停当的经过，无须多费笔墨。我们十点刚过便回来了，只见温妮和鲍勃西把屋里可施展的本事都使尽了之后，得了许可，从前门口动起手来，拿着一把旧火铲和一块木板，一条通到井边的路差不多已经修成，两人一路往前走一路把雪堆起来，套鞋也就尽够顶事了。

把东西搬进屋后，我替他们向琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）讨了个情，然后告诉两个男孩，准他们带上枪出去两三个钟头，但须答应这一天剩下的工夫里老老实实帮忙。

眼下就要用的粮食、工具等物，我在梅兹维尔码头（Maizeville Landing）都置办齐了，因此三月的天气变得快、又阴上来像要落雨，我也不犯愁。这会儿地上积着雪，再来一场倾盆大雨，几乎要闹成水灾；可我心里想，只要把门一关，这座老房子便是一只顶舒服的方舟。

===Original===
"A smart warm rain would be the best thing that could happen to yer," said Mr. Jones, as he helped me carry in furniture and put up beds; "it would take the snow off. Nat’rally you want to get out on the bare ground, for there’s allus a lot of clearin’ up to be done in the spring and old man Jamison was poorly last year and didn’t keep things up to the mark."

"Yes," I replied, "I am as eager to get to work outdoors as the boys were to go after rabbits. I believe I shall like the work, but that is not the question. I did not come to the country to amuse myself, like so many city people. I don’t blame them; I wish I could afford farming for fun. I came to earn a living for my wife and children, and I am anxious to be about it. I won’t ask you for anything except advice. I’ve only had a city training, and my theories about farming would perhaps make you smile. But I’ve seen enough of you already to feel that you are inclined to be kind and neighborly, and the best way to show this will be in helping me to good, sound, practical, commonsense advice. But you mustn’t put on airs, or be impatient with me. Shrewd as you are, I could show you some things in the city."

"Oh, I’d be a sight queerer there than you here. I see your p’int, and if you’ll come to me I won’t let you make no blunders I wouldn’t make myself. Perhaps that ain’t saying a great deal, though."

By this time everything had been brought in and either put in place or stowed out of the way, until my wife could decide where and how she would arrange things.

"Now," I said, when we had finished, "carry out our agreement."

===Chinese===
“下场又急又暖的雨，对你们可是再好不过的事，”琼斯先生帮着我往屋里搬家具、支床铺，嘴上说道，“雪一冲就没了。你自然巴不得早点儿上露地干活，开春总有收拾不完的一摊子，再说老贾米森（Jamison）去年身子不济，好多事情都没做到家。”

“不错，”我答道，“我巴望到屋外干活的劲头，就跟孩子们追兔子的劲头一样。我相信我会喜欢这活计，可问题不在这儿。我到乡下来，不像许多城里人那样，是为了消遣。我不怪他们；但愿我也有那份闲钱，种田只图好玩。我是来给妻子儿女谋生计的，恨不能马上动手。除了指教，我什么都不求你。我受的教养全是城里的，我那套务农的空论，说出来只怕你要发笑。不过跟你相处这半天，我已看得出你为人厚道、乐于邻里相帮，你要表示这份情，顶好的法子就是多给我些切实、地道、合乎常识的指点。可你不能摆架子，也不能对我性急。你精明归精明，城里的门道，我也能指给你几样。”

“嗐，我到了城里，比你在这儿还傻气得多。你那点意思我明白；你只管来找我，凡是连我自己都不会犯的错，我决不让你犯。不过，这话兴许也说不上多大分量。”

这会儿东西都搬进来了，该放地方的放好了，该挪开的也挪开了，只等我妻子拿定主意，看东西搁在哪儿、怎么搁。

“好了，”我们收拾停当，我说，“照咱们的约办吧。”

===Original===
Mr. Jones gave me a wink and drove away.

Our agreement was this—first, that he and Mr. Rollins, the owner of the other team, should be paid in full before night; and second, that Mrs. Jones should furnish us our dinner, in which the chief dish should be a potpie from the rabbit caught by Merton, and that Mr. Jones should bring everything over at one o’clock.

My wife was so absorbed in unpacking her china, kitchen utensils, and groceries that she was unaware of the flight of time, but at last she suddenly exclaimed, "I declare it’s dinnertime!"

"Not quite yet," I said; "dinner will be ready at one."

"It will? Oh, indeed! Since we are in the country we are to pick up what we can, like the birds. You intend to invite us all down to the apple barrel, perhaps."

===Chinese===
琼斯先生朝我挤了挤眼，赶车去了。

我们的约定是这么两条：第一，他和罗林斯先生（Mr. Rollins）——另一辆车的主人——工钱须在天黑前付清；第二，琼斯太太给我们备一顿饭，主菜得是一只锅派，用默顿（Merton）打来的那只兔子做，琼斯先生一点钟把东西一并送过来。

我妻子一心开她的瓷器、厨具和杂货包裹，光阴溜走了也不觉得，到末了忽然失声叫道：“哎呀，都到开饭的时辰了！”

“还差一点儿呢，”我说；“饭一点钟就好。”

“是吗？哦，敢情好！咱们既然进了乡下，就得学鸟儿那样，能捡着什么吃什么。说不定你还打算请我们全家上苹果桶跟前开饭呢。”

===Original===
"Certainly, whenever you wish to go; but we’ll have a hot dinner at one o’clock, and a game dinner into the bargain."

"I’ve heard the boys’ guns occasionally, but I haven’t seen the game, and it’s after twelve now."

"Papa has a secret—a surprise for us," cried Mousie; "I can see it in his eyes."

"Now, Robert, I know what you’ve been doing. You have asked Mrs. Jones to furnish a dinner. You are extravagant, for I could have picked up something that would have answered."

"No; I’ve been very prudent in saving your time and strength, and saving these is sometimes the best economy in the world. Mousie is nearer right. The dinner is a secret, and it has been furnished chiefly by one of the family."

===Chinese===
“去就是，你什么时候想去都行；不过一点钟咱们有一顿热饭，外带一桌野味宴。”

“孩子们的枪声我倒时不时听见，野味却没见着影儿，这都十二点过了。”

“爸爸有桩秘密——要给我们个意外，”小耗子嚷道；“我从他眼睛里看得出来。”

“嗳，罗伯特（Robert），我知道你干的什么名堂。你请琼斯太太给我们备饭了。你可真够破费的，其实我自己随手弄点什么，也就将就过去了。”

“不；我精打细算得很呢，省下的是你的时间和气力，而省这两样，有时正是世上头一等的节省。小耗子说的倒更沾边儿。这顿饭是桩秘密，置办它的主要是自家人。”

===Original===
"Well, I’m too busy to guess riddles today; but if my appetite is a guide, it is nearly time we had your secret."

"You would not feel like that after half an hour over a hot stove. Now you will be interrupted, in getting to rights, only long enough to eat your dinner. Then Mousie and Merton and Winnie will clear up everything, and before night you will feel settled enough to take things easy till tomorrow."

"I know your thoughtfulness for me, if not your secret," she said, gratefully, and was again putting things where, from housewifely experience, she knew they would be handy.

Mr. and Mrs. Jamison had clung to their old-fashioned ways, and had done their cooking over the open fire, using the swinging crane which is now employed chiefly in pictures. This, for the sake of the picture it made, we proposed to keep as it had been left, although at times it might answer some more prosaic purpose.

===Chinese===
“唉，今儿我忙得很，没工夫猜谜；可要拿我的胃口作准，你这秘密也快到时候了。”

“要在热炉子跟前站上半个钟头，你就不会这么说了。眼前归置东西，只打断你吃顿饭的工夫。回头小耗子、默顿和温妮会把什么都拾掇干净，不到天黑，你心里就安顿得下，可以松松泛泛歇到明天了。”

“你的秘密我不知道，你待我的这份体贴我可知道，”她感激地说，随即又把东西归置到她凭主妇的经验知道顺手好取的地方去。

贾米森先生和太太守着老派的过日子法，做饭一直在敞开的火上做，用的就是那副如今多半只在画片上见得到的摆动吊钩。为着这一幅画似的景致，我们打算让它照原样留着，虽说偶尔也不妨派点更平常的用场。

===Original===
At the eastern end of the house was a single room, added unknown years ago, and designed to be a bedchamber. Of late it had been used as a general storage and lumber room, and when I first inspected the house, I had found little in this apartment of service to us. So I had asked Mr. Jones to remove all that I did not care for, and to have the room cleansed, satisfied that it would just suit my wife as a kitchen. It was large, having windows facing the east and south, and therefore it would be light and cheerful, as a kitchen ever should be, especially when the mistress of the house is cook. There Mr. Jones and I set up the excellent stove that I had brought from New York—one to which my wife was accustomed, and from which she could conjure a rare good dinner when she gave her mind to it. Now as she moved back and forth, in such sunlight as the clouding sky permitted, she appeared the picture of pleased content.

"It cheers one up to enter a kitchen like this," she said.

"It is to be your garden for a time also," I exclaimed to Mousie. "I shall soon have by this east window a table with shallow boxes of earth, and in them you can plant some of your flower seeds. I only ask that I may have two of the boxes for early cabbages, lettuce, tomatoes, etc. You and your plants can take a sunbath every morning until it is warm enough to go out of doors, and you’ll find the plants won’t die here as they did in the dark, gas-poisoned city flat."

"I feel as if I were going to grow faster and stronger than the plants," cried the happy child.

===Chinese===
房子的东头有一间屋，不知哪一年添盖的，原打算做卧房。近来一直拿它堆放杂七杂八的物件，我头一遭看房时，就觉得这屋里没有什么对我们有用的东西。于是我便请琼斯先生把我不要的统统搬走，把屋子打扫干净，满心以为拿它做厨房，正合我妻子的心意。屋子很宽敞，窗户朝东又朝南，因此必定亮堂而爽朗——厨房本该如此，何况这一家的主妇亲自掌勺。琼斯先生和我在这里安好了我从纽约带来的那口好炉子——妻子使惯了的，只要她一上心，就能从那上头变出一顿少有的好饭来。这会儿她在阴云肯漏下的那点阳光里来回走动，那模样活是一幅心满意足的画儿。

“走进这样的厨房，人心里就敞亮，”她说。

“这一阵子，这儿也归你当花园，”我对小耗子大声说。“我很快就在这扇东窗底下安一张桌子，摆上几只浅浅的土箱，你把花籽儿种在里面。我只要讨两只箱子，种早甘蓝、莴苣、番茄什么的。往后每天早上，你和你的花草一同晒晒日光浴，等暖和到能出屋门为止；你会看见，花草在这儿死不了，不像在城里那又黑又给煤气熏毒了的公寓里那样。”

“我觉得我都要长得比花草还快、还壮了，”那快活的孩子嚷道。

===Original===
Junior and Merton now appeared, each carrying a rabbit. My boy’s face, however, was clouded, and he said, a little despondently, "I can’t shoot straight—missed every time; and Junior shot ’em after I had fired and missed."

"Pshaw!" cried Junior; "Merton’s got to learn to take a quick steady sight, like everyone else. He gets too excited."

"That’s just it, my boy," I said. "You shall go down by the creek and fire at a mark a few times every day, and you’ll soon hit it every time. Junior’s head is too level to think that anything can be done well without practice. Now, Junior," I added, "run over home and help your father bring us our dinner, and then you stay and help us eat it."

Father and son soon appeared, well laden. Winnie and Bobsey came in ravenous from their pathmaking, and all agreed that we had already grown one vigorous rampant Maizeville crop—an appetite.

The potpie was exulted over, and the secret of its existence explained. Even Junior laughed till the tears came as I described him, his father, and Merton, floundering through the deep snow after the rabbit, and we all congratulated Merton as the one who had provided our first country dinner.

===Chinese===
这时小琼斯（Junior）和默顿回来了，一人拎着一只兔子。可我儿子一脸愁云，有点儿丧气地说：“我打枪瞄不准——回回都落空；都是我放了空枪之后，小琼斯才打中的。”

“嗐！”小琼斯嚷道；“默顿得跟大伙儿一样，学会又快又稳地瞄准。他就是太沉不住气。”

“毛病正出在这儿，孩子，”我说。“往后你天天到小溪边去，对着靶子放几枪，要不了多久，回回都能打中。小琼斯头脑清楚得很，决不会以为凡事不经练习就能做好。好了，小琼斯，”我接着说，“跑回家去，帮你父亲给咱们送饭来，然后你就留下，帮我们把饭吃掉。”

爷儿俩不多时就来了，大包小包满载而归。温妮和鲍勃西修完了路，饿坏了，一头撞进屋来；大家一致认定，我们已经种出了头一茬梅兹维尔的庄稼，又茁壮又疯长——一副胃口。

那只锅派叫人欢喜得不得了，它怎么会有的秘密也交代了。我把小琼斯、他父亲和默顿深一脚浅一脚在深雪里追那兔子的狼狈相学了一遍，连小琼斯都笑得眼泪直流；我们一齐向默顿道贺——我们头一顿乡下饭就是他供给的。

## XXV Owls and Antwerps / XXV 猫头鹰与安特卫普树莓

===Original===
Each day at dawn, unless it was stormy, Merton patrolled the place with his gun, looking for hawks and other creatures which at this season he was permitted to shoot. He had quite as serious and important an air as if he were sallying forth to protect us from deadlier foes. For a time he saw nothing to fire at, since he had promised me not to shoot harmless birds. He always indulged himself, however, in one shot at a mark, and was becoming sure in his aim at stationary objects. One evening, however, when we were almost ready to retire, a strange sound startled us. At first it reminded me of the half-whining bark of a young dog, but the deep, guttural trill that followed convinced me that it was a screech owl, for I remembered having heard these birds when a boy.

The moment I explained the sound, Merton darted for his gun, and my wife exclaimed: “O dear! what trouble is coming now? Mother always said that the hooting of an owl near a house was a bad omen.”

I did not share in the superstition, although I disliked the uncanny sounds, and was under the impression that all owls, like hawks, should be destroyed. Therefore, I followed Merton out, hoping that he would get a successful shot at the night prowler.

The moonlight illumined everything with a soft, mild radiance; and the trees, with their tracery of bough and twig, stood out distinctly. Before we could discover the creature, it flew with noiseless wing from a maple near the door to another perch up the lane, and again uttered its weird notes.

===Chinese===
每天清晨，只要不是风雨天气，默顿（Merton）便挎着枪在园中四处巡查，寻找鹰隼和其他这时节准许射猎的动物。他那份神情煞是庄重要紧，仿佛正出门迎击更凶恶的敌人、护卫我们全家。起初他一直没瞧见可打的目标，因为他答应过我不射无害的鸟儿。不过他总要让着自己，对一处靶子放上一枪，打固定目标的准头也日渐长进。然而一天傍晚，我们差不多正要歇息的时候，一个怪声把我们惊动了。起初它让我想起小狗呜呜咽咽的半截吠声，可随后那阵低沉的喉音颤鸣使我断定，这是一只鸣角鸮（screech owl），因为我记得小时候听过这种鸟的叫声。

我一说明这声音的来历，默顿便飞身去取枪，妻子却惊呼道：“哎呀！这回又是什么祸事临头了？我母亲常说，房屋附近有猫头鹰啼叫可不是好兆头。”

我并不信这套迷信，只是也嫌恶这些阴森可怖的声音，而且一向以为猫头鹰和鹰隼一样，都该除掉。于是我跟着默顿走了出去，盼他能一枪命中这个夜间的潜行者。

月光以柔和温润的清辉照澈万物；树木连同枝枝杈杈的疏影，轮廓分明地挺立着。我们还没寻见那小东西，它已悄无声息地振翅飞离门旁一株枫树，落到小路更远处一根栖枝上，又发出那怪异的啼鸣。

===Original===
Merton was away like a swift shadow, and, screening himself behind the fence, stole upon his game. A moment later the report rang out in the still night. It so happened that Merton had fired just as the bird was about to fly, and had only broken a wing. The owl fell to the ground, but led the boy a wild pursuit before he was captured. Merton’s hands were bleeding when he brought the creature in. Unless prevented, it would strike savagely with its beak, and the motions of its head were as quick as lightning. It was, indeed, a strange captive, and the children looked at it in wondering and rather fearful curiosity. My wife, usually tenderhearted, wished the creature, so ill-omened in her eyes, to be killed at once, but I granted Merton’s request that he might put it in a box and keep it alive for a while.

“In the morning,” I said, “we will read all about it, and can examine it more carefully.”

My wife yielded, and I am not sure but that she thought we might avert misfortune by showing mercy.

===Chinese===
默顿像一道疾掠的影子去了，借着篱笆掩住身形，悄悄逼近猎物。片刻之后，枪声在寂静的夜里炸响。也是凑巧，默顿开枪时那鸟正要起飞，只打断了一只翅膀。猫头鹰跌落在地，却领着这孩子好一阵疯追，才被逮住。默顿把那东西拿进屋时，双手都流了血——稍不拦阻，它便用喙凶狠地啄击，脑袋转动快如闪电。这确实是个奇特的俘虏，孩子们带着又惊又怯的好奇打量它。一向心慈的妻子，巴不得立刻弄死这个在她眼中不吉利的家伙，我却准了默顿的请求，把它放进一只箱子，暂且留它一条活命。

“到明早，”我说，“我们把书上关于它的一切都读一遍，也好更仔细地察看它。”

妻子让步了，而且我拿不准，她心里是不是觉得，发一回善心，兴许还能消灾免祸。

===Original===
Among my purchases was a recent work on natural history. But our minds had been engrossed with too many practical questions to give it much attention. Next morning we consulted it, and found our captive variously described as the little red, the mottled, or the screech owl. Then followed an account of its character and habits. We learned that we had made war upon a useful friend, instead of an ill-boding, harmful creature. We were taught that this species is a destroyer of mice, beetles, and vermin, thus rendering the agriculturist great services, which, however are so little known that the bird is everywhere hunted down without mercy or justice.

“Surely, this is not true of all owls,” I said, and by reading further we learned that the barred, or hoot owl, and the great horned owl, were deserving of a surer aim of Merton’s gun. They prey not only upon useful game, but also invade the poultry yard, the horned species being especially destructive. Instances were given in which these freebooters had killed every chicken upon a farm. As they hunt only at night, they are hard to capture. Their notes and natures are said to be in keeping with their deeds of darkness; for their cry is wild, harsh, and unearthly, while in temper they are cowardly, savage, and untamable, showing no affection even for each other. A female has been known to kill and eat the male.

“The moral of this owl episode,” I concluded, “is that we must learn to know our neighbors, be they birds, beasts, or human beings, before we judge them. This book is not only full of knowledge, but of information that is practical and useful. I move that we read up about the creatures in our vicinity. What do you say, Merton? wouldn’t it be well to learn *what* to shoot, as well as how to shoot?”

===Chinese===
我置办的东西里，有一本新近出版的博物学著作。只是我们的心思早被太多实际问题占去了，无暇多理会它。第二天早晨我们翻检此书，发现我们的俘虏名称不一，有叫小红鸮的，有叫斑鸮的，也有叫鸣角鸮的。接着便是对它习性的记述。我们这才明白，我们讨伐的不是什么报凶的恶鸟，而是一位有益的朋友。书里告诉我们，这一族类专捕老鼠、甲虫和种种害虫，给农人立下大功；只是这功劳鲜为人知，以致这种鸟到处被人赶尽杀绝，既无怜悯，也不公道。

“总不会所有的猫头鹰都是这样吧，”我说。再往下读，我们才知道，横斑林鸮，也叫呼鸮，还有大雕鸮，才真正该挨默顿枪下更准的一击。它们不仅捕食有益的猎物，还闯进家禽场，雕鸮一族破坏力尤其惊人。书里举了实例，说这些强盗曾把一整个农场的鸡杀得一只不剩。它们只在夜里出猎，所以很难捕捉。据说它们的啼声与禀性，同那些黑暗行径正相称：叫声狂野、刺耳、不似人间之音；性子却又怯懦、凶残、难以驯服，彼此之间也毫无情义。据说有一只雌鸟，竟杀死并吃掉了自己的配偶。

“这场猫头鹰风波给我们的教训，”我下了结论，“就是要先学会认识我们的左邻右舍——无论他们是鸟、是兽、还是人——然后再下判断。这本书不光知识渊博，里头的信息还样样切实有用。我提议，我们把附近出没的动物都好好读一读。默顿，你说呢？不光要学会怎么开枪，还得弄明白*该打什么*，这不是更好吗？”

===Original===
Protecting his hands with buckskin gloves, the boy applied mutton suet to our wounded owl’s wing. It was eventually healed, and the bird was given its liberty. It gradually became sprightly and tame, and sociable in the evening, affording the children and Junior much amusement.

By the 7th of April there was a prospect of warmer and more settled weather, and Mr. Jones told us to lose no time in uncovering our Antwerp raspberries. They had been bent down close to the ground the previous winter and covered with earth. To remove this without breaking the canes, required careful and skillful work. We soon acquired the knack, however, of pushing and throwing aside the soil, then lifting the canes gently through what remained, and shaking them clear.

“Be careful to level the ground evenly,” Mr. Jones warned us, “for it won’t do at all to leave hummocks of dirt around the hills;” and we followed his instructions.

The canes were left until a heavy shower of rain washed them clean; then Winnie and Bobsey tied them up. We gave steady and careful attention to the Antwerps, since they would be our main dependence for income. I also raked in around the hills of one row a liberal dressing of wood ashes, intending to note its effect.

===Chinese===
那孩子戴上鹿皮手套护住双手，往我们这位伤员的翅膀上敷羊脂。伤处终于痊愈，鸟儿也重获自由。它渐渐精神起来，也驯顺了，晚间还爱与人亲近，给孩子们和小琼斯（Junior）添了不少乐趣。

到了四月七日，天气眼看要转暖，也趋于稳定，琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）嘱咐我们抓紧时间，把安特卫普（Antwerp）树莓的覆土揭开。去岁入冬时，这些枝条都被压弯得贴着地面，再覆上泥土。要不折断枝条而除掉覆土，得细心又有技巧地干。不过我们很快便摸到了窍门：先把土推开、拨到一旁，再把枝条轻轻从余土里穿提出来，抖搂干净。

“务必当心把地面耙平，”琼斯先生叮嘱我们，“株丛周围留下一堆堆土疙瘩，那是万万不行的。”我们一一照办。

枝条先那样晾着，等一场大雨冲刷干净；随后温妮（Winnie）和鲍勃西（Bobsey）便把它们捆扎起来。我们对安特卫普树莓照料得又勤又细，因为它们将是我们赖以进项的主要指望。我还往其中一行的株丛周围搂进足量的草木灰，打算看看它的功效如何。

===Original===
Months since, with much doubt and diffidence, I began this simple story. I had never before written expressly for young people, and I knew that the honest little critics could not be beguiled with words which did not tell an interesting story. How far I have succeeded, the readers of this volume, and of the “St. Nicholas” magazine, wherein the tale appeared as a serial, alone can answer.

I have portrayed no actual experience, but have sought to present one which might be verified in real life. I have tried to avoid all that would be impossible or even improbable. The labors performed by the children in the story were not unknown to my own hands, in childhood, nor would they form tasks too severe for many little hands now idle in the cities.

The characters are all imaginary; the scenes, in the main, are real: and I would gladly lure other families from tenement flats into green pastures.

===Chinese===
几个月前，我怀着满心疑虑与忐忑，动笔写下了这个简朴的故事。我此前从未专门为年轻人写作，而且我知道，要是文字讲不出一个有趣的故事，那些诚实的小批评家是哄不住的。我究竟成功了几分，唯有本书的读者，以及刊载过这个连载故事的《圣尼古拉斯》（St. Nicholas）杂志的读者，方能作答。

我笔下描绘的并非实有的经历，而是力图呈现一段可以在现实生活中得到印证的经历。我尽力避开一切不可能、甚或不大近情的事。故事里孩子们所操持的那些劳作，我童年时也曾亲手做过；而对如今许多在城里闲着无事的小手来说，这些活计也算不得过分繁重。

书中人物纯出虚构；场景则大体真实：我十分乐意把别的家庭从经济公寓的套房里，引到青草地上来。

===Original===
E. P. R.
Cornwall-on-the-Hudson, 

 August 10, 1885.

===Chinese===
E. P. R.
哈德逊河畔康沃尔（Cornwall-on-the-Hudson），

1885年8月10日。

## XXXVII Rallying from the Blow / XXXVII 从打击中振作

===Original===
Our house was far enough from the barn to prevent the shock of the thunderbolt from disabling us beyond a moment or two. Merton had fallen off his chair, but was on his feet almost instantly; the other children were soon sobbing and clinging to my wife and myself.

In tones that I sought to render firm and quiet, I said: “No more of this foolish fear. We are in God’s hands, and He will take care of us. Winifred, you must rally and soothe the children, while Merton and I go out and save what we can. All danger to the house is now over, for the worst of the storm has passed.”

In a moment my wife, although very pale, was reassuring the younger children, and Merton and I rushed forth.

“Lead the horse out of the barn basement, Merton,” I cried, “and tie him securely behind the house. If he won’t go readily, throw a blanket over his eyes.”

===Chinese===
好在我们住得离谷仓足够远，那声霹雳的震撼只叫我们慌乱了一两分钟，还没到彻底吓瘫的地步。默顿（Merton）从椅子上跌了下来，却几乎立刻站起了身；其余的孩子也很快呜呜咽咽，紧紧偎着我和妻子。

我竭力让自己的声音显得沉着而平静：“别再这么瞎害怕了。我们在上帝手里，他会照看我们的。温妮弗蕾德（Winifred），你定定神，把孩子们安抚好；我和默顿出去，能抢出多少是多少。房子已经没有危险了，最凶的风头已经过去。”

转眼之间，妻子虽然脸色煞白，已在宽慰年幼的几个孩子；我和默顿冲了出去。

“默顿，把马从谷仓底层牵出来，”我喊道，“拴牢在房后。它要是不肯老实走，就拿条毯子蒙住它的眼睛。”

===Original===
I spoke these words as we ran through the torrents of rain precipitated by the tremendous concussion which the lightning had produced.

I opened the barn doors and saw that the hay was on fire. There was not a second to lose, and excitement doubled my strength. The load of hay on the wagon had not yet caught. Although nearly stifled with sulphurous smoke, I seized the shafts and backed the wagon with its burden out into the rain. Then, seizing a fork, I pushed and tossed off the load so that I could draw our useful market vehicle to a safe distance. There were a number of crates and baskets in the barn, also some tools, etc. These I had to let go. Hastening to the basement, I found that Merton had succeeded in getting the horse away. There was still time to smash the window of the poultry-room and toss the chickens out of doors. Our cow, fortunately, was in the meadow.

By this time Mr. Jones and Junior were on the ground, and they were soon followed by Rollins, Bagley, and others. There was nothing to do now, however, but to stand aloof and witness the swift destruction. After the first great gust had passed, there was fortunately but little wind, and the heavy downpour prevented the flames from spreading. In this we stood, scarcely heeding it in the excitement of the hour. After a few moments I hastened to assure my trembling wife and crying children that the rain made the house perfectly safe, and that they were in no danger at all. Then I called to the neighbors to come and stand under the porch roof.

From this point we could see the great pyramid of fire and smoke ascending into the black sky. The raindrops glittered like fiery hail in the intense light and the still vivid flashes from the clouds.

===Chinese===
这些话是我在滔滔雨幕中奔跑着喊出来的——那阵大雨，正是闪电掀起的巨大震荡从半空震落下来的。

我推开谷仓大门，干草已经烧着了。一秒钟也耽搁不得，亢奋使我平添了一倍力气。车上那垛干草还没引着，我虽然几乎被硫磺味的浓烟呛得透不过气，仍一把抓住车辕，把载着草的货车倒退着拖进雨里。接着我又抓起一把草叉，连推带掷地把草卸下来，好把我们这辆顶用的上市货车拉到安全的地方。谷仓里还有不少板条箱和篮子，也有些工具之类，这些只好舍弃了。我赶到底层，发现默顿已经把马牵了出去。还来得及砸开家禽室的窗子，把鸡抛到外面。万幸的是，我们那头牛正在草地上。

这时琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）和小琼斯（Junior）已经赶到现场，罗林斯（Rollins）、巴格利（Bagley）和旁人相继而来。可是眼下除了站开一旁，眼睁睁看着大火迅速吞没一切，再没有事情可做。头一阵狂风刮过之后，幸而风势已很小，倾盆大雨又压住了火头，不许它蔓延。我们就站在这大雨里，当此人心激荡的时刻，几乎没有把雨放在心上。过了一小会儿，我赶紧去安抚浑身发抖的妻子和啼哭的孩子们，叫他们放心：大雨已使房子万无一失，他们丝毫没有危险。随后我招呼邻居们过来，站进门廊顶棚底下。

从那里望得见烈火浓烟堆成的巨大金字塔，直冲漆黑的天空。雨点在炽亮的火光和云中依然夺目的闪电映照下闪闪发亮，宛如一片片火红的冰雹。

===Original===
“This is hard luck, neighbor Durham,” said Mr. Jones, with a long breath.

“My wife and children are safe,” I replied, quietly.

Then we heard the horse neighing and tugging at his halter. Bagley had the good sense and will to jerk off his coat, tie it around the animal’s eyes, and lead him to a distance from the fatal fascination of the flames.

In a very brief space of time the whole structure, with my summer crop of hay, gathered with so much labor, sunk down into glowing, hissing embers. I was glad to have the ordeal over, and to be relieved from fear that the wind would rise again. Now I was assured of the extent of our loss, as well as of its certainty.

===Chinese===
“真是飞来横祸啊，达勒姆（Durham）邻居，”琼斯先生长长叹了口气说。

“我的妻子和孩子都平安。”我平静地答道。

随即我们听见那匹马嘶叫着，拽住笼头拼命挣扎。巴格利倒有见识也有魄力，唰地扯下外衣，蒙住马眼，把它牵到远处，离开那片对牲口有致命迷惑力的火光。

顷刻之间，整座建筑连同我费尽辛劳收进来的夏天那一茬干草，一起塌陷成红亮的、咝咝作响的余烬。这场煎熬总算到了头，我也不必再提心吊胆，怕风重新刮起来。如今损失的范围有多大、又确凿到了什么地步，我心里都有了底。

===Original===
“Well, well,” said the warmhearted and impulsive Rollins, “when you are ready to build again, your neighbors will give you a lift. By converting Bagley into a decent fellow, you’ve made all our barns safer, and we owe you a good turn. He was worse than lightning.”

I expressed my thanks, adding, “This isn’t as bad as you think; I’m insured.”

“Well, now, that’s sensible,” said Mr. Jones. “I’ll sleep better for that fact, and so will you, Robert Durham. You’ll make a go of it here yet.”

“I’m not in the least discouraged,” I answered; “far worse things might have happened. I’ve noticed in my paper that a good many barns have been struck this summer, so my experience is not unusual. The only thing to do is to meet such things patiently and make the best of them. As long as the family is safe and well, outside matters can be remedied. Thank you, Bagley,” I continued, addressing him, as he now led forward the horse. “You had your wits about you. Old Bay will have to stand under the shed tonight.”

“Well, Mr. Durham, the harness is still on him, all ’cept the headstall; and he’s quiet now.”

===Chinese===
“唉，唉，”热心肠而急性子的罗林斯说道，“等你们打算重建的时候，街坊们都会来搭把手。你把巴格利感化成了个正派人，我们大家的谷仓都跟着更保险了，我们欠你一回情。这家伙可比闪电还厉害。”

我道了谢，又添上一句：“事情没有你们想的那么糟；我是保过险的。”

“嘿，这才是明白话，”琼斯先生说，“听了这话，我睡觉能踏实些，你也会踏实些的，罗伯特·达勒姆（Robert Durham）。你在这个地方终究能干出个样子来。”

“我丝毫不气馁，”我回答，“再糟的事本来都可能出呢。我在报上注意到，今年夏天遭雷劈的谷仓多得很，所以我这点遭遇并不稀罕。唯一的办法就是沉着地对待这类事情，尽力往好处安排。只要一家人平安康健，身外之物总有法子补救。谢谢你，巴格利，”这时他把马牵上前，我转向他说下去，“你当时头脑很清楚。老枣红马（Old Bay）今晚只好委屈它在棚子底下站一宿了。”

“是了，达勒姆先生，马具还全套在它身上呢，就差个笼头没戴；它这会儿也安静下来了。”

===Original===
“Yes,” I replied, “in our haste we didn’t throw off the harness before the shower, and it has turned out very well.”

“Tell ye what it is, neighbors,” said practical Mr. Jones; “ ’tisn’t too late for Mr. Durham to sow a big lot of fodder corn, and that’s about as good as hay. We’ll turn to and help him get some in.”

This was agreed to heartily, and one after another they wrung my hand and departed, Bagley jogging in a companionable way down the road with Rollins, whose chickens he had stolen, but had already paid for.

I looked after them and thought: “Thank Heaven I have not lost my barn as some thought I might at one time! As Rollins suggested, I’d rather take my chances with the lightning than with a vicious neighbor. Bagley acted the part of a good friend tonight.”

Then, seeing that we could do nothing more, Merton and I entered the house.

===Chinese===
“是啊，”我答道，“我们当时慌里慌张，没赶在暴雨前把马具卸下来，如今看来倒也不坏。”

“我跟你们说句实在话，各位街坊，”讲究实际的琼斯先生开了腔，“达勒姆先生眼下种上一大片饲料玉米还不算晚，那玩意儿差不多顶得上干草。咱们都来搭把手，帮他种上一些。”

大家都由衷赞成，随后一个个紧紧握了我的手，相继散去。巴格利同罗林斯亲亲热热结伴，一路小跑着去了——他偷过罗林斯家的鸡，不过早已照数赔清。

我望着他们的背影，心里想道：“谢天谢地，我的谷仓总算没有像有些人一时以为的那样完蛋！正像罗林斯说的，我宁可跟闪电碰碰运气，也不愿跟一个恶邻居较量。今晚巴格利倒真够朋友。”

随后，看看再没有什么可做的了，我和默顿便走进屋里。

===Original===
I clapped the boy on the shoulder as I said: “You acted like a man in the emergency, and I’m proud of you. The bringing out a young fellow strong is almost worth the cost of a barn.”

My wife came and put her arm around my neck and said:

“You bear up bravely, Robert, but I fear you are discouraged at heart. To think of such a loss, just as we were getting started!” and there were tears in her eyes.

“Yes,” I replied, “it will be a heavy loss for us, and a great inconvenience, but it might have been so much worse! All sit down and I’ll tell you something. You see my training in business led me to think of the importance of insurance, and to know the best companies. As soon as the property became yours, Winifred, I insured the buildings for nearly all they were worth. The hay and the things in the barn at the time will prove a total loss; but it is a loss that we can stand and make good largely before winter. I tell you honestly that we have no reason to be discouraged. We shall soon have a better barn than the one lost; for, by good planning, a better one can be built for the money that I shall receive. So we will thank God that we are all safe ourselves, and go quietly to sleep.”

With the passing of the storm, the children had become quiet, and soon we lost in slumber all thought of danger and loss.

===Chinese===
我拍着这孩子的肩膀说：“危急关头，你表现得像个男子汉，我为你骄傲。能把一个年轻后生磨炼得这样坚强，几乎抵得过一座谷仓的价钱了。”

妻子走过来，一条胳膊搂住我的脖颈，说道：

“你挺得住，显得很有勇气，罗伯特，可我怕你心里正在泄气。偏偏在咱们刚刚起步的时候，遭这么大的损失！”她说着，眼里噙满了泪。

“是啊，”我答道，“这笔损失对我们确实沉重，也会带来极大的不便，可是本来还可能糟糕得多！大家都坐下，我来说件事。你们知道，我做生意的历练使我早就想到保险的重要，也熟悉最好的几家保险公司。温妮弗蕾德，产业刚一划到你名下，我就给全部房子保了险，保额几乎与它们的实价相等。干草和当时谷仓里的东西自然算是全毁了；可这笔损失我们承受得起，入冬以前就能大体弥补回来。我跟你们说老实话，我们毫无理由灰心。要不了多久，我们就会有一座比失去的那座更好的谷仓；只要筹划得当，用我领到的赔款就能盖起更好的来。所以，让我们感谢上帝保全了我们每个人，然后安安静静地睡觉去。”

风暴一过，孩子们都安静了下来；不多时，什么危险、什么损失，我们都在酣睡中忘得一干二净。

===Original===
In the morning the absence of the barn made a great gap in our familiar outlook, and brought many and serious thoughts; but with the light came renewed hopefulness. All the scene was flooded with glorious sunlight, and only the blackened ruins made the frightful storm of the previous evening seem possible. Nearly all the chickens came at Winnie’s call, looking draggled and forlorn indeed, but practically unharmed, and ready to resume their wonted cheerfulness after an hour in the sunshine. We fitted up for them the old coop in the orchard, and a part of the ancient and dilapidated barn which was to have been used for cornstalks only. The drenching rain had saved this and the adjoining shed from destruction, and now in our great emergency they proved useful indeed.

The trees around the site of the barn were blackened, and their foliage was burned to a crisp. Within the stone foundations the smoke from the still smouldering debris rose sluggishly.

I turned away from it all, saying: “Let us worry no more over that spilled milk. Fortunately the greater part of our crates and baskets were under the shed. Take the children, Merton, and pick over the raspberry patches carefully once more, while I go to work in the garden. That has been helped rather than injured by the storm, and, if we will take care of it, will give us plenty of food for winter. Work there will revive my spirits.”

The ground was too wet for the use of the hoe, but there was plenty of weeding to be done, while I answered the questions of neighbors who came to offer their sympathy. I also looked around to see what could be sold, feeling the need of securing every dollar possible. I found much that was hopeful and promising. The lima bean vines had covered the poles, and toward their base the pods were filling out. The ears on our early corn were fit to pull; the beets and onions had attained a good size; the early peas had given place to turnips, winter cabbages, and celery; there were plenty of green melons on the vines, and more cucumbers than we could use. The remaining pods on the first planting of bush beans were too mature for use, and I resolved to let them stand till sufficiently dry to be gathered and spread in the attic. All that we had planted had done, or was doing, fairly well, for the season had been moist enough to ensure a good growth. We had been using new potatoes since the first of the month, and now the vines were so yellow that all in the garden could be dug at once and sold. They would bring in some ready money, and I learned from my garden book that strap-leaved turnips, sown on the cleared spaces, would have time to mature.

After all, my strawberry beds gave me the most hope. There were hundreds of young plants already rooted, and still more lying loosely on the ground; so I spent the greater part of the morning in weeding these out and pressing the young plants on the ends of the runners into the moist soil, having learned that with such treatment they form roots and become established in a very few days.

===Chinese===
早晨，谷仓的空缺使我们看惯的景致豁开一道大口子，也引来许多沉重的思虑；但是随着天色放亮，希望又重新萌生。四下里都沐浴在灿烂的阳光中，唯有那片烧黑的废墟，令人相信前一晚那场可怕的风暴确乎发生过。温妮（Winnie）一唤，鸡几乎全都应声而来，模样实在拖沓狼狈、可怜巴巴，实际上却毫发无损，在太阳地里晒上个把钟头，便又准备欢叫如常了。我们把果园里的旧鸡棚收拾出来给它们安身，又腾出那座破败老谷仓的一部分——它本来只打算用来堆玉米秸秆。倾盆大雨救下了这座老仓和毗邻的棚屋，如今我们遭此大难，它们果然大有用处。

谷仓原址四周的树木熏得乌黑，叶子都烤成了焦炭。石基之内，尚未熄透的残骸冒着烟，懒洋洋地缓缓上升。

我转过身去，不再看这一切，说道：“泼出去的牛奶，哭也无益，咱们别再为它烦恼了。幸而我们的板条箱和篮子大半都放在棚子底下。默顿，你带孩子们把树莓地再仔细摘一遍，我到园子里去干活。那场风雨对菜园有助而无损，只要我们用心照管，冬天就不愁没有充足的吃食。在那儿干干活，我的精神会重新振作起来的。”

地里的土太湿，锄头还下不去，可是要除的草有的是，我便一边除草，一边回答前来慰问的邻居们的问话。我还四下察看有什么可以出卖，深觉必须把能到手的每一块钱都抓牢。我发现满眼都是叫人有盼头、有指望的东西。利马豆的藤蔓爬满了架子，靠根部的豆荚已经饱满起来；早玉米的穗子可以掰了；甜菜和洋葱都长得很有个头；早豌豆收过的地里换了茬，种上了芜菁、冬甘蓝和芹菜；藤上挂着许多青皮瓜，黄瓜多得我们吃不完。头一茬矮丛菜豆剩下的豆荚已经老得没法吃，我决定让它们留在棵上，等干得透了再收，摊开存进阁楼。我们种下的东西，样样长得——或正在长得——相当不坏，因为这个季节雨水充足，保住了好长势。打这个月初起，我们就在吃新土豆，如今秧子已经黄透，园里的土豆可以一次起完卖掉。它们能换回一点现钱，我还从园艺书上得知，在腾出来的空地上撒上带叶芜菁的种子，来得及长熟。

说到底，最给我指望的还是我的草莓地。已经有数以百计的幼苗扎了根，还有更多松松地趴在地上；于是一上午的大半时间，我都用来给它们拔草，把匍匐茎顶端的小苗按进湿润的泥土里——我早已知道，这样处置之后，它们不出几天就能生根站住。

===Original===
After dinner Mr. Jones appeared with his team and heavy plow, and we selected an acre of upland meadow where the sod was light and thin.

“This will give a fair growth of young corn leaves,” he said, “by the middle of September. By that time you’ll have a new barn up, I s’pose; and after you have cut and dried the corn, you can put a little of it into the mows in place of the hay. The greater part will keep better if stacked outdoors. A horse will thrive on such fodder almost as well as a cow, ’specially if ye cut it up and mix a little bran meal with it. We’ll sow the corn in drills a foot apart, and you can spread a little manure over the top of the ground after the seed is in. This ground is a trifle thin; a topdressin’ will help it ’mazin’ly.”

Merton succeeded in getting several crates of raspberries, but said that two or three more pickings would finish them. Since the time we had begun to go daily to the landing, we had sent the surplus of our vegetables to a village store, with the understanding that we would trade out the proceeds. We thus had accumulated a little balance in our favor, which we could draw against in groceries, etc.

On the evening of this day I took the crates to the landing, and found a purchaser for my garden potatoes, at a dollar a bushel. I also made arrangements at a summer boardinghouse, whose proprietor agreed to take the largest of our spring chickens, our sweet corn, tomatoes, and some other vegetables, as we had them to spare. Now that our income from raspberries was about to cease, it was essential to make the most of everything else on the place that would bring money, even if we had to deny ourselves. It would not do for us to say, “We can use this or that ourselves.” The question to be decided was, whether, if such a thing were sold, the proceeds would not go further toward our support than the things themselves. If this should be true of sweet corn, lima beans, and even the melons on which the children had set their hearts, we must be chary of consuming them ourselves. This I explained in such a way that all except Bobsey saw the wisdom of it, or, rather, the necessity. As yet, Bobsey’s tendencies were those of a consumer, and not of a producer or saver.

Rollins and one or two others came the next day, and with Bagley’s help the corn was soon in the ground.

===Chinese===
饭后，琼斯先生赶着牲口、带着重犁来了，我们选定一英亩高地草场，那里草皮又松又薄。

“到九月中旬，”他说，“这儿就能长出一片不坏的嫩玉米叶。到那时候，我猜你的新谷仓也立起来了；等你把玉米割倒晒干，可以往草棚里放一点，顶替干草。大部分还是堆在露天，保存得更久。这样的饲料喂马，几乎跟喂牛一样能让牲口上膘，只要切碎些，再拌上一点儿麸子面。咱们按一英尺行距条播玉米，种子下地以后，你可以往地面上撒点儿粪肥。这块地土头稍薄；面上追一层肥，管保好得出奇。”

默顿总算摘出了好几板条箱树莓，但他说，再摘两三遍也就摘完了。自从我们开始每天往梅兹维尔码头跑，就把吃不完的蔬菜送到村里一家铺子，讲定货款折成店里的货物取用。这样我们名下已积起一点结余，随时可以支取，购些杂货之类。

这天傍晚，我把板条箱送到码头，给园里的土豆找到了买主，价钱是一蒲式耳一块钱。我还在一家消夏包饭的公寓里谈妥了交易，店主答应，只要我们有富余，他就收下我们个头最大的春鸡、甜玉米、西红柿和一些别的蔬菜。眼见树莓的收入就要断了，务必把庄上其余能换钱的东西统统用到极致，哪怕自家省吃俭用。我们不能说“这个那个留着自己吃吧”。要拿定主意的问题是：一样东西卖掉之后，换来的钱是不是比东西本身更能维持全家的生计。倘若甜玉米、利马豆，甚至孩子们心心念念的瓜也都是这个道理，那我们就得节省着，不能自家享用。我把这层道理讲明白，除鲍勃西（Bobsey）而外，人人都看出这样做是明智的，或者不如说，是势在必行。鲍勃西眼下的禀性还是个消费者，还算不上生产者或攒家。

第二天，罗林斯和另外一两个人来了；有巴格利帮忙，玉米很快便下了地。

===Original===
Then I set Bagley to work with the cart spreading upon the soil the barnyard compost that had accumulated since spring. There was not enough to cover all the ground, but that I could not help. The large pile of compost that I had made near the poultry house door could not be spared for this purpose, since it was destined for my August planting of strawberries.

Perhaps I may as well explain about these compost heaps now as at any other time. I had watched their rapid growth with great satisfaction. Some may dislike such homely details, but since the success of the farm and garden depend on them I shall not pass them over, leaving the fastidious reader to do this for himself.

It will be remembered that I had sought to prepare myself for country life by much reading and study during the previous winter. I had early been impressed with the importance of obtaining and saving everything that would enrich the soil, and had been shown that increasing the manure pile was the surest way to add to one’s bank account. Therefore all rakings of leaves had been saved. At odd times Merton and I had gone down to the creek with the cart and dug a quantity of rich black earth from near its bank. One pile of this material had been placed near the stable door, and another at the entrance to the poultry-room in the basement of our vanished barn. The cleanings of the horse stable had been spread over a layer of this black soil. When the layer of such cleanings was about a foot thick, spread evenly, another layer of earth covered all from sun and rain. Thus I had secured a pile of compost which nearly top-dressed an acre for fodder corn.

In the poultry-room we managed in this fashion. A foot of raked-up leaves and rich earth was placed under the perches of the fowls. Every two or three weeks this layer was shovelled out and mixed thoroughly, and was replaced by a new layer. As a result I had, by the 1st of August, a large heap of fertilizer almost as good as guano, and much safer to use, for I had read that unless the latter was carefully managed it would burn vegetation like fire. I believe that this compost heap by the poultry-room window would give my young strawberry plantation a fine start, and, as has been shown, we were making great calculations on the future fruit.

I also resolved that the burning of the barn should add to our success in this direction. All the books said that there was nothing better for strawberries than wood ashes, and of these there was a great heap within the foundations of the destroyed building. At one time I proposed to shovel out these ashes and mix them with the compost, but fortunately I first consulted my book on fertilizers, and read there that this would not do at all—that they should be used separately.

===Chinese===
接着我派巴格利用小车把开春以来积攒下的厩肥堆肥撒到地里。肥料不够铺满整块地，可这也无可奈何。我在家禽屋门口堆的那一大堆堆肥，却万万不能挪作这个用场，因为它是留着给我八月里栽草莓用的。

关于这些堆肥堆，此刻交代一番，同在任何别的时候交代一样合宜。眼看着它们迅速堆高，我心里着实满意。或许有人不喜欢这类家常琐细，可是既然农庄和菜园的成败全系于此，我便不愿一笔带过，只好劳烦讲究的读者自己跳过去。

读者想必还记得，前一年冬天我曾埋头读书研究，为乡居生活做预备。我很早就深深记住了这样一点：凡是能肥田的东西都要设法收积起来；也有人指点过我，添高粪堆是往银行存折上添钱的顶稳当的路子。因此搂来的落叶我都存着。一有空闲，默顿和我就赶着车下到溪边，从穆德纳溪岸近旁挖回大量肥沃的黑土。这样的黑土一堆放在马厩门口，另一堆放在家禽室入口——那底层家禽室，就在我们那座如今已化为乌有的谷仓里。马厩里起出来的牲口粪，就铺在这种黑土上面。这一层粪匀匀实实铺到约莫一英尺厚，便再盖上一层土，遮住日晒雨淋。就这样，我攒起了一大堆堆肥，几乎够给一英亩饲料玉米地面上追一层肥。

家禽室里我们是这样料理的。在鸡的栖架下铺一英尺搂来的落叶和肥土。每隔两三个星期，这一层便铲出来充分拌匀，另换上新鲜的一层。结果到八月一日，我已有了一大堆肥料，几乎同鸟粪一样好，用起来却安全得多，因为我读到过，鸟粪若不细心调理，会像火一样把庄稼烧坏。我相信，家禽室窗口旁的这堆堆肥，会叫我那片幼小的草莓园有个很好的开端；而且如前文所示，我们对将来的果子正抱着莫大的指望。

我还打定主意，要让谷仓这场火也在这上面助我们一臂之力。所有的书都说，种草莓再没有比草木灰更好的东西，而烧塌的谷仓石基之内，正躺着好大一堆草木灰。有一阵子我打算把这些灰铲出来拌进堆肥，幸而先请教了我的肥料书，读到上面说这样做万万使不得——灰与堆肥必须分开使用。

## XX Raspberry Lessons / XX 树莓课业

===Original===
It must be remembered that I had spent all my leisure during the winter in reading and studying the problem of our country life. Therefore I knew that March was the best month for pruning trees, and I had gained a fairly correct idea how to do this work. Until within the last two or three years of his life, old Mr. Jamison had attended to this task quite thoroughly; and thus little was left for me beyond sawing away the boughs that had recently died, and cutting out the useless sprouts on the larger limbs. Before leaving the city I had provided myself with such tools as I was sure I should need; and finding a ladder under a shed, I attacked the trees vigorously. The wind had almost died out, and I knew I must make the most of all still days in this gusty month. After playing around for a time, Winnie and Bobsey concluded that gathering and piling up my prunings would be as good fun as anything else; and so I had helpers again.

By the middle of the afternoon Mr. Jones appeared, and I was glad to see him, for there were some kinds of work about which I wanted his advice. At one end of the garden were several rows of blackcap raspberry bushes, which had grown into an awful snarl. The old canes that had borne fruit the previous season were still standing, ragged and unsightly; the new stalks that would bear the coming season sprawled in every direction; and I had found that many tips of the branches had grown fast in the ground. I took my neighbor to see this briery wilderness, and asked his advice.

===Chinese===
要说明的是，整个冬天，我一有闲暇便用来读书，研究我们乡下生计这个难题。因此我知道，三月是修剪果树的最好月份，对这门活计该怎么做，心里也已有相当准确的底稿。老贾米森先生（Mr. Jamison）直到去世前两三年，都一直把这件事料理得十分周到，所以留给我做的，无非是锯掉近来枯死的枝子，剔去粗枝上无用的萌条。离开城里之前，我已备齐了自己盘算必定用得着的工具；又在棚子底下找到一架梯子，便劲头十足地朝果树动起手来。风已经差不多停息，我知道，在这个多风的月份里，每一个无风的日子都必须充分利用。温妮（Winnie）和鲍勃西（Bobsey）先在一旁疯玩了一阵，随后断定，把我剪下的枝条捡拢来堆成一堆，同别的玩耍一样有趣，于是我又有了帮手。

到午后过半时分，琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）来了，我见他很高兴，因为有几种活计正想向他讨教。园子一头长着几行黑帽树莓（blackcap raspberry），已经纠缠成一大团乱麻。上一季结过果的老枝还立在那儿，蓬乱不雅观；要应接下来一季的新枝则四下里横生蔓长；我还发现许多枝梢已经在土里扎牢了根。我领着这位邻人去看这一片荆棘丛生的荒地，向他讨主意。

===Original===
“Have you got a pair of pruning nippers?” he asked.

Before going to the house to get them, I blew a shrill whistle to summon Merton, for I wished him also to hear all that Mr. Jones might say. I carried a little metallic whistle one blast on which was for Merton, two for Winnie, and three for Bobsey. When they heard this call they were to come as fast as their feet could carry them.

Taking the nippers, Mr. Jones snipped off from one third to one half the length of the branches from one of the bushes and cut out the old dead cane.

===Chinese===
“你有一副修枝剪吗？”他问。

进屋去取剪子之前，我先吹了一声尖利的哨子召唤默顿（Merton），因为琼斯先生要说的话，我也想让他一字不落地听到。我随身带着一只小金属哨：吹一声是叫默顿，两声是叫温妮，三声是叫鲍勃西；他们听见召唤，就得撒开腿尽快赶来。

琼斯先生接过修枝剪，从一丛树上把枝条剪短三分之一到二分之一，又把枯死的老枝剔除掉。

===Original===
“I raise these berries myself for home use,” he said; “and I can tell you they go nice with milk for a July supper. You see, after taking off so much from these long branches the canes stand straight up, and will be self-supporting, no matter how many berries they bear; but here and there’s a bush that has grown slantwise, or is broken off. Now, if I was you, I’d take a crowbar ’n’ make a hole ’longside these weakly and slantin’ fellers, put in a stake, and tie ’em up strong. Then, soon as the frost yields, if you’ll get out the grass and weeds that’s started among ’em, you’ll have a dozen bushel or more of marketable berries from this ’ere wilderness, as you call it. Give Merton a pair of old gloves, and he can do most of the job. Every tip that’s fast in the ground is a new plant. If you want to set out another patch, I’ll show you how later on.”

“I think I know pretty nearly how to do that.”

“Yes, yes, I know. Books are a help, I s’pose, but after you’ve seen one plant set out right, you’ll know more than if you’d ’a’ read a month.”

===Chinese===
“这些莓子我自己也种，是自家吃的，”他说；“我告诉你，七月里晚间吃饭时配上牛奶，那滋味才叫好。你瞧，这些长枝子剪掉这么多以后，枝条便直立挺着，不管挂多少果子都能自己撑得住；只是这儿那儿总有一两丛长歪了的，或是折断了的。这么着吧，换了我，就拿一根撬棍在这些瘦弱歪斜的家伙旁边凿个洞，打进一根木桩，把它们牢牢捆上。然后，等霜一化透，你再把它们中间长起来的草和杂草清出去，你管它叫荒地的这片地方，就能出十二蒲式耳还多能上市的莓子。给默顿一副旧手套，这活计的大半他都能干。凡是梢头在土里扎牢的，便是一株新苗。你要是想再开辟一片地，往后我再教你怎么办。”

“我想这一套我差不多摸着门道了。”

“对，对，我知道。书本嘛，我想也帮得上点忙，可你只要亲眼看过一株苗栽得端正，就比你念上一个月书懂得还多。”

===Original===
“Well, now that you’re here, Mr. Jones, I’m going to make the most of you. How about those other raspberries off to the southeast of the house?”

“Those are red ones. Let’s take a look at ’em.”

Having reached the patch, we found almost as bad a tangle as in the blackcap patch, except that the canes were more upright in their growth and less full of spines or briers.

===Chinese===
“那好，琼斯先生，既然您来了，我就要把您的本事用个够。房子东南边远处那些别的树莓，又该怎么办？”

“那些是红树莓。咱们去瞧瞧。”

到了那片地里，我们发现纠缠杂乱几乎不亚于黑帽树莓那边，只是枝条长得更直立，刺和荆棘也少些。

===Original===
“It’s plain enough,” continued Mr. Jones, “that old man Jamison was too poorly to take much care of things last year. You see, these red raspberries grow different from those black ones yonder. Those increase by the tips of the branches takin’ root; these by suckers. All these young shoots comin’ up between the rows are suckers, and they ought to be dug out. As I said before, you can set them out somewhere else if you want to. Dig ’em up, you know; make a trench in some out-of-the-way place, and bury the roots till you want ’em. Like enough the neighbors will buy some if they know you have ’em to spare. Only be sure to cut these long canes back to within six inches of the ground.”

“Yes,” I said, “that’s all just as I have read in the books.”

“So much the better for the books, then. I haven’t lived in this fruit-growin’ region all my life without gettin’ some ideas as to what’s what. I give my mind to farmin’; but Jamison and I were great cronies, and I used to be over here every day or two, and so it’s natural to keep comin’.”

===Chinese===
“看得很明白，”琼斯先生接着说，“贾米森老头去年病得太厉害，顾不上照管这些营生了。你瞧，这些红树莓的长法跟那边那些黑的不一样：那些靠枝梢扎土生根来繁衍，这些靠根出条。行子中间冒出来的这些嫩条全是根出条，都该挖掉。我方才说过，你愿意的话，可以把它们移栽到别处去。要知道，把它们挖起来，在什么僻静角落开一道沟，把根埋在里面存着，等要用的时候再取。邻里们要是知道你有多余的，多半会来买些。只是千万记住，这些长枝要截短到离地六英寸以内。”

“是，”我说，“这些跟我书上读到的完全一样。”

“那书本就更值了。我在这块种果树的乡野住了一辈子，总不曾白住，好歹也攒下几分眼力，分得清什么是什么。我是一心扑在农活上的；不过贾米森跟我是顶要好的老朋友，我从前隔一天两天就过来一趟，如今照旧常来，也是自然而然的事。”

===Original===
“That’s my good luck.”

“Well, p’raps it’ll turn out so. Now Merton’s just the right age to help you in all this work. Jamison, you see, grew these raspberries in a continuous bushy row; that is, say, three good strong canes every eighteen inches apart in the row, and the rows five feet apart, so he could run a horse cultivator between. Are you catchin’ on, Merton?”

“Yes, sir,” said the boy, with much interest.

===Chinese===
“这是我的好运气。”

“嗯，兴许真是这样。眼下默顿的年纪正合适，这些活计样样都能帮你做。你要明白，贾米森把这些树莓培成一垄接连不断的灌木行；就是说，比方说，行内每隔十八英寸留三根粗壮结实的枝条，行距五英尺，这样他就能在行间过一匹马拉的中耕机。听明白了没有，默顿？”

“听明白了，先生，”少年答道，兴致很浓。

===Original===
“Well, all these suckers and extra plants that are swampin’ the ground are just as bad as weeds. Dig ’em all out, only don’t disturb the roots of the bearin’ canes you leave in the rows much.”

“How about trimming these?” I asked.

“Well, that depends. If you want early fruit, you’ll let ’em stand as they be; if you want big berries, you’ll cut ’em back one third. Let me see. Here’s five rows of Highland Hardy; miserable poor-tastin’ kind; but they come so early that they often pay the best. Let them stand with their whole length of cane, and if you can scatter a good topdressin’ of fine manure scraped up from the barnyard, you’ll make the berries larger. Those other rows of Cuthbert, Reliance, and Turner, cut back the canes one third, and you’ll get a great deal more fruit than if you left more wood on ’em. Cuttin’ back’ll make the berries big; and so they’ll bring as much, p’raps, as if they were early.”

===Chinese===
“喏，所有这些挤占地面的根出条和多余的苗，跟杂草一样碍事。统统挖掉，只是别太惊动你留在行里的那些结果枝条的根。”

“那么这些枝子该怎么修剪？”我问。

“这个要看情形。你要果子出得早，就由它们照现在的样子长着；你要莓子结得大，就把枝子剪掉三分之一。我想想。这儿有五行高地耐寒（Highland Hardy）；味道差劲得很；可是成熟得那么早，倒常常最来钱。让它们保留整根的枝条，你要是能从谷仓场院刮拢些细粪肥，好好铺上一层作追肥，莓子就能长得大些。其余那几行卡思伯特（Cuthbert）、雷兰斯（Reliance）和特纳（Turner），把枝条剪去三分之一，结的果子会比多留枝干多得多。剪短了莓子就大，这样价钱兴许就跟早熟的卖得一样好了。”

===Original===
“Well, Merton, this all accords with what I’ve read, only Mr. Jones makes it much clearer. I think we know how to go to work now, and surely there’s plenty to do.”

“Yes, indeed,” resumed Mr. Jones; “and you’ll soon find the work crowdin’ you. Now come to the big raspberry patch back of the barn, the patch where the canes are all laid down, as I told you. These are Hudson River Antwerps. Most people have gone out of ’em, but Jamison held on, and he was makin’ money on ’em. So may you. They are what we call tender, you see, and in November they must be bent down close to the ground and covered with earth, or else every cane would be dead from frost by spring. About the first week in April, if the weather’s mild, you must uncover ’em, and tie ’em to stakes durin’ the month.”

“Now, Mr. Jones, one other good turn and we won’t bother you any more today. All the front of the house is covered by two big grapevines that have not been trimmed, and there are a great many other vines on the place. I’ve read and read on the subject, but I declare I’m afraid to touch them.”

===Chinese===
“喏，默顿，这一切都跟我读到的相符，只是琼斯先生讲得更明白得多。我想咱们如今知道该怎么下手了，而且活计肯定少不了。”

“正是，少不了，”琼斯先生接着说；“你很快就会发觉活计追着人来了。现在到谷仓后头那大片树莓地去吧，就是我跟你讲过的那片、枝条全放倒了的。这些是哈德逊安特卫普树莓（Hudson River Antwerps）。种的人多半已经退了出去，贾米森却守着不放，而且靠它们挣了钱。你也一样挣得着。这一种，你瞧，就是我们所说的娇嫩货色：十一月里必须把枝条压弯贴地、培土盖住，不然开春一根也活不成。大约到四月头一个星期，天气若是暖和，就得给它们揭去覆土，这个月里再把枝条捆上木桩。”

“琼斯先生，如今再劳驾您一件好事，今天就不多打扰了。房子正面全叫两棵从没修剪过的大葡萄藤爬满了，这地方另有许多葡萄藤。这件事我读了又读，可我老实说，实在不敢下手去碰。”

===Original===
“Now, you’re beyond my depth. I’ve got a lot of vines home, and I trim ’em in my rough way, but I know I ain’t scientific, and we have pretty poor, scraggly bunches. They taste just as good, though, and I don’t raise any to sell. There’s a clever man down near the landin’ who has a big vineyard, and he’s trimmed it as your vines ought to have been long ago. I’d advise you to go and see him, and he can show you all the latest wrinkles in prunin’. Now, I’ll tell you what I come for, in the first place. You’ll remember that I said there’d be a vandoo tomorrow. I’ve been over and looked at the stock offered. There’s a lot of chickens, as I told you; a likely-looking cow with a calf at her side; a fairish and quiet old horse that ought to go cheap, but he’d answer well the first year. Do you think you’ll get more’n one horse to start with?”

“No; you said I could hire such heavy plowing as was needed at a moderate sum, and I think we can get along with one horse for a time. My plan is to go slow, and, I hope, sure.”

“That’s the best way, only it ain’t common. I’ll be around in the mornin’ for you and such of the children as you’ll take.”

===Chinese===
“喏，这可超出我的深浅了。我家里也有一大片葡萄藤，我也照自己那套粗率办法修剪，可我知道自己不讲科学，咱们家的葡萄串便长得挺寒碜、七零八落。不过味道一样好，我也不种来卖。码头附近住着一位能干人，有一座大葡萄园，修剪得正是你家这些葡萄藤早该有的样子。我劝你去看看他，他能把修剪上的新鲜门道一样不落指给你看。好了，我索性告诉你我今天头一遭是为什么来的。你还记得我说过，明天有一场拍卖会。我已经去看过出售的货色了。有一大批鸡，我跟你说过；一头模样周正的奶牛，身边还带着一头牛犊；一匹还算体面、性子安稳的老马，本该卖得便宜，头一年使唤起来也还顶用。你看你起头会不会买不止一匹马？”

“不；你说过，要用得着的那种重活犁地，可以花不多的钱雇人来犁，我想暂时一匹马也尽够了。我的打算是慢慢来，但愿也走得稳。”

“这是最好的办法，只是这么做的人不多。明天早上我来约你，还有你愿意带的哪个孩子。”

===Original===
“On one condition, Mr. Jones. You must let me pay you for your time and trouble. Unless you’ll do this in giving me my start, I’ll have to paddle my own canoe, even if I sink it.”

“Oh, I’ve no grudge against an honest penny turned in any way that comes handy. You and I can keep square as we go along. You can give me what you think is right, and if I ain’t satisfied, I’ll say so.”

I soon learned that my neighbor had no foolish sensitiveness. I could pay him what I thought the value of his services, and he pocketed the money without a word. Of course, I could not pay him what his advice was really worth, for his hard common sense stood me in good stead in many ways.

===Chinese===
“有一个条件，琼斯先生。您得让我付给您时间和辛劳的酬劳。在帮我起步的这件事上，您要是不肯收钱，我就只好自己撑自己的船了——哪怕把它撑沉了也认了。”

“噢，一个规规矩矩的便士，只要来路顺手，挣到谁兜里我都没意见。你我尽可以边走边把账算清。你觉着该给多少就给多少，我要是不满意，自会开口。”

我不久便看出，我这位邻人没有那种可笑的过分拘谨。我按自己估量的他这番效劳的价值付他钱，他一言不发就把钱收进兜里。当然，他这番指点真正的价值，我是付不起的，因为他那套实打实的常识，在许多方面都让我大得其益。

## XIII Rescued and at Home / XIII 获救回家

===Original===
All that the poets from the beginning of time have written about light could not express my joy as I saw that glimmer approaching on the left. Before it appeared I had been awed by the tempest, benumbed with cold, shivering in my wet clothes, and a prey to many terrible fears and surmises; but now I cried, "Cheer up; here comes a light."

Then in my gladness I shouted the greeting that met Mr. Jones everywhere, "How *are you*, **John**?"

A great guffaw of laughter mingled with the howl of the storm, and my neighbor's voice followed from the obscurity: "That's famous—keepin' up your courage like a soldier."

"Oh, I won't brag about keeping up my courage."

===Chinese===
自有诗人以来，历代诗人写光的一切诗句，也道不尽我望见左边那点微光渐渐挨近时心里的欢喜。它出现之前，暴风雨叫我胆战心惊，湿衣裹身，冻得浑身发麻，种种可怕的忧惧和揣测轮番折磨着我；此刻我却喊道：“打起精神；瞧，灯来了。”

接着，我满心欢喜，用那路人人见了琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）都要招呼一句的话高声喊道：“*你好*哇，**约翰**？”

一阵呵呵的大笑混进风暴的呼啸里，接着黑暗深处传来我这位邻居的声音：“好极了——像个士兵似的，胆气一直没垮。”

“嗐，胆气撑没撑住，我可不敢自夸。”

===Original===
"Guess you didn't know what had become of me?"

"You're right and we didn't know what was to become of us. Now aren't we nearly home? For we are all half frozen."

"Just let me spy a bit with the lantern, and I'll soon tell you everything." He bobbed back and forth for a moment or two like a will-o'-the-wisp. "Now turn sharp to the left, and follow the light."

A great hope sprung up in my heart, and I hushed Winnie's and Bobsey's crying by saying, "Listen, and you'll soon hear some good news."

===Chinese===
“猜猜看，你们不知道我怎么样了吧？”

“你说得对，我们不光不知道你怎么样，连自己会落到什么地步都不知道。这会儿咱们是不是快到家了？我们全冻得半僵了。”

“让我提着灯笼瞅一瞅，马上就什么都告诉你。”他提着灯像一星磷火似的，前前后后晃动了一会儿。“好，往左急转，跟着灯光走。”

我心里腾起一股大大的希望，一面哄住温妮（Winnie）和鲍勃西（Bobsey）的哭声，一面说：“听着，好消息马上就来了。”

===Original===
Our wheels crunched through the deep snow for a few moments, and soon I saw a ruddy light shining from the window of a dwelling, and then Mr. Jones shouted, "Whoa! 'Light down, neighbors; you're at your own door."

There was a chorus of delighted cries. Merton half tumbled over me in his eagerness to get down. A door opened, and out poured a cheerful glow. Oh the delicious sense of safety and warmth given by it already!

I seized Mousie, floundered through the snow up to my knees, and placed her in a big rocking chair. Mr. Jones followed with Winnie, and Merton came in with Bobsey on his back. The little fellow was under such headway in crying that he couldn't stop at once, although his tears were rapidly giving place to laughter. I rushed back and carried in my wife, and then said, in a voice a little unsteady from deep feeling, "Welcome home, one and all."

Never did the word mean more to a half-frozen and badly frightened family. At first safety, warmth, and comfort were the uppermost in our thoughts, but as wraps were taken off, and my wife and children thawed out, eager-eyed curiosity began to make explorations. Taking Mousie on my lap, and chafing her hands, I answered questions and enjoyed to the full the exclamations of pleasure.

===Chinese===
车轮在深雪里咯吱咯吱轧了一阵，不多时我便看见一座住宅的窗口透出红彤彤的灯光，琼斯先生随即吆喝道：“吁！下吧，街坊们；到你们自己家门口啦。”

一片欢叫响成一片。默顿（Merton）急着下车，差点儿从我身上翻过去。门开了，涌出一股暖融融的光亮。啊，单是这光叫人提前尝到的那份安全与温暖的甜美滋味，就已经够醉人的了！

我一把抱起小耗子（Mousie），在深及膝盖的雪里深一脚浅一脚地走过去，把她安放在一把大摇椅里。琼斯先生抱着温妮跟上来，默顿背着鲍勃西也进了屋。小家伙哭得正起劲，一时收不住，虽然眼泪很快就让笑声顶了回去。我又赶忙折回去，把妻子抱进屋，然后带着因百感交集而微微发颤的声音说：“欢迎回家，欢迎大伙儿。”

对一家冻得半僵、又受了大惊吓的人来说，这句话还从来没有这么受用过。起先，我们满脑子只有安全、温暖、舒服；可等裹着的衣物脱下来，妻子和孩子们缓过暖来，眼巴巴的好奇心便开始四处探查了。我把小耗子抱在膝上，搓着她的手，一答一应，尽情受用那一声声欢喜的惊叹。

===Original===
Mr. Jones lingered for a few moments, then gave one of his big guffaws by way of preface, and said: "Well, you do look as if you was at home and meant to stay. This 'ere scene kinder makes me homesick; so I'll say good night, and I'll be over in the mornin'. There's some lunch on the table that my wife fixed up for you. I must go, for I hear John junior hollerin' for me."

His only response to our profuse thanks was another laugh, which the wind swept away.

"Who is John junior?" asked Merton.

"Mr. Jones's son, a boy of about your age. He was here waiting for us, and keeping the fire up. When we arrived he came out and took the horses, and so you didn't see him. He'll make a good playmate for you. To use his father's own words, 'He's a fairish boy as boys go,' and that from John Jones means that he's a good fellow."

===Chinese===
琼斯先生又逗留了片刻，先呵呵来了一声他的大笑权作开场白，然后说：“呃，你们这模样，瞧着真像安了家、打算长住了。这景致叫我都有点儿想家了；那就晚安吧，明儿一早我再过来。桌上备了点吃食，是我家里给你们弄的。我得走了，听见小琼斯（John junior）在喊我呢。”

对我们千恩万谢，他唯一的回答又是一阵大笑，风一刮就散了。

“小琼斯是谁？”默顿问。

“琼斯先生的儿子，一个跟你年纪相仿的男孩。他刚才在这儿等我们，还一直看着火。我们到的时候，他出来把马牵走了，所以你们没见着。他会是你的好玩伴。用他父亲自己的话说，‘照男孩子里头数，他还算不赖’；这话出自约翰·琼斯（John Jones）之口，就是说这孩子顶好。”

===Original===
Oh, what a happy group we were, as we gathered around the great, open fire, on which I piled more wood!

"Do you wish to go and look around a little?" I asked my wife.

"No," she replied, leaning back in her rocking chair: "let me take this in first. O Robert, I have such a sense of rest, quiet, comfort, and hominess that I just want to sit still and enjoy it all. The howling of the storm only makes this place seem more like a refuge, and I'd rather hear it than the Daggetts tramping overhead and the Ricketts children crying downstairs. Oh, isn't it nice to be by ourselves in this quaint old room? Turn the lamp down, Robert, so we can see the firelight flicker over everything. Isn't it splendid?—just like a picture in a book."

"No picture in a book, Winifred—no artist could paint a picture that would have the charm of this one for me," I replied, leaning my elbow on the end of the mantelpiece, and looking fondly down on the little group. My wife's face looked girlish in the ruddy light. Mousie gazed into the fire with unspeakable content, and declared she was "too happy to think of taking cold." Winnie and Bobsey were sitting, Turk-fashion, on the floor, their eyelids drooping. The long cold ride had quenched even their spirit, for after running around for a few moments they began to yield to drowsiness. Merton, with a boy's appetite, was casting wistful glances at the lunch on the table, the chief feature of which was a roast chicken.

===Chinese===
啊，我们围着那膛敞开的大火聚坐在一起，我又往火上添了些木柴——我们是多快活的一群人啊！

“想不想先去四下里看看？”我问妻子。

“不，”她靠在摇椅背上答道：“先让我把这滋味品个够。哦，罗伯特（Robert），我浑身都是安歇、清静、舒适、有家的感觉，只想这么坐着不动，好好受用这一切。暴风雨的呼啸，只叫这地方越发像个避难的港湾；我宁可听它，也不愿再听达格茨（Daggett）一家在头顶上咚咚地走来走去，还有里克茨（Rickett）家的孩子们在楼下哭。哦，就这么一家人守着这间古雅的老屋子，多好？把灯拧暗些，罗伯特，好看火光在每样东西上跳动。多美啊？——简直像书里的一幅画。”

“书里可没有这样的画，温妮弗蕾德（Winifred）——再没有哪个画家，能画出一幅在我眼里有这么大魅力的画来，”我应声道，胳膊肘支在壁炉台的一头，满眼怜爱地望着这一小群人。红彤彤的火光里，妻子的脸显得像个少女。小耗子凝望着炉火，那份心满意足无以言表，还宣称她“快活得都顾不上怕着凉了”。温妮和鲍勃西像小土耳其人似的盘腿坐在地板上，眼皮直往下耷拉。这趟又冷又长的车程把他们连精神头都磨光了，乱跑了一小阵之后，两人便渐渐瞌睡起来。默顿带着男孩的大胃口，眼馋地直瞟桌上那些吃食，吃食的主打是一只烤鸡。

===Original===
There seemed to be no occasion for haste. I wished to let the picture sink deep into my heart. At last my wife sprang up and said:—

"I've been sentimental long enough. You're not of much account in the house, Robert"—with one of her saucy looks—"and I must see to things, or Winnie and Bobsey will be asleep on the floor. I feel as if I could sit here till morning, but I'll come back after the children are in bed. Come, show me my home, or at least enough of it to let me see where we are to sleep."

"We shall have to camp again tonight. Mrs. Jones has made up the one bed left in the house, and you and Mousie shall have that. We'll fix Winnie and Bobsey on the lounge; and, youngsters, you can sleep in your clothes, just as soldiers do on the ground. Merton and I will doze in these chairs before the fire. Tomorrow night we can all be very comfortable."

I took the lamp and led the way—my wife, Mousie, and Merton following—first across a little hall, from which one stairway led to the upper chambers and another to the cellar. Opening a door opposite the living room, I showed Winifred her parlor. Cozy and comfortable it looked, even now, through Mr. and Mrs. Jones's kind offices. A Morning Glory stove gave out abundant warmth and a rich light which blended genially with the red colors of the carpet.

===Chinese===
看来用不着着急。我巴不得把这幅画深深印进心里。末了，妻子终于一跃而起，说道：

“我这儿多愁善感得也够久了。家务上你可派不上多少用场，罗伯特”——她俏皮地瞟了他一眼——“我得张罗去了，不然温妮和鲍勃西要在地板上睡着啦。我觉得自己能在这儿坐到天亮，可等孩子们上了床我就回来。来，带我看看我的家，至少也看个大概，好让我知道咱们睡在哪儿。”

“今晚咱们还得再露营一回。琼斯太太把屋里剩下那张唯一的床铺好了，给你和小耗子睡。温妮和鲍勃西安置在长榻上；小家伙们，你们就穿着衣裳睡，跟士兵们在地上睡一个样。默顿和我就窝在炉前这几把椅子里打盹。明天夜里，咱们就都能舒舒服服的了。”

我提起灯在前面引路——妻子、小耗子和默顿跟在后面——先穿过一个小门厅，从那儿一道楼梯通向楼上的卧室，另一道通向地窖。我推开正对起居室的一扇门，让温妮弗蕾德看她的客厅。多亏琼斯先生和太太的一番好意，这客厅此刻瞧着就已经又舒适又熨帖。一只牵牛花牌（Morning Glory）炉子散发出充足的暖意和浓郁的亮光，那光与地毯的红色亲切地融成一片。

===Original===
"Oh, how pretty I can make this room look!" exclaimed my wife.

"Of course you can: you've only to enter it."

"You hurt your head when you fell out of the wagon, Robert, and are a little daft. There's no place to sleep here."

"Come to the room over this, warmed by a pipe from this stove."

===Chinese===
“哦，我能把这屋子收拾得多好看哪！”妻子大声说。

“那当然：你人一进去就行了。”

“罗伯特，你从马车上摔下来的时候磕了脑袋，有点儿说胡话了。这儿可没地方睡觉。”

“到这间上头那间去，有管子从这炉子通上去，那儿是暖的。”

===Original===
"Ah, this is capital," she cried, looking around an apartment which Mrs. Jones had made comfortable. "Wasn't I wise when I decided to come home? It's just as warm as toast. Now let the wind blow—Why, I don't hear it any more."

"No, the gale has blown itself out. Finding that we had escaped, it got discouraged and gave up. Connected with this room is another for Mousie and Winnie. By leaving the door open much of the time it will be warm enough for them. So you see this end of the house can be heated with but little trouble and expense. The open fire in the living room is a luxury that we can afford, since there is plenty of wood on the place. On the other side of the hall there is a room for Merton. Now do me a favor: don't look, or talk, or think, any more tonight. It has been a long, hard day. Indeed"—looking at my watch—"it is already tomorrow morning, and you know how much we shall have to do. Let us go back and get a little supper, and then take all the rest we can."

Winifred yielded, and Bobsey and Winnie waked up for a time at the word "supper." Then we knelt around our hearth, and made it an altar to God, for I wished the children never to forget our need of His fatherly care and help.

===Chinese===
“啊，这屋真是好极了，”她环顾着琼斯太太拾掇得舒舒服服的房间，大声说。“我当初决意回家来，不是挺明智的吗？这屋暖烘烘的，跟烤热的面包片似的。现在风爱怎么刮就怎么刮——咦，我怎么一点儿也听不见了。”

“是啊，大风刮到头了。它一看咱们逃出来了，就泄了气，罢手了。跟这屋相通的还有一间，给小耗子和温妮。门大多时候开着，她们那边也就够暖和了。你瞧，房子的这一头花不了多少事、费不了多少钱就能取暖。起居室里那膛敞火，是咱们享受得起的一份奢侈，庄子上木柴有的是。门厅那一头还有一间是默顿的。好了，依我一回：今晚别再看了，也别再说了、想了。这一天又长又累。可不是”——我看了看表——“已经是明天早上了，你知道咱们有多少事要干。咱们回去吃点简单的夜宵，然后能歇多少歇多少。”

温妮弗蕾德依了。一听“夜宵”两个字，鲍勃西和温妮也醒了一会儿。随后我们围着自家的炉床跪下，把它当作献给上帝的祭坛，因为我要孩子们永远不忘，我们需要天父般的眷顾和扶助。

===Original===
"I will now take the children upstairs and put them to bed, and then come back, for I can not leave this wood fire just yet," remarked my wife.

I burst out laughing and said, "You have never been at home until this night, when you are camping in an old house you never saw before, and I can prove it by one question—When have you taken the children *upstairs* to bed before?"

"Why—why—never."

"Of course you haven't—city flats all your life. But your nature is not perverted. In natural homes for generations mothers have taken their children upstairs to bed, and, forgetting the habit of your life, you speak according to the inherited instinct of the mother heart."

===Chinese===
“我现在就领孩子们上楼睡去，然后再回来，我这会儿还舍不得离开这炉子木火呢，”妻子说。

我放声大笑，说：“直到今夜——你在一座从没见过的老房子里露营的今夜——你才算真正到过家。这一点，我只消一个问题就能证明——从前你可曾领孩子们*上楼*去睡觉？”

“这——这——从来没有。”

“你当然没有——你一辈子住的都是城里的公寓。可你的天性并没有变坏。一代又一代的天然人家里，母亲们都是领着孩子上楼去睡的；你一时忘了自己过惯的日子，说话便依着母亲心里世代相传的天性来了。”

===Original===
"O Robert, you have so many finespun theories! Yet it is a little queer. It seemed just as natural for me to say upstairs as—"

"As it was for your mother, grandmother, and great-grandmother."

"Very well. We are in such an old house that I suppose I shall begin to look and act like my great-grandmother. But no more theories tonight—nothing but rest and the wood fire."

===Chinese===
“哦，罗伯特，你那些细究起来的空论可真多！不过这事也确实有点儿奇怪。我说‘上楼’，只觉得再自然不过，就好像——”

“——就好像你母亲、外祖母、曾外祖母当年一样。”

“好啊。这房子这么老，只怕过不了多久，我的一颦一笑、一举一动都要像我的曾祖母了。不过今晚可再不许谈理论了——除了歇息和这炉子木火，什么都不要。”

===Original===
She soon joined me at the hearth again. Merton meanwhile had stretched himself on the rag carpet, with his overcoat for a pillow, and was in dreamless sleep. My wife's eyes were full of languor. She did not sit down, but stood beside me for a moment. Then, laying her head on my shoulder, she said, softly, "I haven't brains enough for theories and such things, but I will try to make you all happy here."

"Dear little wife!" I laughed; "when has woman hit upon a higher or better wisdom than that of making all happy in her own home? and you half asleep, too."

"Then I'll bid you good night at once, before I say something awfully stupid."

===Chinese===
她不多时就回到炉边，来到我身旁。这工夫，默顿已经在旧布地毯上直挺挺躺下，拿大衣当枕头，睡得无梦无扰。我妻子满眼倦意。她没有坐下，只在我身边站了一会儿，然后把头靠在我肩上，轻声说：“理论啊什么的，我脑瓜不够使；可我会想法子，叫你们大家在这儿过得快活。”

“亲爱的小妻子！”我笑道；“女人几时发现过比让自家人都在家里过得幸福更高明、更美好的智慧来？何况你还半睡半醒呢。”

“那我就立刻跟你道晚安，趁我还没说出什么傻得要命的话来。”

===Original===
Soon the old house was quiet. The wind had utterly ceased. I opened the door a moment, and looked on the white, still world without. The stars glittered frostily through the rifts in the clouds. Schunemunk Mountain was a shadow along the western horizon, and the eastern highlands banked up and blended with the clouds. Nature has its restless moods, its storms and passions, like human life; but there are times of tranquillity and peace, even in March. How different was this scene from the aspect of our city street when I had taken my farewell look at a late hour the previous night! No grand sweeping outlines there, no deep quiet and peace, soothing and at the same time uplifting the mind. Even at midnight there is an uneasy fretting in city life—someone not at rest, and disturbing the repose of others.

I stole silently through the house. Here, too, all seemed in accord with nature. The life of a good old man had quietly ceased in this home; new, hopeful life was beginning. Evil is everywhere in the world, but it seemed to me that we had as safe a nook as could be found.

===Chinese===
不多时，老房子里静了下来。风已经完全停了。我开门站了片刻，望着外面雪白而安宁的世界。星星从云隙里闪着清寒的光。舒内芒克山（Schunemunk Mountain）在西边地平线上横着一道暗影，东边的高地层层隆起，与云层交融成一片。大自然也像人生一样，有它躁动不安的脾气，有它的风暴与激情；可就在三月，也有恬静安宁的时刻。这景象，跟我前夜深夜向我们的城市街道投去告别一瞥时的那副模样，是多么不同啊！那里没有这般廓然开阔的雄浑轮廓，没有这般深沉的静谧与安宁，能抚慰人心，同时又令人心胸昂扬。即使在午夜，城市生活里也有一缕不安的烦躁——总有人睡不安稳，搅扰着别人的安眠。

我蹑手蹑脚地轻轻穿过屋子。这里的一切，也都仿佛与自然相契。一位善良老人的生命已在这户人家安然终了；新的、有希望的生活正在开始。世上处处有罪恶，可在我看，我们已经安身在一处再稳妥不过的角落里了。

## X Scenes on the Wharf / X 码头上的光景

===Original===
The last night in the city flat was in truth like camping out, the fatigues of the day brought us sound sleep, and we looked and felt like emigrants. But in the morning we rose with the dawn, from our shakedowns on the floor, to begin eagerly and hopefully our final preparations for departure. In response to my letters John Jones had promised to meet us at the Maizeville Landing with his strong covered rockaway, and to have a fire in the old farmhouse. Load after load was despatched to the boat, for I preferred to deal with one trusty truckman. When all had been taken away, we said goodbye to our neighbors and took the horsecar to the boat, making our quiet exit in the least costly way. I knew the boat would be warm and comfortable, and proposed that we should eat our lunch there.

The prospect, however, of seeing the wharves, the boats, and the river destroyed even the children’s appetites. We soon reached the crowded dock. The great steamer appeared to be a part of it, lying along its length with several gangways, over which boxes, barrels, and packages were being hustled on board with perpetual din. The younger children were a little awed at first by the noise and apparent confusion. Mousie kept close to my side, and even Bobsey clung to his mother’s hand. The extended upper cabin had staterooms opening along its sides, and was as comfortable as a floating parlor with its arm and rocking chairs. Here, not far from the great heater, I established our headquarters. I made the children locate the spot carefully, and said: “From this point we’ll make excursions. In the first place, Merton, you come with me and see that all our household effects are together and in good order. You must learn to travel and look after things like a man.”

We spent a little time in arranging our goods so that they would be safer and more compact. Then we went to the captain and laughingly told him we were emigrants to Maizeville, and hoped before long to send a good deal of produce by his boat. We therefore wished him to “lump” us, goods, children, and all, and deliver us safely at the Maizeville wharf for as small a sum as possible.

===Chinese===
在城里公寓的最后一夜，说实话倒像是在露营；一天的劳乏换来了酣畅的沉睡，我们看上去、感觉上都像一户移民人家了。可第二天一早，天刚亮，我们就从地板上的临时地铺上爬起来，热切而满怀希望地着手动身前的最后准备。约翰·琼斯（John Jones）接了我的信，答应驾他那辆结实的带篷轻便四轮马车到梅兹维尔码头（Maizeville Landing）来接我们，并预先把老农舍的炉火生好。行李一车接一车地运往船上，因为我情愿只跟一个靠得住的搬运车夫打交道。等东西都运完了，我们向邻舍道过别，乘有轨马车去搭船，用最省钱的方式悄悄离开了这座城市。我知道船上又暖和又舒服，便提议午饭就在船上吃。

可是一想到能看看码头、轮船和大河，连孩子们都把胃口忘到了脑后。我们很快到了拥挤的码头。那艘大轮船仿佛成了码头的一部分，顺着码头长长的身子泊着，搭了几道跳板；箱子、木桶、包裹正手忙脚乱地被往船上搬，喧闹声不绝于耳。年纪小的几个起初被那噪音和表面的乱哄哄吓着了一点儿。小耗子（Mousie）紧紧挨在我身边，连鲍勃西（Bobsey）也攥着妈妈的手不放。上层加长的客舱两侧开着一间间客房，配着扶手椅和摇椅，舒服得像一间漂在水上的客厅。就在这儿，离大火炉不远，我把我们的大本营安下了。我让孩子们把这块地方牢牢记准，然后说：“咱们就打这儿出发，四处去逛。第一件事，默顿（Merton），你跟我来，看看咱们的全部家用什物是不是都在一处、完好无缺。你得学会像个男子汉那样出门旅行、照管东西。”

我们花了一点工夫，把货物归置得更稳妥、更紧凑些，然后去见船长，笑呵呵地告诉他，我们是迁往梅兹维尔的移民，盼着不多久就能有许多出产搭他这条船运下来。所以我们想请他把我们连人带货、连大带小“一揽子”全包了，用最省的价钱，把我们平平安安送到梅兹维尔码头。

===Original===
He good-naturedly agreed, and I found that the chief stage of our journey would involve less outlay than I had expected.

Thus far all had gone so well that I began to fear that a change must take place soon, in order that our experience should be more like the common lot of humanity. When at last I took all the children out on the afterdeck, to remove the first edge of their curiosity, I saw that there was at least an ominous change in the weather. The morning had been mild, with a lull in the usual March winds. Now a scud of clouds was drifting swiftly in from the eastward, and chilly, fitful gusts began to moan and sigh about us. A storm was evidently coming, and my hope was that we might reach our haven before it began. I kept my fears to myself, and we watched the long lines of carts converging toward the gangplanks of our own and other steamboats.

“See, youngsters,” I cried, “all this means commerce. These loads and loads of things will soon be at stores and homes up the river, supplying the various needs of the people. Tomorrow the residents along the river will bring what they have to sell to this same boat, and by daylight next morning carts will be carrying country produce and manufactured articles all over the city. Thus you see commerce is made by people supplying themselves and each other with what they need. Just as soon as we can bring down a crate of berries and send it to Mr. Bogart we shall be adding to the commerce of the world in the best way. We shall become what are called the ‘producers,’ and but for this class the world would soon come to an end.”

===Chinese===
他和和气气地答应了。我于是发现，我们这趟旅程最主要的一段，花费竟比预想的还省。

到此为止，一切顺利得出奇，我反倒开始担心：过不多久必定要出点变故，我们的经历才更合乎世人共同的命运。后来我把孩子们统统带到后甲板上去，想先消一消他们的好奇心，一眼就看出，天气至少起了不祥的变化。早晨本来挺温和，惯常三月里的风也歇着。这会儿一团团碎云正从东边飞快地飘来，冷飕飕的阵风忽起忽落，开始在我们四周呜咽叹息。一场暴风雨分明要来了，我只盼我们能在它来之前先抵达安身的港湾。我把这份忧虑闷在心里，一家人望着长长的一列列大车，朝我们这条船和别的几条轮船的跳板汇聚过去。

“瞧见没有，小家伙们，”我大声说，“这一切说的就是商业。这一车又一车的货物，很快就会送到河上游的店铺和人家里去，供应当地人的种种需要。明天，河沿岸的住户又要把他们要卖的东西送到这同一条船上，后天一清早，大车便载着乡下的出产和城里制造的货品，满城奔走。这样你们就明白了，商业就是人们用彼此需要的东西互相供应。等哪天我们能运下一板条箱浆果，送给博加特先生（Mr. Bogart），我们就是在用顶好的法子给世界的商业添份量了。我们也就成了人们所说的‘生产者’；要是没有这一类人，这世界用不了多久就要完蛋。”

===Original===
“ ’Rah!” cried Bobsey, “I’m goin’ to be a p’oducer.”

He promised, however, to be a consumer for a long time to come, especially of patience. His native fearlessness soon asserted itself, and he wanted to go everywhere and see everything, asking questions about machinery, navigation, river craft, the contents of every box, bale, or barrel we saw, till I felt that I was being used like a town pump. I pulled him back to the cabin, resolving to stop his mouth for a time at least with the contents of our lunch basket.

Winnie was almost as bad, or as good, perhaps I should say; for, however great the drain and strain on me might be, I knew that these active little brains were expanding to receive a host of new ideas.

===Chinese===
“好哇！”鲍勃西嚷道，“我也要当个‘生产者’。”

不过，往后好长一阵子，他注定还是个消费者——尤其是耐心的消费者。他天生的胆大很快便冒了头，哪儿都想去，什么都想看：机器、航行、河上的船具，还有我们见到的每只箱子、每个货包、每只木桶里装的是什么，样样都要问，问得我觉得自己简直给当成街头的一台公用水泵在使。我把他拽回客舱，决意至少先拿午餐篮子里的东西堵住他的嘴。

温妮（Winnie）也差不了多少——也许我该说，也差不了多少地好；因为，不管我多么劳神费力，我知道这些活跃的小脑瓜正在张开，好装进一大批新的观念。

===Original===
Mousie was quiet as usual, and made no trouble, but I saw with renewed hope that this excursion into the world awakened in her a keen and natural interest. Ever since the project of country life had been decided upon, her listless, weary look had been giving place to one of greater animation. The hope of flowers and a garden had fed her life like a deep, hidden spring.

To Merton I had given larger liberty, and had said: “It is not necessary for you to stay with me all the time. Come and go on the boat and wharf as you wish. Pick up what knowledge you can. All I ask is that you will use good sense in keeping out of trouble and danger.”

I soon observed that he was making acquaintances here and there, and asking questions which would go far to make good his loss of schooling for a time. Finding out about what one sees is, in my belief, one of the best ways of getting an education. The trouble with most of us is that we accept what we see, without inquiry or knowledge.

The children were much interested in scenes witnessed from the side of the boat farthest from the wharf. Here in the enclosed water space were several kinds of craft, but the most curious in their eyes was a group of canal boats—“queer travelling houses” Mousie called them; for it was evident that each one had a family on board, and the little entrance to the hidden cabin resembled a hole from which men, women, and children came like rabbits out of a burrow. Tough, hardy, barefooted children were everywhere. While we were looking, one frowsy-headed little girl popped up from her burrow in the boat, and, with legs and feet as red as a boiled lobster, ran along the guards like a squirrel along a fence.

===Chinese===
小耗子照旧安安静静，不惹一点麻烦，可我怀着重新燃起的希望看出，这趟出来见世面，在她心里唤起了一种热切而出于天性的兴趣。自从乡下过日子的计划定了下来，她那副没精打采、倦怠的神情，就渐渐换上了比较有生气的模样。对花草和花园的盼望，像一眼深藏的清泉，滋养着她的生命。

对默顿，我给了他更大的自由，而且说过：“你不必一直守在我身边。船上岸上，随你来去，能捡到什么学问就捡什么。我只要你有脑子，别惹上麻烦、碰上危险，就够了。”

我很快便看出，他在这儿那儿结交相识，问东问西，这些问答大有助于弥补他一时失学的损失。依我看，把自己眼见的东西弄个明白，正是受教育的最好门径之一。我们大多数人的毛病，就在于对看见的东西照单全收，既不打听，也不求懂得。

孩子们对轮船背码头那一侧的景象极感兴趣。这边一圈围起来的水面上泊着好几种船，在他们眼里顶稀奇的，是一伙运河船——小耗子管它们叫“古怪的旅行房子”；因为显然每条船上都住着一户人家，通往隐蔽船舱的那扇小舱口活像个地洞，男人、女人、孩子打里面钻出钻进，就像兔子从洞里出来一样。粗壮结实、光着脚丫的孩子随处可见。我们正看着，一个头发乱蓬蓬的小姑娘忽然从船上的洞里冒出来，腿脚红得像煮熟的龙虾，沿着船舷的护板一路飞跑，活像松鼠顺着篱笆窜。

===Original===
“O dear!” sighed Mousie, “I’d rather live in a city flat than in such a house.”

“I think it would be splendid,” protested Winnie, “to live in a travelling house. You could go all over and still stay at home.”

I was glad on our return to find my wife dozing in her chair. She was determined to spend in rest the hours on the boat, and had said that Mousie also must be quiet much of the afternoon.

Between three and four the crush on the wharf became very great. Horses and drays were so mixed up that to inexperienced eyes it looked as if they could never be untangled. People of every description, loaded down with parcels, were hurrying on board, and it would seem from our point of view that American women shared with their French sisters an aptness for trade, for among the passengers were not a few substantial, matronly persons who appeared as if they could look the world in the face and get the better of it.

===Chinese===
“哎呀！”小耗子叹了口气，“这样的房子，我还不如住城里的公寓呢。”

“我觉得住在一座旅行房子里才叫棒呢，”温妮不服气地说，“哪儿都能去，还一直待在自己家里。”

回到客舱，我高兴地看见妻子正在椅子里打盹。她打定主意，要把在船上的时辰都花在歇息上，还说过，这天下午大部分时间，小耗子也必须安安静静的。

三点到四点之间，码头上的拥挤达到了极点。马匹和运货大车搅作一团，在没经验的人看来，仿佛永远也理不出个头绪。各色人等，大包小裹压得身子发沉，急匆匆往船上赶；照我们看来，美国妇女在经商的本事上，似乎同她们的法国姐妹不相上下，因为乘客当中颇有一些体面富态、一派主妇气度的人物，瞧那样子，她们敢直视这个世界，而且还能胜它一筹。

## XIV Self-Denial and Its Reward / XIV 克己与报偿

===Original===

I remember little that followed until I was startled out of my chair by a loud knocking. The sunlight was streaming in at the window and John Jones’s voice was at the door.

“I think we have all overslept,” I said, as I admitted him.

“Not a bit of it. Every wink you’ve had after such a day as yesterday is like money put in the bank. But the sleighing is better now than it will be later in the day. The sun’ll be pretty powerful by noon, and the snow’ll soon be slush. Now’s your chance to get your traps up in a hurry. I can have a two-hoss sled ready in half an hour, and if you say so I can hire a big sleigh of a neighbor, and we’ll have everything here by dinnertime. After you get things snug, you won’t care if the bottom does fall out of the roads for a time. Well, you *have* had to rough it. Merton might have come and stayed with us.”

“Oh, I’m all right,” said the boy, rubbing his eyes open as he rose from the floor, at the same time learning from stiff joints that a carpet is not a mattress.

===Chinese===

此后的情形我记不大清了，直到一阵响亮的敲门声把我从椅子里惊起。阳光正涌进窗口，门外响着约翰·琼斯（John Jones）的嗓音。

“我看咱们全都睡过头了，”我一面放他进门，一面说。

“哪儿的话。像昨天那样熬了一天，往后合的每一眼觉，都跟存进银行的钱一样金贵。不过要趁雪橇赶路，眼下正合适，再晚些今天就不行了。到晌午太阳就相当毒了，雪眼看要化成烂泥。现在正是赶紧把你们的行李家什运上来的机会。半个钟头我就能备好一架双马平板雪橇；你若点头，我再上邻居家雇一辆大雪橇，赶在晌午饭前，咱们就能把什么东西都弄到这儿。等一切安顿停当，哪怕往后路烂上一阵子，你也满不在乎了。嗐，你们*可*真受了一场罪。默顿（Merton）本来可以上我们家去住的。”

“哦，我没事儿，”男孩说着从地板上爬起来，一边揉开眼睛，一边从僵酸的骨节里领教到：地毯终究不是褥垫。

===Original===

“Nothing would suit me better, Mr. Jones, than your plan of prompt action, and I’m the luckiest man in the world in having such a longheaded, forehanded neighbor to start with. I know you’ll make a good bargain for the other team, and before I sleep tonight I wish to square up for everything. I mean at least to begin business in this way at Maizeville.”

“Oh, go slow, go slow!” said Mr. Jones. “The town will mob you if they find you’ve got ready money in March. John junior will be over with a pot of coffee and a jug of milk in a few minutes, and we’ll be off sharp.”

There was a patter of feet overhead, and soon Bobsey came tearing down, half wild with excitement over the novelty of everything. He started for the door as if he were going head first into the snow.

I caught him, and said: “Do you see that chair? Well, we all have a busy day before us. You can help a good deal, and play a little, but you can’t hinder and pester according to your own sweet will one bit. You must either obey orders or else be put under arrest and tied in the chair.”

===Chinese===

“琼斯先生（Mr. Jones），什么也比不上你这雷厉风行的章程更合我的心意；起步就遇上你这样一位有远见、又会打算的邻居，我是天底下最有福气的人。另外那一套车马，我知道你去准能讲个好价钱。今夜就寝之前，我要把一切账目结算清楚。至少在梅兹维尔（Maizeville），我打算就这样开个头。”

“嗳，慢着，慢着！”琼斯先生说，“要叫镇上人察觉你三月里兜里就揣着现钱，他们非把你团团围住不可。小琼斯（John junior）过几分钟就提着一壶咖啡、一罐牛奶过来，咱们说走就走。”

头顶上响起一阵啪嗒啪嗒的小脚步声，转眼鲍勃西（Bobsey）便飞奔下楼，样样都新鲜，欢喜得半疯。他径直朝门口冲去，像要一头栽进雪地里。

我一把逮住他，说：“看见那把椅子没有？好，咱们今天人人有一整天的忙。你可以帮不少忙，也可以玩一小会儿，可就是不能由着性子捣乱缠人，一丝一毫也不行。你要么服从命令，要么就被押起来，绑在椅子上。”

===Original===

To go into the chair today would be torture indeed, and the little fellow was sobered at once.

The others soon joined us, eager to see everything by the broad light of day, and to enter upon the task of getting settled. We had scarcely come together before John junior appeared with the chief features of our breakfast. The children scanned this probable playmate very curiously, and some of us could hardly repress a smile at his appearance. He was even more sandy than his father. Indeed his hair and eyebrows were nearly white, but out of his red and almost full moon face his mother’s black eyes twinkled shrewdly. They now expressed only goodwill and bashfulness. Every one of us shook hands with him so cordially that his boy’s heart was evidently won.

Merton, to break the ice more fully, offered to show him his gun, which he had kept within reach ever since we left the boat. It made him feel more like a pioneer, no doubt. As he took it from its stout cloth cover I saw John junior’s eyes sparkle. Evidently a deep chord was touched. He said, excitedly: “Today’s your time to try it. A rabbit can’t stir without leaving his tracks, and the snow is so deep and soft that he can’t get away. There’s rabbits on your own place.”

“O papa,” cried my boy, fairly trembling with eagerness, “can’t I go?”

===Chinese===

今天再给押进那把椅子，可真是活受罪，小家伙立刻收起了嬉皮笑脸。

不多时，其余的人也都聚拢来，个个急着想趁大天白日把样样东西看个分明，着手安顿这个家。我们刚一聚齐，小琼斯就端着我们早餐的主要吃食出现了。孩子们好奇之至地打量这位未来的游伴，我们有几个见了他那副长相，差点憋不住笑。他生得比父亲还要沙黄几分：头发和眉毛几乎全白；可是从那张红扑扑、圆得近乎满月的脸庞里，一双母亲的黑眼睛却机灵地闪着，此刻只透出善意和羞怯。我们每个人都亲亲热热同他握了手，他那颗孩子的心显然就这样被赢得了。

为了把生分气破得更透，默顿提出给他看自己的猎枪——打从下了船，这枪他一直搁在手边。无疑，枪握在手里，人便更觉得自己像个开路的拓荒者。他从结实的布枪套里往外取枪的时候，我看见小琼斯两眼放光，显然有一根深深的心弦被拨动了。他兴奋地说：“今天正是你们试枪的日子。兔子一动弹就留脚印，雪又深又软，它休想逃脱。你们自家的地界上就有兔子。”

“哦，爸爸，”我的儿子嚷道，急得浑身直颤，“带我去不行吗？”

===Original===

“I need you very much this morning.”

“But, papa, others will be out before me, and I may lose my chance;” and he was half ready to cry.

“Yes,” I said; “there is a risk of that. Well, *you* shall decide in this case,” I added, after a moment, seeing a chance to do a little character building. “It is rarely best to put pleasure before business or prudence. If you go out into the snow with those boots, you will spoil them, and very probably take a severe cold. Yet you may go if you will. If you help me we can be back by ten o’clock, and I will get you a pair of rubber boots as we return.”

“Will there be any chance after ten o’clock?” he asked, quickly.

===Chinese===

“今天上午我很用得着你。”

“可是，爸爸，别人会抢在我前头出去，我的机会就要落空了；”他差不多要哭出来。

“不错，”我说，“这种风险是有的。那么好，这桩案子就由*你*自己来断，”停了片刻我又补上一句，因为看出这正是个陶冶品性的机会。“把玩乐摆在事务和谨慎前头，很少是上策。你穿着那双靴子踏进雪里，靴子会毁掉，你还十有八九要得一场重感冒。不过你执意要去，也未尝不可。你若帮我干活，咱们十点钟就能回来，回来路上我给你买一双胶靴。”

“十点钟以后还有机会吗？”他急忙问。

===Original===
