“Well,” said John junior, in his matter-of-fact way, “that depends. As your pa says, there’s a risk.”

The temptation was too strong for the moment. “O dear!” exclaimed Merton, “I may never have so good a chance again. The snow will soon melt, and there won’t be any more till next winter. I’ll tie my trousers down about my boots, and I’ll help all the rest of the day after I get back.”

“Very well,” I said quietly: and he began eating his breakfast﻿—the abundant remains of our last night’s lunch﻿—very rapidly, while John junior started off to get his gun.

I saw that Merton was ill at ease, but I made a sign to his mother not to interfere. More and more slowly he finished his breakfast, then took his gun and went to the room that would be his, to load and prepare. At last he came down and went out by another door, evidently not wishing to encounter me. John junior met him, and the boys were starting, when John senior drove into the yard and shouted, “John junior, step here a moment.”

===Chinese===

“这个嘛，”小琼斯照他一贯就事论事的口气说，“得看情形。就像你爸说的，有风险。”

这一诱惑，一时实难抵挡。“哎呀！”默顿嚷道，“这样的好机会也许再也不会有了。雪眼看就要化，再下场得等到明年冬天。我把裤脚扎在靴子外头；回来以后，这一天剩下的工夫我全帮你干活。”

“很好。”我平静地说。他随即飞快地吃起早饭来——早饭就是昨夜那顿午餐剩下的一大堆吃食——小琼斯则动身回家取枪去了。

我看得出默顿如坐针毡，便向母亲递了个眼色，叫她不要干预。他的早饭越吃越慢，总算吃完，便提了枪，走进那间日后归他住的屋子，装弹预备。末了他下了楼，从另一扇门出去了，显然不愿撞见我。小琼斯来同他会合，两个孩子正要动身，老琼斯赶车进了院子，扬声喊道：“小琼斯，过来站一会儿。”

===Original===

The boy returned slowly, Merton following. “You ain’t said nothin’ to me about goin’ off with that gun,” continued Mr. Jones, severely.

“Well, Merton’s pa said he might go if he wanted to, and I had to go along to show him.”

“That first shot wasn’t exactly straight, my young friend John. I told Merton that it wasn’t best to put pleasure before business, but that he could go if he would. I wished to let him choose to do right, instead of making him do right.”

“Oho, that’s how the land lays. Well, John junior, you can have your choice, too. You may go right on with your gun, but you know the length and weight of that strap at home. Now, will you help me? or go after rabbits?”

===Chinese===

孩子慢吞吞地回来了，默顿跟在后面。“你扛着那杆枪出门，可一个字也没向我交代过，”琼斯先生板起脸接下去说。

“这个，默顿的爸爸说了，他想去就可以去，我总得跟着给他带路。”

“你这头一枪放得可不怎么正，我的小朋友约翰。我对默顿说过，玩乐不该摆在正事和谨慎前头，但他执意要去也随他。我要的是让他自己拣对的路走，而不是逼着他走对的路。”

“哦嗬，原来是这么回事。好吧，小琼斯，你也可以自作挑选。你尽可以扛着枪接着走，可你心里有数，家里那条皮带有多长、有多沉。现在，你是帮我干活，还是追兔子去？”

===Original===

The boy grinned pleasantly, and replied, “If you had said I couldn’t go, I wouldn’t; but if it’s choosin’ between shootin’ rabbits and a strappin’ afterward﻿—come along, Merton.”

“Well, go along then,” chuckled his father; “you’ve made your bargain square, and I’ll keep my part of it.”

“Oh, hang the rabbits! You shan’t have any strapping on my account,” cried Merton; and he carried his gun resolutely to his room and locked the door on it.

John junior quietly went to the old barn, and hid his gun.

===Chinese===

孩子乐呵呵地咧嘴一笑，答道：“要是你说不许去，我就不去；可要是叫我在打兔子跟回头挨一顿皮带之间拣一样——走吧，默顿。”

“那好，去吧，”他父亲呵呵一笑，“这桩买卖你做得公道，我这一头也照办。”

“哦，让兔子见鬼去吧！不能为我叫你挨一顿打，”默顿嚷道；他把枪毅然拿回房间，反锁在屋里。

小琼斯不声不响地走进老谷仓，把枪藏了起来。

===Original===

“Guess I’ll go with you, pa,” he said, joining us.

“Ha, ha, ha!” laughed Mr. Jones. “It was a good bargain to back out of. Come now, let’s all be off as quick as we can. Neighbor Rollins down the road will join us as we go along.”

“Merton,” I said, “see if there isn’t a barrel of apples in the cellar. If you find one, you can fill your pockets.”

He soon returned with bulging pockets and a smiling face, feeling that such virtue as he had shown had soon brought reward. My wife said that while we were gone she and the children would explore the house and plan how to arrange everything. We started in good spirits.

===Chinese===

“我想我还是跟你们去吧，爸，”他说着，回到我们身边。

“哈，哈，哈！”琼斯先生大笑。“这笔买卖，退得好。来吧，咱们尽快动身。顺路下去，罗林斯（Rollins）邻居会同咱们一道走。”

“默顿，”我说，“去看看菜窖里有没有一桶苹果。找着了，你就把口袋装满。”

他很快回来了，口袋鼓鼓囊囊，满脸堆笑，觉得自己方才显出的那点美德这么快就得了报偿。妻子说，我们不在的工夫，她带孩子们把房子各处查看一遍，筹划怎样布置摆设。我们兴头十足地动身了。

===Original===

“Here’s where you thought you was cast away last night,” Mr. Jones remarked, as we passed out of the lane.

The contrast made by a few short hours was indeed wonderful. Then, in dense obscurity, a tempest had howled and shrieked about us; now, in the unclouded sunshine, a gemmed and sparkling world revealed beauty everywhere.

For a long distance our sleighs made the first tracks, and it seemed almost a pity to sully the purity of the white, drift-covered road.

“What a lot of mud’s hid under this snow!” was John Jones’s prose over the opening vistas. “What’s more, it will show itself before night. We can beat all creation at mud in Maizeville, when once we set about it.”

===Chinese===

“昨儿晚上，你就是当自己遇难流落到这儿的，”我们驶出巷口时，琼斯先生说。

短短几个钟头，前后对照竟这般奇妙。那时，浓得化不开的黑暗里，暴风雪围着我们嗥叫尖啸；此刻，万里无云的阳光下，一个镶珠嵌玉、晶莹闪烁的世界，把美展现在四面八方。

走出去很远，路上都还是我们雪橇碾出的头一道辙；眼看这被雪堆封得一片纯白的路面沾上辙印，几乎叫人于心不忍。

“这雪底下藏着好大的泥啊！”——在眼前渐次展开的景致面前，约翰·琼斯道出的却正是这样一番散文。“再说，天黑以前它就要露头。讲到泥，咱们梅兹维尔只要一认真起来，能把普天下都比下去。”

===Original===

Merton laughed, and munched his apples, but I saw that he was impressed by winter scenery such as he had never looked upon before. Soon, however, he and John junior were deep in the game question, and I noted that the latter kept a sharp lookout along the roadside. Before long, while passing a thicket, he shouted, “There’s tracks,” and floundered out into the snow, Merton following.

“Oh, come back,” growled his father.

“Let the boys have a few moments,” I said. “They gave up this morning about as well as you could expect of boys. Would Junior have gone and taken a strapping if Merton hadn’t returned?”

“Yes, indeed he would, and he knows my strappin’s are no make-believe. That boy has no sly, mean tricks to speak of, but he’s as tough and obstinate as a mule sometimes, especially about shooting and fishing. See him now a-p’intin’ for that rabbit, like a hound.”

===Chinese===

默顿笑了，咔嚓咔嚓地嚼着苹果；但我看得出，这等他从未见过的冬景深深打动了他的心。可是不多时，他和小琼斯便一头栽进打猎的话题里，我还留意到后者一路死死盯着路边。没多久，经过一丛灌木时，他喊了起来：“有脚印！”便扑扑腾腾地闯进雪里，默顿跟在后头。

“嗐，回来，”他父亲闷声呵斥。

“让孩子们玩上几分钟吧，”我说。“今儿早上，他们让步让得也算不坏了，男孩家能这样也就顶了天。要是默顿没有回来，小琼斯当真会去挨那顿皮带吗？”

“会，当真会，而且他知道我动起皮带来可不做假。那孩子阴一套损一套的把戏倒说不上，可有时候倔得像头骡子，又硬又拗，尤其打猎钓鱼这两桩。瞧他这会儿，正照着那只兔子的去向直插过去呢，活像条猎狗。”

===Original===

True enough, the boy was showing good woodcraft. Restraining Merton, he cautiously approached the tracks, which by reason of the lightness and depth of the snow were not very distinct.

“He can’t be far away,” said Junior, excitedly. “Don’t go too fast till I see which way he was a-p’intin’. We don’t want to follow the tracks back, but for’ard. See, he came out of that old wall there, he went to these bushes and nibbled some twigs, and here he goes﻿—here he went﻿—here﻿—here﻿—yes, he went into the wall again just here. Now, Merton, watch this hole while I jump over the other side of the fence and see if he comes out again. If he makes a start, grab him.”

John Jones and I were now almost as excited as the boys, and Mr. Rollins, the neighbor who was following us, was standing up in his sleigh to see the sport. It came quickly. As if by some instinct the rabbit believed Junior to be the more dangerous, and made a break from the wall almost at Merton’s feet, with such swiftness and power as to dash by him like a shot. The first force of its bound over, it was caught by nature’s trap﻿—snow too deep and soft to admit of rapid running.

John Jones soon proved that Junior came honestly by his passion for hunting. In a moment he was floundering through the bushes with his son and Merton. In such pursuit of game my boy had the advantage, for he was as agile as a cat. But a moment or two elapsed before he caught up with the rabbit, and threw himself upon it, then rose, white as a snowman, shouting triumphantly and holding the little creature aloft by its ears.

===Chinese===

一点不假，这孩子很有一手林间本事。他拦住默顿，小心翼翼地向脚印凑近——雪又轻又厚，脚印已不太分明。

“它跑不远，”小琼斯兴奋地说，“先别走急，等我看看它奔的是哪一头。咱们不能顺着脚印往回跟，得往前追。瞧，它是打那道旧石墙里出来的，再到这几丛矮树啃了几口嫩枝，瞧这儿，它往这边去了——往这儿去了——这儿——这儿——对，它又正打这儿钻回墙里。这样，默顿，你守住这个洞，我跳过篱笆那一头，看它出不出来。它一起身，你就抓。”

这时约翰·琼斯和我几乎同孩子们一样激动，那位跟在后面的邻居罗林斯先生也从雪橇里站起来观战。好戏转瞬就来。那兔子仿佛凭着一股本能，认定小琼斯更危险，竟几乎打默顿脚边的墙根破壁而出，其疾其猛，像一颗出膛的枪弹从他身旁掠过。头一跳的势头一尽，它便落进了天设的陷坑——雪又深又软，容不得它撒腿飞奔。

约翰·琼斯随即证明，小琼斯这股打猎的痴劲确实是家传的。眨眼间，他已同儿子和默顿一道在灌木丛里深一脚浅一脚地扑腾。这样的追捕，我儿子占着便宜，他敏捷得像只猫。不过也追了一两分钟，他才赶上兔子，纵身把它扑住，随即站起身来，浑身雪白，活像个雪人，一手拎着那小东西的耳朵高举过头，欢声报捷。

===Original===

“Never rate Junior for hunting again,” I said, laughingly, to Mr. Jones. “He’s a chip of the old block.”

“I rather guess he is,” my neighbor acknowledged, with a grin. “I own up I used to be pretty hot on such larkin’. We all keep forgettin’ we was boys once.”

As we rode on, Merton was a picture of exultation, and Junior was on the sharp lookout again. His father turned on him and said: “Now look a’ here, enough’s as good as a feast. I’ll blindfold you if you don’t let the tracks alone. Mrs. Durham wants her things, so she can begin to live. Get up there;” and a crack of the whip ended all further hopes on the part of the boys. But they felt well repaid for coming, and Merton assured Junior that he deserved half the credit, for only he knew how to manage the hunt.

===Chinese===

“往后可再别数落小琼斯打猎了，”我笑着对琼斯先生说。“有其父必有其子。”

“我想也八九不离十，”我的邻居咧嘴承认。“不瞒你说，我从前玩起这路闹腾来，也疯得够呛。咱们老是记不得，自己也当过孩子。”

我们继续前行，默顿一副心花怒放的样子，小琼斯却又眼尖地瞭望起来。他父亲扭头冲他说：“喂，听着，见好就收。你再不肯放过那些脚印，我就蒙上你的眼。达勒姆太太（Mrs. Durham）等着她的家当，好开张过日子呢。给我走起来；”一记响鞭，把两个孩子余下的指望斩断殆尽。不过他们觉得这一趟没有白来，默顿还向小琼斯保证，这份功劳足有一半归他，因为只有他懂得怎样掌这场猎事的局。

## XXVII Strawberry Visions and “Pertaters” / XXVII 草莓憧憬与“土豆”

===Original===
I had decided that I would not set out any more raspberries until I had learned the comparative value of those already on the place. After I had seen my varieties in bearing and marketed the crop, I should be better able to make a wise selection, “Why not plant only the best and most profitable?” I reasoned. At Mr. Jones’s suggestion I had put up notices at public resorts, and inserted a brief advertisement in a local paper, stating that I had plants for sale. As a result, I sold, at a low price, it is true, the greater part of the young plants that had been trenched in, and the ready money they brought was very acceptable.

From the first, my mind had often turned toward strawberries as one of our chief crops. They promised well for several reasons, the main one being that they would afford a light and useful form of labor for all the children. Even Bobsey could pick the fruit almost as well as any of us, for he had no long back to ache in getting down to it. The crop, also, could be gathered and sold before the raspberry season began, and this was an important fact. We should also have another and earlier source of income. I had read a great deal about the cultivation of the strawberry, and I had visited a Maizeville neighbor who grew them on a large scale, and had obtained his views. To make my knowledge more complete I wrote to my Washington-Market friend, Mr. Bogart, and his prompt letter in reply was encouraging.

“Don’t go into too many kinds,” he advised, “and don’t set too much ground. A few crates of fine berries will pay you better than bushels of small, soft, worthless trash. Steer clear of high-priced novelties and fancy sorts, and begin with only those known to pay well in your region. Try Wilson’s (they’re good to sell if not to eat) and Duchess for early, and Sharpless and Champion for late. Set the last two kinds out side by side, for the Champions won’t bear alone. A customer of mine runs on these four sorts. He gives them high culture, and gets big crops and big berries, which pay big. When you want crates, I can furnish them, and take my pay out of the sales of your fruit. Don’t spend much money for plants. Buy a few of each kind, and set ’em in moist ground and let ’em run. By winter you’ll have enough plants to cover your farm.”

===Chinese===
我早已打定主意，在弄清庄上已有的那些树莓价值究竟如何之前，不再新栽树莓。等我亲眼见过手头各个品种的挂果情形、又把收成卖上市之后，才更有把握作出高明的取舍。“为什么不只种最好、最来钱的呢？”我这样盘算。经琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）提议，我在人们聚集的公共场所张贴了告示，又在本地一家报纸上登了一则简短广告，说明我有树莓苗出售。结果，价钱固然卖得低，原先开沟假植的幼苗却出手了大半，换来的现钱也很是顶用。

打一开始，我心里就时常惦着草莓，把它算作我们的主要作物之一。有几条理由叫我看好它，主要一条是：它能让所有的孩子都担起一份轻省而有用的活计。就连鲍勃西（Bobsey）摘起果来也几乎不比我们谁差，他背不长，弯腰俯身也不至于腰酸。这种作物还能赶在树莓季开始之前采收卖掉，这一层利害关系非同小可。这样我们便又多了一项更早的进项。关于草莓的栽培，我读过许多书，也拜访过梅兹维尔（Maizeville）一位大面积种植草莓的邻人，听取了他的见解。为了把这门学问补得更齐全，我又写信给在华盛顿市场（Washington Market）结识的朋友博加特先生（Mr. Bogart），他迅速寄来的回信着实令人鼓舞。

“别弄太多品种，”他出主意说，“也别铺太大的地面。几板条箱上等莓子，比几蒲式耳又小又软、一无可取的次货来钱得多。那些要价高昂的新奇品种和花哨货色，躲远一点，起头只种你们那一带公认赚钱的几样。早熟的试试威尔逊草莓（Wilson，卖是好卖，未必好吃）和公爵草莓（Duchess），晚熟的用夏普利斯（Sharpless）和冠军（Champion）。后两种要并排挨着栽，因为冠军单栽不结果。我有个主顾专种这四样，伺候得精细，收成大、果子大，赚头也大。你要板条箱，我供得上，钱从你果子卖得的钱里扣。买苗别花大钱。每个品种买上几株，栽到潮润的地里，任它们发蔓。到冬天，你手头的苗就够把你的农场都铺满了。”

===Original===
I found that I could buy these standard varieties in the vicinity; and having made the lower part of the garden very rich, I procured, one cloudy day, two hundred plants of each kind and set them in rows, six feet apart, so that by a little watchfulness I could keep them separate. I obtained my whole stock for five dollars; therefore, counting our time and everything, the cost of entering on strawberry culture was slight. A rainy night followed, and every plant started vigorously.

In spite of occasional frosts and cold rains, the days grew longer and warmer. The cherry, peach, plum, and pear buds were almost ready to burst into bloom, but Mr. Jones shook his head over the orchard.

“This ain’t apple year,” he said. “Well, no matter. If you can make it go this season, you will be sure of better luck next year.”

===Chinese===
我发现这些标准品种在左近就买得到；园子下段的地已让我施得十分肥沃，便趁一个阴天，每个品种各买了两百株，栽成一行行，行距六英尺，这样只要稍加留心，就能把它们彼此分开。全部苗子只花了五块钱；所以，把我们搭上的工夫和一切开销都算在内，踏进草莓栽培这一行的本钱也算轻微。当夜下了一场雨，株株苗子都勃勃地长了起来。

尽管偶有霜冻和冷雨，白昼却一天长似一天、一天暖似一天。樱桃、桃、李、梨的花苞眼看就要绽开了，琼斯先生对着果园却直摇头。

“今年不是苹果的年成，”他说。“嗯，不要紧。这一季你只要能顺当撑过去，明年运气准保更好。”

===Original===
He had come over to aid me in choosing a two-acre plot of ground for corn and potatoes. This we marked out from the upper and eastern slope of a large meadow. The grass was running out and growing weedy.

“It’s time it was turned over,” my neighbor remarked; “and by fall it’ll be in good condition for fruit.”

I proposed to extend my fruit area gradually, with good reason, fearing that much hired help would leave small profits.

===Chinese===
他这回过来，是帮我挑一块两英亩的地，好种玉米和土豆。我们选定的是一片大草甸上首偏东的坡地。那里的草已经衰败，杂草却日渐旺盛。

“这块地也该翻个身了，”我的邻人说；“到秋天，它就是一块种果树的好地了。”

我打算渐次扩大果树面积，这样打算自有道理——生怕雇工太多，到头来所剩利润微薄。

===Original===
That very afternoon Mr. Jones, with his sharp steel plow, began to turn over clean, deep, even furrows; for we had selected the plot in view of the fact that it was not stony, as was the case with other portions of our little farm.

When at last the ground was plowed, he said: “I wouldn’t harrow the part meant for corn till you are ready to plant it, say about the tenth of next month. We’d better get the pertater ground ready and the rows furrowed out right off. Early plantin’ is the best. How much will ye give to ’em?”

“Half the plot,” I said.

===Chinese===
就在当天下午，琼斯先生便用他那口锋利的钢犁翻开地来，犁出一道道干净、深匀、齐整的犁沟；我们选中这块地，正是看中了它不像我们小农场其余地块那样多石。

地总算犁完时，他说：“种玉米的那片，我劝你先别耙，等你预备下种时再说，比方下月十号上下。土豆这片的地，咱们最好马上整好，行子也马上开出来。早种最好。你打算分给它们多少地？”

“这片地的一半，”我说。

===Original===
“Why, Mr. Durham, that’s a big plantin’ for pertaters.”

“Well, I’ve a plan, and would like your opinion. If I put Early Rose potatoes right in, when can I harvest them?”

“Say the last of July or early August, accordin’ to the season.”

“If we keep the ground clean and well worked the sod will then be decayed, won’t it?”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，达勒姆先生（Mr. Durham），这土豆种的面积可不小啊。”

“是这样，我有个打算，想听听你的意见。要是我当下就把早玫瑰（Early Rose）土豆种下去，什么时候能收？”

“看年成，七月底八月初吧。”

“只要我们把地拾掇得干净、耕作得勤，到那时草皮土也就沤烂了，对不对？”

===Original===
“Yes, nigh enough. Ye want to grow turnips or fodder corn, I s’pose?”

“No, I want to set out strawberries. I’ve read more about this fruit than any other, and, if the books are right, I can set strong plants on enriched ground early in August and get a good crop next June. Won’t this pay better than planting next spring and waiting over two years from this time for a crop?”

“Of course it will, if you’re right. I ain’t up on strawberries.”

“Well,” I continued, “it looks reasonable. I shall have my young plants growing right here in my own garden. Merton and I can take them up in the cool of the evening and in wet weather, and they won’t know they’ve been moved. I propose to get these early potatoes out of the ground as soon as possible, even if I have to sell part of them before they are fully ripe; then have the ground plowed deep and marked out for strawberries, put all the fertilizers I can scrape together in the rows and set the plants as fast as possible. I’ve read again and again that many growers regard this method as one of the best.”

===Chinese===
“对，差不多烂透了。你想种芜菁或是饲用玉米，我猜？”

“不，我要栽草莓。这种果子的书我读得最多，书本要是不骗人，八月初我就能把壮苗栽上肥沃的地，明年六月收得一场好收成。这岂不比明年开春才栽、从眼下算起要等上两年多才有收成，划算得多？”

“你要是算得对，那自然划算。草莓的行道，我不在行。”

“嗯，”我接着说，“听着是合情合理。我的幼苗本来就在我自家园子里长着。默顿（Merton）和我可以在傍晚凉快时、或是雨天里起苗，它们压根儿不会觉得挪过窝。我打算尽早把这些早土豆起出土，哪怕有一部分没熟透就得先卖掉；随后把地深耕，划行定好草莓的位置，把能张罗到的肥料统统下到行里，尽快把苗栽下去。许许多多种植的人都把这条路数看作最好的法子之一，这样的说法我读了一遍又一遍。”

===Original===
“Well, you’re comin’ on for a beginner. I’m kind o’ shy of book plans, though. But try it. I’ll come over, as I used to when old man Jamison was here, and sit on the fence and make remarks.”

Planting an acre of potatoes was no light task for us, even after the ground was plowed and harrowed, and the furrows for the rows were marked out. I also had to make a half day’s journey to the city of Newtown to buy more seed, since the children’s appetites had greatly reduced the stock in the root cellar. For a few days we worked like beavers. Even Winnie helped Merton to drop the seed; and in the evening we had regular potato-cutting “bees,” Junior coming over to aid us, and my wife and Mousie helping also. Songs and stories enlivened these evening hours of labor. Indeed, my wife and Mousie performed, during the day, a large part of this task, and they soon learned to cut the tubers skilfully. I have since known this work to be done so carelessly that some pieces were cut without a single eye upon them. Of course, in such cases there is nothing to grow.

===Chinese===
“喏，就一个新手说，你长进得不慢。不过书本上那套章程，我总有点不敢全信。你只管试。我会常过来，就像贾米森老头（Jamison）在世那会儿一样，坐在栅栏上说长道短。”

种一英亩土豆，对我们来说绝非轻省活计，纵然地已犁好耙好、各行下种的沟也都划了出来。我还得花半天工夫跑一趟纽敦（Newtown）城，再买些种薯，因为孩子们的胃口已把菜窖（root cellar）里的存货吃去大半。接连几天，我们像河狸似的卖力苦干。连温妮（Winnie）也帮着默顿往沟里下种；到了晚上，我们还要像模像样地开切种薯的“互助会”，小琼斯（Junior）过来搭手，我妻子和小耗子（Mousie）也来帮忙。歌声和故事给这些夜间劳作的时辰添了生气。其实，这道切薯活计的大半，白天正是我妻子和小耗子做的，她们很快便学会了熟练地把块茎切开。我后来见过这活计做得马虎到家的：有些切块上连一个芽眼都不留。那样的切块，自然什么也长不出来。

===Original===
One Saturday night, the last of April, we exulted over the fact that our acre was planted and the seed well covered.

Many of the trees about the house, meantime, had clothed themselves with fragrant promises of fruit. All, especially Mousie, had been observant of the beautiful changes, and, busy as we had been, she, Winnie and Bobsey had been given time to keep our table well supplied with wildflowers. Now that they had come in abundance, they seemed as essential as our daily food. To a limited extent I permitted blooming sprays to be taken from the fruit trees, thinking, with Mousie, that “cherry blossoms are almost as nice as cherries.” Thus Nature graced our frugal board, and suggested that, as she accompanied her useful work with beauty and fragrance, so we also could lift our toilsome lives above the coarse and sordid phase too common in country homes.

===Chinese===
四月最后一个星期六的晚上，我们欢天喜地：一英亩地全部种完，种薯也盖得严严实实。

这其间，屋旁的许多树木也都披挂上芬芳的果实预兆。一家人，尤其是小耗子，始终留心着这些美丽的变易；我们纵然忙碌，也一直给她们留着工夫，让她、温妮和鲍勃西把我们家餐桌上的野花供得源源不断。如今野花盛开，便仿佛同每日的饭食一样不可或缺。我也有限度地容许从果树上折取开花的枝条，同小耗子一样心想：“樱花开起来，几乎同樱桃一样好。”就这样，大自然装点了我们俭朴的餐桌，也向我们昭示：她既让有益的劳作伴随着美与芬芳，我们同样可以把辛劳的日子，超拔于乡下人家习见的粗陋寒酸之上。

## VIII Telling About Eden / VIII 讲述伊甸园

===Original===
Never was a traveller from a remote foreign clime listened to with more breathless interest than I as I related my adventures at our late supper after my return. Mousie looked almost feverish in her excitement, and Winnie and Bobsey exploded with merriment over the name of the mountain that would be one of our nearest neighbors. They dubbed the place “Schunemunks” at once. Merton put on serious and sportsmanlike airs as he questioned me, and it was evident that he expected to add largely to our income from the game he should kill. I did not take much pains to dispel his illusions, knowing that one day’s tramp would do this, and that he would bring back increased health and strength if nothing else.

No fairy tale had ever absorbed the children like the description of that old house and its surroundings; and when at last they were induced to retire I said to my wife, after explaining more in practical detail the pros and cons to be considered: “It all depends on you. If you wish I will take you up the first pleasant day, so that you can see for yourself before we decide.”

She laughed as she said, “I decided two minutes after you arrived.”

“How is that?”

===Chinese===
还从来没有一个从遥远异邦归来的旅人，能像我这样被人屏住呼吸、竖着耳朵听过。我回来那天，晚饭吃得很迟，席间我便讲起自己的这番经历。小耗子（Mousie）兴奋得两颊几乎发烧；温妮（Winnie）和鲍勃西（Bobsey）一听到那座将同我们做紧邻的大山的名字，乐得炸开了锅，当场就给那地方起了个诨名，叫做“舒内芒克”（Schunemunks）。默顿（Merton）盘问我的时候，摆出一副一本正经的行猎架势，明摆着指望日后靠他亲手猎到的野味，给家里的进项添上大大一笔。我也不怎么费心去戳破他的幻梦，知道只消他徒步跑上一天，这幻梦自会破灭；就算别的都捞不着，他至少还能带回来更旺盛的健康和体力。

童话从来不曾像这番对老屋及其四邻光景的描绘这样，把孩子们牢牢迷住；好容易劝得他们去睡觉，我把种种要权衡的利弊更切实地细说了一遍，然后对妻子说：“全看你了。你若愿意，赶头一个晴和的日子，我就带你上去，等你亲眼看过，咱们再拿主意。”

她一面笑一面说：“你进门才两分钟，我就拿定主意了。”

“这话怎么讲？”

===Original===
“I saw you had the place in your eyes. La, Robert! I can read you like a book. You give in to me in little things, and that pleases a woman, you know. You must decide a question like this, for it is a question of support for us all, and you can do better on a place that suits you than on one never quite to your mind. It has grown more and more clear to me all the evening that you have fallen in love with the old place, and that settles it.”

“Well, you women have a way of your own of deciding a question.”

My wife was too shrewd not to make a point in her favor, and she remarked, with a complacent nod, “*I* have a way of my own, but there are women in the world who would have insisted on a smart new house.”

“Little wife,” I said, laughing, “there was another girl that I was a little sweet on before I met you. I’m glad you are not the other girl.”

She put her head a little to one side with the old roguish look which used to be so distracting when the question of questions with me was whether pretty Winnie Barlow would give half a dozen young fellows the go-by for my sake, and she said, “Perhaps the other girl is glad too.”

===Chinese===
“我瞧出你眼里已经装着那地方了。哎呀，罗伯特（Robert）！你这个人，我像读书一样一读就透。小事上你样样让着我——你也知道，这最叫女人心里受用。可这样的问题得由你来定，因为它是关系到全家生计的问题；住在一处合你心意的地方，你一定比住在一处总不对你脾胃的地方干得出色。整个晚上，我越来越看得明白：你已经爱上那老地方了。这一来就算定了。”

“唉，你们女人家决断一件事，自有一套你们自己的门道。”

我妻子精明得很，决不肯放过向着自己的一分理。只见她得意地点点头说：“*我*自然有我的办法；可世上也有些女人，是会非一幢漂亮新房子不可的。”

“小妻子，”我笑着说，“遇见你以前，我还对一个姑娘有过几分痴心呢。幸亏你不是那个姑娘。”

她微微歪着头，露出当年那副淘气的神情——想当年，压在我头上天字第一号的问题，就是漂亮的温妮·巴洛（Winnie Barlow）肯不肯为了我的缘故，撇下半打的小伙子们；那副神情曾叫我意乱神迷。她说：“说不定那位姑娘如今也正高兴呢。”

===Original===
“I’ve no doubt she is,” I sighed, “for her husband is getting rich. I don’t care how glad she is if my girl is not sorry.”

“You do amuse me so, Robert! You’d like to pass for something of a philosopher, with your brown studies into the hidden causes and reasons for things, yet you don’t half know yet that when a woman sets her heart on something, she hasn’t much left with which to long for anything else. That is, if she has a heart, which seems to be left out of some women.”

“I think it is, and others get a double allowance. I should be content, for I was rich the moment I won yours.”

“I’ve been more than content; I’ve been happy—happy all these years in city flats. Even in my tantrums and bad days I knew I was happy, deep in my heart.”

===Chinese===
“她高兴，那还用说，”我叹了口气，“她丈夫正发家呢。只要我的姑娘不懊恼，她多高兴我也不在乎。”

“罗伯特，你可真叫我乐不可支！你一心想充个半吊子哲学家，成天板着脸苦思事物背后藏着的缘由道理；可你到这会儿还半点不懂：女人一旦把心放在一件东西上头，就剩不下多少心思再去巴望别的了。当然，这话的前提是她得有一颗心——有的女人，那颗心好像压根儿就漏装了。”

“我也觉得有的女人就是漏装了那颗心，省下的那一份，叫旁人双份领了去。我本该知足了，因为赢得你那颗心的那一刻，我就是个财主了。”

“我又何止知足，我是幸福——在城里公寓的这些年，我一直幸福。就连我闹性子、日子不顺的当口，心底深处也明白自己是幸福的。”

===Original===
“I only hope you will remain as blind about your plodding old husband who couldn’t make a fortune in the city.”

“I’ve seen men who made fortunes, and I’ve seen their wives too.”

I thanked God for the look on her face—a look which had been there when she was a bride, and which had survived many straitened years.

So we chose our country home. The small patrimony to which we had added but little—(indeed we had often denied ourselves in order not to diminish it)—was nearly all to be invested in the farm, and a debt to be incurred, besides. While yielding to my fancy, I believed that I had at the same time chosen wisely, for, as John Jones said, the mature fruit trees of the place would begin to bring returns very soon.

===Chinese===
“我只盼你看你那个闷头苦干、在城里也没发得了财的老丈夫，一直这么瞎着眼睛才好。”

“发了财的男人我见过，他们的太太我也见识过。”

我为她脸上的那份神情感谢上帝——她做新娘的时候就有这份神情，多少拮据的年月也没能把它磨灭。

就这样，我们选定了乡间的家。那笔小小的祖产——我们没往里添过多少（说实话，为了不动它的本，我们常常省吃俭用）——几乎要尽数投进这座农场，此外还得再背上一笔债。我这一步虽是依着自己的心愿，却相信同时也挑得高明；因为，照约翰·琼斯（John Jones）的说法，地里那些已然长成的果树，很快就能开始见收益了。

## XLIII Thanksgiving Day / XLIII 感恩节

===Original===
November weather was occasionally so blustering and stormy that I turned schoolmaster in part, to relieve my wife. During the month, however, were bright, genial days, and others softened by a smoky haze, which gave me opportunity to gather and store a large crop of turnips, to trench in my celery on a dry knoll, and to bury, with their heads downward, all the cabbages for which I could not find a good market. The children still gave me some assistance, but, lessons over, they were usually permitted to amuse themselves in their own way. Winnie, however, did not lose her interest in the poultry, and Merton regularly aided in the care of the stock and in looking after the evening supply of firewood. I also spent a part of my time in the wood lot, but the main labor there was reserved for December. The chief task of the month was the laying down and covering of the tender raspberries; and in this labor Bagley again gave me his aid.

===Chinese===
十一月的天气偶尔狂暴得风雨大作，我便客串起半个教书先生，替妻子分劳。不过这个月里也有晴朗温和的日子，还有些日子被烟霭般的天色烘得柔和，这就给了我机会，收贮下一大茬芜菁，在一处干燥的小丘上开沟壅埋芹菜，又把所有卖不上好价钱的白菜一棵棵头朝下埋进土里。孩子们照旧给我搭把手，不过功课一完，通常就由着他们自寻乐趣去了。但温妮（Winnie）没有丢开她对家禽的兴趣，默顿（Merton）也照例帮着照料牲口，并经管晚间的柴火。我自己也把一部分时间花在林地里，只是那里主要的活计留待十二月。这个月的主要任务，是把娇嫩的树莓枝压倒、覆盖起来；这项活计巴格利（Bagley）又来帮了我一把。

===Original===
Thanksgiving Day was celebrated with due observance. In the morning we all heard Dr. Lyman preach, and came home with the feeling that we and the country at large were prosperous. Mr. and Mrs. Jones, with Junior, dined with us in great state, and we had our first four-course dinner since arriving in Maizeville, and at the fashionable hour of six in the evening. I had protested against my wife’s purpose of staying at home in the morning, saying we would “browse around during the day and get up appetites, while in the afternoon we could all turn cooks and help her.” Merton was excepted, and, after devouring a hasty cold lunch, he and Junior were off with their guns. As for Bobsey, he appeared to browse steadily after church, but seemed in no wise to have exhausted his capacity when at last he attacked his soup, turkey drumstick, and the climax of a pudding. Our feast was a very informal affair, seasoned with mirth and sauced with hunger. The viands, however, under my wife’s skill, would compare with any eaten in the great city, which we never once had regretted leaving. Winifred looked after the transfers from the kitchen at critical moments, while Mousie and Winnie were our waitresses. A royal blaze crackled in the open fireplace, and seemed to share in the sparkle of our rustic wit and unforced mirth, which kept plump Mrs. Jones in a perpetual quiver, like a form of jelly.

===Chinese===
感恩节过得一应如仪。上午我们全家去听莱曼博士（Dr. Lyman）讲道，回到家时心里都觉得，我们自家连同举国上下，日子都过得兴旺。琼斯先生和琼斯太太带着小琼斯（Junior），极隆重地来与我们共进晚餐；这是我们到梅兹维尔（Maizeville）之后头一回吃四道菜的宴席，而且开席赶的还是时兴的钟点——傍晚六点。我原先还抗议过妻子上午守在家里的打算，说我们要“白天四处觅食游逛，养出好胃口来，下午再一齐改行当厨子，给她搭把手”。默顿是例外，他三口两口吞下一顿匆匆的冷饭，便和小琼斯挎着枪出发了。至于鲍勃西（Bobsey），做完礼拜之后他好像就一直在稳稳当当地“啃食”个没停，可到最后对汤、火鸡腿和压轴的布丁发起总攻时，那份肚量似乎丝毫不曾告罄。我们的宴席极不拘礼数，以欢笑调味，以饥饿佐汁。不过菜肴经妻子妙手调理，足可与大城里吃到的任何一餐媲美——而对于离开那座大城，我们从来没有后悔过一回。温妮弗蕾德（Winifred）在要紧关头照管着从厨房到餐桌的转运，小耗子（Mousie）和温妮则充当我们的侍者。敞开的壁炉里一大蓬旺火噼啪作响，似乎也来分享我们乡野妙语与天然欢笑的光彩；那欢笑逗得富态的琼斯太太像一具果冻似的颤个不停。

===Original===
Her husband came out strong in his comical resume of the past year’s experience, concluding: “Well, we owe you and Mrs. Durham a vote of thanks for reforming the Bagley tribe. That appears to me an orthodox case of convarsion. First we gave him the terrors of the law. Tell yer what it is, we was a-smokin’ in wrath around him that mornin’, like Mount Sinai, and you had the sense to bring, in the nick of time, the gospel of givin’ a feller a chance. It’s the best gospel there is, I reckon.”

“Well,” I replied, becoming thoughtful for a moment with boyish memories, “my good old mother taught me that it was God’s plan to give us a chance, and help us make the most of it.”

“I remembered the Bagleys today,” Mrs. Jones remarked, nodding to my wife. “We felt they ought to be encouraged.”

“So did we,” my wife replied, sotto voce.

===Chinese===
她丈夫把过去一年的经历作了一番滑稽的回顾，讲得格外卖力，末了说道：“呃，为着把巴格利一家子改造过来，我们真该给你和达勒姆太太（Mrs. Durham）投上一票谢意。照我看，这就是一桩正正经经的皈依案例。起先，我们叫他领教了律法的可怕。不瞒你说，那天早晨，我们一伙人满腔怒火，围着他烟气腾腾，活像西奈山；亏得你有见识，赶在节骨眼上，把‘给人一个机会’的福音带了来。要我说，这是天底下顶好的福音。”

“呃，”我答道，一时让儿时的回忆勾得出了神，“我那位善良的老母亲教导过我：给我们机会，再帮我们把机会用到十足，这正是上帝的安排。”

“今天我想起了巴格利家，”琼斯太太朝我妻子点点头，说道。“我们觉得，他们应当得到鼓励。”

“我们也是这么想的，”我妻子低声应道。

===Original===
We afterward learned that the Bagleys had been provisioned for nearly a month by the goodwill of neighbors, who, a short time since, had been ready to take up arms against them.

By eight o’clock everything was cleared away, Mrs. Jones assisting my wife, and showing that she would be hurt if not permitted to do so. Then we all gathered around the glowing hearth, Junior’s *rat-a-tat-snap*! proving that our final course of nuts and cider would be provided in the usual way.

How homely it all was! how free from any attempt at display of style! yet equally free from any trace of vulgarity or ill-natured gossip. Mousie had added grace to the banquet with her blooming plants and dried grasses; and, although the dishes had been set on the table by my wife’s and children’s hands, they were daintily ornamented and inviting. All had been within our means and accomplished by ourselves; and the following morning brought no regretful thoughts. Our helpful friends went home, feeling that they had not bestowed their kindness on unthankful people whose scheme of life was to get and take, but not to return.

===Chinese===
我们后来才知道，巴格利一家靠着街坊们的好意，已经备下了差不多够吃一个月的口粮；而不久之前，这些街坊还恨不得抄起家伙来对付他们呢。

到八点钟，席面全收拾停当——琼斯太太帮着我妻子的忙，而且分明表示，若不许她插手，她是要伤心的。随后我们全都围拢到红彤彤的炉火旁；小琼斯那里*嗒嗒——咔*！地响成一片，证明我们最后一道坚果和苹果酒，正照老法子张罗出来。

这一切多么家常！全然没有一点摆阔炫排场的企图，却也全然没有一丝粗俗或恶意闲话的影子。小耗子用她那些开花的盆栽和干草穗给宴席添了几分雅致；菜盘虽是妻子和孩子们亲手端上桌的，却点缀得精致诱人。一切都量力而行，一切都亲手办成；第二天早晨也没有带来半点追悔的念头。我们那些古道热肠的朋友回家去了，心里觉得，这份好意并没有错施给不知感恩的人——那种人的活法只知捞取和领受，从想不到回报。

## XXI The “Vandoo” / XXI “拍卖会”

===Original===
The next morning at about eight o’clock Mr. Jones arrived in a long farm wagon on springs, with one seat in it; but Junior had half filled its body with straw, and he said to Merton, “I thought that p’raps, if you and the children could go, you’d like a straw ride.”

The solemnity with which Winnie and Bobsey promised to obey orders gave some hope of performance; so I tossed them into the straw, and we drove away, a merry party, leaving Mousie consoled with the hope of receiving something from the vendue.

“There’s allers changes and breakin’s up in the spring,” said Mr. Jones, as we drove along; “and this family’s goin’ out West. Everything is to be sold, in doors and out.”

The farmhouse in question was about two miles away. By the time we arrived, all sorts of vehicles were converging to it on the muddy roads, for the weather had become mild again. Stylish-looking people drove up in top buggies, and there were many heavy, springless wagons driven by rusty-looking countrymen, whose trousers were thrust into the top of their cowhide boots. I strolled through the house before the sale began, thinking that I might find something there which would please Mousie and my wife. The rooms were already half filled with the housewives from the vicinity; red-faced Irish women, who stalked about and examined everything with great freedom; placid, peach-cheeked dames in Quaker bonnets, who softly cooed together, and took every chance they could to say pleasant words to the flurried, nervous family that was being thrust out into the world, as it were, while still at their own hearth.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨八点钟光景，琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）赶着一辆装了弹簧的长身农用马车来了，车上只安着一个座位；小琼斯（Junior）却已在车斗里垫了半车稻草，还对默顿（Merton）说：“我琢磨着，兴许你们爷儿几个都能去，铺着稻草坐车，该挺乐呵的。”

温妮（Winnie）和鲍勃西（Bobsey）保证听话时那副一本正经的架势，倒叫人有几分指望他们真能做到；于是我把他们俩扔进稻草里，我们一行人欢欢喜喜地上路了，留下小耗子（Mousie）守在家里——好在她还盼着能从拍卖会上得点什么回来，心里也就得了安慰。

“开春嘛，总免不了些变动、散伙的事儿，”琼斯先生赶着车说；“这一家子要上西部去了。屋里屋外，样样都要卖掉。”

说的那座农舍在两英里开外。等我们赶到时，各式各样的车马正顺着泥泞的道路朝那里汇拢，天气又转和暖了。衣着体面的人驾着带篷的轻便马车翩然而至，还有许多笨重的、不带弹簧的大车，赶车的是些土头土脑的乡下汉子，裤脚都塞在牛皮靴筒里。开卖之前，我先在屋里转了一圈，想着兴许能碰上什么让小耗子和妻子喜欢的东西。屋里已经聚了半屋子左近的主妇：有红脸膛的爱尔兰妇人，大摇大摆地四处走动，样样东西都拿起细看，毫无拘束；也有头戴贵格会女帽、神态安然、面颊如蜜桃的太太们，彼此柔声细语地咕哝着，一有机会就对那仓皇失措、心神不宁的一家子说几句宽心话——这一家人，可以说还守在自家炉边，就已经被推搡到外面的世界去了。

===Original===
I marked with my eye a low, easy sewing chair for my wife, and a rose geranium, full of bloom, for Mousie, purposing to bid on them. I also observed that Junior was examining several pots of flowers that stood in the large south window. Then giving Merton charge of the children, with directions not to lose sight of them a moment, I went to the barnyard and stable, feeling that the day was a critical one in our fortunes. True enough, among the other stock there was a nice-looking cow with a calf, and Mr. Jones said she had Jersey blood in her veins. This meant rich, creamy milk. I thought the animal had a rather ugly eye, but this might be caused by anxiety for her calf, with so many strangers about. We also examined the old bay horse and a market wagon and harness. Then Mr. Jones and I drew apart and agreed upon the limit of his bids, for I proposed to act solely through him. Everyone knew him and was aware that he would not go a cent beyond what a thing was worth. He had a word and a jest for all, and “How *are you*, **John**?” greeted him wherever he went.

At ten o’clock the sale began. The auctioneer was a rustic humorist, who knew the practical value of a joke in his business. Aware of the foibles and characteristics of the people who flocked around and after him, he provoked many a ripple and roar of laughter by his telling hits and droll speeches. I found that my neighbor, Mr. Jones, came in for his full share, but he always sent back as good as he received. The sale, in fact, had the aspect of a country merrymaking, at which all sorts and conditions of people met on common ground, Pat bidding against the best of the landed gentry, while boys and dogs innumerable played around and sometimes verged on serious quarrels.

===Chinese===
我相中了一把矮矮的、坐着舒服的针线椅，打算给妻子；还有一盆繁花满枝的玫瑰天竺葵，打算给小耗子，打定主意要竞买这两样。我还留意到，小琼斯正在端详摆在朝南大窗前的几盆花。随后我把孩子们交给默顿照看，叮嘱他一刻也不能让他们离开视线，自己便往谷仓场院和牲口棚走去，心里觉得这一天关系到我们全家的前程，非同小可。果然，在一众牲口里，有一头模样周正的母牛，还带着一头小牛犊；琼斯先生说，它有着泽西牛的血统。这就意味着有浓稠、乳脂丰厚的牛奶。我看那牲口的眼睛长得有些凶，不过这也许是因为身边生人太多，它惦记着自己的小牛犊。我们还看了那匹老枣红马，以及一辆赶集用的马车和马具。然后琼斯先生同我避到一旁，商定了他出价的最高限额，因为我打算一切全由他出面。人人都认识他，也都知道他决不多出一分东西所值的钱。他对谁都能搭上一句话、开一个玩笑，走到哪儿都有人热络地招呼：“*你好*哇，**约翰**？”

十点钟，拍卖开场。拍卖师是个乡土味十足的滑稽角色，深知笑话在自己这行当里的实用价值。他摸透了那些围着他、追着他转的乡邻们的脾性与毛病，妙语连珠，俏皮话说个不停，惹得人群里时时荡起笑浪，间或爆出哄堂大笑。我发现我的邻居琼斯先生也挨了不少打趣，可他有来必有往，回敬得半点不吃亏。说实在的，这场拍卖活像一场乡间欢聚，三教九流济济一堂，平起平坐：帕特这样的爱尔兰人也敢和顶体面的乡绅地主争相抬价，孩子们和数不清的狗在四下里撒欢，有时眼看就要闹成真的争吵。

===Original===
Junior, I observed, left his mark before the day was over. He was standing, watching the sale with his usual impassive expression, when a big, hulking fellow leered into his face and cried.

> “Tow head, whitehead,
 
 Thickhead, go to bed.”

The last word was scarcely out of his mouth before Junior’s fist was between his eyes, and down he went.

“Want any more?” Junior coolly asked, as the fellow got up.

Evidently he didn’t, for he slunk off, followed by jeers and laughter.

===Chinese===
我注意到，这一天还没过完，小琼斯就已经让人实打实地记住了他。他正站在那儿，照旧是一副不动声色的神情看拍卖，忽然一条五大三粗的汉子凑到他脸前，斜着眼不怀好意地打量他，高声嚷道：

> “黄毛头，白毛头，
 
 笨头壳，睡觉喽。”

那句话还没说利索，小琼斯的拳头已经捣在他两眼中间，那人应声栽倒。

“还来吗？”那汉子爬起身来时，小琼斯冷冷地问。

显然他不想再来了，只见他灰溜溜地缩了回去，身后跟着一片讥笑和哄笑。

===Original===
At noon there was an immense pot of coffee with crackers and cheese, placed on a table near the kitchen door, and we had a free lunch. To this Bobsey paid his respects so industriously that a great, gawky mountaineer looked down at him and said, with a grin, “I say, young ’un, you’re gettin’ outside of more fodder than any critter of your size I ever knowed.”

“ ’Tain’t your fodder,” replied Bobsey, who had learned, in the streets, to be a little pert.

The day came to an end at last, and the cow and calf, the old bay horse, the wagon, and the harness were mine. On the whole, Mr. Jones had bought them at reasonable rates. He also bid in for me, at one dollar per pair, two cocks and twenty hens that looked fairly well in their coop.

For my part, I had secured the chair and blooming geranium. To my surprise, when the rest of the flowers were sold, Junior took part in the bidding for the first time, and, as a result, carried out to the wagon several other pots of houseplants.

===Chinese===
正午时分，厨房门边的桌上摆出一大壶咖啡，配上饼干和干酪，人人自便，吃一顿免费的午餐。鲍勃西对这顿午餐殷勤备至，埋头大吃，引得一个大高个、笨手笨脚的山里汉子低头望着他，咧嘴笑道：“我说，小家伙儿，俺活这么大，还没见过跟你一般大的活物，有比你更能往肚里扒拉草料的。”

“又不是你的草料，”鲍勃西回敬道；在街面上混过日子的他，嘴上已有几分不饶人了。

这一天总算到了头，母牛和牛犊、老枣红马、马车和马具都归了我。总的说来，这些东西琼斯先生买得价钱公道。他还替我拍进了两只公鸡、二十只母鸡，每对一美元，那些鸡在笼里看着都还挺精神。

至于我，也把那把椅子和那盆开得正艳的天竺葵拿到了手。叫我意外的是，其余的花卖到最后，小琼斯头一回也掺和进竞买，结果另外好几盆室内花草便由他抱出来，放上了马车。

===Original===
“Why, Junior,” I said, “I didn’t know you had such an eye for beauty.”

He blushed, but made no reply.

The chickens and the harness were put into Mr. Jones’s conveyance, the wagon I had bought was tied on behind, and we jogged homeward, the children exulting over our new possessions. When I took in the geranium bush and put it on the table by the sunny kitchen window, Junior followed with an armful of his plants.

“They’re for Mousie,” he said; and before the delighted child could thank him, he darted out.

Indeed, it soon became evident that Mousie was Junior’s favorite. She never said much to him, but she looked a great deal. To the little invalid girl he seemed the embodiment of strength and cleverness, and, perhaps because he was so strong, his sympathies went out toward the feeble child.

The coop of chickens was carried to the basement that we had made ready, and Winnie declared that she meant to “hear the first crow and get the first egg.”

===Chinese===
“嗬，小琼斯，”我说，“真没瞧出来，你还有这样一双识美的眼睛呢。”

他红了脸，却没有作声。

鸡和马具装上了琼斯先生的车，我买下的那辆马车拴在车后，我们慢悠悠地一路往家去，孩子们为这些新添的家当欢喜雀跃。我把那盆天竺葵抱进屋，摆到向阳的厨房窗边的桌上，小琼斯抱着一怀他自己的花草跟了进来。

“这些是给小耗子的，”他说；那孩子欢喜得还没来得及道谢，他一溜烟又出去了。

的确，没过多久就看得分明，小耗子是小琼斯最心疼的人。她同他话不多，眼睛里的话却不少。在这个体弱多病的小女孩看来，他就是力气和能耐的化身；也许正因为他自己那样壮实，他的同情心反倒格外向着这个孱弱的孩子。

那笼鸡抬进了我们早已收拾停当的地下室，温妮宣称，她非要“听头一声鸡叫、捡头一个鸡蛋”不可。

===Original===
The next day the horse and the cow and calf were brought over, and we felt that we were fairly launched in our country life.

“You have a bigger family to look after outdoors than I have indoors,” my wife said, laughingly.

I was not long in learning that some of my outdoor family were anything but amiable. The two cocks fought and fought until Junior, who had run over before night, showed Merton that by ducking their heads in cold water their belligerent spirit could be partially quenched. Then he proceeded to give me a lesson in milking. The calf was shut up away from the cow, which was driven into a corner, where she stood with signs of impatience while Junior, seated on a three-legged stool, essayed to obtain the nectar we all so dearly loved. At first he did not succeed very well.

“She won’t let it down—she’s keepin’ it for the calf,” said the boy. But at last she relented, and the white streams flowed. “Now,” said Junior to me, “you see how I do it. You try.”

===Chinese===
第二天，马和母牛、牛犊也牵了过来，我们觉得自己算是稳稳当当地在乡间生活里扬帆启程了。

“你在屋外照看的这一大家子，可比我在屋里的还要多呢，”妻子笑着说。

没过多久我就发现，屋外这些家眷里头，有的可半点和善都谈不上。那两只公鸡斗个没完，直到小琼斯赶在天黑前跑了过来，才教给默顿一个法子：把它们的脑袋往冷水里一浸，那股好斗的火气便能压下去几分。接着他又着手教我挤奶。小牛犊同母牛分开关了起来，母牛被赶进一个角落，站在那里，一副不耐烦的样子；小琼斯坐在一张三脚小凳上，试着去取我们大家都那么珍爱的琼浆。起头他挤得并不怎么顺。

“它不肯下奶呢——它把奶留给小牛犊了，”男孩说。可到末了它还是心软了，白花花的奶水汩汩而流。“瞧着，”小琼斯对我说，“我就是这样挤的。你来试试。”

===Original===
As I took his place, I noticed that Brindle turned on me a vicious look. No doubt I was awkward and hurt her a little, also; for the first thing I knew the pail was in the air, I on my back, and Brindle bellowing around the yard, switching her tail, Junior and Merton meanwhile roaring with laughter. I got up in no amiable mood and said, roughly, to the boys, “Quit that nonsense.”

But they couldn’t obey, and at last I had to join in the laugh.

“Why, she’s ugly as sin,” said Junior. “I’ll tell you what to do. Let her go with her calf now, and in the morning we’ll drive her down to one of the stalls in the basement of the barn and fasten her by the head. Then we can milk her without risk. After her calf is gone she’ll be a great deal tamer.”

This plan was carried out, and it worked pretty well, although it was evident that, from some cause, the cow was wild and vicious. One of my theories is, that all animals can be subdued by kindness. Mr. Jones advised me to dispose of Brindle, but I determined to test my theory first. Several times a day I would go to the barnyard and give her a carrot or a whisp of hay from my hand, and she gradually became accustomed to me, and would come at my call. A week later I sold her calf to a butcher, and for a few days she lowed and mourned deeply, to Mousie’s great distress. But carrots consoled her, and within three weeks she would let me stroke her, and both Merton and I could milk her without trouble. I believe she had been treated harshly by her former owners.

===Chinese===
我接过他的位子坐下，发觉花斑儿（Brindle）朝我翻起一个凶巴巴的眼色。无疑，我手法笨拙，也把它弄疼了一点；冷不防眨眼之间，奶桶飞上了半空，我四仰八叉摔在地上，花斑儿哞哞叫着满院子乱转，尾巴甩个不停，小琼斯和默顿在旁边笑得直打滚。我爬起来，满肚子的和气荡然无存，粗声粗气地对两个男孩喝道：“别胡闹了。”

可能们实在憋不住，到头来我只得自己也跟着大笑一场。

“唉，它凶得要命，”小琼斯说。“我给你出个主意。这会儿先让它带着小牛犊，明儿一早，我们把它赶下谷仓地下室的一格牛栏里，把头拴牢。那样挤奶就没有风险了。等小牛犊一离手，它还会温驯得多。”

这个法子照办了，收效相当不错，尽管看得出，这头牛不知为什么，野性又凶顽。我有个信条：一切牲畜都能以善意驯服。琼斯先生劝我把花斑儿处理掉，我却决意先验证一下自己的信条。每天我要到谷仓场院去好几趟，从手心里喂它一根胡萝卜，或一小束干草；它渐渐同我熟了，一唤就到。一个星期后，我把小牛犊卖给了屠户，接连几天，它悲声哞叫，哀伤不已，看得小耗子难受极了。好在胡萝卜给了它安慰；不到三个星期，它便肯任我抚摩了，默顿和我挤它的奶都毫无困难。我想，它从前的主人待它一定很苛刻。

## XLIV We Can Make a Living in Eden / XLIV 我们能在伊甸园挣得生计

===Original===
Well, our first year was drawing to a close. The 1st of December was celebrated by an event no less momentous than the killing of our pigs, to Winnie’s and Bobsey’s intense excitement. In this affair my wife and I were almost helpless, but Mr. Jones and Bagley were on hand, and proved themselves veterans, while Mrs. Jones stood by my wife until the dressed animals were transformed into souse, headcheese, sausage, and well-salted pork. The children feasted and exulted through all the processes, especially enjoying some sweet spareribs.

I next gave all my attention, when the weather permitted, to the proper winter covering of all the strawberries, and to the cutting and carting home of old and dying trees from the wood lot.

The increasing cold brought new and welcome pleasures to the children. There was ice on the neighboring ponds, and skates were bought as premature Christmas presents. The same was true of sleds after the first fall of snow. This white covering of the earth enabled Merton and Junior to track some rabbits in the vicinity, which thus far had eluded their search.

===Chinese===
就这样，我们的头一个年头眼看要过完了。十二月一日那天，我们家以一桩大事来庆贺，这事的隆重，不亚于宰我们自家养的猪，惹得温妮（Winnie）和鲍勃西（Bobsey）兴奋万分。这桩事情上，我和妻子几乎帮不上手，好在琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）和巴格利（Bagley）都在跟前，一出手便显出是老把式；琼斯太太则守在我妻子身旁，直到宰好收拾停当的那几口猪，统统变作了醋渍肉、头肉冻、香肠和腌得透透的咸肉。孩子们在道道工序里又是大嚼又是欢呼，尤其美美地享用了几根香甜的肋条。

接着，趁天公作美，我把全副心思放在两件事上：一是给全部草莓盖好妥帖的越冬覆盖物，二是把林子里那些老树枯树砍倒，一车一车拉回家来。

寒意日甚一日，却给孩子们捎来了新的、受欢迎的乐子。附近的池塘上了冰，冰鞋当作提前的圣诞礼物买了回来；头场雪一落，又照方抓药置办了雪橇。大地上这一层白色的覆盖，让默顿（Merton）和小琼斯（Junior）得以在左近追踪几只野兔——这些兔子先前一直躲着他们的搜寻，未曾落网。

===Original===
By the middle of the month we realized that winter had begun in all its rather stern reality; but we were sheltered and provided for. We had so far imitated the ants that we had abundant stores until the earth should again yield its bounty.

Christmas brought us more than its wonted joy, and a better fulfilment of the hopes and anticipations which we had cherished on the same day of the previous year. We were far from regretting our flight to the country, although it had involved us in hard toil and many anxieties. My wife was greatly pleased by my many hours of rest at the fireside in her companionship, caused by days too cold and wintry for outdoor work; but our deepest and most abiding content was expressed one evening as we sat alone after the children were asleep.

“You have solved the problem, Robert, that was worrying you. There is space here for the children to grow, and the Daggetts and the Ricketts and all their kind are not so near as to make them grow wrong, almost in spite of us. A year ago we felt that we were virtually being driven to the country. I now feel as if we had been led by a kindly and divine hand.” I had given much attention to my account book of late, and had said, “On New Year’s morning I will tell you all the result of our first year’s effort.”

===Chinese===
到了月中，我们意识到，冬天已带着它那相当严峻的真面目开场了；可我们有屋遮身，有粮备足。到此为止，我们一直效法蚂蚁的榜样储藏，存下的物什丰丰富富，尽可支撑到大地重新慷慨出产的那一天。

圣诞节给我们带来的欢乐胜过往年，去年同一天我们怀抱的那些希望与憧憬，这一年也有了更好的应验。对于自己这场逃往乡下的行动，我们非但没有半点懊悔，尽管它让我们陷进了繁重的劳苦与重重忧虑。那些冷得没法干户外活的日子，让我得着好些炉边安坐的时辰，又有妻子在旁作伴，她为此大为高兴；而我们最深切、最绵长的满足，则在一天晚上吐露了出来——那时孩子们已睡熟，屋里只剩我们两个静静地坐着。

“罗伯特（Robert），那个一直愁得你不安的难题，你已经解开了。这里地方宽敞，孩子们尽可以舒舒展展地长；达格茨家（the Daggetts）和里克茨家（the Ricketts）以及他们那一类人家，住得都不算近，不致把孩子带得长歪，这一点几乎不消我们操心。一年前，我们只觉得自己实际上是被逼到乡下来的；如今我却觉得，我们像是被一只仁慈而神圣的手引领着，走到了这里。”近来我把不少心思用在账本上，还说过一句话：‘到新年那天早晨，我要把咱们头一年劳作的成绩，原原本本讲给你听。’

===Original===
At breakfast, after our greetings and good wishes for the New Year, all looked expectantly at me as I opened our financial record. Carefully and clearly as possible, so that even Winnie might understand in part, I went over the different items, and the expense and proceeds of the different crops, so far as I was able to separate them. Bobsey’s attention soon wandered, for he had an abiding faith that breakfast, dinner, and supper would follow the sun, and that was enough for him. But the other children were pleased with my confidence, and tried to understand me.

“To sum up everything,” I said, finally, “we have done, by working all together, what I alone should probably have accomplished in the city—*we have made our living*. I have also taken an inventory or an account of stock on hand and paid for; that is, I have here a list on which are named the horse, wagon, harness, cow, crates and baskets, tools, poultry, and pigs. These things are paid for, and we are so much ahead. Now, children, which is better, a living in the city, I earning it for you all? or a living in the country toward which even Bobsey can do his share?”

“A living in the country,” was the prompt chorus. “There is something here for a fellow to do without being nagged by a policeman,” Merton added.

===Chinese===
新年早晨，贺过喜、道过祝愿之后，我翻开我们的财务账册，一家人都满眼期待地望着我。我尽量讲得又仔细又明白，好叫连温妮也能听懂个大概——凡是我能分开归账的，我都把一项项账目、各样庄稼的开销和收益，一一数给他们听。鲍勃西的注意力没多久就飘走了，因为他有一个雷打不动的信念：早饭、午饭、晚饭自会一场接一场跟着太阳来，有这一条他就心满意足了。可另外几个孩子很受用我对他们的这份信任，都努力想听懂我的话。

“把一切归结起来，”我终于说道，“咱们全家一齐出力，做成的事，大约就是我在城里一个人挣到的那些——*我们挣得了自己的生计*。我还盘了一份家底，立了一册现有并已付清的财产账；就是说，这里有一张单子，开列着马、大车、马具、奶牛、板条箱和篮筐、各色农具、家禽和猪。这些东西全都付清了钱，这便是我们净赚下的家当。好了，孩子们，你们说哪样更好：是城里的生计，由我一个人挣钱养活你们大家？还是乡下的生计，为着它，连鲍勃西也能出上自己的一份力？”

“乡下的生计！”大家不假思索，异口同声。“在这儿，小伙子有的是正经事可做，还没有警察在耳边絮叨个没完，”默顿补上一句。

===Original===
“Well, children, mamma and I agree with you. What’s more, there wasn’t much chance for me to get ahead in the city, or earn a large salary. Here, by pulling all together, there is almost a certainty of our earning more than a bare living, and of laying up something for a rainy day. The chief item of profit from our farm, however, is not down in my account book, but we see it in your sturdier forms and in Mousie’s red cheeks. More than all, we believe that you are better and healthier at heart than you were a year ago.

“Now for the New Year. Let us make the best and most of it, and ask God to help us.”

And so my simple history ends in glad content and hope.

===Chinese===
“好了，孩子们，妈妈和我都跟你们想法一样。况且，在城里，我本来就没有多少出头的指望，也挣不来大薪水。在这儿，只要全家拧成一股绳，我们几乎十拿九稳能挣到不止糊口之资，还能为不时有雨的日子攒下几个钱。不过，我们农场最大的一项收益，并没有记在我的账本上，它显在你们更加壮实的身板里，显在小耗子（Mousie）红扑扑的脸蛋上。而最要紧的是，我们相信，比起一年前，你们的心地更善良、也更健康了。”

“现在，说新年。让我们把这一年过得最好、最充实，并祈求上帝帮助我们。”

就这样，我这部朴素的传记，在欢喜、知足与盼望里收束了。

## XXIX We Go A-Fishing / XXIX 我们去钓鱼

===Original===
The following day, happily, proved all that we could desire. The children were up with the dawn, and Junior was not long in joining us. By eight o’clock we had finished breakfast and the morning work, our lunch basket was packed, and the market wagon stood at the door. Mr. Jones had good-naturedly promised to take a look at the premises occasionally to see that all was right. I had put but one seat in the wagon for my wife and myself, since the young people decided that a straw ride to the river would be “more fun than a parlor car.”

My wife entered into the spirit of this little outing with a zest which gave me deep content. Her face indicated no regretful thoughts turning toward the Egypt of the city; her mother love was so strong that she was happy with the children. The robins, of which there seemed no end about the house, gave us a tuneful and hilarious sendoff; the grown people and children whom we met smiled and cheered, following us with envious eyes. Each of the children held a pole aloft, and Merton said that “the wagon looked as if our lima bean patch was off on a visit.”

===Chinese===
第二天，真个天遂人愿，一切正如我们所盼。孩子们天一亮就起了床，小琼斯（Junior）也没过多久就来同我们会合。到八点钟，早饭和晨间的活计都已做完，午饭篮子装好了，赶集马车也停在了门口。琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）和和气气地应承下来，不时照看一眼宅子，看看一切是否如常。车上我只给妻子和我自己安了一个座位，因为年轻人认定，坐干草车到河边去，要比头等客厅车厢“更好玩”。

妻子把这次小小出游的兴味领会得十足，那份兴致让我深感快慰。她脸上看不出丝毫回望城市——那座“埃及”——的怅惘；她的母爱如此深厚，同孩子们在一起便觉快乐。宅子四周的知更鸟似乎多得没有尽头，啭鸣欢腾地给我们送行；一路遇见的大人小孩都笑着欢呼，用艳羡的目光目送我们。每个孩子都高高举着一根钓竿，默顿（Merton）说，这车看上去“就像我们那片利马豆地出门做客去了”。

===Original===
In the village we increased our stock of lines and hooks, and bought a few corks for floats. We soon reached the mouth of the Moodna Creek, where stood a weather-beaten boathouse, with a stable adjoining, in which old Bay could enjoy himself in his quiet, prosaic way. A good-sized boat was hired, and, as the tide was in, we at first decided to go up the creek as far as possible and float down with the ebb. This, to the children, was like a voyage of discovery, and there was a general airing of geography, each little bay, point, and gulf receiving some noted name. At last we reached a deep, shaded pool, which was eventually dubbed “Bobsey’s Luck;” for he nearly fell into it in his eagerness to take off a minnow that had managed to fasten itself to his hook.

Merton and Junior, being more experienced anglers, went ashore to make some casts on the ripples and rapids of the stream above, and secured several fine “winfish.” The rest of us were content to take it easy in the shade and hook an occasional cat and sun fish. At last the younger children wanted variety, so I permitted them to land on the wooded bank, kindle a little fire, and roast some clams that we had bought at the boathouse. The smoke and the tempting odors lured Merton and Junior, who soon proved that boys’ appetites can always be depended upon.

===Chinese===
在村子里，我们添置了几副钓线和鱼钩，又买了几只做浮子的软木塞。不多时就到了穆德纳溪（Moodna Creek）的溪口；那里立着一座饱经风霜的船屋，旁边连着马厩，好让老枣红马（old Bay）在里头照它那安安静静、平平常常的性子自得其乐。我们雇了一条不小的船；正赶上涨潮，起初大家决定尽量往溪的上游划，再趁退潮漂下来。在孩子们看来，这简直就是一场发现新大陆的航行，人人卖弄起地理学问来，每一处小湾、小岬、小汊都被安上某个响当当的名号。最后我们到了一泓荫翳的深潭，这潭后来得了个“鲍勃西的运气”（Bobsey’s Luck）的名号；因为他急着去摘一条死死咬在钩上的小鱼，险些一头栽进潭里。

默顿和小琼斯是老练些的钓手，便上了岸，到上游波纹荡漾、水流湍急的地方甩了几竿，钓着了好几条上好的“白鲦”。我们其余的人安于在树荫下悠然自得，偶尔钓起一条鲇鱼或太阳鱼，也就心满意足。到后来，小些的孩子想换换花样，我便许他们登上林木葱茏的岸坡，生起一小堆火，烤几只在船屋买的蛤蜊吃。炊烟和馋人的香气把默顿和小琼斯也勾了过去；这两位随即证明，男孩子的胃口任何时候都靠得住。

===Original===
Time passed rapidly, and I at last noticed that the tide had fallen to such a degree as to fill me with alarm.

“Come, youngsters,” I cried, “we must go back at once, or we shall have to stay here till almost night.”

They scrambled on board, and we started downstream, but soon came to shallow water, as was proved by the swift current and the ripples. A moment later we were hard aground. In vain we pushed with the oars; the boat would not budge. Then Junior sat down and coolly began to take off shoes and stockings. In a flash Merton followed his example. There was no help for it, and we had no time to lose. Over they splashed, lightening the boat, and taking the “painter,” or tie rope, at the bow, they pulled manfully. Slowly at first, but with increasing progress, the keel grated over the stones, and at last we were again afloat. A round of applause greeted the boys as they sprung back into the boat, and away we went, cautiously avoiding shoals and sandbars, until we reached Plum Point, where we expected to spend the remainder of the day. Here, for a time, we had excellent sport, and pulled up sunfish and white perch of a very fair size. Bobsey caught so large a specimen of the former variety that he had provided himself with a supper equal even to his capacity.

===Chinese===
时光过得飞快，我终于察觉，潮水已退得叫我暗暗心惊。

“喂，小家伙们，”我喊道，“咱们必须马上往回走，不然就得困在这儿，一直待到天快黑了。”

他们连爬带跳地上了船，我们便顺流而下；可是不久水流湍急、波纹乍起，表明水浅了。转眼间，船就结结实实地搁了浅。我们用桨推了又推，全然白费，船纹丝不动。这时小琼斯坐了下来，不动声色地开始脱鞋褪袜。默顿一转眼也照办起来。事已至此别无他法，而且一刻也耽搁不得。两个孩子噗通噗通蹚进水里，给船减了载，又抓住船头的“painter”——就是系船的缆绳——使出全身力气往前拖。龙骨起先慢慢蹭着石子挪动，越走越顺，我们终于重新浮了起来。两个孩子纵身跳回船上时，迎来一片喝彩；我们随即继续下行，小心翼翼地避开浅滩和沙洲，来到普拉姆角（Plum Point）——我们打算在这里消磨这天余下的时光。这地方有一阵子鱼汛极佳，我们拉上来的太阳鱼和白鲈个头都相当可观。鲍勃西钓着的那条太阳鱼尤其大，单是这一条，就为他备下了一顿连他那副肚量也对付得了的晚饭。

===Original===
The day ended in unalloyed pleasure, and never had the old farmhouse looked so like home as when it greeted us again in the evening glow of the late spring sun. Merton and Junior divided the finny spoils to their satisfaction, while Winnie and I visited the chicken coops and found that there had been no mishaps during our absence. I told my boy that I would milk the cow while he cleaned the fish for supper, and when at last we sat down we formed a tired, hilarious, and hungry group. Surely, if fish were created to be eaten, our enjoyment of their browned sweetness must have rounded out their existence completely.

“O papa!” exclaimed Merton, at the breakfast table, on Monday morning; “we haven’t planted any musk and water melons!”

“That is true,” I replied. “I find that I overlooked melons in making out my list of seeds. Indeed, I passed them over, I imagine, as a luxury that we could dispense with the first year.”

===Chinese===
这一天在毫无杂质的欢乐中收了尾；当老农舍在暮春夕阳的余晖里再度迎候我们时，它从来没有显得这么像个家。默顿和小琼斯把那一堆鳞光闪闪的渔获分得两下称心，温妮（Winnie）则随我去看了鸡舍，见我们不在的这段工夫没出一点儿岔子。我对儿子说，他去收拾晚饭吃的鱼，我来挤牛奶；等末了大家坐下来，凑成的是又疲惫、又欢腾、又饥饿的一群。说真的，倘若鱼生来就是要给人吃的，那么我们品尝它们烤得焦黄的鲜美的这番享受，想必也把它们的鱼生成全得十全十美了。

“哎呀，爸爸！”星期一早晨的早饭桌上，默顿嚷了起来，“咱们还没种香瓜和西瓜呢！”

“这话不假，”我答道，“我发觉拟种子单子的时候把瓜漏掉了。说来，我想当时是把它们当成了奢侈品，寻思头一年不种也过得去，就这么放过去了。”

===Original===
“I’ll take care of ’em if you will only let us have some,” persisted the boy; and the other children joined in his request.

“But the garden is all filled up,” I said, thoughtfully; “and I fear it is too late to plant now.”

Looks of disappointment led me to think further and I got one of my seed catalogues.

“Here are some early kinds named and perhaps they would mature; but where shall we put them?”

===Chinese===
“只要您肯让我们种点儿，往后就都归我来照管，”男孩缠着不放；其余的孩子也一齐帮他央求。

“可园子已经种得满满的了，”我沉吟着说，“而且我怕现在下种已经太迟。”

一张张失望的小脸促使我接着往下想，我便取来一册种子目录。

“这儿列着几个早熟品种，也许还赶得上成熟；可咱们把它们种到哪儿去呢？”

===Original===
“Seems to me we had better have a little less corn, if room can be made for melons,” was Merton’s suggestion.

“I’ll tell you what we’ll do,” I continued. “We’ve had such good fortune in accomplishing our early work, and you have helped so nicely, that you shall try your hand at melons. Drive your mother and Mousie down to the village this morning, and get some seeds of the nutmeg muskmelon and Phinney’s early watermelon. I’ll take two rows in the early corn on the warm garden slope, pull up every third hill, and make, in their places, nice, warm, rich beds for the seed which we will plant as soon as you come back. I don’t believe the corn will shade the melon vines too much; and as soon as we have taken off the green ears we will cut away the stalks. Thus we shall get two crops from the same ground.”

This plan was carried out, and the melon seed came up in a very promising way.

===Chinese===
“要我说，要是能腾出地方种瓜，玉米少种点儿也使得，”这是默顿出的主意。

“我看这么办，”我接着说。“咱们早先的活计办得这样顺当，你们又帮得这样像样，这一回就由你们试着种瓜吧。今天上午赶车送你妈妈和小耗子（Mousie）到村里去，买些肉豆蔻香瓜（nutmeg muskmelon）和菲尼早熟西瓜（Phinney’s early watermelon）的种子。我在菜园向阳坡的早玉米里要上两行，每隔两穴拔掉一穴，在原处弄成又暖和又肥沃的好苗床，等你们一回来就把种子播下去。我不信玉米会给瓜藤遮太多荫；再说一等掰下嫩玉米棒子，我们就把秸秆砍掉。这样，同一块地就能收两茬。”

这个计划照办了，瓜种出苗的情形也十分喜人。

## XXXV “We Shall All Earn Our Salt” / XXXV “我们都得挣出自己的口粮”

===Original===
Raspberries and milk, with bread and butter and a cup of tea, made a supper that we all relished, and then Merton and I started for the boat landing. I let the boy drive and deliver the crates to the freight agent, for I wished him to relieve me of this task occasionally. On our way to the landing I saw Rollins, who readily agreed to Bagley’s wish, on condition that I guaranteed payment for the chickens. Stopping at the man’s cottage further on, I told him this, and he, in his emphatic way, declared: “I vow ter you, Mr. Durham, ye shan’t lose a feather’s worth o’ the chickens.”

Returning home, poor Merton was so tired and drowsy that he nearly fell off the seat. Before long I took the reins from his hands, and he was asleep with his head on my shoulder. Winifred was dozing in her chair, but brightened up as we came in. A little judicious praise and a bowl of bread and milk strengthened the boy wonderfully. He saw the need of especial effort at this time, and also saw that he was not being driven unfeelingly.

As I sat alone with my wife, resting a few minutes before retiring, I said: “Well, Winifred, it must be plain to you by this time that the summer campaign will be a hard one. How are we going to stand it?”

===Chinese===
树莓和牛奶，配上面包黄油和一杯茶，这顿晚饭我们个个吃得津津有味；随后，默顿（Merton）和我便动身去船码头。我让男孩赶车，把板条箱交给货运员，因为我希望他不时替我分担这份差事。去码头的路上，我遇见了罗林斯（Rollins），他爽快地应承了巴格利（Bagley）的心愿，条件是由我担保那笔鸡钱。再往前，我在那人的小屋前停了停，把这话告诉了他；他用他那副斩钉截铁的腔调宣称：“俺对您起誓，达勒姆先生（Mr. Durham），这些鸡您一根羽毛的价钱也折损不了。”

回家的路上，可怜的默顿又乏又困，险些从座位上栽下来。不多时我便从他手里接过缰绳，他头枕着我的肩膀睡着了。温妮弗蕾德（Winifred）正在椅子里打盹，见我们进门，便又精神起来。几句恰到好处的夸奖，外加一碗面包泡牛奶，让男孩的气力奇妙地恢复了。他看得出，眼下这个节骨眼需要格外出力；也看得出，自己并非被人无情地驱赶着。

就寝前几分钟，我独自陪妻子坐着歇息，说道：“好了，温妮弗蕾德，事到如今，你总该看得明白，今年夏天这场战役必将是一场硬仗。咱们可怎么撑得过去？”

===Original===
“I’ll tell you next fall,” she replied, with a laugh. “No problems tonight, thank you.”

“I’m gathering a queer lot of helpers in my effort to live in the country,” I continued. “There’s old Mr. Jacox, who is too aged to hold his own in other harvest fields. Bagley and his tribe—”

“And a city wife and a lot of city children,” she added.

“And a city greenhorn of a man at the head of you all,” I concluded.

===Chinese===
“等到明年秋天再告诉你吧，”她笑着答道，“难题嘛，今晚就免了，多谢。”

“我这乡下安家的营生，招来的帮手真是一班怪角色，”我接着说，“有老杰科克斯先生（Mr. Jacox），年纪老得在旁的收割场上已站不住脚；有巴格利和他那一族人——”

“外加一个城里媳妇和一群城里娃娃，”她补上一句。

“领着你们大家的，还是个城里的生瓜蛋子，”我收住话头。

===Original===
“Well,” she replied, rising with an odd little blending of laugh and yawn, “I’m not afraid but that we shall all earn our salt.”

Thus came to an end the long, eventful day, which prepared the way for many others of similar character, and suggested many of the conditions of our problem of country living.

Bagley appeared bright and early the following morning with his two elder children, and I was now confronted with the task of managing them and making them useful. Upon one thing I was certainly resolved—there should be no quixotic sentiment in our relations, and no companionship between his children and mine.

===Chinese===
“嗯，”她应道，一边站起身来，笑和呵欠古怪地搀作一团，“我可不担心咱们挣不出各自的那份口粮。”

这个漫长而多事的日子就此落幕；它为日后许多同样性质的日子开了路，也让我们掂量出乡居生活这道难题的种种条件。

第二天一大早，巴格利便带着他两个大些的孩子来了，我于是面临着管束他们、让他们派上用场的课题。有一件事我是打定了主意的——我们的关系里决不容有堂吉诃德式的滥情，他的孩子和我的孩子之间也决不容结伴厮混。

===Original===
Therefore, I took him and his girl and boy aside, and said: “I’m going to be simple and outspoken with you. Some of my neighbors think I’m a fool because I give you work when I can get others. I shall prove that I am not a fool, for the reason that I shall not permit any nonsense, and you can show that I am not a fool by doing your work well and quietly. Bagley, I want you to understand that your children do not come here to play with mine. No matter whom I employed, I should keep my children by themselves. Now, do you understand this?”

They nodded affirmatively.

“Are you all willing to take simple, straightforward directions, and do your best? I’m not asking what is unreasonable, for I shall not be more strict with you than with my own children.”

“No use o’ beatin’ around the bush, Mr. Durham,” said Bagley, good-naturedly; “we’ve come here to ’arn our livin’, and to do as you say.”

===Chinese===
于是我把他和他的女孩、男孩拉到一旁，说：“我要跟你们把话说得简简单单、直来直去。明明能雇到别人，我却把活儿给你们做，有些邻居因此当我是傻瓜。我要证明自己不是傻瓜，办法是不容许任何胡闹；你们呢，把活儿干得又好又安分，也就证明了我不是傻瓜。巴格利，我要你明白，你的孩子到这儿来，不是来跟我的孩子玩的。不管我雇的是谁，我的孩子都会自家过自家的。现在，这话你听明白了没有？”

他们点头应是。

“你们都愿意听简单直截的吩咐、尽力去做吗？我要求的并非不讲道理的事，因为我不会比对我自己的孩子更严厉地对待你们。”

“甭拐弯抹角啦，达勒姆先生，”巴格利和和气气地说，“俺们到这儿来就是挣饭吃的，您咋说俺们咋做。”

===Original===
“I can get along with you, Bagley, but your children will find it hard to follow my rules, because they are children, and are not used to restraint. Yet they must do it, or there’ll be trouble at once. They must work quietly and steadily while they do work, and when I am through with them, they must go straight home. They mustn’t lounge about the place. If they will obey, Mrs. Durham and I will be good friends to them, and by fall we will fix them up so that they can go to school.”

The little arabs looked askance at me and made me think of two wild animals that had been caught, and were intelligent enough to understand that they must be tamed. They were submissive, but made no false pretences of enjoying the prospect.

“I shall keep a gad handy,” said their father, with a significant nod at them.

“Well, youngsters,” I concluded, laughing, “perhaps you’ll need it occasionally. I hope not, however. I shall keep no gad, but I shall have an eye on you when you least expect it; and if you go through the picking season well, I shall have a nice present for you both. Now, you are to receive so much a basket, if the baskets are properly filled, and therefore it will depend on yourselves how much you earn. You shall be paid every day. So now for a good start toward becoming a man and a woman.”

I led them to one side of the raspberry patch and put them under Merton’s charge saying, “You must pick exactly as he directs.”

===Chinese===
“我同你处得来，巴格利，可你的孩子会觉得守我的规矩不容易，因为他们毕竟是孩子，不习惯受拘束。可他们非做到不可，不然马上就会闹出乱子。干活的当口，就得安安静静、踏踏实实地干；我打发他们收工，他们就得径直回家，不许在宅子里闲逛荡。只要他们肯听话，达勒姆太太和我都会好好地待他们，到秋天还要把他们收拾齐整，好送他们上学去。”

这两个小野孩子斜着眼瞟我，让我想起两头被逮住的野兽——它们倒够机灵，明白自己非驯服不可。它们一副驯顺的样子，却也毫不虚情假意地装出喜欢这番前景的模样。

“俺会随手备一根赶牲口的刺棒，”他们的父亲说，冲他们意味深长地点了点头。

“好啦，小家伙们，”我笑着收场，“说不定你们偶尔也用得着它，不过我但愿用不着。我这儿不留刺棒，却会在你们最料不到的时候盯住你们；摘果这一季你们要是表现得好，我还要给你们两个一人一份漂亮的礼物。要知道，你们的工钱按篮子计，篮子装得合乎规矩才作数，能挣多少全看你们自己。工钱每天现结。好，往长成男子汉和大姑娘的路上，现在就迈出漂亮的第一步吧。”

我把他们领到树莓地的一头，交给默顿管带，说：“你们必须完全照他的指点摘。”

===Original===
Winnie and Bobsey were to pick in another part of the field, Mousie aiding until the sun grew too warm for the delicate child. Bagley was to divide his time between hoeing in the garden and spreading the grass after the scythe of old Mr. Jacox. From my ladder against a cherry tree, I was able to keep a general outlook over my motley forces, and we all made good progress till dinner, which, like the help we employed, we now had at twelve o’clock. Bagley and his children sat down to their lunch under the shade of an apple tree at some distance, yet in plain view through our open door. Their repast must have been meagre, judging from the time in which it was despatched, and my wife said, “Can’t I send them something?”

“Certainly; what have you to send?”

“Well, I’ve made a cherry pudding; I don’t suppose there is much more than enough for us, though.”

“Children,” I cried, “let’s take a vote. Shall we share our cherry pudding with the Bagleys?”

“Yes,” came the unanimous reply, although Bobsey’s voice was rather faint.

===Chinese===
温妮（Winnie）和鲍勃西（Bobsey）在地里另一头摘，小耗子（Mousie）在一旁帮衬，直到日头晒得那娇弱的孩子受不住为止。巴格利则把工夫分成两半，一半在菜园里锄草，一半跟在老杰科克斯先生的大镰刀后面摊晒割下的草。我靠着一架斜倚樱桃树的梯子，把这支杂牌军尽收眼底；到吃正餐时大家都进展顺利，而正餐呢，同我们雇的帮工一样，如今也改在十二点吃。巴格利和他的孩子们在离得老远的一棵苹果树荫下坐定，吃他们的午饭，可透过我们敞开的门仍看得一清二楚。瞧他们收拾那顿饭的工夫，就知道这顿饭准是寒素的；妻子便说：“能不能给他们送点什么去？”

“当然可以；你有什么可送的？”

“嗯，我做了一个樱桃布丁；只怕除了咱们自己吃的，也多不出多少了。”

“孩子们，”我嚷道，“咱们来个举手表决。咱们的樱桃布丁，分不分给巴格利家？”

“分！”众口一声地答道，只是鲍勃西的嗓音未免有气无力。

===Original===
Merton carried the delicacy to the group under the tree, and it was gratefully and speedily devoured.

“That is the way to the hearts of those children,” said my wife, at the same time slyly slipping her portion of the pudding upon Bobsey’s plate.

I appeared very blind, but asked her to get me something from the kitchen. While she was gone, I exchanged my plate of pudding, untouched as yet, for hers, and gave the children a wink. We all had a great laugh over mamma’s well-assumed surprise and perplexity. How a little fun will freshen up children, especially when, from necessity, their tasks are long and heavy!

===Chinese===
默顿把这份美味送到树荫下那一伙人跟前，转眼间就被感激不尽地一扫而光。

“要得这些孩子的心，就得这么走，”妻子说，一边悄悄把她自己那份布丁拨到鲍勃西的盘子里。

我装出一副视而不见的样子，却请她去厨房替我拿点东西。她一走开，我就把我那盘还没动过的布丁同她那盘换了个个儿，又朝孩子们眨了眨眼。妈妈那副装得惟妙惟肖的惊讶和惶惑，惹得我们个个大笑。一点点玩笑多么能使孩子焕然一新啊，尤其是当他们的活计又长又重、原属迫不得已的时候！

===Original===
We were startled from the table by a low mutter of thunder. Hastening out, I saw an ominous cloud in the west. My first thought was that all should go to the raspberries and pick till the rain drove us in; but Bagley now proved a useful friend, for he shambled up and said: “If I was you, I’d have those cherries picked fust. You’ll find that a thundershower’ll rot ’em in one night. The wet won’t hurt the berries much.”

His words reminded me of what I had seen when a boy—a tree full of split, half-decayed cherries—and I told him to go to picking at once. I also sent his eldest boy and Merton into the trees. Old Jacox was told to get the grass he had cut into as good shape as possible before the shower. My wife and Mousie left the table standing, and, hastening to the raspberry field, helped Winnie and Bobsey and the other Bagley child to pick the ripest berries. We all worked like beavers till the vivid flashes and great drops drove us to shelter.

Fortunately, the shower came up slowly, and we nearly stripped the cherry trees, carrying the fruit into the house, there to be arranged for market in the neat peck baskets with coarse bagging covers which Mr. Bogart had sent me. The little baskets of raspberries almost covered the barn floor by the time the rain began, but they were safe. At first, the children were almost terrified by the vivid lightning, but this phase of the storm soon passed, and the clouds seemed to settle down for a steady rain.

“ ’Tisn’t goin’ to let up,” said Bagley, after a while. “We might as well jog home now as any time.”

===Chinese===
一声低低的闷雷把我们从饭桌上惊得站了起来。我急忙出去，只见西边涌来一团不祥的乌云。我头一个念头是，大家统统去树莓地，摘到雨把我们赶进屋为止；这当口，巴格利倒显出是个有用的朋友，他拖着脚步蹭上前来，说：“俺要是您，就先摘那些樱桃。雷阵雨一夜就能把樱桃沤烂，您瞧着吧。淋湿了，对树莓倒害处不大。”

他的话让我记起小时候见过的景象——满满一树裂了口、半腐烂的樱桃——我便叫他立刻动手去摘，又打发他的大儿子和默顿上了树。老杰科克斯也得到吩咐，要赶在阵雨之前，把割下的草尽量收拾妥帖。妻子和小耗子撇下饭桌，匆匆赶往树莓地，帮温妮、鲍勃西和巴格利家另一个孩子拣最熟的摘。我们个个像河狸似的卖力苦干，直到耀眼的闪电和大颗的雨点把我们赶进屋里。

所幸阵雨来得慢，我们几乎把樱桃树摘了个精光，把果子搬进屋里，用博加特（Bogart）先生送我的那些带粗麻布盖的齐整配克篮子装盛妥当，预备上市。雨落下来时，一小篮一小篮的树莓差不多铺满了谷仓的地板，好在都安然无恙。起初，孩子们几乎被耀眼的闪电吓着，但风暴的这一幕很快就过去了，云层似乎渐渐沉坠下来，化作一场绵绵的稳雨。

“这雨不会停了，”过了一会儿，巴格利说，“俺们这就动身家去，早走晚走都一样。”

===Original===
“But you’ll get wet,” I objected.

“It won’t be the fust time,” answered Bagley. “The children don’t mind it any more’n ducks.”

“Well, let’s settle, then,” I said. “You need some money to buy food at once.”

“I reckon I do,” was the earnest reply.

===Chinese===
“可你们要淋湿的，”我不以为然。

“又不是头一回淋，”巴格利答道，“孩子们不在乎，跟鸭子一个样。”

“那么，咱们就来结账吧，”我说，“你眼下就要钱买吃的。”

“俺寻思是要的，”他恳切地答道。

===Original===
“There’s a dollar for your day’s work, and here is what your children have earned. Are you satisfied?” I asked.

“I be, and I thank you, sir. I’ll go down to the store this evenin’,” he added.

“And buy food only,” I said, with a meaning look.

“Flour and pork only, sir. I’ve given you my hand on’t;” and away they all jogged through the thick-falling drops.

===Chinese===
“这是你一天活儿的一块钱，这里是你孩子们挣下的。你可满意？”我问。

“满意，俺谢谢您，先生。俺今儿傍晚就下铺子去，”他补了一句。

“只买吃食，”我说，递去一道意味深长的目光。

“光买面粉和腌肉，先生。这话俺已经跟您握手为凭了；”说着，他们一家便冒着密密下落的雨点，一路小跑家去了。

===Original===
We packed our fruit for market, and looked vainly for clearing skies in the west.

“There’s no help for it,” I said. “The sooner I start for the landing the better, so that I can return before it becomes very dark.”

My wife exclaimed against this, but I added: “Think a moment, my dear. By good management we have here, safe and in good order, thirty dollars’ worth of fruit, at least. Shall I lose it because I am afraid of a summer shower? Facing the weather is a part of my business; and I’d face a storm any day in the year if I could make thirty dollars.”

===Chinese===
我们把果子装好预备上市，朝西边望了又望，天却没有放晴的意思。

“没有别的法子，”我说，“我越早动身去码头越好，好赶在天完全黑透之前回转。”

妻子听了连连反对，我却接着说：“想一想吧，亲爱的。经营得法，咱们这里已经安安稳稳、齐齐整整地备下了至少值三十块钱的果子。难道要我因为怕一场夏天的阵雨，就把它丢了吗？跟天气打交道，本就是我这一行当里的事；只要能挣三十块钱，一年里哪一天的风雨我都肯去顶。”

===Original===
Merton wished to go also, but I said, “No; there must be no risks of illness that can possibly be avoided.”

I did not find it a dreary expedition, after all, for I solaced myself with thoughts like these, “Thirty dollars, under my wife’s good management, will go far toward providing warm winter clothing, or paying the interest, or something else.”

Then the rain was just what was needed to increase and prolong the yield of the raspberry bushes, on which there were still myriads of immature berries and even blossoms. Abundant moisture would perfect these into plump fruit; and upon this crop rested our main hope.

===Chinese===
默顿也想同去，我说：“不行；凡是能够避免的病痛风险，一概不容有。”

这一趟出门，到头来竟也不觉得阴郁凄清，我一路用这样的念头自宽自解：“三十块钱，经妻子那双巧手调度，置办过冬的寒衣也好，付利息也好，总之能顶大用。”

何况这场雨恰是树莓所需要的，正能使收成更多、采摘期更长——枝头上还有数不清的未熟青果，甚至连花都还开着。水分充足，这些便能长成饱满的果实；我们主要的指望，正寄托在这一茬收成上。

## XXX Weeds and Working for Dear Life / XXX 杂草与拼命劳作

===Original===
The beautiful transition period of spring passing into summer would have filled us with delight had we not found a hostile army advancing on us﻿—annual weeds. When we planted the garden, the soil was brown and clean. The early vegetables came up in well-defined green rows, the weeds appearing with them, too few and scattered to cause anxiety. Now all was changed. Weeds seemed created by magic in a night. The garden was becoming evenly green throughout; and the vegetables, in some cases, could scarcely be distinguished from the ranker growth of crowding, unknown plants among and around them. I also saw that our corn and potato field would soon become, if left alone, as verdant as the meadow beyond. I began to fear that we could not cope with these myriads of foes, little now, but growing while we slept, and stealing a march on us in one part of the place while we destroyed them in another.

With something like dismay I called Mr. Jones’s attention to these silent forces, invading, not only the garden and fields, but the raspberries and, indeed, all the ground now devoted to fruit.

He laughed and said: “The Philistines are on you, sure enough. I’m busy whackin’ them over myself, but I guess I’ll have to come and give you a lift, for you must get these weeds well under before hayin’ and raspberry-pickin’ time comes. It’s warm today, and the ground’s middlin’ dry. I’ll show you what can be done in short metre. By the way, I’ll give you a little wrinkle worth knowin’. I’ve observed that you didn’t bring the children to the country to be like weeds﻿—just ter grow and run ter seed, ye know. It’s ’stonishin’ how soon weeds, whether they’re people or pusley, get seedy. Well, now, call the children and come with me to the garden.”

===Chinese===
春去夏来的这段美丽时节，本该叫我们满心欢喜，无奈我们发现一支敌军正步步进逼——那便是一年生的杂草。我们下种的时候，泥土还褐黑而洁净；早熟菜蔬出了苗，一行行绿线清清楚楚，杂草虽然也跟着冒头，却稀稀拉拉，不足以叫人担心。如今却全变了样。杂草仿佛一夜之间凭魔法生了出来。整座园子眼看着绿成浑然一片；有些地方的菜苗几乎辨认不出，全都混在行间和四周那些长得更疯的不知名野草当中。我还看出，我们那块玉米土豆地若听凭下去，要不了多久也会绿得同远处那片草场一般无二。我开始担心，我们对付不了这千千万万的敌人：它们眼下还小，却在我们睡觉的时候一个劲儿地长，我们在这头剿灭它们，它们便在那头偷偷抢了先。

我怀着近乎惊惶的心情，请琼斯先生（Mr. Jones）留意这些无声进犯的大军——它们侵入的不只是菜园和田地，还有树莓地，实在说，凡是如今种着果木的地，一处都没有放过。

他笑道：“你这是叫非利士人（Philistines）围上了，千真万确。我自己这会儿也正忙着收拾他们呢，不过我想我非得来搭把手不可，因为不等割草晒草和采树莓的时节到来，你就得把这些杂草收拾得服服帖帖。今儿天暖，地里的土也算挺干。我来露一手给你瞧瞧，看转眼工夫能干出个什么名堂。对了，我再奉送你一条顶值得知道的窍门。我瞧出来了，你们把孩子们带到乡下来，可不是让他们当杂草的——光知道傻长、结籽去，你明白吧。真是了不得，杂草这东西，不管是人，还是马齿苋，多快就撒起籽来、变得寒碜了。好了，快把孩子们叫上，跟我到园子里去。”

===Original===
We were all soon there, including my wife, who shared my solicitude.

“You see,” resumed Mr. Jones, “that these weakly little rows of carrots, beets, and onions would soon be choked by these weeds, not an inch high yet. The same is true of the corn and peas and other sags. The pertaters are strong enough to take care of themselves for a time, but not long. I see you and Merton have been tryin’ to weed and hoe them out at the same time. Well, you can’t keep up with the work in that way. Take now this bed of beets; the weeds are gettin’ even all over it, and they’re thicker, if anywhere, right in the row, so that it takes a good eye to see the beets. But here they are, and here they run across the bed. Now look at me. One good showin’ is worth all the tellin’ and readin’ from now to Christmas. You see, I begin with my two hands, and pull out all the weeds on each side of the little row, and I pull ’em away from the young beets so as not to disturb them, but to leave ’em standin’ straight and saucy. Careless hands will half pull out the vegetables at the same time with the weeds. I had to strap Junior once before he learned that fact, and it was amazin’ how I helped his eyesight and trained his fingers through his back. Well, now, you see, I’ve cleared out this row of beets half across the bed and the ground for an inch or two on each side of it. I drop the weeds right down in the spaces between the rows, for the sun will dry ’em up before dinnertime. Now I’ll take another row.”

By this time Merton and I were following his example, and in a few moments a part of three more rows had been treated in the same way.

===Chinese===
我们很快便都到了园里，我妻子也来了，她同我一样悬心。

“你瞧，”琼斯先生接着说，“胡萝卜、甜菜、洋葱这些细弱的秧垄，眼看就要叫这些还不满一寸高的杂草闷死了。玉米、豌豆和别的菜蔬也是一样。土豆倒是够壮实，一时半会儿能自己照应自己，可也撑不了多久。我看出来了，你跟默顿（Merton）想把拔草、锄地两样活儿一块儿干。嗐，这么干，这活儿你永远赶不完。就拿这畦甜菜说吧，杂草已经长得满畦都是，齐齐整整，要说哪里最稠，恰恰就在垄上，不长一双好眼力，简直找不出甜菜在哪儿。可它们就在这儿，横着穿过整畦。这会儿瞧着我。好好看上一遍示范，比从今儿讲到圣诞节、读到大年夜都顶用。你们瞧，我先动用两只手，把小垄两边的杂草统统拔掉；拔的时候要离开小甜菜下手，别惊动了它们，要让它们直挺挺、精神抖擞地立着。毛手毛脚的人拔草，会连菜苗也一齐半拔出来。小琼斯（Junior）就是挨了我一顿皮带，才把这个道理记牢的；说来也神，我抽他的脊背，倒把他的眼力和手指头都训练出来了。好了，你们瞧，这垄甜菜我已经清到畦中央，垄两边一两寸的地面也拾掇干净了。拔下来的草，我就撂在行间的空地上，不出晌午，日头就会把它们晒蔫。现在我来再清一垄。”

这工夫，默顿和我已经照他的样子做起来，不多时，又有三垄各清出了一段，如法炮制。

===Original===
“Now,” continued Mr. Jones, “the weeds are all out of the rows that we’ve done, and for a little space on each side of ’em. The beets have a chance to grow unchoked, and to get ahead. These other little green varmints in the ground, between the rows, are too small to do any harm yet. Practically the beets are cleaned out, and will have all the ground they need to themselves for three or four days; but these weeds between the rows would soon swamp everything. Now, give me a hoe, and I’ll fix *them*.”

He drew the useful tool carefully and evenly through the spaces between the rows, and our enemies were lying on their sides ready to wither away in the morning sun.

“You see after the rows are weeded out how quickly you can hoe the spaces between ’em,” my neighbor concluded. “Now the children can do this weedin’. Your and Merton’s time’s too valyble. When weeds are pulled from right in and around vegetables, the rest can stand without harm for a while, till you can get around with the hoe and cultivator. This weedin’ out business is ’specially important in rainy weather, for it only hurts ground to hoe or work it in wet, showery days, and the weeds don’t mind it a bit. Warm, sunny spells, when the soil’s a little dry, is the time to kill weeds. But you must be careful in weedin’ then, or you’ll so disturb the young, tender sass that it’ll dry up, too. See, I’ll pull some weeds carelessly. Now obsarve that the beets are half jerked up also. Of course that won’t answer. I’ll come over this afternoon with my cultivator, and we’ll tackle the corn and pertaters, and make such a swath among these green Philistines that you’ll sleep better tonight. But ye’re goin’ to come out right, mind, I tell ye so; and I’ve seen mor’n one city squash come to the country with the idee that they were goin’ to beat us punkins all holler.”

===Chinese===
“好，”琼斯先生又说道，“咱们清过的这几垄，垄上和垄边一小片地里的杂草，全数除净了。甜菜得着机会，能不受闷、一个劲儿往前长了。行间土里那些绿色的小坏蛋，眼下还小，还成不了气候。实际上，甜菜畦就算清理完了，往后三四天，这块地尽够它们独享；可行间这些杂草要是撒起野来，用不了多久就会把一切统统淹没。来，把锄头递给我，看我收拾*它们*。”

他把这称手的家伙沿着行间又匀又稳地拉过去，我们的敌人便齐齐躺倒，只等上午的日头把它们晒枯。

“你瞧，垄里的草一拔净，行间的空地锄起来多么快当，”我的邻居收尾道。“眼下这种拔草的活儿，尽可以交给孩子们。你跟默顿的工夫金贵着呢。菜棵正当间和紧挨着的杂草一拔掉，其余的暂且立着也碍不了事，等你腾出手来，再用锄头和中耕机去收拾。赶下雨的天气，这种拔草除苗的事就格外要紧，因为地皮湿漉漉的日子去锄地去动土，糟蹋的只是地，杂草倒满不在乎。暖和晴朗、土面微干的时候，才是杀草的节令。不过这当口拔草也须留神，不然会把娇嫩的菜苗搅动得连菜也跟着蔫掉。瞧着，我毛手毛脚拔几棵给你们看。看见没有，甜菜也叫半拽起来了。这自然不成话。今天下午我带上中耕机过来，咱们去对付玉米和土豆，在这些绿色的非利士人当中杀出一条大路来，保管你今儿夜里睡得香甜。不过你们横竖会顺顺当当地成事，记住我的话；我见过的城里嫩瓜还少么——一个个跑到乡下来，总当真能把我们这些老南瓜打得一败涂地。”

===Original===
And he left us laughing and hopeful.

“Come, Winnie and Bobsey, begin here on each side of me. I’ll show you this morning and then I trust you can be left to do the weeding carefully by yourselves tomorrow. Pressing as the work is, you shall have your afternoons until the berries are ripe.”

“Can’t I help, too?” asked Mousie.

I looked into her eager, wistful face, but said, firmly: “Not now, dear. The sun is too hot. Toward night, perhaps, I’ll let you do a little. By helping mamma in the house you are doing your part.”

We made good progress, and the two younger children speedily learned the knack of working carefully, so as not to disturb the little vegetables. I soon found that weeding was back-aching work for me, and therefore “spelled” myself by hoeing out the spaces between the rows. By the time the music of the dinner bell sounded, hosts of our enemies were slain.

===Chinese===
他大笑着走了，留给我们一腔指望。

“来，温妮（Winnie）、鲍勃西（Bobsey），就在我两边这儿动手。今天上午我做给你们看，明天我想就能放心让你们自己仔细地拔草了。活儿虽然急迫，果子熟透之前，每天下午照旧归你们。”

“我也帮点忙不行吗？”小耗子（Mousie）问。

我望着她那热切而怅然的小脸，却口气坚决地说：“现在不行，亲爱的。日头太毒。也许傍晚可以让你干一点儿。你在家里帮妈妈做事，也就是出了你的一份力了。”

我们进展顺当，两个小的很快学会了细心干活儿的诀窍，不再惊扰那些小小的菜苗。我很快发觉，拔草这活儿对我是实实的折腰苦役，便轮换着给自己“换班”，去锄行间的空地。待到午饭铃声悠扬地响起来，我们的敌人已经尸横遍野。

===Original===
Mr. Jones, true to his promise, was on hand at one o’clock with his cultivator, and began with the corn, which was now a few inches high. Merton and I followed with hoes, uncovering the tender shoots on which earth had been thrown, and dressing out the soil into clean flat hills. As our neighbor had said, it was astonishing how much work the horse cultivator performed in a short time. I saw that it would be wise for us, another year, to plant in a way that would permit the use of horsepower. Even in the garden this method should be followed as far as possible.

Mr. Jones was not a man of halfway measures. He remained helping us, till he had gone through the corn, once each way, twice between the long rows of potatoes, then twice through all the raspberry rows, giving us two full days of his time altogether.

I handed him a dollar in addition to his charge, saying that I had never paid out money with greater satisfaction.

===Chinese===
琼斯先生言而有信，一点钟便带着中耕机来了，先从玉米下手——玉米如今已有几寸高。默顿和我跟在后面锄，把先前培了土的嫩芽扒出来，再把泥土拢成干净平整的圆垛。果然如邻居所说，马拉中耕机短工夫里干出的活儿令人吃惊。我看明白了，来年下种，我们该讲个章法，好让畜力也使得上。就是菜园里，这法子也应尽量照办。

琼斯先生做事从不半途而废。他留下来帮我们，直把玉米地纵横各过了一遍，又在长长的土豆垄间来回锄了两遍，随后把所有的树莓垄也过了两遍，前后一共搭上了整整两天工夫。

我在他的工钱之外又递给他一块钱，说我掏钱从来没有这么痛快过。

===Original===
