“Well,” he said, with a short, dry laugh, “I’ll take it this time, for my work is sufferin’ at home, but I didn’t want you to get discouraged. Now, keep the hoes flyin’, and you’re ahead once more. Junior’s at it early and late, I can tell ye.”

“So I supposed, for we’ve missed him.”

“Good reason. When I’m through with him he’s ready enough to crawl into his little bed.”

So were we for a few days, in our winning fight with the weeds. One hot afternoon, about three o’clock, I saw that Merton was growing pale, and beginning to lag, and I said, decidedly: “Do you see that tree there? Go and lie down under it till I call you.”

===Chinese===
“呃，”他干干地短笑一声，“这回我就收下，家里我自己的活儿实在耽搁不起了。不过我不愿看着你灰心。好生让锄头飞起来吧，你们就又跑到前头去了。小琼斯起早贪黑地干着呢，我跟你说。”

“我也这么猜，因为他好久没在我们这儿露面了。”

“那还用说。等我跟他交代完，他也巴不得一头钻进自己的小床。”

接连几天，在这场节节得胜的除草之战里，我们也是一挨床便睡熟了。一个炎热的下午，约莫三点钟，我见默顿脸色渐渐发白，手脚也慢了下来，便断然说：“看见那边那棵树没有？去，躺到树底下，我叫你再起来。”

===Original===
“I guess I can stand it till night,” he began, his pride a little touched.

“Obey orders! I am captain.”

In five minutes he was fast asleep. I threw my coat over him, and sat down, proposing to have a half hour’s rest myself. My wife came out with a pitcher of cool buttermilk and nodded her head approvingly at us.

===Chinese===
“我想我撑得到天黑，”他开口道，自尊心多少有点受不住。

“服从命令！我是队长。”

不到五分钟，他已经酣然睡熟。我把外衣搭在他身上，坐了下来，打算自己也歇上半个钟头。妻子端着一罐凉津津的白脱牛奶走出来，朝我们俩赞许地点了点头。

===Original===
“Well, my thoughtful Eve,” I said, “I find that our modern Eden will cost a great many backaches.”

“If you will only be prudent like this, you may save me a heartache. Robert, you are ambitious, and unused to this kind of work. Please don’t ever be so foolish as to forget the comparative value of vegetables and yourselves. Honestly now” (with one of her saucy looks), “I’d rather do with a few bushels less, than do without you and Merton;” and she sat down and kept me idle for an hour.

Then Merton got up, saying that he felt as “fresh as if he had had a night’s rest,” and we accomplished more in the cool of the day than if we had kept doggedly at work.

===Chinese===
“唉，我这体贴的夏娃（Eve），”我说，“我发现，我们这个现代的伊甸园，要叫人赔上数不清的腰酸背痛呢。”

“只要你肯这样爱惜自己，也就省得我心疼了。罗伯特（Robert），你有志气，却干不惯这种活儿。求你千万别傻到忘了菜蔬跟你们爷儿俩相比，究竟哪个值钱。老实说吧”（她递来一个娇嗔的眼色），“我宁可少收几蒲式耳，也不肯少了你和默顿；”说着她坐下身来，硬叫我闲了一个钟头。

随后默顿爬起来，说自己觉得“神清气爽，就像睡过了一整夜”；趁着一天里凉快的时辰，我们干出的活儿，反比一味死撑着干要多。

===Original===
I found that Winnie and Bobsey required rather different treatment. For a while they got on very well, but one morning I set them at a bed of parsnips about which I was particular. In the middle of the forenoon I went to the garden to see how they were getting on. Shouts of laughter made me fear that all was not well, and I soon discovered that they were throwing lumps of earth at each other. So absorbed were they in their untimely and mischievous fun that I was not noticed until I found Bobsey sitting plump on the vegetables, and the rows behind both the children very shabbily cleaned, not a few of the little plants having been pulled up with the weeds.

Without a word I marched them into the house, then said: “Under arrest till night. Winnie, you go to your room. I shall strap Bobsey in his chair, and put him in the parlor by himself.”

The exchange of the hot garden for the cool rooms seemed rather an agreeable punishment at first, although Winnie felt the disgrace somewhat. When, at dinner, nothing but a cup of water and a piece of dry bread was taken to them, Bobsey began to howl, and Winnie to look as if the affair was growing serious. Late in the afternoon, when she found that she was not to gather the eggs or feed her beloved chickens, she, too, broke down and sobbed that she “wouldn’t do so any more.” Bobsey also pleaded so piteously for release, and promised such saintlike behavior, that I said: “Well, I will remit the rest of your punishment and put you on trial. You had no excuse for your mischief this morning, for I allow you to play the greater part of every afternoon, while Merton must stand by me the whole of the week.”

===Chinese===
我发觉，温妮和鲍勃西却须另有一套管法。起初一阵，他们干得蛮好；可一天早晨，我把他们派去侍弄一畦我顶讲究的欧防风。上午过了一半，我到园里看看他们干得怎样。一阵阵哄笑叫我心里直打鼓，只怕情形不妙；很快便发现，两个孩子正拿土坷垃互相投掷呢。他们玩得不合时宜，又闹得入迷，竟谁也没察觉我来到，直到我看见鲍勃西一屁股结结实实坐在了菜苗上，两个孩子身后的几垄拔得七零八落，不少小苗连着杂草一齐遭了殃。

我一言不发，把他们押进屋去，然后说：“禁闭到天黑。温妮，回你自己的屋子去。鲍勃西，我这就把你绑在你的椅子上，放到客厅里一个人待着。”

从滚烫的园子里换到凉快的屋里，这惩罚起初倒像桩美事，虽说温妮多少也觉出了丢脸。到了饭时，送去给他们吃的只有一杯清水和一块干面包，鲍勃西立刻嚎啕起来，温妮的神色也像是觉得事情严重了。傍晚时候，她发现自己既不能去捡鸡蛋，也不能去喂她心爱的那些鸡，也终于垮了下来，抽抽噎噎地说她“再不敢了”。鲍勃西央求放他出来时那副可怜样儿，加上满口圣徒一般的保证，叫我说道：“好吧，剩下的处罚给你们免了，姑且放你们出来，看你们往后的表现。今天早晨闹出那样的祸，可没有什么借口好讲——每天下午我本就许你们玩上一大半，默顿却得整整个星期跟在我身边。”

===Original===
My touch of discipline brought up the *morale* of my little squad effectually for a time. The next afternoon even the memory of trouble was banished by the finding of the first wild strawberries. Exultation and universal interest prevailed as clusters of green and red berries were handed around to be smelled and examined. “Truly,” my wife remarked, “even roses can scarcely equal the fragrance of the wild strawberry.”

From that day forward, for weeks, it seemed as if we entered on a diet of strawberries and roses. The old-fashioned bushes of the latter, near the house, had been well trimmed, and gave large, fine buds in consequence, while Mousie, Winnie, and Bobsey gleaned every wild berry that could be found, beginning with the sunny upland slopes and following the aromatic fruit down to the cool, moist borders of the creek.

“Another year,” I said, “I think you will be tired even of strawberries, for we shall have to pick early and late.”

===Chinese===
我这一番小小的惩戒，一时间着实提振了这支小队伍的*士气*。第二天下午，连祸事的记忆也被抛到了九霄云外——头一茬野草莓找到了。一簇簇青红相间的果子递到各人手里，大家又闻又端详，欢腾雀跃，人人入迷。“真是的，”妻子说，“就连玫瑰，也未必赛得过野草莓的香气呢。”

从那天起，一连好几个星期，我们仿佛顿顿吃的是草莓和玫瑰。房前屋后那些老式玫瑰丛修整得很好，因此绽出的花苞又大又美；小耗子、温妮和鲍勃西则把见得着的野草莓搜拾得一干二净，先从向阳的高坡起步，又循着这芬芳的果子，一路采到穆德纳溪清凉湿润的岸边。

“到明年，”我说，“我想你们连草莓也会吃腻的，因为我们就得从早到晚地摘了。”
